# Project State

This file is the running status of Relii. Read it at the start of every session.

==> **HOW TO READ THIS FILE, AND IT IS A RULE ABOUT THE FILE ITSELF (owner ruling, 2026-09-15). PROJECT_STATE IS AN ACCRETING HISTORICAL RECORD, NOT A CURRENT-STATE DATABASE.** A number in it is true **as of its own date** and may be superseded further down the file **without either entry saying so**. Neither entry is wrong; they are snapshots of different days sitting in the same document. <==

==> **SO: MEASURE THE PRESENT VALUE. CITE THIS FILE FOR HISTORY AND REASONING.** Use it to learn what a thing is, why it was done, and what it cost. Do not use it as the source for what a count is right now. <==

==> **AND CITE THIS FILE BY ITS TEXT, NEVER BY LINE NUMBER (owner ruling, 2026-09-16).** This file accretes at the TOP, in Current Stage, so every line below moves with every session that writes here. **A line number quoted from it is guaranteed to go stale.** Quote the words, or name the heading, and find them by search. <==

### AFTER THE GATE: THE ORDER THE OWNER SET, AND WHY (rulings, 2026-09-16)

1. **REGION PIN: APPROVED, FIRST, AND ALONE. `pdx1`, NOT `sfo1`.** Supabase is `us-west-2`, which is Oregon, which is `pdx1`; `sfo1` is `us-west-1`, a different AWS region. **No code rode along, so the improvement can be attributed.**
2. **THE ADMIN N+1: APPROVED REGARDLESS OF WHAT THE PIN SHOWED.** 36 sequential counts is an algorithmic defect, not a latency one: at 10 ms a trip it is still 360 ms, and it grows with every class.
3. **PARALLELISING THE LIST PAGES: NOT YET. DECIDE AFTER THE REGION RE-MEASURE.** Its value is proportional to trip latency, and it edits the code that enforces class scoping, the riskiest code in this piece.
4. **SPEEDUP (a), profile and class list together inside `requireInstructor()`: RE-ASSESS WITH (3), ONLY IF THE NUMBERS JUSTIFY IT.**
5. **SPEEDUP (b), NOT RUNNING `requireInstructor()` TWICE ON A FULL LOAD: MEASURED AND DECLINED.** The duplicate trips run at the same moment as the originals (identical start and end offsets in every full-load trip list), so removing them saves Supabase calls but close to no waiting. **Recorded as declined, not left open.**

### WAS THE PROXY'S LOGIN CHECK ALREADY CROSSING FROM DC TO OREGON BEFORE THE PIN? YES. PRE-EXISTING, EXPOSED BY THE PIN, NOT CAUSED BY IT.

**Answered from this session's own measurements, with no auth code changed. The deployment logs were not read**: the Vercel CLI is denied in this repo, and the measurements answered it without them.

**THE REGION.** The proxy reports `VERCEL_REGION` in its own header, and **every sample from the first one at 16:22:44 UTC, two and a half hours before the pin, read `region=iad1`.** Supabase has been in Oregon throughout. **So the login check crossed the continent before the pin exactly as it does after it.** The pin moved the functions, and the docs say Routing Middleware does not follow the region setting, which the unchanged header confirms.

**ITS DURATION, THE SAME CALL ON THE SAME PATH, BEFORE AND AFTER:**

| Samples | n | Median | Range |
| :-- | --: | --: | :-- |
| Warm full loads, before the pin | 15 | **130 ms** | 106 to 423 |
| Warm full loads, on the pinned build | 15 | 163 ms | 105 to 347 |
| **Warm full loads, on the live build, after the pin** | 15 | **126 ms** | 92 to 320 |
| Warm clicks, before the pin | 15 | 120 ms | 92 to 349 |
| Warm clicks, on the pinned build | 15 | 272 ms | 109 to 478 |
| Warm clicks, on the live build | 5 | 335 ms | 108 to 355 |

**The full-load medians before and after are the same (130 and 126 ms), so the pin did not make the login check itself slower.** The click medians after the pin are higher, and nothing in the pin touches that call's path, so **that difference is recorded as unexplained variation between runs taken hours apart, not attributed to the pin.** No interleaved before-and-after exists, because the pin cannot be switched per request.

==> **THE ONE COST THE PIN DID ADD IS SMALL, AND IT IS NOT THE LOGIN CHECK.** Before the pin the proxy in DC handed each request to a function also in DC; now it hands it across the country to Oregon. On clicks, the time that is neither the proxy's login check nor the page's own trips (the whole response, less those two) **rose from a median of 140 ms (126 to 223, n 15) to 181 ms on the pinned build (157 to 249, n 15) and 171 ms on the live build (161 to 223, n 5): about 30 to 40 ms.** That is consistent with the new DC to Oregon hop. **It is an estimate: the instrument cannot separate that hop from rendering and serialisation.** Against it, the pin typically took about 60 to 240 ms off each of the page's own trips. <==

| Cost | Measured | Why it was not attempted |

| **The proxy's login check** | **109 to 478 ms** on warm clicks on the pinned build; 0.66 to 0.76 s on a cold load, 1.2 s when the hourly session refresh lands on the same request | **It sits in auth verification, which this project does not open without its own piece** (owner ruling) |

### THE REGION PIN, ALONE

**CONFIRMED FROM VERCEL'S DOCS BEFORE RELYING ON IT, QUOTED:**

| Question | Vercel's words | Page |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Does Hobby allow a region other than the default? | Plan table: **"Hobby: Single region"**; "Pro: 5 regions"; "Enterprise: All regions" | `vercel.com/docs/functions/configuring-functions/region` |
| Is `pdx1` Oregon? | **"pdx1, us-west-2, Portland, USA"**; and "sfo1, us-west-1, San Francisco, USA" | `vercel.com/docs/regions` |
| What was the app on? | "The default Function region is Washington, D.C., USA (`iad1`) for all new projects." | the region page |
| The syntax | `"regions": ["sfo1"]` in `vercel.json` (the docs' own example) | the region page |
| **Does the proxy follow it?** | **"Vercel deploys Routing Middleware to all regions by default, regardless of your region settings. On the Hobby plan, Routing Middleware runs in fewer regions."** | the region page |

**THE CHANGE.** `vercel.json`, new, three keys: the schema and `"regions": ["pdx1"]`. Commit `73c0125`.

==> **PROVED LIVE BY AN OBSERVATION ONLY THE PIN CAN PRODUCE.** The new etag `f00dcd182a0cd5f65aa5ff1fe979969a` appeared at 18:51:45 UTC, and the first page load after it answered **`x-vercel-id: cle1::pdx1::wwtdc-1789584715750-e0bb5f71aabb`** with the instrument reading **`region: pdx1`**. Every page load and click afterwards read `pdx1`. <==

==> **AND THE PROXY DID NOT MOVE, EXACTLY AS THE DOCS WARNED: IT STILL REPORTS `region=iad1`.** So every request still starts with a login check made from Washington DC to Oregon, and is then handed across the country to the function. **That is now the largest single server-side cost on a warm click** (see below). <==

**Every cold sample on the pin was certified by the counter** (0 renders; copy 6, 37, 6, 8 and 30 ms old; `/admin` 13 ms, with a second fresh copy at 2,985). **Two warm samples on the pin were set aside** as first renders on copies already running (Invite Users 754 ms, code loaded 33.8 s earlier; Manage Assignments 838 ms, 41.1 s), which is why those two medians are of two runs. **The fix's cold `/admin` was the first request after its deploy** (0 renders, 18 ms). The idle gap before each cold sample was about 17 minutes from the last request, and one timer stopped by the Mac for low memory was finished out with no request in between.

**CHECK 3, EVERY PAGE STILL SHOWS EXACTLY THE SAME DATA, BY HASH.** On the pin: all five B1 pages identical to their baseline hashes, and `/admin` identical (`867ac044e379ce25`, 37 lines). On the fix: `/admin` identical again, in production and in a local render beforehand. **Nothing from Tom's class was captured: `/admin` was only ever hashed, whole page.**

### WHAT THE RE-MEASURE SAYS

1. **THE PIN CUT EVERY TRIP BY ROUGHLY TWO THIRDS OR MORE, BUT NOT TO SINGLE-DIGIT MILLISECONDS.** Warm trips from `pdx1` measure **about 25 to 95 ms**, with occasional outliers near 190 (the admin counts averaged 59 ms cold), against about 90 to 330 ms from `iad1`. **The expectation of single-digit ms was reasonable and the measurement did not bear it out.** Supabase sits behind Cloudflare, so a request still leaves AWS, reaches Cloudflare and comes back; that is the likeliest reason, and it is labelled as a likely reason rather than a measured one.
2. **WARM FULL LOADS FELL BY 43 TO 67 PERCENT ON THE INSTRUCTOR PAGES (Roster least, Manage Assignments most) AND 75 PERCENT ON `/admin`; WARM CLICKS IMPROVED LESS, AND THE TRIP LISTS SAY WHY.** On a warm click the page's own trips now total only **83 to 511 ms**, while the proxy's login check from `iad1` takes **109 to 478 ms** on the same clicks. **The proxy is now the largest single server-side cost of a warm click.**
3. **COLD LOADS FELL BY 0.7 TO 1.7 SECONDS ON THE THREE PAGES WHERE NEITHER SAMPLE CARRIED A SESSION REFRESH (Invite Users 0.68, AI Persona Framework 1.07, Submissions 1.72), NOT MORE, BECAUSE MOST OF A COLD LOAD WAS NEVER TRIPS.** Roster (refresh before) fell 1.72 and Manage Assignments (refresh after) 0.42, and neither is a clean comparison. On the pin, the gap between the last trip ending and the page arriving, less the proxy's check, is **1.6 to 2.3 seconds on every cold sample**: server start-up and first render, which the pin does not touch.
4. **THE ADMIN FIX DID WHAT IT WAS FOR, INDEPENDENT OF THE PIN.** Trips counted **43 → 12** in the render (**44 → 13** with the proxy), and the count reads are now **5 whatever the number of classes**, sent together (they leave within 7 ms of each other in the trip list). Warm `/admin` went **2,579 → 701 ms** on top of the pin; cold **4,317 → 2,103**.

### THE COLD-LOAD EXPECTATION, STATED PLAINLY SO NOBODY READS IT AS A FAILURE LATER (owner instruction)

==> **ABOUT 1.6 TO 2.3 SECONDS OF EVERY COLD LOAD IS SERVER START-UP, AND NOTHING IN THIS SESSION TOUCHES IT.** <==

**The owner's expectation was that a cold load would land near 2 seconds after all of this. The measurement says about 3.5 seconds on the instructor pages, and the record follows the measurement.** The split, read off the cold trip lists on the pin: roughly **2 seconds of start-up**, **0.66 to 0.76 seconds of the proxy's login check from `iad1`** (1.2 when the hourly session refresh lands on the same request), and **about 0.9 to 1.1 seconds of the page's own trips on a fresh copy**, whose first two trips are slow (the profile read 333 to 423 ms) while its connections are new. **Warm pages are now under a second. A cold instructor page is about 3.5 seconds, and that is the expected result of this work, not a shortfall of it.** Moving the remaining cold seconds would mean the start-up or the proxy, and neither is in scope.

**That is about 5 to 13 percent of a warm click and about 2 to 4 percent of a cold load**, bought by editing the pages and the one function that decide which class an instructor sees. **The larger remaining costs are elsewhere: server start-up (about 2 s cold) and the proxy's login check from `iad1` (about 0.1 to 0.5 s warm, 0.7 cold).** Each of those is its own decision with its own risk, and neither is proposed here as work.

### THE ADMIN COUNT FIX (`7f08560`)

**WHAT CHANGED.** `app/admin/page.tsx` only. The per-class loop of six head-only counts is replaced by `readAllRows()`, which reads the `class_id` column of `rubrics`, `scenarios`, `assignments`, `sessions` (with `video_path`, for recordings) and `allowed_emails` once each, sent together, and a tally in code. **The labels are created in the same order the loop printed them in**, which the unchanged page hash confirms.

**THE TRUNCATION GUARD, WHICH THE OLD COMMENT WAS RIGHT TO WORRY ABOUT.** Each read asks for the exact count in the same response, pages by primary key until it holds that many rows, and **throws if it comes up short or the read fails.** **One behaviour change, deliberate and stated:** the old loop rendered `0` for a failed count; the page now shows an error instead of a confidently wrong number.

**VERIFIED BEFORE PUSHING, ON LIVE DATA, PRINTING ONLY BOOLEANS AND REQUEST COUNTS:**

- **The shipped function, extracted from `page.tsx` verbatim and transpiled**, run at page size 1000 and at page size 2 on all five tables: **the same rows both ways, equal to the exact count on every table**, with 1 request at 1000 and 3 to 27 requests at 2, so the multi-page path really ran.
- **Both refusal branches exercised**: a short read throws `Read 0 of 5 rows...`, and a read error throws `Could not read x for the class counts: boom`.
- **The page built locally and rendered against live data: 12 trips, and byte-identical to the production baseline** (37 lines, `867ac044e379ce25`, zero differing lines).

**`tsc` 0, `eslint .` 0, `npm run build` 0** after `rm -rf .next`, 20:21:10 to 20:21:27 UTC, on the tree at `8a8960a`, whose only code change over the pin is `7f08560`. The one Turbopack warning is the pre-existing one naming `next.config.ts`. **Five check scripts: 54 / 10 / 53 / 23 / 45, all exit 0, not one count moved.**

**PROVED LIVE.** New etag `44bbe4e06d65ad6fa93bf1862350bec9` at 20:41:19 UTC; **the first `/admin` load after it counted 12 trips, which only the fix can produce.**

### THE ADMIN-CREDENTIALS EXCEPTION, AFTER THE GATE

**Used for `/admin` only, three more times, and nowhere else:** the post-pin cold and warm loads, the post-fix cold and warm loads, and **once against the LOCAL build of the fix** (`localhost:3457/admin`), to prove the page hash before pushing. **That local load is the same page reading the same data, and it is named here so it is not found later and read as a breach.** Every sign-in blocked `/` and `/instructor`. **The browser's cookies for the account were cleared from both `localhost` and the live site when the admin work ended.** ==> **CLOSED, AND CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED (2026-09-16).** Before this entry was committed, every cookie left in the session's browser was listed and its login token decoded: **exactly one cookie remains in the whole browser, on `relii.vercel.app`, and it belongs to `instructor@test.com`. None belongs to the admin account, on the live site or on `localhost`.** The exception authorised nothing beyond this session, and this session is done using it. <==

==> **WHAT THE TABLE SAYS ABOUT THE COMPLAINT, IN ONE PLACE. THE WARM NUMBERS DO NOT REPRODUCE IT; THE COLD ONES DO.** The owner reports 2 to 6 seconds on every instructor page. **Warm full loads measured 1.2 to 1.5 s and warm clicks 0.6 to 1.3 s, all below his range. Every cold full load, 4.2 to 5.2 s, lands inside it.** An instructor who opens the app now and then is usually the first request in a while, so the cold path is the normal experience rather than an edge case. **Even an idle-but-warm copy was slow**: the two samples excluded below for landing on warm copies still took 3.1 and 4.3 s, with their first trips at about 300 to 1,289 ms. <==

**WHAT THE PLANNED FIX CAN AND CANNOT MOVE ON THAT COLD PATH.** It CAN move the trips: 3.2 to 4.5 s of trip time on a cold load, where each trip costs 3 to 5 times what it costs warm. It CANNOT move the roughly 1.7 to 1.9 s between the last trip ending and the page arriving, which is the copy starting up and rendering for the first time. **So the honest claim for the fix is fewer and shorter waits, not a fast cold page.**

**THE REGION, AS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN AS "THE LARGEST LEVER".** Postgres does 1 to 2 ms of each trip (the six admin counts together: 1.07 ms; Supabase's gateway: 1 or 2 ms on 19 of 20 requests). A warm trip from `iad1` takes about 90 to 330 ms. **So about 95% or more of every trip is the journey between Washington DC and Oregon**, and a page pays it once per trip: 13 times on a Submissions load, 9 times on a Submissions click, 44 times on `/admin`. **A function in Oregon (`pdx1`) runs in the same AWS region as the database, so each trip would likely cost tens of milliseconds rather than hundreds. That is an estimate from the measured split, not a measurement**, and if it holds, pinning the region would cut every trip on every page with no code change, plausibly more than the two code fixes together on the instructor pages. It remains the owner's call.

==> **FIVE SAMPLES WERE KEPT OUT OF BOTH COLUMNS, BECAUSE THE COUNTER SAID THEY WERE NEITHER CLEANLY COLD NOR CLEANLY WARM. THEY ARE LISTED, NOT AVERAGED IN** (owner instruction: a contaminated cold number is the one most likely to flatter the after-fix comparison). <==

| `/instructor` | 2,335 | 0 before, but the code loaded 1,081 ms earlier | A fresh copy that appeared during the warm burst, started by another request; neither a cold start for this request nor warm |
| `/admin` | 11,428 | 0 before, code loaded 17.9 s earlier | The first render on a copy that was already up |
| `/admin` | 8,871 | 0 before, code loaded 40.7 s earlier | Same |

**HOW COLD WAS PRODUCED, AND WHAT IT TOOK.** An idle gap of about 17 minutes with no request from this session, then one load; the counter decided. **10.5 minutes was not enough once**, and **a 17-minute gap was spoiled once by other traffic.** Two idle timers were stopped by the Mac for low memory and restarted with no request in between; each gap is recorded from the last request, not from the timer.

### THE REGION, VERBATIM, AND IT IS A MISMATCH

| Request | `x-vercel-id` | What it shows |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |

==> **SUPABASE IS `us-west-2`, OREGON. THE APP'S SERVER CODE RUNS IN WASHINGTON DC. EVERY ROUND TRIP BELOW CROSSES THE CONTINENT.** <==

**NO REGION IS PINNED ANYWHERE IN THE REPO.** No `vercel.json`, no `.vercel` folder, an empty `next.config.ts`, and a search for `preferredRegion` or `regions` across `app`, `lib`, `components`, `next.config.ts`, `proxy.ts` and `package.json` returns nothing. **A Vercel dashboard setting cannot be read from here** (the CLI is denied in this repo), but the header shows the region actually in effect.

### THE INSTRUMENT, AND WHAT PROVED IT LIVE

**WHAT IT IS.** `lib/server-timing.ts` hands both Supabase clients a pass-through `fetch` that records every round trip to Supabase, database reads and login checks alike, with its start and end offset. `components/server-timing-report.tsx` writes those into each measured layout and page as a JSON block the browser never runs. The proxy reports its own login check as a real `Server-Timing` header. **Offsets rather than durations, because the layout and the page render at the same time and durations alone make parallel look serial.** Trip descriptions carry table names, selected columns and filter NAMES only, never a value.

**A PER-COPY COUNTER DECIDES WHAT "COLD" MEANS.** Each report carries `storesBefore` (how many page renders this server copy had measured before this one) and `instanceAgeMs` (how long since this copy loaded the module). `storesBefore 0` with an age of a few milliseconds is a copy that started for this request. **A sample is labelled cold only when the counter says so, not because time passed.**

**"END" MEANS THE RESPONSE HEADERS ARRIVED, NOT THE BODY.** Reading the body inside a pass-through would have meant rebuilding the response, which a pass-through must not do. These bodies are a few kilobytes.

**PROVED INERT WITH THE FLAG OFF, BEFORE IT REACHED PRODUCTION.** The flag was already on in production when this was written, so a broken instrument would have broken the live site for Tom's students. **One local production build was run both ways**: flag on, the page carried its reports, a click navigation carried its report, and an API route that creates both clients outside a page render (`/api/instructor/class/select`) returned 200; flag off, the same build sent no `Server-Timing` header and rendered zero report blocks. `tsc` 0, `eslint .` 0, and the five check scripts 54 / 10 / 53 / 23 / 45 before the push.

==> **THE FLAG TOOK EFFECT, PROVED BY TWO OBSERVATIONS ONLY THE NEW BUILD WITH THE FLAG ON CAN MAKE.** At **16:22:44 UTC**, `/login` returned a new etag `6a6243f12105df04ed3d2a13cc11c74d` (before the push: `4761bd902136828ab0a27ec7875a0119`) AND the header `server-timing: proxy-auth;dur=0.7;desc="trips=0 region=iad1"`, which no earlier build sends. **Every page load and click afterwards carried its report block**; a single load without one would have been reported. <==

**ONE THING THE OWNER'S FLAG NOTE HAD SLIGHTLY DIFFERENT, RECORDED BECAUSE IT DECIDES HOW THE FLAG IS TURNED OFF.** The flag is read when each request runs, not when the app is built: the same local build rendered reports with it on and none with it off. **On Vercel that changes nothing practical**: a changed variable reaches production only on the next deploy either way.

### THE ROUND TRIPS, AS COUNTED

**Counted by the instrument on every run, not reasoned. The count never varied between runs of the same page.** A full load runs the layout AND the page; a click runs the page only, because Next.js keeps the shared layout on navigation. Both halves were measured.

| Page | Full load: layout | Full load: page | Full load: proxy | **Full load total** | **Click total** (page + proxy) |
| :-- | --: | --: | --: | --: | --: |
| `/instructor/invites` | 4 | 3 | 1 | **8** | **4** |
| `/instructor/scaffold` | 4 | 7 | 1 | **12** | **8** |
| `/instructor/roster` | 4 | 7 | 1 | **12** | **8** |
| `/instructor/assignments` | 4 | 6 | 1 | **11** | **7** |
| `/instructor` | 4 | 8 | 1 | **13** | **9** |
| `/admin` | 2 | 41 | 1 | **44** | 44 (a click from the instructor nav runs the admin layout too) |

**What those trips are, from the instrument's own trip lists:**

- **The proxy, every request:** 1 login check (`GET auth/user`).
- **The instructor layout, full loads only:** login check, profile, class list, consent. Its first three are the SAME three `requireInstructor()` makes in the page, and the trip list shows them in **identical pairs at identical offsets**: they run at the same moment, so the duplicate costs Supabase calls but almost no waiting.
- **`requireInstructor()` in every page:** login check, then profile, then class list. **Three trips, one after another, on every click.**
- **Invite Users:** nothing beyond `requireInstructor()`. **The kickoff's "1 await" is 4 trips on a click and 8 on a full load.**
- **AI Persona Framework:** + scenarios, then sessions and assignments usage together (the one existing `Promise.all`), then assignment titles.
- **Roster:** + class members, then their profiles, then invites, then every profile's email.
- **Manage Assignments:** + scenarios, then assignments, then the session tally.
- **Submissions:** + class members, then their profiles, then assignments, then submissions, then reset history. (The stray-assignment check did not fire: no `?assignment=` was given.)

==> **THE ADMIN ARITHMETIC, COUNTED: 6 CLASSES x 6 COUNT TRIPS = 36, PLUS 7 OTHERS = 43 IN THE RENDER, PLUS 1 IN THE PROXY = 44.** The 7 others are the admin layout's login check and profile, the page's own login check and profile (again in identical parallel pairs), then all profiles, all classes, all memberships. **All 36 counts run one after another**: in the cold run they span 1,511 ms to 9,324 ms, **7.8 seconds, 217 ms per count on average.** <==

**NOTHING RUNS IN PARALLEL ON ANY OF THE SIX PAGES EXCEPT THE LAYOUT-AGAINST-PAGE OVERLAP AND THE ONE `Promise.all` INSIDE THE PERSONA LIST.** Every other trip starts within a few milliseconds of the previous one ending (the largest gap in any list is about 20 ms), in every trip list.

### THE DATABASE SIDE: IT IS NOT THE CAUSE, AND THE NUMBERS SAY SO

**`explain (analyze, buffers)` on all six admin counts for B1, as one statement:** **Execution 1.072 ms in total, planning 2.103 ms**, every buffer a cache hit, each count between 0.012 and 0.096 ms.

**`pg_stat_statements`, accumulated since 2026-06-22 17:13 UTC:**

| What | Calls | Mean execution |
| :-- | --: | :-- |
| PostgREST reads of the app's named tables, grouped by table and role | about 25,000 | **0.04 to 1.31 ms**, worst single run 36 ms |
| PostgREST statements whose table cannot be read from the text (function calls, embedded joins) | about 2,400 | 2.1 to 19.6 ms |
| The five statements Supabase Auth runs per login check (sessions, MFA claims, MFA factors, identities, users) | about 29,000 to 29,700 each | **0.02 to 0.08 ms** |
| PostgREST's per-request setup (`set_config`) | 17,878 service role, 9,471 signed in | 0.03 to 0.08 ms |

==> **SO THE EXPECTATION HELD EXACTLY: EXECUTION TIME IS TINY AND THE CALL COUNT IS LARGE.** A trip measured from Vercel takes about 90 to 330 ms warm and up to 1,213 ms at its worst; Postgres spends one or two of those milliseconds. <==

**AND SUPABASE'S OWN GATEWAY SAYS THE SAME, MEASURED FROM THE OWNER'S MAC RATHER THAN FROM VERCEL, SO IT IS SUPPORTING EVIDENCE ONLY.** Twenty requests over one connection: **91 to 292 ms each, with `x-envoy-upstream-service-time` reading 1 or 2 ms on nineteen of them and 13 on one.** Supabase sits behind Cloudflare, so the request reaches a nearby edge quickly and then travels to Oregon. **About 99% of a trip is that journey.** The first request on a fresh connection took 1,013 ms.

**ONE CURIOSITY, NOT A CAUSE.** PostgREST's schema reload (`SELECT name FROM pg_timezone_names`) averages 816 ms on this instance across 53 calls. It runs only when PostgREST reloads, so it could explain a rare slow request and cannot explain a steady one.

==> **DOES THE DATA SUPPORT A COMPUTE UPGRADE? NO.** Database execution is one to two milliseconds per trip, CPU was 2% on the owner's dashboard, and the seconds are measured in the network and in the serial ordering. **A bigger instance would speed up the one percent. NO UPGRADE WAS MADE.** <==

### WHAT THE MEASUREMENT SAYS, AND WHERE IT DIFFERS FROM THE READING

1. **The kickoff's diagnosis held: the time is round trips, one at a time, across the continent.** Every page's total is close to the sum of its trips.
2. **THE ADMIN PAGE'S COLD START IS NOT WHAT MAKES IT SLOW.** Cold 10,029 ms; the one certified warm run 11,314 ms. **The 43 serial trips are the cost, cold or warm.** The owner's 30 seconds would need slower trips than this session saw; a single login check took 2,248 ms on one run, so they happen.
3. **ON THE INSTRUCTOR PAGES, A COLD COPY IS A REAL SHARE OF THE OWNER'S NUMBER.** **Cold full loads were 4.2 to 5.2 seconds on every instructor page, against 1.2 to 1.5 warm, so a fresh copy costs about 3 to 3.7 seconds more.** The trip lists show where it goes. **About 1.7 to 1.9 seconds belongs to no trip at all**: the gap between the last trip ending and the page arriving is 2.35 to 2.96 s cold against about 0.35 s warm, less the proxy's own check. That is the copy starting up and rendering for the first time, and **no fix in this piece touches it.** The rest is slower trips: **on every cold sample the slowest call was the page's profile read, 569 to 722 ms**, and the trips add up to 3.2 to 4.5 s against 1.3 to 2.1 warm. **That half does shrink with fewer and parallel trips.** Clicks were not measured cold.
4. **"EVERY CALL SETS UP ITS OWN CONNECTION" IS NOT WHAT THE TRIPS LOOK LIKE ON A WARM COPY.** Warm trips of about 90 to 130 ms are faster than a fresh TLS handshake plus a request across the continent could be, so connections are being reused at least some of the time. **The first trips on a cold copy (300 to 720 ms) look like fresh connections.** The instrument cannot see connection reuse directly, so this is an inference from timing, labelled as one.
5. **THE CLICK AND THE FULL LOAD ARE DIFFERENT COSTS, AND THE OWNER'S COMPLAINT IS THE CLICK.** A click skips the layout's 4 trips. Browser navigation timing sees only full loads, so clicks were measured separately (click to the new page's report block appearing).

### THE THREE EXTRA SPEEDUPS, NOW WITH NUMBERS (OWNER'S DECISION, NOT ACTED ON)

| | What | What the measurement says it is worth |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| **(a)** | Read the profile and the class list together inside `requireInstructor()` | **One trip's wait (typically 100 to 300 ms) off every click and every load on all five instructor pages.** It touches the most security-sensitive file, and by owner ruling it gets its own look and its own approval |
| **(b)** | Stop a full load running `requireInstructor()` twice | **Close to nothing in time.** The duplicate trips run at the same moment as the originals (identical offsets). It would halve those Supabase calls, not the wait |
| **(c)** | Pin the function region to Oregon (`pdx1`) | **The largest single lever, and not measured, because it cannot be without deploying there.** Every trip carries roughly 100 ms of cross-country travel against 1 to 2 ms of Supabase work. The owner's call |

### KICKOFF DEFECTS, FOUND AND FIXED

**Three the owner named as corrections to his own kickoff (ruling, 2026-09-16), and recorded here the way the earlier kickoffs' defects were:**

1. **THE `/login` HEADER DOES NOT SHOW THE FUNCTION REGION.** It shows the edge server, because `/login` is a cached static page and no function runs. The region came from a request that ran one.
2. **A PAGE CANNOT SET A `Server-Timing` HEADER.** `headers()` is read-only in a Server Component (the Next.js API reference shipped with the app, `headers.md` lines 34 and 47). The substitute writes the same numbers into the page; the proxy, which CAN set headers, sends a real one.
3. **THE AWAIT TABLES MISSED MOST OF THE TRIPS.** `proxy.ts` makes a login check on every request, the layout re-runs `requireInstructor()` plus a consent read on every full load, and `requireInstructor()` is itself three trips. **Invite Users, "1 await", is 4 trips on a click and 8 on a full load. That is the finding that explains the complaint.**

**Four more, accepted by the owner:**

4. **B1 BELONGS TO `instructor@test.com`, NOT TO THE OWNER'S ADMIN ACCOUNT.** The admin account owns "Relii Demo (Historical)" and "Pro Selling test". So the instructor pages were measured on `instructor@test.com` in B1, and `/admin` on the admin account.
5. **A GROUPED COUNT IS NOT AVAILABLE THROUGH THE API.** `GET /rest/v1/classes?select=instructor_id,count()` answered `{"code":"PGRST123","message":"Use of aggregate functions is not allowed"}`, and the same request without `count()` answered `[]`, so the refusal is about the aggregate and not the query. A true grouped count would need a database function, which is a migration.
6. **NAVIGATION TIMING SEES ONLY FULL LOADS**, and the complaint is about clicks.
7. **"EACH ROUND TRIP HAS ITS OWN CONNECTION SETUP" WAS UNPROVEN**, and the warm timings argue against it (item 4 above).

**AND THE ONE CORRECTION THE KICKOFF ASKED TO BE RECORDED:** the change brief said `Promise.all` appears in three API routes. **At `26c99c4` it appears in TWO** (`assignment/[id]/delete` and `submission/[id]/reset`), plus `app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx` and three libraries. None of the six measured pages.

2. **TOM'S CLASS ON `/admin`: LOAD IT, RECORD NOTHING FROM IT.** No student names, no emails, no screenshots of that section, nothing from his class in this record or in a scratch file. **The before-and-after comparison of `/admin` uses line hashes only**, and the whole page was hashed rather than just his section, so nothing readable from it was ever captured.
3. **THE FLAG.** `RELII_SERVER_TIMING=1` set by the owner under Production in the Vercel dashboard before the instrument was pushed.
4. **THE THREE EXTRA SPEEDUPS: MEASURE FIRST, DECIDE AFTER THE BASELINE.** (a) in particular gets its own careful look and its own approval rather than riding along with the page-level work.

**IT MAKES THREE OLDER LINES FALSE, AND THEY ARE MARKED SUPERSEDED IN PLACE RATHER THAN DELETED:** the T1 entry's statement that no Claude Code session has ever held these credentials (Current Stage as it stood, and its limit 6), and Phase 3b's note that the owner's instruction was not to sign in as his own account. **Found by their text, not their line numbers, which is now this file's own rule:** "He also recorded that no Claude Code session has ever held those credentials" and "THE SUPER-ADMIN ACCOUNT WAS NOT SIGNED IN TO, AND NO CLAUDE CODE SESSION HAS EVER HELD THOSE CREDENTIALS" (both in the T1 record under Completed), and "the owner's instruction was not to sign in as his own account" (Phase 3b, under Completed). Each was true when written, which is the same shape as the stale `assignment_deletes` number: a line true on its date, read later as current.

### THE LIMITS, STATED AT THEIR REAL STRENGTH

1. **Measured from one machine** (the owner's Mac, reaching Vercel through Cleveland). The owner's own network and Safari will add or subtract; the server-side trip counts and trip times do not depend on the browser.
2. **Three warm runs per page is a small sample**, and trip times vary three- to tenfold between runs. Medians are reported with the runs beside them.
3. **The `/admin` warm column is thin.** Four warm runs (8,835 to 16,402 ms) after the counter set aside two first-renders; two cold. The spread is single slow trips (1,482 and 3,117 ms), not the count, which was 43 every time.
4. **Trip end times are header arrival, not body completion.**
5. **The region estimate in (c) is an estimate.** Nothing here measured a trip from Oregon.

### WHAT THIS SESSION HAS NOT TOUCHED

- **No fix code.** No schema change, no migration, no policy change, no new dependency, no compute change, no region change.
- **Nothing in Tom's class** was read by query or recorded. `/admin` rendered his class's row as it always does, and only hashes of the page were taken.
- **No app row written anywhere.** The four sign-ins (three on the live site, one against the local build) created ordinary Supabase Auth sessions, which is Supabase's own bookkeeping. The one API call made, `/api/instructor/class/select`, ran against the LOCAL build during the instrument check, selecting B1, the class `instructor@test.com` already works in; it sets a browser cookie and writes no row.

### THE PRE-FLIGHT, VERBATIM, AND WHY IT WAS RUN BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE

**D3 lost a session to `languages`, a field every current OpenAI doc page describes, because this app runs a legacy transcription model that answers it with HTTP 400. `app/api/voice/token/route.ts` turns that throw into a 502, which is NO VOICE AT ALL for the student. A refused voice string fails down exactly the same path.** So `marin` was measured rather than read off a page.

**THE HARNESS SHAPE, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THE RESULT MEAN ANYTHING.** The shipped `mintRealtimeClientSecret()` was compiled untouched and run through a **pass-through fetch spy**, which captured the exact body the shipped code sends. That captured body was then re-issued twice with **only `audio.output.voice` different**. A difference between the two can therefore only be the voice.

| Call | What it is | Voice | Status |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| **A** | The shipped `mintRealtimeClientSecret()`, unmodified | `cedar` | **200** |
| **B** | Call A's captured body, re-issued unchanged | `cedar` | **200** |
| **C** | The same bytes, one field changed | **`marin`** | **200** |
| **D** | **NEGATIVE CONTROL** | `notavoice` | **400** |

The harness printed `identical apart from audio.output.voice: true` for B against C before sending.

**CALL C'S RESPONSE, THE PART THAT MATTERS:**

```
"audio": {
  "output": { "format": {...}, "voice": "marin", "speed": 1 }
}
```

==> **THE ECHO IS THE POINT, NOT THE 200. A 200 that silently dropped the voice would read exactly like success.** The accepted session comes back naming `marin`, so it was accepted rather than ignored. <==

==> **AND THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES THE 200 EVIDENCE RATHER THAN INDIFFERENCE.** `"notavoice"` is refused, and the refusal enumerates the server's own list, which independently confirms both of this project's voices are on it:

```
Invalid value: 'notavoice'. Supported values are: 'alloy', 'ash', 'ballad',
'coral', 'echo', 'sage', 'shimmer', 'verse', 'marin', and 'cedar'.
```
<==

**THE MODEL WAS THE RIGHT ONE, AND THAT WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** `getRealtimeModel()` resolved to `gpt-realtime` in every call. **`OPENAI_REALTIME_MODEL` is set neither locally nor in Vercel production** (the production variable list holds only `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`), so the pre-flight measured the model the live site actually uses. A Vercel override would have made the whole pre-flight measure the wrong thing.

### THE VERCEL CLI IS NOW DENIED IN THIS REPO, AND THE MECHANISM IS THE POINT

==> **`vercel env ls` IS NOT A READ. IT TRIGGERS THE CLI DEVICE-LOGIN FLOW AND LEAVES THE MACHINE AUTHENTICATED.** With no stored credentials it blocks on a `vercel.com/oauth/device?user_code=...` URL, and on success the machine stays signed in to the hosting account until somebody logs out. **Reading one environment variable costs a full CLI login.** <==

**WHY IT WAS RUN, BECAUSE THE INSTINCT WAS RIGHT AND ONLY THE METHOD WAS WRONG.** The question was whether `OPENAI_REALTIME_MODEL` is overridden in production. It genuinely mattered: if Vercel set a different realtime model, the whole pre-flight would have measured a model no student ever hits, and `marin` would have shipped on evidence about the wrong thing. **The answer was worth having. The method was broader than the question**, and a deployed route that echoes `model` back in its response would have answered it with no login at all.

**THE GUARDRAIL, AND WHY IT IS DENY RATHER THAN ASK.** `.claude/settings.json`, **tracked**, with `Bash(vercel *)` and seven `npx`/`bunx`/`dlx` spellings in `permissions.deny`. **There is no legitimate use for the Vercel CLI in this project**: deploys happen through the GitHub integration on push to main, which is how every deploy this week was confirmed. **`ask` is what an auto-mode session approves in passing**, which is exactly how the login happened.

==> **IT IS `settings.json` AND NOT `settings.local.json`, AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING.** `.gitignore` line 28 ignores the local file, so a rule written there would never reach another checkout and would die with this one. <==

**IT TOOK EFFECT WITHOUT A RESTART, AND THAT WAS TESTED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** `npx vercel --version` and `vercel --version` were both refused immediately after the write. The settings watcher only watches directories that held a settings file when the session began, and `.claude/` already held `settings.local.json`, so the new file was picked up.

==> **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THE RULE HAD A HOLE, FOUND BY PROBING IT RATHER THAN BY TRUSTING IT.** The two rules originally specified are PREFIX rules, so `npx --yes vercel --version` did NOT match `Bash(npx vercel *)` and **ran**. A flag between `npx` and `vercel` walked straight through. Six more spellings were added, including the middle-wildcard `Bash(npx * vercel *)`, and `npx --yes vercel --version` and `npx -y vercel whoami` are both refused now. **A guardrail nobody attacked is a guardrail nobody has tested.** <==

### THE FUNCTION SIGNATURE, BEFORE AND AFTER, READ FROM THE DATABASE

**Not from the file, and the COUNT is the check rather than the existence.**

| | Signature | Args | `prosecdef` | Execute grants | **Count with this name** |
| :-- | :-- | --: | :-- | :-- | --: |

| **After** | `...timestamptz, timestamptz, integer, text)` | **14** | false | `postgres=X`, `service_role=X` | **1** |

==> **"THE NEW ONE EXISTS" WOULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT THE FAILURE. A SURVIVING OVERLOAD IS THE FAILURE MODE**, and only a count shows it. `anon` and `authenticated` appear in neither row, so the re-applied revokes landed: the drop took the old grants with it, and those statements are the only thing between the function and every browser. <==

**THE BODY WAS REPRODUCED, NOT REWRITTEN, AND THAT IS MEASURED.** Diffed against Phase 2a's original: **two changed lines out of 23**, the insert column list and the values list, each gaining one token **at the same index** (position 9 in both). The third change is the parameter itself, outside the body.

==> **WHY THAT POSITIONAL CHECK EXISTS, AND IT IS THE OWNER'S CATCH RATHER THAN THIS SESSION'S: A SIGNATURE CHECK CANNOT SEE A VOICE WRITTEN INTO THE WRONG COLUMN.** Had the two lists disagreed by one position, the function would have written the voice into `created_by` silently. Verified from the STORED body: `voice` at column-list position 9, `p_voice` at values-list position 9, `positions_match` **true**, both lists 12 long, and `created_by` at position 10 still receiving `p_created_by`. <==

**AND THE STORED BODY IS BYTE-IDENTICAL TO THE FILE**, because a transcription into an MCP call is only trustworthy if it is hashed rather than read: **838 characters, md5 `4248c44400dbd69f2dbd8b5cef1773cb`, 24 lines**, matching `supabase/t1-persona-voice.sql` exactly.

==> **THE REVERT'S RECREATED 13-ARGUMENT BODY WAS HASHED TOO, AND THIS IS THE ONE NOBODY WOULD HAVE CAUGHT.** It is hand-transcribed, it is never exercised by any check, and an error in it would sit undetected until the day somebody actually needed to roll back. Extracted from `supabase/t1-persona-voice-revert.sql` and compared to Phase 2a's original: **822 characters, md5 `143044de413338068ab4a934dc754417`, byte-identical**. <==

==> **WHAT WENT INTO THE MCP CALL WAS AN ABRIDGEMENT OF THE 273-LINE FILE THE OWNER READ, AND THAT IS SAID RATHER THAN LEFT IMPLIED.** The prose header was trimmed and section 5's verification queries were run separately so their output could be read. **Every DDL statement is the same, and every effect was then verified from the catalogue rather than from the text that was sent**: the signature and count from `pg_proc`, the body by md5 against the file, the constraint definition from `pg_constraint`, the column from `information_schema`. So the file and the database agree, proved from the database end. <==

### THE COLUMN-GRANT QUESTION, ANSWERED BY MEASUREMENT

==> **`public.assignments` NEEDS NO GRANT. It carries a TABLE-level grant and ZERO column-level grants.** `anon` and `authenticated` both hold `arwdDxtm` on the table, and no row in `pg_attribute.attacl` names any of its columns, so a new column is readable and writable automatically. <==

**`public.scenarios` IS THE OPPOSITE CASE, WHICH IS WHY THE QUESTION HAD TO BE ASKED AT ALL.** Its table ACL is `awdDxtm` for those roles, **with no `r`**: Gate 2 revoked SELECT at table level and granted it back on **12 of 14 columns**, holding back `persona_system_prompt` and `scenario_context`. **A voice column on `scenarios` would have needed an explicit grant, and forgetting it would have failed the browser-side edit form on save, at the exact error whose Postgres hint Open Issues says never to follow.** This is an independent argument for the decision to put the voice on the assignment.

==> **AND THE MEASUREMENT HAD TO BE TAKEN THE RIGHT WAY, WHICH IS A TRAP WORTH RECORDING. `information_schema.column_privileges` CANNOT ANSWER THIS QUESTION.** It expands a table-level grant across every column, so both tables come back looking identical, every column named for every privilege. The discriminating read is `pg_class.relacl` against `pg_attribute.attacl`, where `attacl` is null unless a column-level grant was actually made. **The first query run in this session was the useless one, and it looked like an answer.** <==

### THE DEPLOY, CONFIRMED BY A REAL REQUEST

| | etag | age |
| :-- | :-- | --: |
| Before the push | `7dc39e45a7f471d44df3cc40ee2948a0` | 0 |
| **After, 15:50:32 UTC** | **`fc1db7e49c02dd2fdeccfc1b8a2dcf45`** | 0 |

**THAT ETAG IS HISTORICAL AND WILL NOT MATCH A CURL RUN LATER.** It is the deploy of `8416926`, the commit that carries the code. Two documentation commits (`cfe52cc` and `620f42b`) redeployed on top of it afterwards, each moving the etag again without changing any behaviour. **The pair above is the evidence that T1's CODE landed**, not a value anyone should expect to reproduce.

==> **`age` CANNOT CONFIRM A DEPLOY, AND THE REASON IS NOT THE ONE AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS ENTRY GAVE.** That draft said `age` "does not move" and "does not track deploys". **Both are wrong, and the measurement that corrects them is this session's own.** <==

**WHAT `age` ACTUALLY IS: seconds since the edge cached the response it is serving.** A deploy creates a new cache entry, so `age` **does** reset to 0 at a deploy, and then counts up. Measured here: the `6dbf7e9` deploy landed at **17:00:38 UTC**, and a sample at **17:04:29** read **`age: 230`** against **231 seconds** elapsed. It tracks precisely. A sample taken seconds after the next deploy read **`age: 0`**.

==> **SO WHY IT STILL CANNOT CONFIRM A DEPLOY: `/login` SENDS `cache-control: public, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, SO THE EDGE REVALIDATES CONSTANTLY AND EVERY REVALIDATION RESETS `age` TOO.** A reading of 0 is produced by a deploy and by ordinary revalidation alike, so **`age: 0` proves nothing**, and waiting for "age to reset to 0" is satisfied instantly by a stale build. That is why two samples minutes apart both read 0 earlier in this session. <==

**WHAT IT IS GOOD FOR, WHICH IS NARROWER AND REAL: a NON-ZERO `age` is a consistency check against a deploy time you already know.** 230 against 231 corroborates. It is never the primary evidence. **The etag change is the signal, and identifying the build still needs an observation only the new code can produce.**

**AND THE DEPLOY WAS CONFIRMED TO BE THIS COMMIT RATHER THAN JUST A DEPLOY**, because the new Voice control renders on the live instructor page, which only this commit's code can produce. **That is the positive identification the standing note asks for, and `8416926` is the ONLY build this session identified that way.** The two later commits changed a comment and this file, so they produce no observable difference and **no discriminating observation exists for them**: their deploys were confirmed by a genuine etag change alone (`620f42b` landed at 16:24:20 UTC on etag `47b1cce6953e71fabd7c35f988412dd4`). Said plainly rather than implied, because an etag alone proves that *a* build landed and not *which*.

==> **A FALSE PASS THIS SESSION PRODUCED AND CAUGHT, RECORDED BECAUSE THE SHAPE RECURS.** The first deploy watcher was an `until` loop comparing `curl -sI ... | grep '^etag:'` against the old value. **It announced "ETAG CHANGED" and then printed the SAME ETAG.** A curl that fails, times out, or returns no etag header yields an EMPTY string, which compares unequal to the old value, so the loop exits and every downstream step reads as confirmed. **"Not equal to the old value" is not the same test as "is a new value", and only one of them is the check.** The fix is to require a NON-EMPTY result that also differs, with `curl -sf --max-time`. It was caught only by printing the value alongside the verdict rather than trusting the verdict, which is the same habit that makes [[read-the-query-not-the-prose]] a standing rule here. <==

A second row landed between them. **A session taking "1" from the older passage would have read the true value of 2 as damage, which is exactly the misreading that passage was written to prevent.** The live value is 2. <==

The app cleaned it up itself and no manual delete was needed. **THE FOUR PROBE SESSIONS ARE GONE.****THE FOUR PROBE SESSIONS ARE GONE.** `supabase/t1-session-probes.sql` created them and `supabase/t1-session-probes-restore.sql` removed them, each named by full id. **Confirmed after: 0 probe rows left, 25 real null-assignment sessions still there, 52 sessions in total, which is the pre-probe baseline exactly.**

"delete where assignment_id is null" WOULD HAVE TAKEN THE TWENTY-FIVE REAL PRE-STAGE-7 SESSIONS WITH IT**, and nothing anywhere would have reported it. The token route needs no media at all.

### TWO DEFECTS IN THE KICKOFF, FOUND AND FIXED

==> **1. IT SAYS THE FUNCTION HAS TWELVE PARAMETERS. IT HAS THIRTEEN.** Read from `pg_proc` before any change: `pronargs` 13. A drop written against a twelve-type signature would simply not have matched, and `create or replace` would then have left the real function standing beside the new one, which is precisely the overload the kickoff's own warning exists to prevent. <==

==> **2. IT NAMES THREE SIGNATURE-KEYED STATEMENTS TO RE-APPLY. THERE ARE FOUR.** `comment on function` at `supabase/phase-2a-rubric-intake.sql` line 276 is also keyed by the full signature and would have errored against the dropped function. All four are in the migration. <==

**Recorded the way D3 recorded its kickoff's ordering defect: the kickoff was right about the hazard and wrong about two of its details, and both were caught by reading the database rather than the file.**

### A FILE THE KICKOFF'S TABLE MISSED

==> **`app/instructor/assignments/page.tsx` IS MODIFIED AND IS NOT IN THE KICKOFF'S FILE TABLE. Line 131 is the select that loads an assignment and line 235 is the object handed to the edit form.** Without both, the new control would have rendered on the edit form with nothing behind it: the form would have shown Cedar for every assignment regardless of what was stored, and check 5 would have failed with no obvious cause. **It is named here because a modified file with no entry reads as scope creep later.** <==

**A SECOND OMISSION IN THE SAME TABLE, SMALLER:** `app/api/voice/token/route.ts` line 44 selected `"id, student_id, scenario_id, status, kind, created_at"` with **no `assignment_id`**. The kickoff said the voice was "one more read or one join away"; it was one more read away **plus** a column the session select did not have.

### THE SCOPE CHANGED FROM THE PHASE 3c HANDOFF AND THE ROADMAP, AND WHY

==> **BOTH OLDER DESCRIPTIONS ARE SUPERSEDED AND NEITHER IS A CONTRADICTION.** The Phase 3c handoff below, and the roadmap's T1 entry, describe a **voice picker per persona, stored on `public.scenarios`, controlled on the AI Persona Framework page**. **THE OWNER CHANGED THAT ON 2026-09-14.** <==

**WHAT IT BECAME, AND THE ONE REASON THAT DECIDES IT.** Two fixed voices rather than a picker, stored on the **assignment**. `assignments.scenario_id` is a plain foreign key with an index and no uniqueness rule, so one scenario backs many assignments. **A control that sat on the assignment form but wrote to `scenarios` would mean an instructor creating a second assignment on Alex and picking the other voice silently changed the first assignment's voice too.** Storing the value where it is chosen makes that impossible.

**HANDOFF ITEM 3 IS NO LONGER THE GOVERNING ARGUMENT.** It argued for a column over a `generation_fields` key because an imported persona carries null `generation_fields`. Correct for a persona-level voice, and now moot: an assignment always exists and needs no fallback. "Do not store the voice in `generation_fields`" remains true and is now trivially true.

**HANDOFF ITEM 7'S COST HAZARD IS RETIRED.** It warned that a picker invites sampling and every sample is a billable realtime session. **There is no picker and no preview button**, so there is nothing to sample.

**HANDOFF ITEM 5 WAS WRONG ABOUT THE ROUTE.** It named `app/api/voice/start/route.ts`. **`voice/start` mints nothing**; the mint is in `app/api/voice/token/route.ts`.

| 3 | **Alex** | `If a film delivery is late or we run out, it can halt our production line, which is a significant issue. It disrupts our schedule, causes downtime, and ultimately affects our ability to meet customer demands. I take continuity of supply very seriously to avoid those situations.` |
| 4 | You | `That makes sense. Has that actually come close to happening recently? I'm curious whether it's a theoretical risk for you or something your team has had to scramble on in the last year.` |
| 5 | **Alex** | `We did have a late delivery last quarter that nearly stopped a line. It was a close call and a reminder of the importance of reliable supply. It's something we're very cautious about.` |
| 6 | You | `That's the thing I'd want to put a number on. A near miss like that has a cost even when the line doesn't stop: the expediting, the schedule you rebuilt, the overtime. And it sits alongside the quieter costs, freight on every delivery, film you scrapped to a bad seal, cash tied up in a minimum order you didn't want. Per roll we're probably a wash. My argument is that the per roll number isn't what this actually costs you.` |
| 7 | **Alex** | `I agree that total cost of ownership includes more than just the per roll price. We consider factors such as freight, waste, rework, and downtime as part of the overall cost, and minimizing those is important to us. Can you elaborate on how your approach addresses those aspects?` |
| 8 | You | `I'd rather not answer that from a brochure today, because the honest answer depends on things I don't know yet: your seal failure rate, what your minimum order actually forces you to carry, and what your plant manager would say is the real cost of a bad week. Let me take what you've told me and come back with a picture of your total cost, built on your numbers rather than mine. Could I have another block of your time to walk you through that?` |
| 9 | **Alex** | `I appreciate your approach. If you can demonstrate how your solution could genuinely reduce our total cost of ownership and address the concerns we've discussed, I'd be open to scheduling a follow-up meeting. Let me know what you need from me to prepare for that.` |

==> **D3, ENGLISH-ONLY TRANSCRIPTION, IS DONE, DEPLOYED AND PROVED (2026-09-14). Commit `ba3e8db`, pushed and live.** One field added to one object in one file, `lib/realtime.ts`. **NO MIGRATION, NO SCHEMA CHANGE, NO NEW DEPENDENCY, NO DATABASE WRITE OF ANY KIND, AND NO SQL RUN AT ALL.** Tom's class was not read and not written. <==

==> **OUTCOME B OCCURRED FIRST, AND IT WAS THE HARD BRANCH: AN EXPLICIT HTTP 400, NOT A SILENT IGNORE.** The change exactly as the kickoff specified it, `languages: ["en"]`, is REFUSED by `gpt-4o-transcribe`: `"The 'languages' parameter is not supported for this model."`, `param` `session.audio.input.transcription.languages`, `code` `invalid_parameter`. **THE SESSION THEN RESOLVED IT INTO A SHIPPABLE OUTCOME A** by measuring which field this model actually has. What shipped is the SINGULAR, `language: "en"`. <==

### WHY OUTCOME B MATTERED, AND IT IS NOT A NEAR MISS

**HAD THE KICKOFF'S ORDER BEEN FOLLOWED, THIS WOULD HAVE TAKEN VOICE DOWN FOR EVERY STUDENT IN PRACTICE WEEK.** `mintRealtimeClientSecret` throws on a non-ok response, and `app/api/voice/token/route.ts` line 94 catches it and returns **502 "Could not start the voice session. Please try again."** That is not a degraded transcript. **It is no voice at all, for everyone, on every attempt**, and the five check scripts would all still have been green because they read source rather than a running API.

==> **THE KICKOFF'S STEP ORDER WAS THE DEFECT, AND THE OWNER RULED IT A CORRECTION RATHER THAN A DEPARTURE (2026-09-14).** The kickoff put checks 1 and 2 at step 5, AFTER the commit, the push and the deploy, while its own fence said "do not push a change that the API rejects." **THOSE TWO INSTRUCTIONS CANNOT BOTH BE OBEYED.** A change is discriminated A from B by calling the API, so that call has to happen BEFORE the push or the fence is decorative. **THE OWNER'S RULING: "You are right and I was wrong. Do the local pre-flight first."** Any future kickoff of this shape inherits that order: **anything that can be refused by an external service is measured before it is committed, not after it is deployed.** <==

**CHECK 1 CANNOT BE DONE ON THE LIVE SITE AT ALL, WHICH IS THE SECOND HALF OF THE SAME POINT.** It asks for the outbound request body read server-side. There is no way to read a deployed route's outbound body without adding logging, and the kickoff separately (and correctly) said no logging was needed. **Check 1 was always a harness check.** It ran as one: the shipped `lib/realtime.ts` compiled with the project's own `tsc`, `fetch` replaced by a PASS-THROUGH spy that records the body and then makes the real call with the real key. Same mechanism as `scripts/check-scorer.mjs`, minus the alias rewrite, because `lib/realtime.ts` has no imports.

### THE PROBE MATRIX, AND THE CONTROL IS THE ROW THAT PROVES IT

All ten posted straight to `https://api.openai.com/v1/realtime/client_secrets`, every field outside `session.audio.input.transcription` byte-identical to what ships. **No shipping code was changed to run any of them and no model was switched in the app.**

| Body variant | Result |
| :-- | :-- |
| **CONTROL: shipped config, nothing added** | **200**, echo `{"model":"gpt-4o-transcribe","language":null,"prompt":null}` |
| `gpt-4o-transcribe` + `languages: ["en"]` | **400** not supported for this model |
| **`gpt-4o-transcribe` + `language: "en"`** | **200**, echo `{"model":"gpt-4o-transcribe","language":"en","prompt":null}` |
| `gpt-4o-transcribe` + `prompt` | **200**, echoed back |
| `gpt-4o-transcribe` + `keywords` | **400** not supported for this model |
| `gpt-4o-transcribe` + `delay: "low"` | **400** not supported for this model |
| `gpt-transcribe` + `languages: ["en"]` | 200, echo carries `language` AND `languages` as separate keys |
| `gpt-live-transcribe` + `languages: ["en"]` | 200, same two separate keys |
| `gpt-4o-transcribe` + BOTH fields | **400** on `languages` |
| `gpt-transcribe` + BOTH fields | **400** "The 'language' and 'languages' parameters cannot be used together." |

==> **THE CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES THIS A MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN AN ANECDOTE.** The shipped config returns 200 in the same run, from the same key, against the same model. **So `languages` and nothing else caused the 400.** Without that row the 400 could have been a bad key, a dead model or a transient fault. <==

**THE SECOND STRONGEST ROW IS ALSO THE CONTROL'S ECHO.** It advertises this model's own schema as **`language` and `prompt`, singular, with no plural key present at all.** The singular is a first-class field on `gpt-4o-transcribe`, not a tolerated unknown.

==> **THE KICKOFF WAS RIGHT ABOUT THE FIELD NAME AND RIGHT ABOUT "DON'T SEND BOTH", AND THE LAST ROW PROVES THE API ENFORCES THE SECOND ONE. SAY THE DEFECT AT ITS REAL SIZE.** Plural `languages` IS the field, and "don't send both" IS the rule, **for the CURRENT transcription models**, which is exactly the scope the kickoff said it had checked. **The defect is narrower: current-model documentation was carried onto a legacy model.** The kickoff even flagged the gap itself, as "the uncertainty", and asked for it to be settled by measurement. It was, and it went the other way. **This is not a documentation error and it must not be recorded as one.** <==

### WHAT SHIPPED

`lib/realtime.ts`, inside `session.audio.input`, with a comment block that says why it is the singular and what happens if someone "corrects" it:

```
transcription: { model: INPUT_TRANSCRIPTION_MODEL, language: "en" },
```

**THE REQUEST BODY AS SENT**, read off the pass-through spy against the shipped function after the change, HTTP **200**, token minted:

```json
"transcription": { "model": "gpt-4o-transcribe", "language": "en" },
"turn_detection": { "type": "server_vad", "threshold": 0.5, "prefix_padding_ms": 300,
                    "silence_duration_ms": 1600, "create_response": true, "interrupt_response": true },
"output": { "voice": "cedar" }
```

**`turn_detection` is untouched, `silence_duration_ms` is still 1600, and `REALTIME_VOICE` is still `cedar`.** No handler was added for a failed transcription event.

### THE PAIRED TEST, WHICH IS THE EVIDENCE THE SHIP DECISION ACTUALLY RESTS ON

==> **THE OWNER GATED THE SHIP ON IT AND THAT GATE WAS RIGHT (ruling, 2026-09-14): "Do not ship the singular on the strength of the echo alone. An echo proves acceptance, not effect. We are one HTTP 400 into learning that documentation-shaped confidence is worth nothing on this surface."** <==

Built in the scratchpad, **never committed, no shipping path touched.** Node 24 has a native WebSocket, so the Realtime API was driven directly: same 42.3 second wav, 9 utterances, 8 of them short ambiguous English fragments of the shape that failed for Tom. **8 sessions each way, INTERLEAVED** (on, off, on, off) so server-side drift over the run could not land on one branch. One deliberate deviation from the shipped config, `create_response: false` so Alex stays silent, which cannot affect how the STUDENT's audio is transcribed and keeps the two branches differing in exactly one field.

==> **THE "OFF" BRANCH IS NOT A NEUTRAL CONTROL, AND THAT IS WHAT GIVES THE RESULT ITS FORCE.** It is byte-for-byte the configuration that was in production, the one that produced Tom's five failures on September 11. **This is a comparison against the real defect, not against an idealised baseline.** <==

| Branch | Runs | Turns | Clean English | Wrong-language | Empty | Truncated | `transcription.failed` |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| **ON**, `language: "en"` | 8 | 72 | **72** | **0** | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| **OFF**, production as it was | 8 | 72 | 64 | **6** | 0 | 0 | 0 |

**THE SIX FAILURES ARE ALL THE SAME TURN.** The short utterance `"Yeah."` came back as German **`"Ja."`** on 6 of 8 OFF runs and on **0 of 8** ON runs, where it was correct every time. Sixteen trials of that one utterance, every mis-detection in one branch: **Fisher exact p = 0.0035.**

**THE OTHER TWO OFF FLAGS WERE THE CLASSIFIER BEING STRICT, NOT DEFECTS**, and they are recorded so the 8 is not misread: `"Right."` to `"Wright"` (an English homophone) and `"Mm-hmm."` to `"Mm, hm."` (punctuation). Neither is a language error. **Spelling variance inside English is noise for a grader; a token in another language is the defect.**

**THE CAP IS PER STUDENT PER ASSIGNMENT, NOT PER CLASS.** `lib/practice.ts` sets `KEPT_PRACTICES = 3`, and `prunePractices()` scopes every query to `(student_id, assignment_id, kind = 'practice', the finished status)`, so **it can never touch another student's rows.** B1's 4 rows are **2studentb1 with exactly 3, which is at the cap, plus 1studentb1 with 1**, which is a different student and therefore **out of the prune's scope by construction rather than by luck.** The "3 of 3" above is 2studentb1's own count, not the class total.

Flags `--use-fake-device-for-media-stream`, `--use-fake-ui-for-media-stream`, `--use-file-for-fake-audio-capture`. Standalone Playwright script in the scratchpad against system Chrome by `executablePath`, module out of the npx cache. **`.mcp.json` was NOT edited.** `page.on("dialog", d => d.accept())` registered before any click.

| **Empty** | **2** | **0 rendered, but see below: line 456 makes an empty turn unrenderable, and one turn is missing outright** |
| **Truncated** | **1** | **2, both in the first pass, one of them unexplained** |
| **Wrong language** | **2** | **0** |

==> **TURN 15 IS THE ONE THAT CERTIFIES THE CHANGE ON THE REAL PATH.** `Yeah.` rendered as **`Yeah.`** The paired test had that exact utterance coming back as German `Ja.` on 6 of 8 runs under the old configuration. **This is the fix working through our own route, our own token mint and our own browser, not just through a probe.** <==

==> **THE FIRST PASS WAS MESSY AND THE SECOND WAS PERFECT, AND BOTH HALVES ARE REPORTED.** Turns 10 to 19 are one complete, uninterrupted pass through the audio and they are **10 for 10 correct, including all five short turns.** `Well,` and `Right.` came back as `Wow!` and `Why?` in the first pass, and those are real mis-hearings, but they are **ENGLISH** mis-hearings, which is a different failure from the one this change addresses. <==

==> **AND THE FIRST PASS IS MISSING A TURN ENTIRELY, WHICH IS THE MOST IMPORTANT OBSERVATION IN THIS SECTION AND IS NOT WHAT AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS ENTRY SAID.** The draft explained the first pass away as audio clipped by the call starting mid-file. **THAT COVERS TURN 1 AND IT DOES NOT COVER THE REST, SO IT WAS WRONG.** Aligned against the second pass, which is complete, the audio's ten utterances land at a fixed offset. **`Right.` should have produced a student turn at about 00:13 and there is NO TURN THERE AT ALL**, and the long answer that follows it at 00:21 rendered as `in multiple forms, ...` with its opening `So disruptions come` gone. **That is twenty-one seconds into a running call, not at connection.** <==

### WHAT THIS DOES NOT FIX, WHICH IS MOST OF THE BASELINE

==> **`language` ADDRESSES MIS-DETECTION ONLY, SO IT CAN TOUCH AT MOST 2 OF TOM'S 5 FAILURES. THREE STAY UNADDRESSED, AND A CLEAN RUN IS NOT EVIDENCE THAT THEY ARE FIXED.** The baseline breaks out as **2 empty, 1 truncated, 2 wrong-language.** Only the last pair is in scope. **DO NOT READ THIS ENTRY AS "THE SHORT-TURN FAMILY IS SOLVED."** <==

**AND THE THREE ARE NOT MERELY UNFIXED, THEY ARE MOSTLY UNMEASURED.** In the paired test, which read the raw API events rather than a saved transcript, **not one empty transcription came back in 144 turns, in either branch.** That is a real negative and it holds at the API level.

==> **BUT THE LIVE PRACTICE COULD NOT HAVE SHOWN AN EMPTY TURN EVEN IF ONE HAPPENED, AND THAT IS A CODE FACT FOUND THIS SESSION.** `components/voice-conversation.tsx` line **456**, inside `saveTranscript()`, is:

```
.filter((t) => t.done && t.text.trim())
```

**A STUDENT TURN WITH NO TEXT IS DROPPED FROM THE SAVED TRANSCRIPT ENTIRELY RATHER THAN SAVED AS AN EMPTY TURN.** Both halves of the filter do it: a buffer that committed and never received a `completed` event never becomes `done`, and one that completes with an empty string keeps `text` at `""` because `finalizeTurn` only assigns when the final text is non-empty. **So "no empty turn appeared in the live transcript" is close to a tautology and must not be read as "no empty turn occurred."** <==

==> **AND THE MISSING TURN AT 00:13 IS EXACTLY WHAT THAT FILTER DOES TO ONE.** A committed-but-untranscribed `Right.` would leave no trace in the saved transcript, which is precisely what was observed. **THE MECHANISM IS STILL NOT ESTABLISHED AND IS NOT BEING CLAIMED**: a VAD that never committed the buffer at all produces the identical absence, and so would a buffer boundary landing inside the following utterance, which would also account for the lost `So disruptions come`. **Three candidates, one observation, and no way to separate them without instrumenting the client.** What is now certain is that the earlier "clipped at connection" reading was not one of them. <==

- ==> **A TENSION THIS SESSION FOUND AND DELIBERATELY DID NOT RESOLVE, BECAUSE RESOLVING IT MEANS READING TOM'S DATA.** The kickoff says two of Tom's September 11 student turns "rendered with no text", and line 456 says a turn with no text is never saved in the first place. **THOSE TWO STATEMENTS CANNOT BOTH BE STRAIGHTFORWARDLY TRUE.** Either those turns carry something that survives `.trim()`, or they reached the transcript by a path other than `saveTranscript()`, or the export renders a header for something that is not a turn. **Tom's class was not read, so this is filed as a question and not as an answer.** It is the first thing to settle before anyone tries to fix the empty turns, because the fix depends on which of those it is.

  ==> **AND THERE IS A LEADING CANDIDATE FOR HOW A TURN PASSES THE FILTER AND STILL RENDERS AS NOTHING. IT IS A HYPOTHESIS TO TEST, NOT AN ANSWER, AND IT MUST NOT HARDEN INTO ONE.** <==

  **THE FILTER WAS IN FORCE WHEN TOM PRACTISED.** Line 456 dates to `427b51d`, **2026-06-22**, three months before September 11. Confirmed with `git log -L 456,456:components/voice-conversation.tsx`, not assumed.

  **THE EXPORT SHOWS A REAL HEADING WITH NOTHING UNDER IT**, a `You` heading followed by empty lines, rather than a page break or an extraction artifact. **So the text passed `t.text.trim()` and still displayed as nothing.** That is only possible if it holds characters that are not whitespace to JavaScript but draw nothing.

  ==> **AND THE TRANSCRIBER IS KNOWN TO EMIT EXACTLY THOSE.** Tom's Arabic turn extracts with **U+202B** and **U+202C** bidi controls around it. **Measured on 2026-09-14 rather than reasoned about:** JavaScript `trim()` strips whitespace and U+FEFF, and it does **NOT** strip U+202B, U+202C or U+200B. A turn whose text is only bidi or zero-width marks therefore **passes line 456 and renders invisible**. <==

  **IT ALSO EXPLAINS THE ASYMMETRY THIS SESSION FOUND, WHICH IS THE REASON TO TAKE IT SERIOUSLY.** In B1 the short `Right.` produced **NO TURN**; in Tom's transcript the equivalent produced a **BLANK** one. **Truly empty is dropped by the filter, blank-but-not-empty survives it. One filter, two symptoms**, and until now the two looked like different defects.

  ==> **THE CAVEAT THAT KEEPS THIS A HYPOTHESIS: THE PDF EXPORTER MAY HAVE INSERTED THOSE BIDI MARKS ITSELF** while laying out right-to-left text, in which case they were never in the stored turn and this explanation collapses. <==

  ==> **THE DECISIVE TEST, AND IT IS CHEAP: DUMP THE CHARACTER CODES OF A BLANK-RENDERING TURN'S TEXT.** Codepoints settle it in one read; nothing else does. **DO IT IN B1 BY REPRODUCING A BLANK-RENDERING TURN FIRST. DO NOT RUN IT AGAINST TOM'S DATA AND DO NOT ASK TO.** This is the first thing the `turn_detection` session should do, before it touches a single value. <==
- **`conversation.item.input_audio_transcription.failed` never fired**, in any of the 165 turns. The switch still has no case for it, so a failed transcription would fall through and leave the turn holding nothing. **Untested, unchanged, and still a separate decision.**
- **`turn_detection` was not touched.** `silence_duration_ms` is still the tuned 1600.

### THE HONEST LIMITS, STATED AT THEIR REAL STRENGTH

1. ==> **THE REAL VERDICT COMES FROM TOM'S COHORT THIS WEEK, NOT FROM THIS SESSION.** The paired test is 16 sessions of SYNTHESISED speech from macOS `say`. Tom's failures came from a real human on a real microphone, and synthesised speech is cleanly articulated in a way that may under-represent the problem. **The 6 of 8 German renders show the rig can reproduce mis-detection, which is the thing that makes the comparison meaningful, but it is still not a student.** <==
2. **The live practice is one sample.** It shows the config did not break anything and that the specific failure shape was not reproducible on the new build. It does not show mis-detection is gone.
3. **The paired test used `session.update` over a WebSocket**, while the app bakes the config into the token mint. **Both paths were confirmed to accept the field**, the mint by the probe matrix and the harness, so this is a noted difference rather than a gap.

They cannot be measured, let alone shipped, without a model change. **`prompt` IS accepted and echoed back, so it is the only one of the three reachable today**, and it is therefore the one remaining candidate if the singular turns out not to be enough for the wrong-language subset. **It is still not in scope and still needs its own measurement**: it changes what the grader is fed. <==

**THE MODEL SWITCH REMAINS AN OWNER RULING AND WAS NOT TAKEN.** `gpt-transcribe` and `gpt-live-transcribe` both accept the plural and both were confirmed reachable, but moving to either changes what every future transcript looks like, mid-semester, with real student work already flowing.

==> **ALL SIX DUSTY VALUES REPRODUCE BYTE FOR BYTE AS `100 * 0.55 * m` IN IEEE 754 DOUBLES**, where `m` runs `1, 0.9, 0.8, 0.7, 0.5, 0.1`, and those six multipliers are literally **row 3** of Tom's workbook, `0.55` is **B7** and `100` is **D18**. Six of six, to the last digit, including the clean `38.5` that `0.7` happens to produce. **DISCOVERY IS DUSTY AND NOTHING ELSE IS BECAUSE 0.55 IS THE ONLY WEIGHT IN THAT RUBRIC THAT IS INEXACT AGAINST 100:** `100 * 0.1`, `100 * 0.15` and `100 * 0.05` are all exact. <==

==> **TWO THINGS ARE RULED OUT, AND ONE IS NOT ESTABLISHED. SAID AT ITS REAL STRENGTH.** **The parser is innocent as documented:** `numericValue()` in `lib/rubric-xlsx.ts` reads `cell.result ?? cell.value` and never multiplies. **The repo's workbook is not the source:** `docs/_Discovery-role-play-rubric-Sales-300-Fall-2026-copy.xlsx` (md5 `e7ea15ecf67c5ff48538ddb745e1fcea`, the copy PROJECT_STATE names) carries **clean cached values on every cell of that row**, `55 / 49.5 / 44 / 38.5 / 27.5 / 5.5`, and its last four are plain numbers with **no formula at all**. **SO THE MULTIPLICATION HAPPENED IN WHATEVER WORKBOOK TOM ACTUALLY UPLOADED, WHOSE CACHED FORMULA RESULTS WERE THE RAW IEEE PRODUCTS. THAT FILE WAS NOT READ AND ITS CONTENTS ARE NOT ESTABLISHED.** Anything past this line would be a guess, and nothing was changed on the strength of one. <==

**ALSO MEASURED AND DELIBERATELY NOT ACTED ON:** `lib/scoring.ts` line 437 sums `points_earned` with plain float addition and `app/api/session/end/route.ts` line 272 stores that sum as `score_total`, **so stored totals may carry dust even though every screen now hides it.** Changing the parser, normalising at parse time and backfilling any row remain **the owner's decision, not taken here.**

### THE COMMENTS THAT BECAME FALSE, AND WHY

**TWO, NOT ONE. The brief named the first; the second is worse and is in the file being changed.**

**`app/instructor/page.tsx` lines 328 to 331** read *"NO INDIVIDUAL SCORE IS ROUNDED ANYWHERE, which is the rule lib/types.ts formatPoints() exists to hold"*. **That stopped being true the moment the function changed**, and a future session reading it would have believed a rule the code no longer holds. It now states what is actually true: individual scores are rounded to **one decimal and never to a whole number**. **The Phase 4a reasoning above it survives intact** and is the reason one decimal is the floor: Tom's ladder awards 49.5, 38.5, 13.5, 4.5 and 0.5, and rounding once made a screen print 69 above a row reading 68.5.

**`lib/types.ts` lines 268 to 272** read *"IT DOES NOT ROUND ANYTHING, INCLUDING TO ONE DECIMAL."* Corrected, and the correction **states what the ruling gives up rather than glossing it**: a value that is neither a whole nor a half, a third say, now prints as one decimal instead of in full. The old wording preferred the opposite trade and was not wrong, it was outvoted by a real screen.
