**ONE FALSE START WORTH KEEPING, BECAUSE IT COST FIVE SIGN-IN ATTEMPTS.** A password pasted into chat is replaced by the literal 10-character token `<password>` inside tool arguments, so Playwright typed ten wrong characters and Supabase returned a real 400. It looks exactly like a wrong password, and it failed IDENTICALLY for two different accounts, which is the tell. One measurement settles it and costs no attempt: read `document.querySelector('input[type=password]').value.length` after filling. It read 10, first character code 60, a `<`. Recorded in memory.

==> **THE WORKAROUND, AND THE RULE THAT IT IS CREATED AND DELETED PER SESSION RATHER THAN LEFT STANDING (2026-09-13).** Writing the credentials to a **gitignored `.env*.local` file** and reading them from there routes around the redaction entirely, because the value never has to survive a trip through a tool argument. `.gitignore` line 12 (`.env*.local`) covers it, so it can never be committed. **The shape that worked, four keys:**

```
TEST_INSTRUCTOR_EMAIL=instructor@test.com
TEST_INSTRUCTOR_PASSWORD=...
B3_STUDENT_EMAIL=b3student@test.com
B3_STUDENT_PASSWORD=...
```

**WRITE IT AT THE START OF A SESSION THAT NEEDS SIGN-IN AND DELETE IT AT THE END OF THAT SESSION.** ==> **NOTHING IN THE REPO REFERENCES THIS FILE.** No script loads it, no check reads it, and no code path depends on it existing. So it is NOT infrastructure that can be left in place: **it has to be created and removed per session**, and a copy left behind is plaintext passwords sitting in a directory every agent session that touches this repo can read, serving nothing. The 2026-09-13 file was deleted at the owner's instruction the same session it was created. <==

==> **AND THE PREMISE ABOVE IS NOT RELIABLE, MEASURED THE SAME DAY. DO NOT TELL THE OWNER A CHAT-PASTED PASSWORD IS UNUSABLE WITHOUT MEASURING IT FIRST.** On 2026-09-13 the owner pasted two passwords as an ordinary chat message and **both reached the page intact**: `value.length` read 20 and 14, neither was the `<password>` token, and both sign-ins succeeded first try. The redaction has now been observed to fire once (2026-09-11, above) and NOT to fire on three later occasions, with no visible difference in how the password arrived. **THE ONLY DURABLE RULE IS THE MEASUREMENT, NOT THE WORKAROUND:** read `value.length` before submitting, whatever route the password came by. Asking for the file first and being told no costs one round trip; refusing to try a pasted one costs the same round trip and the owner's patience. <==

**WHAT WAS BUILT, AND WHAT EACH PIECE IS FOR.**

**THE TWO RENAMES.** "Active Assignments" is now **"Published Assignments"** (`app/instructor/page.tsx`), and the THREE comments naming the old label are corrected (`app/instructor/page.tsx` lines 3 and 363, `components/assignment-selector.tsx` line 19). The kickoff named two of those three; the one in the file header was found by grep and corrected with the owner's approval. "Working in" is now **"Class"**, and the seven sentences that described the working class now say **"the selected class"**: `components/class-picker.tsx` (the label and the screen-reader label), `app/instructor/roster/page.tsx`, `app/instructor/assignments/page.tsx`, `components/bulk-invite-form.tsx`, `components/other-class-notice.tsx`, and `components/class-manager.tsx` (two). The internal id `working-class` in the picker is deliberately unchanged: it is plumbing and never on screen, the same reasoning that kept the `/instructor/scaffold` route when that page was renamed.

**THE ON AND OFF TOGGLE.** A new route, `app/api/instructor/scaffold/enable/route.ts`, the mirror of `disable`. It carries the same class guard and the same refusal for an empty `generation_group_id`, plus two of its own: it refuses a persona that was never chosen, and it refuses to switch on a second row in one generation. The control is `components/persona-toggle.tsx`, and it acts on a PERSONA rather than on a generation, because both routes take a scenario id and a decided generation can still hold two rows.

==> **WHICH ROW OF A GENERATION IS THE CHOSEN ONE, WHICH IS THE ONE REAL DESIGN DECISION IN THIS PIECE.** Three tests, any one of which is enough: the row is switched ON, or it carries the new `generation_fields.chosen_at` marker, or it is the only row left in its generation. Written out in full at `chosenRowOf()` in `lib/scaffold-candidates.ts`.

  **THE TITLE IS DELIBERATELY NOT ONE OF THEM, AND THAT IS THE PART WORTH REMEMBERING.** Choosing a winner renames it from "Scaffold candidate 1 (gatekeeper): ..." to "Discovery-Close: ...", so a title test would work today and would be the shortest code in the file. It is still wrong, for the reason this project already paid for once in the rubric renderer: **DISPLAY NAMES ARE NOT LOAD BEARING**. A title is text an instructor reads and can change; the moment behaviour is decided by parsing one, renaming a persona silently stops it being togglable and nothing errors. The marker and the row count are structure. Three things now enforce this: checks 7, 8 and 11 of the new check script, and the PARAMETER TYPE of `chosenRowOf`, which exposes only `id`, `enabled` and `generation_fields`, so a title read does not compile. That last one was proved by trying it. <==

**THE ONE-ROW RULE IS SOUND BY CONSTRUCTION, NOT BY GUESS.** Generation writes two rows in a single insert, so a pair is never half written; choosing a winner deletes the loser unless something is using it; discarding is all or nothing for a whole group. So a group can only come down to one row by having been decided. Proved against the rows that exist: `cdf87d4f` in "Relii Demo (Historical)" is a chosen persona, switched off, one row.

**THE LIST IS NOW TWO SECTIONS**, which was a bug fix rather than a layout preference. `loadRecentGenerations` showed the 8 most recent generations of any kind, so a chosen persona older than that dropped off the page, and a persona that is not on the page can never be switched back on. `loadPersonaList` replaces it: **CHOSEN PERSONAS, ALL OF THEM HOWEVER OLD, NEVER CAPPED**, then **the 8 most recent UNDECIDED pairs**. The cap sentence sits under the second heading, which is why the sections are not interleaved by date. The raw read lost its `limit * 2 + 10` row cap and is now an unbounded class-scoped read; at this scale a class holds a handful of scenarios (the busiest in the database holds seven).

**PAIRING.** `generate/route.ts` stores `chain_from_scenario_id` as a **SIBLING of `chain`, never inside it**. `assembleScenario` is handed the candidate, the inputs and `chain` and nothing else, so the frozen template cannot see the new key and no stored text moves.

==> **A CORRECTION THE SESSION MADE TO ITSELF, AND THE REASON IT IS WORTH READING.** Three places (both code comments and check 43's failure message) originally justified that placement by saying an id inside `chain` would CHANGE THE ASSEMBLED TEXT. **THAT IS FALSE, AND IT WAS FOUND BY TRYING TO MAKE THE CHECK FAIL.** `lib/persona-assembly.ts` reads the chain by NAMED FIELD (`chain.gatekeeperName`, `chain.gatekeeperRole`), so an unknown extra key in that object is inert: rebuilding a real chained persona with the id injected into its chain produced byte-identical text. The demonstration was a check that could not fail.

  **THE REAL ARGUMENT IS ABOUT MEANING, NOT BYTES.** Kept outside, `ChainContext` stays exactly the five fields the frozen template is handed, so the key can never BECOME an input to it, and the presence of `chain` keeps meaning "this persona had an earlier meeting" rather than "this persona has an id attached", which is the reading `resolveChainForReassembly` depends on. Check **45** now measures the inertness directly, so the wrong reason cannot drift back in. <== **`GENERATION_FIELDS_VERSION` IS NOT BUMPED**: version 2 means "an absent chain on a decision maker proves it never had one", which a new sibling key does not touch, and bumping would refuse to edit every existing version 2 candidate. New generations only: nothing is backfilled and nothing matches on names. The line reads BOTH ways from that single id (`components/persona-pairing.tsx`), and renders nothing at all when there is no pair, because pairing is recorded on new generations only and a "no pair recorded" line would print under every persona that already exists.

The new `loadAssignmentTitles()` is class scoped because it puts assignment TITLES on screen, and unscoped it would print another instructor's assignment title. This is the "THE LISTING IS SCOPED, THE FACT CHECK IS NOT" rule in `lib/class-scope.ts`; do not merge the two.

<summary>The Phase 4b record, as it stood in Current Stage</summary>

==> **PHASE 4b (WHAT THE STUDENT SEES, AND THE CSV) IS DONE AND VERIFIED (2026-09-11).** Commits `30d44d6` and `c1d8dc7`, pushed and live, proved end to end against relii.vercel.app inside B1 only. **THERE IS NO MIGRATION: nothing in this session changed the schema, no column was added, and neither the review route nor `save_submission_review()` was touched.** 4a had already stored every field 4b renders. The artifact is `supabase/phase-4b-baseline.sql`, plus two executable checks, `scripts/check-rubric-render.mjs` (45 checks) and `scripts/check-csv-ai-level.mjs` (10 checks). <==

==> **THE STUDENT CAN NOW TELL WHO GRADED EACH LINE, WHICH IS THE HALF THAT MAKES 4a SAFE TO USE ON REAL WORK.** Until this shipped, a reviewed submission showed the student their instructor's level sitting directly under the AI's own unlabelled comment about a DIFFERENT level, with the instructor's note rendering nowhere at all. **THE PROCESS RULE THAT STOOD BETWEEN 4a AND 4b IS NOW RETIRED**: Tom can review real student work. `reviewed_at` was null on every row in the database at the start of this session, so the rule was never broken. <==

**WHAT WAS BUILT.** One shared component gained attribution (`components/rubric-score.tsx`), two student surfaces gained the review state (`app/student/page.tsx`, `app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx`), the instructor's read-only render declared its audience (`app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx`), and the per-submission CSV gained a Status line and two columns (`app/api/instructor/submission/[id]/csv/route.ts`, plus one new module `lib/submission-ai-level.ts`).

==> **THE OWNER'S RULINGS, AND WHERE EACH IS ENFORCED RATHER THAN INTENDED.**

  1. **"AI feedback" LABELS EVERY COMMENT, UNCONDITIONALLY**, on practices and submissions alike and whichever audience is reading. It is not gated on the score having been reviewed, and that is the point: a label that appears only in the case somebody predicted is a label that will one day be missing from the case nobody did. Render check 25 measures it on an UNREVIEWED score for exactly that reason.
  2. **THE FLAG MARKS THE LEVEL, NEVER THE NOTE.** "Level set by your instructor" renders where `scored_by === "instructor"`, which is true exactly where the chosen level differs from the AI's. A criterion can carry an instructor NOTE while still reading `"ai"`, and it must then show the note with no flag. **THAT CASE WAS BUILT INTO THE PROOF RUN DELIBERATELY** and is render checks 30 and 31.
  3. **THE ORDER INSIDE A CRITERION IS FIXED AND NEVER VARIES:** level badge, then the flag where it applies, then "Instructor note" and the note, then "AI feedback" and the comment. **THE NOTE SITS ABOVE THE AI'S COMMENT WHEREVER A NOTE EXISTS, changed level or not.** On a changed criterion the student is reading a level their instructor chose, and the AI's comment underneath explains the level the AI chose; note-first means the instructor's reasoning arrives beside the instructor's level. **AND A FIXED ORDER IS ITSELF THE FEATURE**: an order that depended on `scored_by` would put the same two paragraphs in two arrangements on one page. Render checks 34 to 36.
  4. **THE FOOTNOTE CHANGES TWO WAYS, NOT THREE, AND "may adjust" IS SAID EXACTLY ONCE.** A practice and a pending submission keep the pre-4b footnote word for word; the amber status line carries the promise. Only a reviewed submission's footnote changes, and the change is not cosmetic: **"Every criterion is graded from the transcript" STOPS BEING TRUE the moment an instructor sets a level**, because they may have judged from the recording or from their own assessment, and the app has no idea which. The reviewed wording attributes that claim to the party it is true of: **"The AI graded every criterion from the transcript."**
  5. **DATES DO NOT MOVE IN 4b**, and the whole app moves to US Central in the session immediately after it. See the timezone entry in Open Issues. <==

==> **ONE COMPONENT, TWO AUDIENCES, AND EXACTLY ONE STRING VARIES.** `RubricScore` takes a **REQUIRED** `audience` prop. Only the flag differs: "Level set by your instructor" for the student, **"Level set in review"** for the instructor's read-only view. "AI feedback" and "Instructor note" NAME WHOSE WORDS FOLLOW and address nobody, so they are written once and are byte-identical on both screens.

  **REQUIRED RATHER THAN DEFAULTED, AND THAT IS NOT FUSSINESS.** A defaulted prop lets a future third call site silently inherit student wording. Required forces every caller, the render check included, to say out loud who it is drawing for. **FORKING THE COMPONENT WAS THE OTHER OPTION AND IT IS WHAT 2b-2 EXISTED TO UNDO**: two renderers of one score drift, and then the two screens tell an instructor and their student different stories about one submission. Render check 44 strips the one varying string and requires the two renders to be otherwise identical, which is the same shape as check 22. <==

==> **THE AI'S ORIGINAL LEVEL IS NEVER SHOWN TO A STUDENT, AND THAT IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN REMEMBERED.** It is not a field on `ScoreItem` and is not reachable from `components/rubric-score.tsx` at all, so there is nothing in that file to accidentally render. **THE CHANGE THAT WOULD BREAK IT IS ONE THAT ADDS THE ORIGINAL TO THAT COMPONENT'S PROPS**, and a comment in the file asks for that review by name. ==> **PHASE 4a (THE INSTRUCTOR REVIEW AND GRADE OVERRIDE) IS DONE AND VERIFIED (2026-09-11).** Commit `7a85dec`, pushed and live, proved end to end against relii.vercel.app inside B1 only. **THERE IS A MIGRATION AND IT WAS APPLIED BEFORE THE CODE WAS PUSHED**: `supabase/phase-4a-review.sql`, recorded in Supabase as `phase_4a_review`. The artifacts are `supabase/phase-4a-baseline.sql`, `phase-4a-review.sql`, `phase-4a-review-revert.sql`, `phase-4a-tests.sql` and `phase-4a-no-rubric-probe.sql`. <==

==> **TOM CAN NOW OVERRULE THE AI, AND THE AI'S ORIGINAL SURVIVES HIM DOING IT.** He picks a LEVEL, never a number; the points are looked up on the server from his own rubric. Confirming a score he agrees with is one click. **THE OVERRIDE IS THE SAFETY NET UNDER EVERY GRADE, NOT A CONVENIENCE**, and it is the mechanism the bimodal-scale entry in Open Issues has been waiting for. <==

**WHAT WAS BUILT.** One route (`app/api/instructor/submission/[id]/review/route.ts`), one client editor (`components/submission-review-form.tsx`), one shared module (`lib/submission-review.ts`), a column, two tables and one database function. The dashboard's Status column and both review-related tiles became real.

==> **THE FIVE RULINGS, AND WHERE EACH ONE IS ENFORCED RATHER THAN INTENDED.**

  1. **A LEVEL, NEVER A NUMBER.** The browser posts level NAMES. `findLevel()` in `lib/scoring.ts` is now EXPORTED and the override calls it: one level lookup in the application, shared with the scorer, not copied. A name off that criterion's ladder is a refusal. Tom's 10 per cent floor is untouched, because every number written came out of his own file.
  2. **THE AI'S ORIGINAL LIVES WHERE NO STUDENT CAN REACH IT**, in `public.submission_ai_scores`, because a student may select every column of their own `sessions` row and there is no way to hide one column from a reader who may read the row.
  3. **THE STUDENT SEES ONE GRADE.** `sessions.score` and `sessions.score_total` are written in the same statement, so the dashboard and the student home cannot disagree.
  4. **THE AI COMMENT STAYS**, taken from the stored original rather than from the row, plus an optional instructor note per criterion, bounded at `MAX_REVIEW_NOTE_CHARS` (1000) and stored as ABSENT when empty. `ScoreItem.scored_by` is now `"ai" | "instructor"` and marks a CHANGED LEVEL only, recomputed against the original every save.
  5. **EVERY SAVE IS LOGGED, AND THE LOG AND THE GRADE MOVE TOGETHER.** `save_submission_review()` writes both in one transaction under `select ... for update` on the session, because PostgREST cannot wrap two writes in a transaction and a rule that holds only when nothing goes wrong is not a rule. <==

==> **"REVIEWED" IS A COLUMN AND NOT A STATUS, AND THAT IS THE LOAD BEARING DECISION.** `sessions_status_check` still permits exactly `in_progress`, `completed`, `scored`, read from `pg_constraint` before AND after the migration and byte-identical both times. Seventeen code sites still key on `'scored'`, and `lib/assignment-sessions.ts` is still TOTAL. A reviewed submission is still finished, scored work. <==

==> **THE ONE GUARD THAT WOULD HAVE BEEN EASY TO GET WRONG, AND THE OWNER ADDED IT.** The route records the AI's original and then calls the function, as two separate requests. **The function now REFUSES (`R0002`) if no original is on record for that session.** Without it, an insert that silently did nothing would let the grade be overwritten and THE AI'S ORIGINAL WOULD BE GONE FOREVER, since it exists nowhere else and the row it was on has just been overwritten. Measured on the real submission before its first review: the refusal fired, no log row was written, and neither the grade nor the marker moved. <==

**THE FUNCTION READS class, assignment and student OFF THE LOCKED ROW** rather than taking them as parameters, also on the owner's instruction. The log row's `class_id` decides whether a class export contains it, so a parameter would be a second source for an answer the row already holds, and a review filed under the wrong class is a grade change the export would not contain and the fingerprint would not notice.

**THE RETAKE NOW RESTORES THE AI'S SCORE AND CLEARS THE REVIEW**, in the same update that returns the row to the pool, and the AI-original read moved ABOVE the audit write so a failed read cannot leave an audit row describing a retake that did not happen. A submission marked reviewed with no original on record is refused outright rather than returned to the pool wearing a human's grade. **The `submission_resets` snapshot deliberately still records the total that was IN FORCE**, which after a review is the instructor's.

==> **AND THE FINGERPRINT HALF OF THE EXPORT CHECK DOES NOT DISCRIMINATE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS NOT THE EVIDENCE.** A review also rewrites `sessions.score`, and sessions were already covered by `select("*")`, so the fingerprint moves whether or not the new tables joined the file. **THE COUNTS ARE THE MEASUREMENT.** Recorded because the kickoff asked for the fingerprint and the fingerprint alone would have certified something it did not test. <==

==> **WHO CHECKED WHAT, BECAUSE MOST OF THIS RUN WAS CHECKED BY ARTIFACTS THE SAME SESSION WROTE.** **THE ONLY EVIDENCE A SECOND PARTY PRODUCED IS THE TWO EXPORT CSVs**, taken by the owner as super admin from his own browser and handed over as files, **AND THE OWNER'S OWN SIGN-INS**, which supplied the accounts the run used. Everything else went through this session's own harness: the tests file, the standalone Playwright rig and the SQL. **WHAT MAKES THAT ACCEPTABLE IS WHERE EACH OUTCOME WAS READ FROM, NOT WHO WROTE THE HARNESS.** No item here passed because an assertion I wrote returned true. Every one was read out of state the APPLICATION produced: an HTTP status and message body from the deployed route, text rendered on a page by the deployed build, or a row read back with SQL after the app wrote it. The harness drove and recorded; it did not decide. **THE ONE ITEM THAT IS PURELY SELF-CHECKED IS THE SCHEMA CATALOGUE** (section A of `phase-4a-tests.sql`, checks 1 to 12), which asks `pg_catalog` whether what the migration said it created exists, and a future reader should treat it as a description of the migration rather than as independent confirmation of it. <==

==> **THREE OF THIS SESSION'S OWN PROBES MISFIRED, AND ALL THREE ARE THE SAME SHAPE: A CHECK THAT ANSWERS CONFIDENTLY ABOUT THE WRONG THING.** (1) Section B of the tests file used `raise notice`, the Supabase MCP does not surface notices, and it returned `[]`: indistinguishable from a block that never ran, one whose every check failed, and a typo. Sections B, C and D now write into a temp table and select it. (2) A status probe matched the word "Reviewed" inside the instructions line above the table rather than the status cell. (3) A section-title grep used a pattern that could not match a title containing lowercase, and silently omitted four sections including the one under test. **NONE OF THESE WERE DEFECTS IN THE APPLICATION**, and every one of them would have been reported as a result. The habit that caught all three was reading what the probe actually matched. <==

==> **PHASE 3a (TWO RECOVERY FIXES PLUS ONE LINT ERROR) IS DONE AND VERIFIED (2026-09-10).** Commit `d5326da`, pushed and live, verified against relii.vercel.app inside B1 only. **THERE IS NO MIGRATION: nothing in this session changed the schema.** The verification artifacts are `supabase/phase-3a-baseline.sql`, `supabase/phase-3a-fix1-probe.sql` and `supabase/phase-3a-fix1-restore.sql`. <==

==> **THE TWO OPEN ISSUES THIS PHASE EXISTED TO CLOSE ARE CLOSED, AND BOTH WERE EXECUTED RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT.** A student whose scoring fails can now recover after a reload, and an assignment held hostage by an invisible session can now be deleted without the confirm that destroys real submissions. <==

**FIX 1. THE REDIRECT LOOP IS GONE, AND THE REPAIR WAS MOSTLY A MOVE RATHER THAN A REWRITE.** `app/student/session/[id]/page.tsx` sent every session that was not `in_progress` to its results, from **line 43, above the assignment read**. Whether a case is graded is the ASSIGNMENT's answer, so up there the page did not yet know what it was redirecting. The check moved below the rubric lookup, and one case now stops instead of redirecting: a graded case with no score.

==> **THE CONDITION IS WRITTEN AS THE LITERAL COMPLEMENT OF THE OTHER PAGE'S RULE, `status !== "scored" || !session.score`, AND THAT PHRASING IS THE POINT.** Both files previously carried comments asserting the loop could not happen, which is exactly how it survived to be found by accident. The two rules are now close enough in shape that a future mismatch is visible on the page rather than deducible from two paragraphs. `score` joined the session page's select for this reason. <==

==> **EVERY ROW THAT REACHES THE CHECK NOW RENDERS AND NONE REDIRECTS, INCLUDING A SUBMISSION, ON THE OWNER'S RULING.** The cheap fix was to leave submissions on the old redirect under a comment saying the state is unreachable. **THAT IS THE PRECISE MOVE THAT KEPT THE ORIGINAL LOOP ALIVE**, so it was refused: a submission gets a screen too, with no retry button, because re-running the scorer on a one-shot graded attempt is the instructor's call. (For the record, and as a REASON rather than a guarantee: no route can produce that state today, since a submission is only converted from an already-scored practice and the live one-shot path is disabled.) The retry reuses `/api/session/end`, so there is still exactly one scoring path in the app.

**FIX 2. THE PLAIN DELETE NOW PROCEEDS WHEN NO FINISHED WORK EXISTS.** What "unfinished" means lives in one place, `lib/assignment-sessions.ts`, and the page, the button and the route all use it, so the three cannot drift. The button receives the tally as ONE object rather than four loose numbers, so it cannot recombine them into a different question from the one the route enforces.

==> **THE RULE IS TOTAL, AND THAT WAS MEASURED BEFORE ANY CODE WAS WRITTEN.** `pg_constraint` was read directly: `sessions_status_check` permits exactly `in_progress`, `completed`, `scored`, and `sessions_kind_check` exactly `practice`, `submission`. All six pairs are decided, so no row the database can hold falls through the helper. A file asserting this existed (`stage-2-data-model.sql` line 84) and was deliberately not trusted, because this project has already paid for three documents quoting each other. <==

**THE PLAIN PATH RUNS THE SAME BODY AS THE IN-USE PATH, NOT A LIGHTER COPY.** A failed-scoring VOICE practice sits at `completed` WITH a recording, so a shortcut that skipped Storage would leak files on the exact case the fix was built for. The audit row is written whenever any session is removed, because it exists since student data was destroyed and not because a strong confirm was shown.

**FIX 3. THE ONE ESLINT ERROR IN THE REPO IS GONE.** `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` at `components/submission-live-gate.tsx`. The decide-once guard moved from a functional updater into a ref. It could not simply be deleted: the effect's cleanup CLEARS the live marker, so Strict Mode's second run reads a marker the component itself just removed, and an unguarded setState flips a legitimate live attempt to LOCKED.

==> **THE STRICT MODE PROOF RAN ON A LOCAL THROWAWAY HARNESS UNDER `next dev`, AND IT IS NOT A LIVE-SITE TEST. THIS IS RECORDED SO NOBODY LATER READS IT AS ONE.** It could not be a live-site test for two independent reasons, either one sufficient: **Strict Mode only double-invokes in DEV**, so a production build never does it; and **the gate does not render in production at all**, because it needs a submission at `in_progress` and the live one-shot path is disabled. The harness imported no Supabase client and made no network calls, was reachable only via a one-line temporary local edit to `PUBLIC_PATHS`, and BOTH the harness page and that edit were reverted and proved gone with `git status` before the commit. <==

**THREE RUNS, BECAUSE ONE GREEN RESULT WOULD HAVE BEEN AN ASSERTION.** Guarded and armed showed LIVE **with the marker already cleared**, which is the double-invoke having demonstrably happened. Guarded and unarmed showed LOCKED. **Guard deleted and armed showed LOCKED**, which is what makes the first result evidence: the harness can detect the defect it rules out. `lockOnReload` is byte-identical, same sha256 before and after, so voice behaviour cannot have changed.

==> **WHAT THE PROOF RUN COVERED, ALL AGAINST relii.vercel.app INSIDE B1.** A REAL scoring failure was produced the honest way, by stripping the levels off one criterion of a throwaway uploaded rubric; **no session's status was ever faked.** The text practice failed to score, and **a RELOAD showed the retry screen instead of ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS.** Navigating straight to `/results` redirected ONCE to the session page and stopped. The retry was pressed while still broken and showed the route's own message (the only place an instructor is mentioned), then the rubric was restored and the retry scored the session 70 out of 100 with all six criteria rendered. The three neighbours all behaved: `in_progress` opened live, `scored` landed on results, and a **conversation-only `completed` landed on results rather than being diverted to the retry screen**, which is the discrimination that matters, since the rule keys on the rubric and never on the status. <==

**FIX 2 WAS PROVED AS A MATCHED SET, MINUTES APART, SAME INSTRUCTOR.** `ZZ 3a fix2` held two unfinished practices, one of them a **failed-scoring VOICE practice made with the fake-media rig**, carrying a real 3,885,010 byte recording. It deleted with the PLAIN confirm; the named Storage object `920c9ea3-.../ebd9ca22-....webm` was present before and **absent after**, the bucket went 23 to 22, and an audit row was written. The matching half: `ZZ 3a fix1`, holding one SCORED practice, still demanded the typed DELETE with its final button disabled. **DELETE WAS NEVER TYPED, ON THE OWNER'S INSTRUCTION**, so cleanup went the other way: the student deleted their own practice first, which left the assignment empty and deletable with the plain confirm.

**THE RACE IS CLOSED AND WAS EXECUTED.** A direct POST without `confirmInUse` against the assignment holding finished work returned **409** and deleted nothing: assignment, session and rubric rows all still 1 afterwards, and **no audit row was written for a refused delete.**

==> **THE DEPLOY WAS CONFIRMED BY A DISCRIMINATING OBSERVATION, AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT AT CONFIRMING IT WAS NOT GOOD ENOUGH. WORTH KNOWING FOR NEXT TIME.** An etag change on `/privacy`, a static page this phase never touched, proves only that A build landed. The real confirmation was the card label: `Not in use (2 unfinished practices, no finished student work)` is a string that **only the new build can produce**, where the old one says `In use: 0 submissions, 2 practices`. **A GREEN RESULT IS EVIDENCE ONLY WHEN YOU KNOW WHICH ARTIFACT PRODUCED IT**, and that applies to the deploy check itself, not only to the feature under test. <==

==> **ONE PERMANENT ROW WAS LEFT BEHIND ON PURPOSE AND IT IS NOT TEST LITTER: `assignment_deletes` id `d1492d69-42bb-418d-8314-991593aafbd2`.** It records the deletion of `ZZ 3a fix2` by `instructor@test.com`, 0 submissions and 2 practices destroyed, at 2026-09-10 22:54 UTC. **THE AUDIT ROW IS CORRECT BEHAVIOUR, NOT A LEFTOVER**: the plain path destroyed student data, so it wrote a record, which is the whole rule. B1's audit count therefore reads **1 rather than 0** from now on, and a later baseline must not read that as damage. <==

**B1 WAS RETURNED TO ITS BASELINE**, measured before anything was touched and re-measured at the end: **1 assignment, 3 sessions, 0 rubrics**, bucket back to 22 objects. **rubrics 0 is the load-bearing one**: both throwaway rubrics are gone, the deliberately broken one included, which is `deleteRubricIfUnused()` having worked on both delete paths.

==> **A SMALL SYMMETRY WORTH RECORDING, BECAUSE IT CLOSES A LOOP FROM 2b-2. THE PLAIN DELETE IS ITSELF THE REVERT FOR A BROKEN THROWAWAY RUBRIC.** `supabase/phase-2b2-scoring-failure-restore.sql` exists only because 2b-2 could not clean up its own probe: the failed-scoring practice was invisible and undeletable, so the assignment could not go, so the broken rubric could not go. Fix 2 IS the repair for that state, and this session used it as such: `ZZ 3a fix2` was left broken and the plain delete took the assignment, both practices, the recording and the broken rubric together. **The workaround that file documents is no longer the only route there is.** <==

==> **PHASE 2c (VERIFICATION) IS DONE (2026-09-10), AND PHASE 2 CLOSES WITH IT. IT WAS A CERTIFICATION SESSION AND IT FOUND A DEFECT, SO IT BECAME A REPAIR TOO.** Commit `3a3ce30`, pushed and live. **THERE IS NO MIGRATION: nothing in this session changed the schema.** <==

==> **THE DEFECT, AND IT WAS OURS. A CORRECTLY GRADED ANSWER WAS BEING SILENTLY CONVERTED INTO THE LOWEST GRADE ON THE RUBRIC.** The prompt wrote every performance level as `Excellent: Ready for an entry-level sales role.` and told the model to return the name "copied exactly as it is written in that criterion's list". `findLevel` matched on the name ALONE. A model that obeyed literally returned the whole string, the match failed, and the criterion took the bottom rung. **Seven of seven criteria at Unsatisfactory, 20 out of 200, with the model's own praise printed underneath each one.** Full record in `docs/Relii-Phase2c-Finding-Level-Match-Failure.md`. <==

**THE MEASUREMENT THAT PROVED IT, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** A scratch copy of the scorer logged the RAW level string beside the level that was stored. Two consecutive calls, same transcript, same seed, same rubric: one returned `"Excellent"` seven times and scored 200/200; the next returned `"Excellent: Ready for an entry-level sales role."` seven times and scored 20/200. **THE MODEL'S JUDGMENT NEVER MOVED. ONLY THE FORMAT DID.**

==> **FOR AN HOUR THIS WAS DIAGNOSED AS MODEL NON-DETERMINISM, AND THE WRONG DIAGNOSIS IS RECORDED RATHER THAN TIDIED AWAY.** The all-or-nothing shape was the tell and it was read backwards: genuine judgment variance gives MIXED levels, never a clean collapse with every criterion moving together. One reply picks one output style and applies it to all of them, which is exactly why it was bistable rather than noisy. **THE OWNER FOUND THE REAL CAUSE BY READING THE PROMPT AGAINST THE MATCHER**, and directed the measurement that confirmed it. The transferable rule: when every item moves together, suspect the contract, not the sampling. <==

**THE FIX, IN THREE PARTS, AND THE THIRD REVERSES A RULING ON PURPOSE.** (a) Each level is now two labelled lines, a `name:` line and a `means:` line, and the instruction says to return only the text after `name:`. **Tom's descriptors STAY**; the 2026-09-09 decision to keep them stands, because without them our prompt becomes the grade boundary instead of his rubric. The ambiguity went, not his words. (b) `findLevel` also accepts an answer whose name portion matches, so a model that echoes the descriptor is still understood; every candidate must still match a real level name exactly and nothing guesses at a nearest rung. (c) **AN UNMATCHED LEVEL, OR AN OMITTED CRITERION, NOW REFUSES TO SCORE RATHER THAN TAKING THE BOTTOM RUNG.**

==> **WHY (c) REVERSES THE OWNER'S OWN 2026-09-09 RULING, IN HIS WORDS: THE OLD FALLBACK COULD NOT TELL "THE MODEL INVENTED A LEVEL" FROM "THE MODEL NAMED THE RIGHT LEVEL AND WROTE IT THE WAY WE ASKED", AND GAVE BOTH THE LOWEST GRADE ON THE RUBRIC. An unmatched level is a SYSTEM failure, not a student performing badly. It now surfaces through the scoring-failure note and retry that 2b-2 already built. TOM'S 10 PER CENT FLOOR IS UNTOUCHED for genuine grading: a student who earns the weakest rung still scores it, because the model NAMED that rung and it matched. What changed is that our parse failure no longer borrows his floor to hide behind. <==

**THE BEFORE AND AFTER, BOTH AGAINST THE REAL API ON THE SAME AFTERNOON.** The `system_fingerprint` turned out to track the PROMPT rather than the clock (`fp_ffd8308b42` old, `fp_27008f43d1` new, both served concurrently), so this is a clean A/B and not a comparison across a backend change. **Old code: 4 of 4 at 20/200, the descriptor echoed in 28 of 28 level strings. New code: 8 of 8 at 200/200, 56 of 56 bare names.** The old code failed 4 of 4 in that control where hours earlier it was roughly half and half, so "it only happens sometimes" was never a safe reading. `scripts/check-scorer.mjs` carries the deterministic half with no API call: **23** checks passing, including a descriptor-echoed level, a `name:`-echoed level, and the two new refusals. ==> **THE NUMBER WAS WRITTEN AS 22 HERE ON 2026-09-10 AND IT WAS A MISCOUNT. THE FILE HAS CARRIED 23 ASSERTIONS SINCE THIS VERY COMMIT.** Corrected 2026-09-11 by Phase 4a, which counted them rather than quoting them: the count at `3a3ce30`, at `71ceeac` and today is 23. **NOTHING IS OR WAS WRONG WITH THE SCORER**: every assertion passes and the file is byte-identical to the one certified here. What was wrong was a number, and it then travelled. From this paragraph into the Phase 4a kickoff prompt, which instructed the next session to confirm "22 passing"; and from there into that session's own plan, which repeated it a third time before anybody re-counted. **THREE DOCUMENTS QUOTING EACH OTHER, WHICH IS THE FAILURE THIS PROJECT HAS ALREADY PAID FOR ONCE.** The rule it re-teaches: a count in prose is not a count. <==

**AND IT WAS FOUND BY A BUG IN THE TEST SCRIPT, NOT BY THE TEST.** The "click the button in the card with this title" helper walked up past the card into the list holding every card, so the first card's button matched the last card's title: it practised the Presentation assignment while reporting the Discovery one. That accident put a seven-criterion score beside a six-criterion one on the same afternoon, and the comparison is what made it obviously wrong. **A script that mislabels which thing it is testing is more dangerous than one that crashes.**

**WHAT WAS BUILT.** `components/rubric-score.tsx` was rewritten: one collapsible section per criterion, the criterion's name and earned over possible in the header, and the description, the chosen level, the points and the comment in the row. Sections are keyed and anchored by criterion ID, not name, so two criteria sharing a name cannot collide. `app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx` and `lib/instructor-submission.ts` moved onto the assignment's rubric, closing the divergence 2b-1 created. The CSV gained a Level column, lost Category, and states the rubric's name once in its header block. A scoring failure now tells the student, on the conversation screen, in the owner's words.

It also does the `Number()` at the boundary, which is 2b-1's `max_total` lesson applied where it belongs. **The results page percentage used to round to a whole number, so a 59.5 out of 100 sat directly under the words "59.5 / 100" reading "60%".** It now keeps one decimal. <==

**WHAT THE RUN PROVED, IN PRODUCTION, IN B1.** The real Discovery rubric uploaded THROUGH THE FORM: the preview showed its name and six criterion names and no point grid; swapping to the Presentation rubric with the confirm already ticked cleared the tick and swapped the preview (`checked: false`, measured, not inferred); creating without confirming was refused with "Please confirm the rubric preview before creating."; then it created. A text practice scored **69 / 100** and rendered as six sections with names, levels, points and comments. **Print was FORCED, not reasoned about**: on screen five of six panels compute to `display:none`, under print emulation all six compute to `display:block` with their full text, and the printed sheet shows every criterion expanded with the chevrons gone. A voice and video practice with a real 1:57 recording was submitted, and the instructor page and the CSV were both taken from that real submission. A broken fixture posted through the form was refused with **"The criterion "Discovery" (row 7) is missing its Developing value in column F. Every criterion needs all 6 performance levels filled in."**

==> **THE HALF-POINT PREDICTION WAS STATED BEFORE THE RUN AND IT MISSED, WHICH IS WHY IT WAS STATED.** The call was Communication Skills at Limited, 3.5, with the base rate attached (four of its six rungs are halves). It landed on Developing, 4. Halves rendered anyway on Close (10.5 / 15) and Overall Impression (2.5 / 5), and at the bottom rung on the voice run (5.5 / 55, 1.5 / 15, 0.5 / 5). <==

==> **NO SCORED TOTAL IN THIS RUN CARRIED A HALF, AND THAT IS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN A DEFECT. RECORDED SO IT IS NOT INVESTIGATED AS ONE.** Four of the Discovery rubric's six criteria have half-valued rungs, and an EVEN number of halves always sums to a whole. The all-bottom-rung run is 1 + 5.5 + 1.5 + 1 + 0.5 + 0.5 = **10.0 exactly**. A half TOTAL needs an ODD number of half-valued rungs to land. Two integer totals in two runs is the expected outcome, and 10.0 landing exactly is itself evidence that nothing is rounding. The total's half is proved instead by `formatPoints(59.5)` in the render check. <==

**THE GRADING FLOOR, OBSERVED LIVE FOR THE FIRST TIME.** Both voice runs scored **10 out of 100**, the bottom rung on all six criteria, and the comments quote the real conversation ("repetitive", "does not adapt to the customer's responses"), so the scorer read the transcript and graded it honestly. That is 2b-1's stated policy happening: the ladder bottoms out at 10 per cent and there is no true zero.

==> **THE VERIFICATION ARTIFACT, BECAUSE A PIECE WITH NO MIGRATION IS NOT A PIECE WITH NO PROOF.** `scripts/check-rubric-render.mjs` compiles the SHIPPED component and renders it on Node against an **invented POTTERY rubric** (Centring, Trimming; Kiln-ready, Cracked) with halves at both ends of the ladder and **two criteria deliberately sharing a name**. 24 checks, all passing: every points pair is compared as a complete multiset rather than hunted for with `includes()`, a score with NO level renders and differs from the same row WITH one by exactly the level and nothing else, an item whose criterion is missing still renders under its id, and every collapsed section is still in the document carrying the print class. **TWO OF ITS CHECKS FAILED ON FIRST RUN AND BOTH WERE THE CHECK'S FAULT**: "4.5 / 15" CONTAINS the substring "5 / 15", and the panel ids also start with "rubric-". Written up in the file, because a check that can fail by accident can pass by accident. <==

==> **CHECK 16 OF `supabase/phase-2a-tests.sql` HAD BEEN RED SINCE 2b-1, NOT "VACUOUSLY GREEN" AS TWO HANDOFFS AND THE 2b-2 KICKOFF ALL SAID. MEASURED AT 2 BY THE PRE-FLIGHT BEFORE ANYTHING WAS BUILT.** The check counts assignments whose SCENARIO carries a rubric and whose own rubric `is distinct from` it, and `null is distinct from <uuid>` is TRUE in SQL, so 2b-1 setting every rubric to null did not empty the count, it FILLED it. The replacement query is applied and the wrong prose in `phase-2b1-tests.sql` is corrected. **THE TRANSFERABLE RULE: a check that will expire needs its expiry condition written in SQL terms, not in project terms.** "When the upload field goes live" is a fact about a roadmap; "when any assignment's rubric_id stops equalling its scenario's, INCLUDING BY BECOMING NULL" is a fact about the query, and that one would have caught it a phase earlier. Checks 10 and 11 of the 2b-1 file became INFORMATION rows carrying their migration-day baseline, because a check that can only ever go red from here is a trap rather than a test. <==

==> **THE KICKOFF'S CAMERA FLAG WAS WRONG AND IT OPENED THE OWNER'S REAL CAMERA.** It says `--use-fake-device-for-media-capture`. **Chrome ignores that spelling and falls through to real hardware**: the first smoke test returned a live stream labelled "FaceTime HD Camera" and "AirPods". Nothing was recorded or written and the browser was closed at once. The documented flag is **`--use-fake-device-for-media-stream`**, which gives `fake_device_0` and "Fake Default Audio Input". Both kickoff files were corrected by the owner the same day. **THE LESSON IS THE ORDER: check the device LABELS BEFORE the run, not after.** Checking after is how you find out; checking before costs nothing. <==

**AND THE FAKE MICROPHONE NEEDED SILENCE, WHICH IS WORTH KNOWING FOR 2c.** Chrome loops `--use-file-for-fake-audio-capture` with no gap, so a file of wall-to-wall speech never lets a turn END: the first voice run produced a real recording and an EMPTY transcript on both sides, because the turn detector never fired. Padding the same speech with six seconds of silence produced a real two-sided conversation. The recording was fine both times; it was the turn boundary that was missing.

**CLEANUP PROVED, NOT ASSERTED, AND ONE PART OF IT WENT BEYOND ROW COUNTS.** B1 is back to its baseline of 1 assignment, 0 rubrics and 3 sessions, and the whole-database counts are unmoved (1 rubric, 5 assignments, 32 scored sessions, 2 scenarios naming a rubric, 25 sessions with no assignment). **THE RECORDINGS WERE CHECKED IN STORAGE ITSELF, BEFORE AND AFTER**, by listing the bucket: both 8 MB recordings this session created are gone and the September 3 baseline recording survives. So deleting a practice through the app really does remove its object, and the deferred orphan-Storage gap is not reachable by that path.

**THE PHASE 2b-1 RECORD FOLLOWS AND IS KEPT AS HISTORY.**

==> **PHASE 2b-1 (THE SCORING ENGINE) IS BUILT, APPLIED AND PROVED BY CLAUDE'S OWN END-TO-END RUN (2026-09-10). THE OWNER HAS NOT CLICKED THROUGH IT HIMSELF.** Commits `aaa9aa9` and `100d3b5`, migration `phase_2b1_scoring_engine`. <==

==> **THE APP NOW SCORES NOTHING, AND THAT IS THE INTENDED STATE, NOT A FAULT.** Scoring moved from the scenario's rubric to the assignment's, and the migration set every `assignments.rubric_id` to null, so every assignment is conversation-only until 2b-2 turns the upload control on. It is safe (no real student exists, all data is demo and test) and **IT IS NOT SAFE TO LEAVE SITTING**: Tom's students register the week of 2026-09-14. 2b-2 follows immediately. <==

**WHAT WAS BUILT.** `lib/scoring.ts` rewritten: **the AI emits a LEVEL NAME and nothing else**, and the points are looked up server-side from that criterion's own ladder, so the model cannot invent a number that looks valid. `ScoreItem` gained `level`. The `Math.round` that turned Tom's 49.5 into 50 is gone, and there is nothing left to round because the value is copied out of the rubric. `contentItemsForMeeting()`, `applies_to` and the `meetings` tags **no longer govern scoring at all**; every criterion is scored, Communication Skills and Overall Impression included, regardless of `meeting_type`. Seven reader files moved from `scenarios.rubric_id` to `assignments.rubric_id`.

**A CRITERION THE MODEL OMITS, OR A LEVEL IT NAMES THAT IS NOT ON THAT CRITERION'S LADDER, TAKES THE BOTTOM RUNG, NOT ZERO.** Tom's ladder bottoms out at 10 per cent and holds no true zero, so awarding a 0 would invent a grade the rubric does not contain. **THE GRADING CONSEQUENCE, STATED SO IT IS NOT DISCOVERED DURING THE PILOT: a student who says nothing at all still scores 10 out of 100 on the Discovery rubric.** That is his ladder working as designed.

==> **THE PARSER NOW KEEPS TOM'S LEVEL DESCRIPTORS, AND THE OWNER'S REASON FOR IT IS THE ONE TO REMEMBER.** 2a stored `Excellent` and discarded `Ready for an entry-level sales role.` The migration-window argument (zero levels-rubrics existed, so it cost one parser change and no backfill; after 2b-2 it would need every rubric re-uploaded) was the SECOND reason. **THE FIRST: without the descriptors the six names are bare labels, and the only thing defining what each level MEANS would be a generic ladder WE wrote, which would make our prompt the grade boundary instead of Tom's rubric.** The system message now carries only a positional note about the list's order; his words do the defining. <==

**WHAT THE RUN PROVED, AND THE ONE NUMBER THAT CARRIES IT.** A crafted assignment in B1 with the real Discovery rubric attached, then an ordinary **text practice through the student's own button** as `2studentb1@gmail.com`, scored **59.5 out of 100**. Every one of the six points values is the exact rubric lookup for the level the model named: Approach/Limited 7, Discovery/Developing 44, Close/Unsatisfactory **1.5**, Objections/Unsatisfactory 1, Communication Skills/Limited **3.5**, Overall Impression/Minimal **2.5**. **HALF POINTS SURVIVED IN THREE CRITERIA AND IN `sessions.score_total`, which holds 59.5 as numeric.** The bottom rung paid 1.5 and 1, never 0.

==> **THE SINGLE MOST CONVINCING ROW IN THE WHOLE RUN: THAT SESSION'S SCENARIO HAS `rubric_id` NULL AND ITS `meeting_type` IS `presentation`, AND IT WAS SCORED 59.5 AGAINST THE DISCOVERY RUBRIC.** Under the old code a scenario with no rubric was conversation-only and could not be scored at all, and a presentation could not be graded by discovery items. The score exists only because the rubric came from the ASSIGNMENT. That is a positive identification that the new code is live and that the source moved, rather than an inference from a passing page. <==

**THE CONVERSATION-ONLY HALF, ALSO EXECUTED.** A second B1 assignment with no rubric: the graded control absent on the student home, `/api/assignment/start` refused **400 "This is a practice-only assignment with no grade"** on a `voice_video` assignment (so the rubric branch fired, not a mode check), the end-of-session confirm reading **"This practice is not scored."** where the graded one reads "and see your score?", and the practice ending at `completed` with score, `score_total` and `scored_at` all null.

==> **TWO THINGS THE 2b-1 KICKOFF ASSERTED THAT THE CODE AND THE DATABASE CONTRADICTED, BOTH FOUND BEFORE ANYTHING WAS BUILT.**

**1. "Every start route sets `assignment_id`, so no such row exists." TWENTY-FIVE DO.** They are pre-Stage-7 test practices. They are not corruption and the rule that governs them is now written into three files and into Open Issues below: **a session with no assignment is conversation-only.** The owner accepted the finding and the arithmetic that came with it: **all 32 scored sessions now render score-less on the STUDENT results page**, 25 because they have no assignment and the rest because their assignment's rubric is now null. Nothing was deleted and an instructor still sees them.

**2. NOTHING HAD EVER READ `assignments.rubric_id` THROUGH ROW LEVEL SECURITY.** Every existing reader uses the service-role key, which bypasses grants entirely; 2b-1 is the first to read it with the ordinary logged-in client. Gate 2 revoked whole-table select on `scenarios` and re-granted it column by column, and its own note says a column added afterwards is granted to nobody. **HAD `assignments` CARRIED THAT TREATMENT, THE STUDENT HOME WOULD HAVE RENDERED WITH NO ASSIGNMENTS AND NOTHING IN THE LOGS**, because that page reads only `data`: the same shape as the ambiguous-foreign-key bug already written up in it. A pre-flight check was added and **PASSED on both tables**, and the live run then listed three assignments, so it is measured twice. <==

==> **A TYPE THAT WOULD HAVE BROKEN THE FIRST REAL SCORE, CAUGHT IN REVIEW AND THEN OBSERVED FOR REAL.** `rubrics.max_total` is a Postgres `numeric`, and `Number.isFinite` does NOT coerce, so a value arriving as a string would have been refused with "this rubric does not state a maximum" on the one value that gates all scoring: a message saying the OPPOSITE of what is true. It is now coerced with `Number()` at the boundary in the end route. **THE READ-BACK DURING THE RUN RETURNED THE STRING `"100"`, NOT THE NUMBER**, so this was a live defect and not a hypothetical. The rule: put the type conversion where the database meets the typed code, not three functions away.

**AND THE FROM-SCRATCH PROOF THAT 2a PAID FOR.** `scripts/check-scorer.mjs` runs 20 checks against an **INVENTED** rubric whose six levels are Gold, Silver, Bronze, Copper, Tin and Slag, with points that are nothing like Tom's, so it cannot pass by resembling the samples. It proves a wrong-case name matches, a padded name matches, an invented level and an omitted criterion both fall to the bottom rung, no fallback is ever zero, `max_total` is the stated one rather than a sum, and all four refusals fire. All 20 passed, executed. The 10 fixtures from 2a were re-run against the changed parser and behave identically. <==

**CLEANUP PROVED, NOT ASSERTED.** B1 is back to its baseline of 1 assignment, 0 rubrics and 3 sessions; the uploaded rubric was removed by 2a's own orphan branch; no `ZZ 2b1 TEST` row survives anywhere; and the whole-database counts (1 rubric, 5 assignments, 32 scored sessions, 2 scenarios naming a rubric) are unmoved. **The practices were deleted BEFORE the assignments, which was necessary twice over:** sessions reference assignments with delete-restrict, and an assignment with sessions demands the instructor type the word DELETE, which is a guard this project forbids typing past. With zero sessions both deletes were the plain two-step confirm.

**THE PHASE 2a RECORD FOLLOWS AND IS KEPT AS HISTORY.**

==> **PHASE 2a (RUBRIC INTAKE) IS DONE AND VERIFIED (2026-09-09). THE INTAKE HALF IS BUILT AND THE ONE CONTROL IT REWROTE WAS TESTED IN PRODUCTION FIRST.** Commits `fea61d4` and `204372c`. Nothing a user can see changed: the rubric field is still disabled and still says so. <==

**WHAT WAS BUILT.** `assignments.rubric_id` nullable with a same-class composite key matching Gate 4a's pattern, `rubrics.max_total` as its own column (NOT smuggled into the `items` array, which is constrained to an array every consumer walks as criteria), a backfill giving both existing assignments their scenario's rubric, and one deliberately dumb function `create_assignment_with_rubric()` that writes a rubric and its assignment in a single transaction because PostgREST cannot. Plus `lib/rubric-xlsx.ts` (the parser), `lib/rubric-upload.ts` (shared by both routes so they cannot drift), and two routes.

**THE CHANGE THAT WAS INVISIBLE AND MATTERED MOST.** Creating an assignment no longer inserts from the browser. `components/create-assignment-form.tsx` POSTs to `app/api/instructor/assignment/create/route.ts`, **WHICH USES THE SERVICE-ROLE KEY AND THEREFORE BYPASSES ROW LEVEL SECURITY COMPLETELY**, so the authorisation the "instructors manage assignments" policy used to provide is re-implemented in the route with `requireInstructorRequest()` and `refuseOutsideClass()`. That policy STAYS as the backstop and now carries a note in `supabase/gate-3a-policies.sql` saying it is untriggered rather than unused.

**WHAT WAS MEASURED, IN THIS ORDER AND FOR THIS REASON.** The first test was NOT the security check. It was an ordinary assignment created through the form the way a human uses it, because that is the path Tom walks the week of 2026-09-14 and it is the one this piece rewrote; a broken create form is a launch-week outage, while a class-check flaw needs somebody to go looking for it.

1. **Ordinary create, no rubric, through the form: PASSED.** Every optional field set to a non-default so the round trip was measured rather than assumed. Landed in B1, author owns that class, `rubric_id` null, `Text` mode, 15 min, both dates and instructions exact.
2. **The class check, as a MATCHED SET, not a lone refusal.** Identical body, identical session, seconds apart, varying only the target's class. Scenario in my working class: **200, created**. Scenario in ANOTHER INSTRUCTOR's class: **404 "That scenario was not found."** Scenario in my OWN other class, not my working class: **404, identical wording**. Server side: the refusals wrote **zero rows**. The third result is the useful extra: owning a class is not enough, you must be working in it, and a stranger and your own other class get byte-identical wording so neither reveals the row exists.
3. **The rubric field renders disabled** with the approved copy and "Excel file (.xlsx), up to 512 KB" rendering from the same constant the server enforces.
4. **BOTH BRANCHES OF THE RUBRIC CLEANUP, EXECUTED RATHER THAN INFERRED.** Two assignments were created in B1 sharing one uploaded rubric. Deleting the second left the rubric ALIVE because the first still referenced it (the "keep" branch). Deleting the first CLEANED THE RUBRIC UP because nothing referenced it (the "orphan" branch). Great Northwoods was never touched, and the whole sequence stayed inside B1 on data created for it. **A rolled-back SQL block would have proved the reference COUNTS and left `deleteRubricIfUnused()` itself unexecuted, which is the gap this project exists to close.**

**CLEANUP PROVED, NOT ASSERTED.** B1 is back to its single baseline assignment and zero rubrics, no test row remains anywhere, one rubric total, and the historical class is untouched at three assignments.

==> **A BUG THE TWO REAL RUBRICS WERE HIDING, FOUND DURING THIS RUN AND FIXED IN `204372c`.** `parseRubricXlsx()` bounded its row scan with `sheet.actualRowCount`, which is the **COUNT of non-empty rows and not the INDEX of the last one**. Both of Tom's workbooks fill every row from 1 to 18, so the two numbers are equal and the wrong property returned the right answer on every sample available. **ONE BLANK ROW MAKES THEM DIVERGE**: a rubric using rows 1 and 3 to 17 with row 2 empty has a count of 16 and a last index of 17, the scan stops a row short, and "Maximum points:" is reported missing when it is plainly there. Tom adding a spacer row on a re-save would have hit it in production. **IT WAS FOUND BY A HAND-BUILT MINIMAL RUBRIC, NOT BY EITHER REAL FILE, AND THAT IS THE LESSON: a fixture derived from the samples can only ever confirm what the samples already show.** A regression fixture with a deliberate blank row is now generated by `scripts/make-rubric-fixtures.mjs` and must parse. <==

</details>

- Phase 3a: two recovery fixes plus the repo's one eslint error (2026-09-10, commit `d5326da`). The failed-scoring redirect loop is closed with a retry screen; the plain assignment delete proceeds when no finished work exists, sharing one definition of "unfinished" across the page, the button and the route; the decide-once guard in the submission live gate moved to a ref and was proved under Strict Mode on a local throwaway harness. No schema change.
- Stage 0: project scaffolding (CLAUDE.md, PROJECT_STATE.md, docs/, .gitignore, git repo, GitHub at https://github.com/mtbanta/relii).
- Stage 1: app skeleton plus invite-only auth, deployed live.
  - Installed Node.js (the engine that runs the app) on the owner's Mac.
  - Created the Next.js app (Next.js 16, React 19, Tailwind for styling).
  - Connected the app to a Supabase project (project ref cmpvyralyungfmjoteex). Public keys live in a git-ignored .env.local locally and in Vercel's environment variables for the live site.
  - Built invite-only email and password login with two roles (student and instructor):
    - Two database tables: allowed_emails (the invite list) and profiles (one row per user, holds their role).
    - A database trigger blocks anyone whose email is not on the invite list from registering. This is enforced in the database, so it cannot be bypassed from the app.
    - Row Level Security so only instructors can read or add to the invite list.
    - The full database setup is saved in supabase/schema.sql.
    - Students land on /student and instructors on /instructor (both mostly empty for now). The instructor page has a working form to add emails to the invite list.
    - Email confirmation is turned OFF in Supabase, so invited users can log in immediately after registering.
    - The first instructor account (michaeltbanta@gmail.com) was seeded into the invite list.
  - Deployed to Vercel. Live and working at https://relii.vercel.app (login and inviting both confirmed working in production).
- Stage 2: data model. Built on Stage 1's two tables, four new tables created in Supabase.
  - rubrics: grading criteria stored as flexible JSON (swappable without code changes). Each item carries its points and a "both" or "voice-only" tag. No rubric loaded yet; the Great Northwoods 290-point criteria is loaded later.
  - scenarios: the role-play cases. Each holds a persona name, a persona system prompt, scenario context, and a required link to the rubric that scores it. Persona details live inside each scenario for now (decision E). No scenarios loaded yet.
  - assignments: an instructor points to a scenario and sets the required mode (text or voice_video). Optional instructions and due date (decision D).
  - sessions: one row per attempt. Holds the student, scenario, optional assignment link, kind (practice or submission), mode, status (in_progress, completed, scored), the transcript, the video path, the itemized score JSON, and a numeric total for sorting.
  - Built-in integrity: a submission must belong to an assignment; you cannot delete a rubric, scenario, or assignment that is still in use; deleting a student removes that student's sessions.
  - Row Level Security: students read only their own sessions; instructors read submissions but not practice runs; any instructor sees all submissions (decision B); scenarios, rubrics, and assignments are readable by any logged-in user and editable only by instructors. Sessions are server-write-only via the service-role key (decision A), so scores cannot be tampered with from the browser. Free practice not tied to an assignment is allowed (decision C).
  - Verified in the database that a student cannot read another student's sessions (a different student saw 0 rows, the owner saw 2). Test data was rolled back, so nothing persisted.
  - SQL saved in supabase/stage-2-data-model.sql (Stage 1's schema.sql untouched). Plain-English description in docs/data-model.md, ready to share with Tom.
  - Not built yet (Stage 7 lifecycle, schema supports both): enforcing the cap of 3 practice sessions per student per assignment, and deleting a student's practices for an assignment when they submit.
- Stage 3: text role-play demo with content-only scoring. Built and deployed live.
  - Seeded the data (no schema change): the Great Northwoods 290-point rubric and the Alex "Discovery-Close" scenario, copied verbatim from docs/persona-alex-procurement.md and docs/rubric-great-northwoods.json. The seed SQL is generated from those files by supabase/build-stage-3-seed.mjs into supabase/stage-3-seed.sql (safe to re-run; it skips rows that already exist).
  - A logged-in student picks the Alex scenario, holds a typed conversation with an AI persona that stays in character, ends the session, and sees an itemized content score alongside the transcript.
  - The conversation is a server-side OpenAI call (model gpt-4o by default, changeable via the OPENAI_MODEL env var). The OpenAI key and the Supabase service-role key are server-only env vars, never sent to the browser.
  - Sessions are written only by the server using the service-role key (decision A). The transcript is saved each turn; the student reads their own session back through Row Level Security.
  - Content-only scoring: when the session ends, the server scores the transcript against the 18 Discovery content items (180 points) in one low-temperature, structured (JSON) pass, with a short evidence comment per item, and saves the itemized score plus the total. Voice and delivery items are not scored in text mode.
  - To keep re-scoring consistent (the requirement was "within a few points"), each item is awarded one of five anchor values (0, 3, 6, 8, 10) with a fixed random seed. Verified: re-scoring the same transcript stayed within a 3-point spread. Trade-off: scores are coarser, so finer calibration is left for the full scoring stage.
  - New code: lib/openai.ts, lib/persona.ts, lib/scoring.ts, lib/types.ts, lib/supabase/admin.ts; the API routes app/api/session/start, app/api/chat, and app/api/session/end; the screens app/student/session/[id] (conversation) and app/student/session/[id]/results (score plus transcript); and the Practice button on the student home.
  - Live on Vercel. The two server-only secrets (OPENAI_API_KEY, SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY) are set in Vercel's Production environment. A fix was added so a key pasted with a stray line break (a common dashboard paste artifact) no longer breaks the request: the code strips whitespace from both keys before use.
- Stage 4: real-time voice role-play. Built and deployed live (confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app).
  - A logged-in student picks the Alex scenario, clicks Practice (voice), and holds a spoken conversation with Alex over the OpenAI Realtime API. Alex greets first, the transcript appears live on screen, and ending the call produces the same itemized content score (out of 180) on the same results screen as the text demo.
  - Architecture: the browser connects directly to OpenAI over WebRTC for low latency. The browser never sees the real OpenAI key. The server mints a short-lived ephemeral token (a client secret that expires after about a minute) using the real key, and the browser uses only that token to open the voice session. Confirmed in testing: latency feels like a real call, with no dropouts.
  - Persona: the voice session uses the same instructions as the text demo (the scenario persona_system_prompt plus scenario_context, with the context fenced off as background), not a rewrite. Alex is nudged to open the meeting with a hidden message that is not saved to the transcript (mirrors the text demo's opening nudge).
  - Turn-taking: OpenAI server-side voice-activity detection. The silence-before-reply is set to 1600ms so the student is not cut off mid-thought (tuned with the owner during testing). It is a single number in lib/realtime.ts (silence_duration_ms) if it needs adjusting.
  - Transcript: captured in the browser from the Realtime events (Alex's speech, and the student's speech transcribed by gpt-4o-transcribe), then saved turn by turn through the server using the service-role key (the server stays the only writer). Transcript accuracy confirmed high in testing.
  - Scoring reuses the Stage 3 content scorer unchanged (the 18 Discovery content items, 180-point max), and the end route (/api/session/end) is shared with the text demo. Scoring confirmed thorough and accurate in testing.
  - Mode: voice sessions are stored as mode voice_video (the schema has no voice-only value) with video_path left null until Stage 5. Confirmed with the owner; no schema change.
  - Model: the voice model defaults to gpt-realtime, changeable via the OPENAI_REALTIME_MODEL env var (not set in Vercel, so it uses the default). Reuses the existing OPENAI_API_KEY; no new secrets were needed to deploy.
  - New code: lib/realtime.ts (mints the token; holds the voice, VAD, and transcription settings); the API routes app/api/voice/start, app/api/voice/token (mints at connect time, since the token expires quickly), and app/api/voice/transcript (saves each finished turn); components/voice-conversation.tsx (the WebRTC client and live transcript). The student session page now picks the voice or text screen by the session's mode; the home has a Practice (voice) button next to Practice (text); the results label is mode-aware.
  - App-wide change: the app is now forced to light mode. The default theme followed the operating system's dark mode, which flipped the page to a black background and washed out the gray transcript text. Reversible later if a polished dark theme is wanted.
  - Checkpoint decision (recorded): real-time WebRTC is stable enough (good latency, accurate transcript, no cutting the student off), so we kept it. The turn-based voice fallback was not needed.
  - Security hardening (after Stage 4, from the Supabase Security Advisor): revoked public/anon/authenticated execute on public.handle_new_user() so it can no longer be called by hand through the REST API. The signup trigger is unaffected (it runs as the function owner). SQL saved in supabase/stage-4-security-hardening.sql, run and verified in production (invited signup still works and lands on the right page; un-invited signup still blocked). Leaked-password protection (the other advisor item) is now enabled in Supabase Auth, after the Supabase Pro upgrade.
- Stage 5: video capture (webcam recording for the voice role-play). Built and deployed live (confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app).
  - In a voice practice with Alex, the student's webcam records alongside the live call, the recording uploads to a private Supabase Storage bucket, the session's video_path is saved, and the student plays their recording back on the results screen next to the content score and transcript. No schema change: the existing sessions.video_path column is now populated, and mode stays voice_video.
  - One camera-plus-microphone permission and one stream. The call sends only the microphone to OpenAI; the same stream also drives a muted live self-view and the recorder, so the student is prompted once and the call and recorder do not fight over the devices.
  - The recording's audio is the full conversation: the student's microphone and Alex's incoming voice are mixed together with the Web Audio API. Each voice has its own volume dial in components/voice-conversation.tsx (MIC_GAIN and ALEX_GAIN, both 1.0); balance confirmed good in testing. The approach is persona-agnostic, so it will cover Jordan later with no change. Recording is capped at about 1 Mbps video plus 96 kbps audio (roughly 8 MB per minute of call) so files stay small and uploads stay reliable.
  - Encoder tuned to stay out of the live call's way. The recorder uses the lighter VP8 codec (not VP9) and captures at 720p/24fps. Early testing showed brief choppiness in Alex's live audio on a couple of responses; it was traced to the recording's encode load (a camera-off call was consistently clean), and these lighter settings fixed it (confirmed clean on the live URL). 720p is still clear enough for later delivery review.
  - Camera defaults on in practice, with an on/off toggle. Off records nothing and video_path stays null. The camera-required-and-locked rule for graded submissions is intentionally NOT here; it is Stage 7 and sits on top of this capture and storage (using the session's kind and mode) with no change needed here.
  - Upload mechanism (decision, tradeoff explained to the owner): a server-minted one-time signed upload URL. The browser uploads the file straight to Supabase Storage, which bypasses the app's request-size limit (about 4.5 MB on Vercel), the server picks the exact storage path, and the service-role key never leaves the server. Sending the file through the app was rejected for that size limit.
  - Storage: a private bucket named session-videos (not publicly readable). Files are stored at <student-user-id>/<session-id>.webm, so the first folder is the owning student's id. Access rule (supabase/stage-5-storage.sql): a student can download only the files in their own user-id folder; there is deliberately no student write rule (uploads use the server-minted signed link), mirroring sessions being server-written (decision A). Playback is gatekept by the server too: the results page mints a short-lived (1 hour) signed download link only after the existing session ownership check. The service-role key never reaches the browser.
  - Cross-student isolation verified in production: each student's recordings sit in their own user-id folder, and a second student who pasted another student's exact results URL got a 404 (the session RLS blocked the read, so no video link was ever minted). This extends the Stage 2 session-isolation check to the video.
  - Failure handling: a failed upload never loses the transcript or score. The student sees a clear message, video_path stays null, and scoring still runs. Confirmed in testing (an over-limit upload failed and the score still saved, with the "could not be saved" note shown).
  - Storage limits: the Supabase project upload limit and the session-videos bucket limit were raised to 200 MB (Pro plan) after the default 50 MB rejected a test clip. At about 8 MB per minute, real-length calls stay well under 200 MB.
  - Deferred to Stage 8 (instructor dashboard): letting instructors watch the recordings attached to graded submissions. The storage access rule for that is added then, mirroring how instructors already read submission rows.
  - New code: lib/storage.ts (bucket name and path helper); the API routes app/api/voice/video/upload-url (mints the signed link) and app/api/voice/video/save (records video_path server-side). components/voice-conversation.tsx gained the camera toggle, the live self-view, the mixed-audio recorder, and an upload-then-score ending. The results screen gained the recording player. SQL saved in supabase/stage-5-storage.sql.
- Stage 7 (lifecycle), first chunk: an instructor can permanently delete a student. Built and deployed live (all four owner checks passed on https://relii.vercel.app).
  - Sequencing: Stage 7 is being built BEFORE Stage 6, a deliberate reorder (Tom's pending feedback bears on scoring). Only this one chunk of Stage 7 is done; the rest is still pending (see Next Steps).
  - On the instructor page, a Students roster lists every registered student, each with a Delete action behind a two-step confirmation that names the student and spells out exactly what is removed (account, sessions, videos, invite-list entry). It cannot be undone.
  - The delete runs SERVER-SIDE ONLY with the service-role key (never the browser), in app/api/instructor/delete-student/route.ts. Order of operations is videos first, then rows: it removes the student's files from the private session-videos bucket (a database cascade does NOT touch Storage, so this is explicit), then deletes the student's auth (login) user, then deletes their invite-list (allowed_emails) entry by email so the address cannot re-register.
  - What cascades vs what is deleted by hand: deleting the auth user automatically removes the profile (profiles.id references auth.users on delete cascade) and all their sessions, transcripts and scores (sessions.student_id references auth.users on delete cascade). Deleted by hand: the Storage video files (not in any database cascade) and the allowed_emails row (keyed by email, with no link to the auth user).
  - Guarded twice: the route independently confirms the caller is an instructor (read from the database, not trusted from the browser) and refuses unless the target's role is exactly student. That blocks deleting yourself or another instructor; an explicit self-delete check is a backstop. Confirmed live: the instructor account shows no Delete option.
  - No schema change. The roster is read server-side with the service-role key (the instructor page runs on the server, so the key never reaches the browser), so the existing "read own profile" rule did not need to change.
  - Verified by the owner on the live site: a throwaway student's profile, login, sessions, video files, and allowed_emails entry were all removed with nothing orphaned, and a different student's data and videos were completely untouched.
  - New code: app/api/instructor/delete-student/route.ts (the server-side delete), components/delete-student-button.tsx (the confirm panel), and the Students roster added to app/instructor/page.tsx. No new dependencies, no schema change.
- Stage 7 (lifecycle), second chunk: the assignment submission flow (the foundation the rest of Stage 7 builds on). Built and deployed live; all owner checks passed on https://relii.vercel.app.
  - Instructor side: on the instructor page, a Create assignment form (pick a scenario, pick the required mode text or voice+video, optional instructions and due date) plus a list of the assignments that instructor created. The assignment row is written straight from the browser under the existing instructors-only database rule (the same way the invite form works), so no new server route was needed for creating assignments. Assignments are instructor-managed content, like the invite list and scenarios; sessions are the opposite (always server-written).
  - Student side: the home now has a "Your assignments" area. Each assignment shows the right control for its state: Start assignment (not started), an "interrupted, contact your instructor" note (started but left), or Submitted with the score and a View result link (finished). Free practice on the scenarios is unchanged below it.
  - Starting a graded submission creates a session with kind = submission, the assignment linked, mode = the assignment's required mode, and runs the existing text or voice+video screen. Text opens with a generated greeting (like text practice); voice+video is greeted live on the call. It ends with an explicit submit-and-score confirmation step (its one official attempt), is scored by the existing content scorer unchanged, stamps submitted_at, and shows the existing results screen. The submission stores kind = submission with the assignment link and the saved score.
  - One official submission per assignment per student (owner-confirmed), enforced two ways: the start route refuses to create a second submission, and a partial unique index (supabase/stage-7-submission.sql, run in production) makes a second one impossible at the database level. Practice rows are unaffected by the index.
  - A graded submission is ONE continuous, one-shot attempt with NO resume (owner decision, a deliberate change from an earlier resume idea). If the student leaves before submitting, the attempt is LOCKED: not discarded, not resumable, not auto-scored. On return they see "your submission was interrupted, contact your instructor." Recovering a locked attempt (granting a retake) is a manual instructor action for now: delete the in_progress submission row (the exact SELECT-then-DELETE steps are saved in supabase/stage-7-submission.sql); the instructor-facing reset button is Stage 8.
  - No-resume is enforced server-side without fragile machinery (no heartbeats, timers, or background jobs): a resume WINDOW (30 minutes from the session's created_at, a single constant SUBMISSION_LIVE_WINDOW_MS in lib/submission.ts; reduced from 60 to 30 on 2026-07-07, see Open Issues) keeps an attempt live only while it is in_progress and inside the window; after the window the server treats it as locked. A short-lived browser marker is the front-line UX that distinguishes an accidental refresh (stays live) from leaving (locks). Because the window is server-enforced, tampering with the marker can at most let a student continue their OWN attempt within the window, never start a second one. The shared routes a submission passes through (chat, voice connect, voice transcript, video upload, scoring) all refuse a locked submission; all session writes stay server-side with the service-role key (decision A).
  - Reload behavior (owner decision): a TEXT reload resumes losslessly (the conversation reloads from the server and continues). A VOICE+VIDEO reload LOCKS, because a page reload physically ends the live call, the AI's short-term memory, and the in-progress webcam recording, so it must not silently continue with a gap; it lands in the same locked screen with the transcript-so-far preserved. A browser warning fires before a reload or tab close on any live graded attempt (the in-app Back button cannot carry that browser prompt, but using Back still lands safely in the locked state with no data lost).
  - Interim, deferred to later Stage 7 pieces (flagged to the owner): the camera-required-and-locked rule for graded voice+video is NOT here yet (graded voice uses today's camera toggle); graceful mid-call reconnect and handling a network drop that is not a page reload are deferred to the camera-lock piece; assignment-linked practice and the 3-practice cap are deferred together (the shared practice routes were left untouched), and practice will be tied to a specific assignment when built.
  - New code: lib/submission.ts (the resume window, the lock check, the marker key); app/api/assignment/start/route.ts (creates the one submission server-side); components/create-assignment-form.tsx, components/start-assignment-button.tsx, components/submission-live-gate.tsx (the front-line marker gate plus the reload warning and voice reload-lock), components/locked-submission.tsx (the interrupted screen); edits to the instructor page, the student home, the session screen, and the text and voice conversation components (the submit-and-score step); the shared routes chat, voice/token, voice/transcript, voice/video/upload-url, and session/end now refuse a locked submission and stamp submitted_at; supabase/stage-7-submission.sql (the unique index and the manual-reset procedure). No new dependencies; the only database change is the additive unique index, no Row Level Security rule weakened.
- Stage 7 (lifecycle), third chunk: the camera-required-and-locked rule for graded voice+video submissions. Built and deployed live; all four owner checks passed on https://relii.vercel.app.
  - For a graded submission (kind = submission, mode = voice_video) the camera is now required and locked on: the student cannot turn it off and cannot proceed without it.
