The rule keys entirely off the session's existing kind and mode (the voice screen only renders for voice_video, and it already knows the session is a submission), so there was NO schema change and NO change to the Stage 5 recording, upload, or scoring pipeline beyond enforcing camera-on.
  - On the pre-call screen, a graded submission shows a fixed note (mockup wording: "Camera stays on for graded assignments. It can be turned off in practice.") in place of the on/off checkbox, so there is no way to turn the camera off. The call always requests the webcam, and the existing Stage 5 recording then runs unchanged.
  - If camera (or microphone) access is denied, blocked, missing, or the camera is in use, a graded submission cannot proceed: it drops back to the start screen with a clear "your camera is required for a graded assignment" message and how to re-enable it (click the camera or lock icon in the address bar, choose Allow, reload). The student can fix the permission and start again.
  - Practice is unchanged: the camera defaults on, the on/off toggle still works, and turning it off records nothing. Graded text submissions are unaffected (text mode has no camera).
  - Frontend-only enforcement (in components/voice-conversation.tsx), which is the right layer because the webcam recording happens in the browser; it mirrors how the existing camera toggle already works. No server route or database change. The interim "leave a graded attempt and it locks, instructor reset recovers it" behavior is unchanged.
  - Scope note (owner-confirmed): voice resilience (graceful mid-call reconnect and recovery from a network drop that is not a page reload) has been intentionally REMOVED from Stage 7 for now and parked pending Tom's feedback. It was previously grouped with this camera-lock piece; it is no longer planned here. A dropped or reloaded voice attempt keeps its existing interim behavior (locks to "contact your instructor," recovered by a manual instructor reset).
  - New code: edits to components/voice-conversation.tsx only (an effective camera-on value forced true for a submission, the locked note in place of the toggle, and the camera-required handling when access is denied). No new dependencies, no schema change.
- Stage 7 (lifecycle), fourth chunk: assignment-linked practice plus the 3-most-recent retention cap. Built and deployed live; verified on https://relii.vercel.app (the FIFO video cleanup confirmed by an orphan-file check that stayed at zero after repeated practices).
  - Practice is now reached ONLY through an assignment (the old standalone free-practice path on scenarios is removed). Each assignment on the student home offers Practice (text) and Practice (voice + video); the student picks the mode each time, independent of the assignment's required graded mode, and practice keeps its camera toggle (the camera-lock rule applies to graded submissions only). A practice is a session with kind = practice and the assignment link set, using that assignment's scenario, and runs the existing conversation, content scoring, and results screen unchanged.
  - Practice attempts are UNLIMITED. The cap is a RETENTION cap, not a usage limit: only the 3 most recent FINISHED (scored) practices per student per assignment are kept and reviewable. The home shows "Saved practices: X of 3" per assignment with a list of the kept practices (mode, date, score, a Review link to the results screen, and a Delete control). The student-facing wording is "we keep your last 3," never "you have 3 left."
  - The cap is enforced server-side when a practice finishes and is scored (NOT at start): /api/session/end, after saving the score, keeps only the newest 3 scored practices for that student and assignment and deletes the older ones, each with its recording. Scoring-time enforcement is an owner-confirmed change from the literal "at start" wording: a video only ever exists once there is something to clean up, the count/list/cap stay one consistent number, and abandoning a half-finished practice never destroys an old one. The end result is identical (only the last 3 finished practices are ever kept).
  - The FIFO delete reuses the chunk-1 Storage cleanup pattern: a database delete does NOT remove Storage files, so the server removes each evicted practice's video by hand (using the stored video_path) BEFORE deleting the rows. Every query is scoped to (this student, this assignment, kind = practice, status = scored), so it can never touch another student's, another assignment's, or any graded submission's data. A null video_path (a text practice, a camera-off voice practice, or a Stage 5 failed upload) is treated as nothing to remove, so the row still deletes cleanly. The prune is best-effort: the score is saved first, so a cleanup hiccup is logged and never costs the student their result, and the next scored practice re-prunes.
  - A student can delete any of their own kept practices (its row and its recording) from the home, which frees a slot. /api/practice/delete confirms the caller owns the row and that it is a practice (it refuses graded submissions and other students' rows). Both deletes run server-side with the service-role key; the browser never holds it.
  - Legacy practice rows with no assignment link (pre-stage test data) are left alone: every practice query filters on the assignment link, so they are ignored in the per-assignment count and never pruned. Testing also confirmed that orphaned Storage files can come from deleting session rows directly in SQL, which bypasses the app and does not remove the video; going forward, deletions should use the app's Delete or the instructor delete-student action so Storage stays in sync.
  - Sequencing during the build (owner workflow): shipped in two pushes so the riskier automatic FIFO delete got its own focused test. Push 1 was assignment-linked practice, the saved-practices list, and the manual delete; Push 2 wired the retention cap into scoring. Both verified live by the owner, including an orphan-file Storage check.
  - New code: lib/practice.ts (the kept-count constant and the shared prune/delete helpers); app/api/practice/delete/route.ts (the manual delete); components/delete-practice-button.tsx. Edited: app/api/session/start and app/api/voice/start (now take an assignmentId and set the assignment link), app/api/session/end (prune after a scored practice), components/practice-button.tsx (assignment-linked, with the voice + video label), and app/student/page.tsx (per-assignment practice buttons and the saved-practices list; the standalone free-practice section removed). No new dependencies, no schema change.
- Stage 7 (lifecycle), fifth chunk: student download and print/save of a kept practice. Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app. Read-only: it adds save affordances only, with no deletion and no schema change.
  - On the results/review page (the page the saved-practices "Review" links open) a "Save a copy for your records" box gives the student two ways to keep a practice before the retention cap or a submission removes it: Download video, and Print / Save results.
  - Download video saves the recording as a .webm file. It REUSES the existing short-lived signed playback link the page already mints (Stage 5), adding only a Content-Disposition download name so the browser saves the file instead of playing it; the service-role key never reaches the browser. The filename is stamped in the browser at click time (relii-practice-<date>-<time>.webm, e.g. relii-practice-2026-06-29-1432.webm), so repeated saves do not collide. When there is no recording (a text practice, the camera was off, or a Stage 5 failed upload), the button is replaced by a brief note (for text: "There is no recording for a text practice").
  - Print / Save results opens the browser's own print dialog, with an on-screen note telling a brand-new student to choose "Save as PDF." Print-only styling (Tailwind print: variants) hides the app nav (the Back to home link), both buttons, and the video player, and stacks the itemized score and transcript into one column, so the saved PDF is a clean results sheet, not a screenshot of the app. This is the simple printable approach (the chosen option), NOT an app-generated PDF.
  - The controls are for the student's own data. Ownership is already enforced on the results page by Row Level Security (a student can only load their own session), so no rule was loosened. The same controls also appear on the student's own graded "View result" page (their own data, acceptable); nothing instructor-facing was added.
  - New code: components/results-actions.tsx (the client component holding both controls and the click-time filename). Edited: app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx (renders the controls, selects the session kind to label the filename, and adds the print: styling). No new dependencies, no schema change, no change to the recording, scoring, cap, or submission logic.
- Stage 7 (lifecycle), sixth and final chunk: auto-delete of a student's practices for an assignment when they submit. Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app. This completes Stage 7.
  - When a student finalizes and scores a graded submission, ALL of that student's practices for THAT assignment (the rows AND their recordings in the session-videos bucket) are permanently deleted, so old practice runs do not linger after the one official attempt. The submission itself and its recording are preserved; everything for other assignments and other students is untouched. Owner-verified live: after a submission, the assignment shows "Saved practices: 0 of 3", the submission still shows its score and plays its recording, the same student's practices for a different assignment are intact, another student's data is untouched, and the submitting student's Storage folder has no practice recordings left.
  - Order of operations: the submission is finalized, scored, and saved FIRST (with submitted_at), THEN the practices are cleared. The cleanup is best-effort: it runs in a try/catch that only logs, so a Storage or delete hiccup can never block or undo the submission or cost the student their graded result. Leftover practices can be re-cleared; the submission is what must always succeed.
  - Reuses the existing rows-plus-Storage cleanup pattern (a database delete does NOT remove Storage files): the server removes each practice's recording by hand using its stored video_path, then deletes the rows in one statement. A null video_path (a text practice, a camera-off voice practice, or a Stage 5 failed upload) is treated as nothing to remove, so the row still deletes cleanly. Every query is scoped to (this student, this assignment, kind = practice), so it can NEVER touch the submission (kind = submission), another assignment, or another student. Owner decision: delete ALL practices for the assignment, not just the scored ones, so the finished assignment is left completely clean.
  - Single hook point: both the text and the voice+video paths finalize and score through one server route, /api/session/end, so the cleanup was added there once and covers both modes. It runs with the service-role key, server-side only; the browser never holds it.
  - The submit confirmation step (text and voice) now warns the student in plain language that submitting will permanently clear their saved practices for this assignment, and tells them to save any they want to keep first (the Download video / Print / Save results controls from the fifth chunk), so practices disappearing is never a surprise.
  - New code: lib/practice.ts gained deletePracticesForAssignment (the same shape as prunePractices, but it keeps none). Edited: app/api/session/end/route.ts (after a submission is scored and saved, best-effort clear that student's practices for the assignment) and the submit confirmation wording in components/conversation.tsx and components/voice-conversation.tsx. No new dependencies, no schema change, no change to the scoring, recording, cap, or submission logic.

- Stage 8 (instructor dashboard), piece 1: the submissions dashboard, the submission detail view, and the three downloads. Built and deployed live. This is the first buildable piece of Stage 8, built while Stage 6 (full scoring) waits on Tom; it does not depend on scoring. No schema change, and no Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened.
  - The instructor page now opens with a Submissions dashboard: an assignment selector (ALL assignments, newest first, defaulting to the newest, per decision B so any instructor sees any submission), three summary tiles, and a table of that assignment's submissions. The existing invite form, create-assignment form and list, and student roster stay below, unchanged.
  - The table lists SUBMISSIONS only (sessions where kind = submission for the selected assignment), one row per student (the one-submission-per-assignment rule guarantees uniqueness). Columns: Student (email; profiles has no name field), Assignment, Mode (Text or V+V), Score (the real stored total out of max, 180 today, never a hardcoded 290), Status, and Actions (View plus compact download links on scored rows). Practice sessions never appear anywhere.
  - Status is a STATIC placeholder: every scored submission shows "To review" (warning color); a not-yet-scored (interrupted) submission shows "Interrupted". There is NO reviewed/awaiting database column and no toggle. A code comment marks it as a placeholder until professor finalization (after Stage 6).
  - Three tiles: Submissions = students-with-a-submission of enrolled (enrolled = the count of registered students, role = student, the same roster the page already reads); Average score = the average of the scored submissions' totals over the real max, shown as a dash when there are none; Awaiting review = the count of scored submissions (a PLACEHOLDER, commented as such, that finalization will make real). Empty states handled for no assignments and an assignment with no submissions.
  - The detail view lives at /instructor/submission/[id]. It is gated on the server (confirms the caller is an instructor from the database, reads the submission with the admin client, and refuses anything that is not kind = submission, so a practice can never be opened), then shows the student, assignment and scenario, mode, total score, the itemized rubric breakdown (the same rendering the student results screen uses), the full transcript, and, for a voice+video submission, the recording inline. Read-only: there are NO finalization controls (no confirm/adjust grade, no emotional-impact critique, no reviewed toggle); those are held until after Stage 6.
  - The instructor video link is minted with the admin client AFTER the instructor gate, exactly the way the student results page mints its link after the ownership check. So the Stage 5 deferred instructor Storage read policy was NOT added and no Storage rule was touched; the service-role key never reaches the browser.
  - Three downloads run through instructor-gated server routes under /api/instructor/submission/[id]: video (redirects to a short-lived signed download link carrying a filename), transcript (a readable .txt built server-side, speaker label then text per turn), and csv (a header block naming the student, assignment, scenario, and total out of max, then one row per rubric item: label, category, points earned, points max, comment). Filenames are relii-submission-<student>-<date> with the matching extension. A text submission (or a voice submission with a failed upload) shows a note in place of the video download. All three share one loader (lib/instructor-submission.ts) that does the gate-and-read once.
  - New code: components/assignment-selector.tsx (the dropdown, the only client piece); app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx (the detail view); lib/instructor-submission.ts (the shared instructor-gated loader plus the filename helper); and the three routes app/api/instructor/submission/[id]/{video,transcript,csv}/route.ts. Edited: app/instructor/page.tsx (the dashboard on top of the existing controls). No new dependencies, no schema change.
  - Built and pushed in three checkpoints (table plus tiles plus selector; the detail view; the downloads), each verifiable on the live site, then this PROJECT_STATE update.

- Stage 8 (instructor dashboard), piece 2: the instructor reset/allow-retake control and the reset audit log. Built and deployed live. This is the second buildable piece of Stage 8, built while Stage 6 (full scoring) waits on Tom; it does not depend on scoring. It retires the interim manual Supabase row-delete for the common case (recovering an interrupted submission). One additive schema change (the audit table); no Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened.
  - Two instructor actions on the submissions dashboard, deliberately different in look and stakes, both scoped to a single submission and both ending with the student able to start/submit that assignment again (the one-submission unique index stops blocking once the row is gone):
    - RESET AN INTERRUPTED ATTEMPT (common, low stakes): for a genuinely locked graded submission (kind = submission, status = in_progress, AND past the 30-minute live window, that is isSubmissionLocked = true), an amber Reset button behind a simple two-step confirm deletes the stuck attempt (its row, partial transcript, and any partial recording). This is exactly the manual procedure that used to live in supabase/stage-7-submission.sql, now a guarded button.
    - ALLOW A RETAKE OF A COMPLETED SUBMISSION (rare, high stakes): for a scored submission, a red Allow-retake button behind a visibly stronger confirm (a red panel, a plain warning that it permanently deletes the finished submission including its score and recording, a reminder to download the video/transcript/CSV first, and a deliberate extra step of typing the word RESET) permanently deletes the completed submission so the student can submit again.
  - Status label refined and recomputed SERVER-SIDE with isSubmissionLocked (never trusting the browser): a scored submission shows "To review" (the unchanged placeholder), an in_progress submission still inside the 30-minute window shows "In progress" (no reset, because the student may still be in it), and one past the window shows "Interrupted" (Reset available). The Reset control therefore appears only once the attempt is definitely locked.
  - The reset runs through one instructor-gated server route (app/api/instructor/submission/[id]/reset). It confirms the caller is an instructor (role read from the database), loads the one target session, and re-verifies it qualifies for the requested path (interrupted requires in_progress AND isSubmissionLocked; retake requires status = scored), refusing anything else, so it can never delete a practice row or an in_progress attempt that is still inside the window. It then writes the audit record FIRST (so the snapshot is captured while the row still exists), deletes the Storage recording if video_path is set (a database delete does not remove Storage files, so it is removed by hand, best-effort like the practice cleanup), then deletes the session row. Everything runs with the service-role key, server-side only, scoped to the single session id, so a reset can never touch another student's, another assignment's, another submission's, or any practice row.
  - New audit table submission_resets (supabase/stage-8-reset-audit.sql, run in production). Because the submission row is deleted, each audit record SNAPSHOTS the key facts as plain copies (no live links, so a later account or assignment delete can never remove or blank an entry): the acting instructor's id and email, the student's id and email, the assignment's id and title, the deleted session's id, its kind and prior status (in_progress vs scored), the reset type (interrupted vs retake), the prior score total and max for a graded retake only, and a timestamp. Row Level Security lets instructors READ it and, like sessions, has no write policy, so only the server writes it. Purely additive; no existing rule weakened.
  - Dashboard reset visibility: a quiet "Resets: N" line under the submissions table for the selected assignment that expands (native HTML details, no JavaScript) to a short, newest-first list, each entry showing the student email, the type (Interrupted recovered, or Graded retake with the score wiped), the prior score for a graded retake only (for example "was 165 / 180"), when it happened, and who did it (instructor email). Read instructor-only with the admin client, server-side, scoped to the assignment. A quiet "No resets for this assignment" when there are none. Deliberately simple: no filtering, search, pagination, or export.
  - The piece-1 View and the Video/Transcript/CSV download links are unchanged; the retake confirmation points the instructor at those same row links to download before deleting.
  - New code: supabase/stage-8-reset-audit.sql (the audit table), app/api/instructor/submission/[id]/reset/route.ts (the destructive server route), components/submission-reset-button.tsx (the two-path control), and components/reset-history.tsx (the per-assignment history). Edited: app/instructor/page.tsx (the server-side lock recompute for the status label, the control on the Actions column, and the reset-history read and render). No new dependencies; the only schema change is the additive audit table.
  - Built and pushed in two checkpoints (the route plus labels plus control; then the history), each verified live by the owner, then this PROJECT_STATE update.

- Stage 8 (instructor dashboard), piece 3: roster management (the combined roster view plus un-invite a pending invite). Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app (a throwaway invite showed as pending, was un-invited, and afterward could no longer register). This is the third buildable piece of Stage 8, built while Stage 6 (full scoring) waits on Tom; it does not depend on scoring. No schema change, and no Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened.
  - The instructor page's "Students" section is replaced by one "Roster" grouped by status: Registered students, Registered instructors, and Invited (pending). A person's status is DERIVED by matching emails case-insensitively (the same way the signup trigger does with lower(email)): the page reads all profiles and all allowed_emails server-side with the admin client, builds a set of registered (lowercased) emails, and treats any invite with no matching profile as pending. An invite that DOES match a profile is not shown as pending (the person appears in the registered lists instead). A registered profile whose email is somehow not on the invite list still shows as Registered (registered rows come from profiles), with no un-invite.
  - Per-row actions by type, exactly as before for existing cases: a registered STUDENT keeps the existing Delete (the full delete-student flow, unchanged); a registered INSTRUCTOR is display-only (no delete: the delete-student route refuses a non-student, and the viewing instructor sees their own account marked "(you)" with no destructive control); a PENDING invite (either role) gets the new Un-invite.
  - Un-invite removes ONE pending invite (a single allowed_emails row) so that address can no longer register. It is a lightweight, low-stakes action (a pending invite has no account, sessions, or videos, so there is no Storage and no cascade), with a short one-click confirm naming the email, deliberately NOT the heavy delete-student confirmation. It runs through a new instructor-gated server route (app/api/instructor/uninvite) that mirrors delete-student's authorization: it confirms the caller is an instructor (role read from the database, never the browser) and runs server-side with the service-role key. Before deleting it VERIFIES the email is on the invite list AND is truly pending (no matching profile, compared case-insensitively in code to avoid the underscore-wildcard risk of ilike); if a profile exists it refuses and points the instructor at the student Delete action, so un-invite can never touch a registered user. It then deletes the single allowed_emails row by exact lowercased email (matching how the list is stored and how delete-student removes its invite row).
  - The existing invite form (components/add-email-form.tsx) is unchanged and pairs naturally with the view: invite here, see the pending entry appear, un-invite if needed. Verified live end to end.
  - Deliberately OUT of this piece and parked pending Tom's input: bulk invite (many emails or CSV at once), changing a person's role after invite or registration (role stays what the invite set), and sending/resending invite/registration emails (today inviting only adds to the allow-list; the app sends no email).
  - New code: app/api/instructor/uninvite/route.ts (the guarded single-row delete) and components/uninvite-button.tsx (the short confirm). Edited: app/instructor/page.tsx (read all profiles and all allowed_emails, derive status, render the combined roster; the dashboard's students slice and enrolled count now come from the same all-profiles read). No new dependencies, no schema change.
  - Built and pushed in two checkpoints (the combined roster view, then the un-invite action), each verified live by the owner, then this PROJECT_STATE update.

- Stage 8 (instructor dashboard), piece 4: assignment management (edit low-risk fields, plus delete an unused or an in-use assignment). Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app (both the clean delete of an unused assignment and the destructive type-DELETE delete of a throwaway in-use assignment, with the recordings gone, the audit row written, and the real assignments untouched). This is the fourth buildable piece of Stage 8, built while Stage 6 (full scoring) waits on Tom; it does not depend on scoring. It fills a real gap: an instructor could create and see assignments but could not edit or delete them. One additive schema change (the audit table); no Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened.
  - EDIT (low-risk fields): each assignment card in the "Assignments" management list has an Edit button that opens an inline form for the title, instructions, and due date only. Saving writes the row straight from the browser under the existing "instructors manage assignments" rule (FOR ALL), exactly the way the Create-assignment and Invite forms already write, so there is NO server route and NO schema change for editing. The required mode and the scenario are deliberately NOT editable (existing sessions and scores were graded under them, so changing them would make that work inconsistent); they are shown read-only in the edit form with a short note explaining why. Cancel returns to the saved values.
  - DELETE decides used vs unused SERVER-SIDE. The instructor page shows a per-assignment "In use: N submissions, M practices" (or "Not in use (no student work)") line, computed with the admin client (instructors cannot read practice rows through ordinary browser rules). The one delete route re-counts the assignment's sessions itself before acting, so the path cannot be spoofed from the browser and the audit numbers are true.
    - UNUSED (0 sessions): a light one-click confirm naming the assignment; the route deletes just the empty assignment row. No audit record (nothing was destroyed).
    - IN-USE (1+ sessions): a visibly stronger confirm (a red panel, a plain warning naming exactly how many submissions and how many practices, and their recordings, will be permanently deleted, cannot be undone, and a reminder to download first) that requires typing the word DELETE. The route requires an explicit destructive acknowledgment (confirmInUse); if it finds the assignment in-use but the request did not carry it (for example a stale clean-delete from before a student submitted), it refuses, so student work is never destroyed by accident.
  - The destructive path runs SERVER-SIDE ONLY with the service-role key (sessions are server-written, decision A), in a new instructor-gated route (app/api/instructor/assignment/[id]/delete). Order of operations, forced by the sessions.assignment_id references assignments ON DELETE RESTRICT foreign key (the database refuses to delete an assignment while any session still points at it): write the audit snapshot FIRST (while the facts still exist), then remove each linked session's recording from Storage by its stored video_path (a database delete does NOT remove Storage files, the same rows-plus-Storage pattern as delete-student and the practice cleanup; best-effort, so a Storage hiccup is logged and never blocks the delete), then delete the session rows (submissions AND practices), then delete the assignment row. Every query is scoped to the single assignment_id, so it can NEVER touch another assignment's data, and it deletes only the students' sessions FOR this assignment, never the students themselves. Any instructor may delete any assignment (the manage rule is FOR ALL, not creator-restricted), verified server-side.
  - New audit table assignment_deletes (supabase/stage-8-assignment-delete-audit.sql, run in production), the same shape and style as the piece-2 reset log. Because the assignment and its sessions are deleted, each record SNAPSHOTS the facts as plain copies (no live links): the acting instructor's id and email, the deleted assignment's id and title, its scenario id and title and required mode, how many submissions and how many practices were destroyed, and a timestamp. Row Level Security lets instructors READ it and, like sessions, has no write policy, so only the server writes it. Owner decision (recorded): only IN-USE deletions are logged (something was actually destroyed); a clean delete of an unused assignment writes no record. Purely additive; no existing rule weakened. No deletion-history UI was built in this piece (the audit rows are readable in Supabase; a small history view like the reset history can be added later if wanted).
  - New code: supabase/stage-8-assignment-delete-audit.sql (the audit table), app/api/instructor/assignment/[id]/delete/route.ts (the destructive server route), components/edit-assignment-form.tsx (the inline edit-or-display card), and components/delete-assignment-button.tsx (the light vs strong type-DELETE confirm). Edited: app/instructor/page.tsx (the per-assignment session-count read, the Edit card in place of the old read-only card, and the in-use indicator plus Delete control on each assignment). No new dependencies; the only schema change is the additive audit table.
  - Built and pushed in two checkpoints (edit the low-risk fields; then the clean and destructive deletes plus the audit table), each verified live by the owner, then this PROJECT_STATE update.

- SPRINT PIECE 1: Jordan, the second persona (the Presentation-Close meeting). Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app (all three tests passed: Alex still scores out of 180, Jordan scores out of 220 in text and in voice+video, and Jordan stayed in character as a plant manager, never referring to himself as Alex and never behaving like the procurement director). This completes the Bunzl case: an instructor can now run an Alex discovery meeting and a Jordan presentation meeting as two INDEPENDENT assignments. There is deliberately NO prerequisite or unlock system between them; the "you earned this meeting with Jordan" framing is narrative only. One additive schema change (the meeting_type column); no Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened.
  - THE CORE PROBLEM THIS SOLVED: lib/scoring.ts hardcoded meetings.includes("discovery"), because Alex was the only scenario. Every session, whoever the persona was, would have been graded against Alex's discovery items. The rubric itself already supported both meetings (each item in docs/rubric-great-northwoods.json carries a per-item "meetings" tag), so NO new rubric was needed; the app simply had no way to say which meeting a scenario was.
  - New column scenarios.meeting_type (supabase/piece-1-meeting-type.sql, run in production): text, not null, with a check constraint allowing only 'discovery' or 'presentation'. Added empty, backfilled to 'discovery', then locked, which is the order that is safe on a live database. Deliberately NO default value, matching every other choice column here (assignments.required_mode, sessions.kind, sessions.mode): a default of 'discovery' would silently mislabel a future presentation scenario, which is the exact bug the column exists to prevent. Before running the backfill it was verified against the live database that Alex was the ONLY scenarios row, so 'where meeting_type is null' could not mis-tag anything.
  - Scoring now reads the field. contentDiscoveryItems became contentItemsForMeeting(items, meetingType), and scoreTranscriptContent takes the meeting and the persona name. Alex gets the 18 discovery content items (180 points), Jordan the 22 presentation content items (220 points), neither hardcoded. The AI still scores only the content-only ("both") subset, exactly as before; the full rubric is larger (23 discovery, 27 presentation) because it includes the voice-only delivery items the professor scores from the video at finalization.
  - The meeting is a REQUIRED input with NO fallback, in both the route and the scorer. A missing or unrecognized meeting_type stops scoring with an error instead of quietly defaulting to discovery, and a meeting with zero matching rubric items throws rather than saving a meaningless 0 out of 0. The reasoning (recorded because it will look over-careful later): a wrong-rubric score still looks completely legitimate on screen, so a visible failure is much cheaper than a silent one. The database check constraint makes it near-impossible anyway; this is a second lock on the same door.
  - Only ONE call site needed changing: both the text and the voice+video paths finish through /api/session/end, which already loaded the scenario, so adding meeting_type and persona_name to that one query covered both modes.
  - ALEX'S SCORING IS UNCHANGED BYTE-FOR-BYTE, on purpose. The grader is given the persona's name, and the risk was that inserting it would change the text sent to the grader for every Alex session too: at temperature 0 with a fixed seed, changed input can shift individual item scores while the total still comes out of 180, so a "180 versus 220" check would pass while Alex's scores quietly drifted. Avoided by substituting the name plainly in the system message (whose wording already read "the persona named Alex") and upper-casing it ONLY in the transcript label, so "ALEX (customer)" stays exactly that and Jordan becomes "JORDAN (customer)". Verified by diffing the generated strings against the previous revision.
  - Fixed a real bug found while reading: lib/persona.ts ended every persona's instructions with "you remain Alex at all times", which is injected into the system message on all four persona routes (chat, voice/token, session/start, assignment/start). It would have literally told the Jordan persona to be Alex. Now worded without a name ("you remain in character as the persona described above"), which fixes text and voice at once and is correct for any later persona. Two error-path strings naming Alex were genericized at the same time.
  - Jordan seeded as a NEW scenarios row from docs/persona-jordan-plant-manager.md (the ACCEPTED July 16, 2026 version, not the June 24 draft), by a new generator supabase/build-piece-1-jordan-seed.mjs writing supabase/piece-1-jordan-seed.sql, mirroring the Stage 3 Alex generator so the text is copied verbatim rather than retyped. Title "Presentation-Close: Jordan (plant manager)", persona Jordan, meeting_type presentation, linked to the SAME Great Northwoods rubric as Alex (looked up by name at run time, no new rubric and no pasted id). The generated SQL was checked before it was run: both sections byte-identical to the source doc, no markdown leaked in, and Jordan's dollar figures ($10,000, $700, $42,000) survived the Postgres dollar-quoting intact.
  - Alex's company descriptor was VERIFIED, not edited. A read-only query against the live database confirmed both scenario_context and persona_system_prompt already read "a mid-sized regional meat and food processing company", matching Jordan, and that Alex's scenario contains no dollar amount. So no UPDATE was written. This mattered because the seed's where-not-exists guard means re-running the seed would have silently changed nothing; any real fix would have had to be an explicit UPDATE scoped to the Alex row.
  - supabase/build-stage-3-seed.mjs now also sets meeting_type = 'discovery', so a from-scratch rebuild does not trip the new not-null rule. This is a no-op against the live database (the guard skips the existing Alex row) and did NOT touch the live Alex scenario.
  - Known limitation, accepted, no schema change made: Jordan speaks with the SAME voice as Alex ("cedar" in lib/realtime.ts), because there is no per-scenario voice setting. Fine for the demo; a natural fit for the admin scaffolding piece.
  - New code: supabase/piece-1-meeting-type.sql (the column), supabase/build-piece-1-jordan-seed.mjs and supabase/piece-1-jordan-seed.sql (the Jordan seed), docs/persona-jordan-plant-manager.md (the accepted persona, now in the repo as the seed's source). Edited: lib/scoring.ts (meeting-aware item selection, persona-aware grader), lib/types.ts (the MeetingType type and its guard), lib/persona.ts (the un-naming fix), app/api/session/end/route.ts (loads and validates the meeting and persona), supabase/build-stage-3-seed.mjs, plus two error strings. No new dependencies; the only schema change is the additive meeting_type column.

- SPRINT PIECE 2a: conversation-only foundations (the first of Piece 2's four sub-sessions). Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app (all six tests passed: the disabled scenario stayed out of the assignment picker, the practice-only assignment showed no graded control, text and voice+video both ended with no score on a results screen that did not redirect, four practices pruned correctly to "3 of 3" with the recording removed, and Alex and Jordan still scored 180 and 220). This piece deliberately builds FOUNDATIONS ONLY: there is NO AI generation in it, and it was tested entirely by hand-seeded scenarios. Two additive schema changes; no Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened.
  - WHAT THIS UNLOCKS: until now every scenario was a graded case pointing at a rubric, and the whole app assumed "a finished session is a scored session." Piece 2 will let an instructor have the AI build personas for other industries, and those cases have no rubric written for them, so there is nothing to score. This piece makes a rubric-less (conversation-only) case a first-class thing the app can run end to end.
  - Schema (supabase/piece-2a-conversation-only.sql, run in production). Two changes, both additive: scenarios.rubric_id became NULLABLE (a null rubric is what represents conversation-only), and a new scenarios.enabled column (boolean, not null, default true) backfilled every existing row to true. Alex and Jordan keep their rubric link and their scoring untouched.
  - The enabled column deliberately DOES take a default, unlike piece 1's meeting_type which deliberately did not. The reasoning is recorded because the two look inconsistent: for meeting_type a default would have silently mislabelled a future scenario, which was the exact bug that column existed to prevent, whereas "a scenario is on unless somebody turns it off" is genuinely the intended meaning here. Chunk 2c will write generated candidates with enabled = false explicitly.
  - Terminal status for a conversation-only session is 'completed', which needed NO schema change because it was already a legal value in the sessions status check. The documented lifecycle (in_progress, completed, scored) is unchanged; a conversation-only session simply stops one step early at completed and is never scored.
  - THE ONE RULE THE WHOLE PIECE HANGS ON, recorded because it will look over-careful later: every branch keys on THE SCENARIO HAVING NO RUBRIC, never on the status being 'completed'. Those are not the same thing. A graded session also passes through 'completed' for the moment between the conversation ending and the score saving, and it STAYS there if scoring fails, which is the case the app already handles by sending the student back to end the session again. Keying any branch off the bare status would have turned a transient OpenAI hiccup during an Alex session into a permanent, entirely legitimate-looking "no score" result. A conversation_only column on sessions was considered and rejected for the same reason the piece stayed additive: the null rubric is the single source of truth, and every branch point already loads the scenario.
  - No-score end path: /api/session/end loads the scenario first and, if its rubric is null, marks the session completed and stops. No score JSON, no total, no scored_at, and the scorer and the meeting_type validation are never reached. The transcript was already saved turn by turn during the conversation, and the voice+video recording still uploads through the unchanged Stage 5 pipeline. Scenarios that DO have a rubric run the identical path they always did. lib/scoring.ts itself needed NO change: the branch sits one level above it.
  - Results screen: it previously redirected unless the session was scored. It now loads the scenario BEFORE that gate and branches, so a finished conversation-only session renders the page without a rubric breakdown (the transcript full width, the recording if any, and the existing Download video and Print / Save results controls) with a clear "conversation-only practice, no score" note, and does not redirect. A conversation-only session still in progress goes back to the live conversation, and every graded case redirects exactly as before.
  - Practice retention cap (FIFO): prunePractices now takes the finished status for that assignment, 'scored' for a graded case and 'completed' for a conversation-only one. It is deliberately ONE value rather than "scored or completed", because every practice under one assignment shares that assignment's scenario, so an assignment is entirely one kind or the other and the caller always knows which. Matching only the right status is also what keeps a graded practice stuck at 'completed' by a scoring failure out of the prune: that is a broken session the student can retry, not a finished practice, and it must never evict a good score. Scoping is otherwise identical (this student, this assignment, kind = practice), the recording-then-row delete order is unchanged, and graded submissions and scored practices are untouched. The student home applies the same per-assignment rule when listing kept practices, and shows "no score" instead of a number for conversation-only entries.
  - Ungraded assignability: an assignment pointing at a rubric-less scenario is practice-only. It offers Practice (text) and Practice (voice + video) and no graded submission path, reads "practice only, not graded" instead of naming a required mode, and explains that there is nothing to submit. Hiding the button is only the UX half: /api/assignment/start independently refuses to create a submission for a rubric-less scenario, which is the actual enforcement and matches how every other session rule in this app is applied server-side. This works under the current pre-Piece-4 model.
  - The create-assignment scenario picker now lists ENABLED scenarios only. In 2a the enabled flag affects the PICKER ONLY; blocking a disabled scenario from being started belongs with 2c and 2d, when disabled candidates actually exist. Nothing else reads the flag yet.
  - Wording was corrected everywhere a conversation-only run would otherwise have promised a score it will not produce. On both conversation screens: the end-of-session confirmation ("End this session? ... This practice is not scored" in place of "End this session and see your score?"), the matching button label, the dropped-connection message, and the error fallbacks. On the results screen, found in a follow-up sweep after the six live tests passed: the failed-upload notice (which said "your score below was saved" when only a transcript exists, and appears only when a voice upload fails, which is why happy-path testing did not surface it) and the Print / Save hint (which offered to save "your score and transcript"). This is messaging correctness, not polish: the old wording would have been a plain lie on these cases.
  - Known and accepted: an assignment's Required mode is still asked for and stored even for a practice-only assignment, where it is ignored (the student picks the mode each time they practice). Piece 4 repurposes required_mode, so it was left alone here rather than special-cased twice. Also, a practice-only assignment appears in the instructor submissions dashboard with zero submissions forever, since nobody can ever submit it. Harmless, and filtering it out was deliberately left out of scope.
  - TEST DATA STILL IN THE DATABASE, DELETE BEFORE THE DAWN DEMO. supabase/piece-2a-test-scenario.sql seeded two throwaway scenarios, both titled with a "TEST - " prefix so they are obvious at a glance: "TEST - Conversation only: Riley (facilities director)" (enabled, no rubric, a commercial HVAC case used for the actual role-play testing) and "TEST - Disabled scenario (should never appear in the picker)" (disabled, used only to prove the picker hides it). There is also a throwaway ASSIGNMENT pointing at the Riley scenario, plus whatever practice sessions and recordings the testing produced. To remove all of it cleanly, in this order:
    - STEP 1, IN THE APP FIRST: on the instructor page, find the test assignment for the Riley scenario and use its Delete button (the strong confirm that asks you to type DELETE). This removes the assignment, its practice sessions, AND their recordings in the right order. Doing this in SQL instead would leave orphaned video files in Storage, which is exactly how the existing orphaned-recordings issue below was created.
    - STEP 2, THEN IN THE SQL EDITOR: delete from public.scenarios where title = 'TEST - Conversation only: Riley (facilities director)'; and delete from public.scenarios where title = 'TEST - Disabled scenario (should never appear in the picker)';
    - STEP 3, CONFIRM NOTHING IS LEFT: select title from public.scenarios where title like 'TEST - %'; should return no rows.
    - If step 2 errors with "violates foreign key constraint", that is the database's safety net telling you some session or assignment still points at the scenario, so go back and finish step 1. These same steps are saved at the bottom of supabase/piece-2a-test-scenario.sql.
  - New code: supabase/piece-2a-conversation-only.sql (the two schema changes) and supabase/piece-2a-test-scenario.sql (the two throwaway rows plus the removal steps). Edited: app/api/session/end/route.ts (the no-score branch), lib/practice.ts (the finished-status argument on prunePractices), app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx (the no-rubric branch), app/student/page.tsx (the per-assignment finished rule, the practice-only card, the "no score" label), app/api/assignment/start/route.ts (the server-side refusal), app/instructor/page.tsx (enabled scenarios only in the picker), app/student/session/[id]/page.tsx (passes the flag down), and components/conversation.tsx and components/voice-conversation.tsx (the wording). docs/Relii-Piece2-Design-Decisions.md was committed as part of this piece; it is the shared design reference for 2a through 2d. No new dependencies; the only schema changes are the two approved additive ones.

- SPRINT PIECE 2b: generation to structured fields (the second of Piece 2's four sub-sessions). Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app. NO schema change and NOTHING is written to the database in this piece: 2b is generate-and-judge-the-quality, and persisting candidates as disabled scenarios is 2c. No Row Level Security or Storage rule was touched.
  - WHAT THIS IS: an instructor opens a scaffolding form (linked from the top of the instructor dashboard, living on its own page at /instructor/scaffold), fills in the case, clicks Generate, and about half a minute later reads TWO candidate buyer personas. Each candidate is shown twice over: as the structured fields, in the same order as the field set in docs/Relii-Piece2-Design-Decisions.md, and then as the scenario_context and persona_system_prompt that the fixed template assembled from exactly those fields. Form inputs: industry, persona role, product or service, prospect company size as an approximate employee count, meeting type, chain position (gatekeeper or decision maker) with who they gate access to, win condition with trial terms, and seller company type.
  - docs/Relii-Piece2b-Persona-Generation-Prompt.md is now COMMITTED and is the source of truth for the prompt (Part A), the JSON output contract (Part B), the fixed assembly template (Part C), and the two-call approach (Part D). All three parts are implemented faithfully rather than paraphrased. If that doc's wording changes, the code must be changed to match.
  - Two layers of validation, and the second one is load-bearing. Part B's shape is enforced mechanically by OpenAI's strict json_schema response format (the same mechanism the scorer already uses), which guarantees the field names and types. It CANNOT enforce "at least 3 hidden_facts", "at least 3 objections", or "not empty", so validateCandidate in lib/persona-scaffold.ts checks those by hand, exactly as Part B lists them, plus both enums. One extra rule beyond the doc: gates_access_to must be filled in when the position is gatekeeper (it may legitimately be null for a decision maker, who gates nobody).
  - Re-request on invalid, bounded to ONE retry per candidate (two attempts each, tried independently). If a candidate still fails, the WHOLE request fails and nothing is shown, so a half-generated case can never reach the screen. The retry is bounded on purpose: every extra attempt adds to the request time, which is the risk this piece was told to watch.
  - THE TWO CANDIDATES RUN IN PARALLEL, not one after the other. The one-persona prompt is called twice with the same inputs, the second call carrying a short STATIC differ nudge (take a different primary pressure, a different angle, different hidden facts and objections). An earlier design fed candidate one into call two so it could differ from something concrete; that was rejected because it serializes two slow calls and so makes the Vercel timeout risk worse, and because Part D asks only for "a short nudge on the second call". Running them at the same time means the whole request takes about as long as ONE call. If the two ever come back too similar, showing candidate one to call two is the fallback.
  - GENERATION USES TEMPERATURE 0.9 AND NO FIXED SEED, which is the deliberate OPPOSITE of lib/scoring.ts. This is recorded because the two will look inconsistent to anyone reading both files: the scorer pins temperature 0 and a fixed seed precisely so re-scoring the same transcript repeats, whereas here variety IS the requirement. Two calls with identical inputs at temperature 0 come back nearly identical no matter what the differ nudge says, which would have quietly defeated the whole point of generating two candidates to compare. The nudge only has room to diverge if the sampling is not pinned.
  - The fixed assembly (Part C) lives in lib/persona-assembly.ts as a PURE function: a candidate's JSON plus the form inputs go in, scenario_context and persona_system_prompt come out. No database, no model call. Built this way so 2c can import it unchanged and only has to persist what it returns. It needs the form inputs as well as the JSON because the template uses the seller company type, which is not part of the model's output.
  - THE STUDENT BRIEFING STRUCTURALLY CANNOT CONTAIN THE HIDDEN NUMBERS. scenario_context is assembled from exactly six things (seller company type, persona name, role, company description, who they gate, and the win condition) and never reads hidden_facts at all. That is a code-level guarantee for every future case, not a spot check that happened to pass. Numbers from the WIN CONDITION are expected to appear there and are correct: that is the goal the student is told to earn.
  - The model call is server-side only, through the existing chatComplete helper and the existing OPENAI_API_KEY and OPENAI_MODEL. No new dependency and no new secret was needed. There is deliberately no web search and no external tool (design decision 8): model knowledge only. Verified after the live test that no file delivered to the browser contains anything matching an API key, that OPENAI_API_KEY is read in only two server files (lib/openai.ts and lib/realtime.ts), and that no client component imports either. Next.js only ships an environment variable to the browser if its name starts with NEXT_PUBLIC_, so the key cannot leak by construction.
  - The route gates ITSELF, rather than relying on the page. The scaffolding page redirects a non-instructor, but a route is reachable without ever loading that page, so app/api/instructor/scaffold/generate re-reads the caller's role from the database with the admin client and refuses otherwise, mirroring uninvite and the submission routes. Any instructor may scaffold; there is no new admin role (design decision 6).
  - THE VERCEL TIMEOUT DID NOT BITE. The route sets an explicit maxDuration of 60 seconds rather than relying on the platform default (which has moved across Vercel versions, and the plan here is not recorded); 60 is allowed on either plan. Real generations completed comfortably inside it across several live runs. So generation stays SYNCHRONOUS and the background-and-poll design (decision 7) was correctly NOT pre-built. If 2c's larger request ever runs out of time, that remains the escalation point.
  - Owner-verified live across several generations: two gatekeeper candidates and two decision-maker candidates, each pair meaningfully different in their decision criteria, all reading like Alex and Jordan, with clear hidden facts, objections, and what earns the win. Then the not-risk-free trial case (a 30-day conveyor trial requiring a working line down for two days): NEITHER candidate claimed a risk-free trial, which was the failure this test existed to catch.
  - OBSERVATION FOR LATER, not a bug and no change made: the two candidates in that trial test put the risk-reduction framing in DIFFERENT PLACES. One stated it inside win_condition, the other left win_condition terse and carried it in earns_win ("show how downtime during installation is minimized and justified by the benefits"). Both behave correctly, because earns_win is what actually gates whether the persona grants the close. Worth knowing which is preferable: win_condition is also shown to the STUDENT in the briefing, so putting the risk argument there hands the student part of the argument they are supposed to build, which makes the terse version arguably the better-built case. Tightening this would mean editing Part A or Part B of the prompt doc, which is the owner's call; the natural time to revisit it is 2c, when candidates sit side by side and the variation is easy to see.
  - New code: lib/persona-scaffold.ts (Parts A and B: the prompt, the strict schema, the validation, the bounded retry, and the parallel two-call generation), lib/persona-assembly.ts (Part C: the fixed template as a pure function), app/api/instructor/scaffold/generate/route.ts (the instructor-gated server route), app/instructor/scaffold/page.tsx (the gated page), components/scaffold-form.tsx (the form and the candidate review). Edited: app/instructor/page.tsx (a "Persona scaffolding" link in the header only). docs/Relii-Piece2b-Persona-Generation-Prompt.md committed. No new dependencies, no schema change, no database write.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece: writing candidates to the database as disabled scenarios with the grouping columns, the polished side-by-side compare view, the pick/enable/delete action, and the second persona in the chain (all 2c and 2d). Also unchanged: no rubric or scoring (scaffolded cases are conversation-only), no .md file or Storage bucket (dropped for v1), and no per-scenario voice.

- SPRINT PIECE 2c: candidates written live, plus the side-by-side compare view (the third of Piece 2's four sub-sessions). Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app (every test passed: two disabled conversation-only rows sharing a group with the right chain_role and meeting_type, the compare view surviving a reload, a turned-off scenario refused in both practice modes and recovering when switched back on, no scaffold candidates in the create-assignment picker, and Alex and Jordan still scoring 180 and 220). One additive schema change (three nullable columns); no Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened.
  - WHAT THIS UNLOCKS: 2b could generate two candidates but saved nothing, so closing the tab lost them. Now a generation produces two REAL scenario rows, turned off and grouped as a pair, and the comparison is a page that reads from the database instead of a screen full of throwaway state.
  - Schema (supabase/piece-2c-candidate-grouping.sql, run in production). Three columns on scenarios, all NULLABLE and additive, so Alex, Jordan, and every hand-written scenario simply leave them empty: generation_group_id (uuid, ties a pair together), chain_role (text, with a check constraint allowing only 'gatekeeper' or 'decision_maker' or empty), and generation_fields (jsonb, the structured generation JSON). Plus an index on generation_group_id. generation_group_id is deliberately a plain column and NOT a link to a new table: there is nothing else to store about a generation, and a "generations" table would be a second row to keep in step for no benefit, since a group is only ever "the rows carrying this id".
  - The generate route now writes both candidates in ONE insert (so a pair can never be half-written), each with enabled = false, rubric_id = null (conversation-only, never scored), the shared generation_group_id made with crypto.randomUUID(), chain_role, the assembled scenario_context and persona_system_prompt from lib/persona-assembly.ts (imported UNCHANGED, exactly as 2b was built to allow), the persona name, a title like "Scaffold candidate 1 (gatekeeper): Riley, facilities director", and created_by. Nothing becomes assignable, because the rows are off.
  - generation_fields holds { version, candidate_index, candidate, inputs }. Two parts of that are load-bearing and will look like clutter later. candidate_index exists because both rows are written by a SINGLE insert, so Postgres stamps them with an IDENTICAL created_at: ordering by date would put "Candidate 1" on the left one reload and on the right the next. inputs (the admin's own form answers) is stored so 2d can generate the paired second persona from the same case without making the instructor retype it. Because this is a JSON column, anything else 2d needs can be added with no migration.
  - OWNER CORRECTION DURING THE BUILD, and the reason it matters: the design doc's 2c outline said "chain role = gatekeeper", and the first plan followed it. That was overruled. 2c supports BOTH chain roles, because v1 requires that an instructor can generate and later assign a STANDALONE decision-maker persona (like Jordan), not only a decision maker produced by the 2d chain. The 2d chain is an added path, not the only one. docs/Relii-Piece2-Design-Decisions.md line for 2c was corrected to match, so the governing spec no longer contradicts the build.
  - SECOND OWNER CORRECTION: the form's "Meeting type" dropdown was REMOVED. It had become redundant with chain position, and two overlapping questions meant an admin could pick a combination that disagreed with itself (a gatekeeper in a presentation meeting). Chain position is now the single source, and meeting_type is derived from it by one shared helper (meetingTypeForChainPosition in lib/persona-scaffold.ts) that both the generation prompt and the saved column call. meetingType was deleted from the ScaffoldInputs type entirely, so there is no longer a field that COULD disagree. The dropdown now names the consequence ("Gatekeeper (discovery meeting)", "Decision maker (presentation and close)") with a line underneath comparing it to Alex and Jordan.
  - The compare view lives at /instructor/scaffold/compare/[groupId], a server page gated on the instructor role, reading the pair with the admin client. It shows the two candidates in ALIGNED ROWS, one row per field, in the field order from the design doc (design decision 3: compare by reading structured fields, not by role-playing). Because the group id is in the web address and the candidates are rows, a reload re-reads it rather than losing it. The assembled scenario text sits below per candidate, collapsed. There is deliberately NO pick, enable, or delete control, and the page says so, so nobody hunts for a button that is 2d.
  - Rows in the table carry a small note saying who can see that field: the win condition is marked SHOWN TO THE STUDENT, the hidden facts and what earns the win are marked hidden. A separate "Where the argument landed" panel repeats the win condition next to what earns the win for both candidates, because in the fixed field order those two sit nine rows apart and which one carries the argument is exactly the thing worth checking.
  - The scaffold page gained a "Recent generations" list (persona names, industry, chain role, date, and a Compare link), so a comparison is never lost just because its web address was not kept.
  - Disabled scenarios are now UNREACHABLE by a student, not merely hidden. All three start routes (session/start, voice/start, assignment/start) refuse a scenario whose enabled flag is false, server-side, the way every other session rule in this app is enforced. session/start and assignment/start added the flag to a scenario query they already ran; voice/start had no reason to read the scenario at all (the voice persona is greeted live on the call) and gained a small read purely for this check. Two things deliberately left alone: a session already IN PROGRESS when a scenario is turned off keeps running (killing a live conversation mid-sentence would be worse), and the scenarios Row Level Security rule was not touched. Note the pre-existing limitation that rule carries: any logged-in user can read any scenario row including persona_system_prompt, which holds the hidden facts. That is equally true of Alex and Jordan today, so it is a known limitation and not something 2c introduced.
  - THE VERCEL TIMEOUT DID NOT BITE, again. This chunk was named as where the risk lands (design decision 7), but the only thing 2c adds over 2b is two database writes against model calls that were already comfortable. Owner-measured across five live generations: 10 to 20 seconds each, against the route's explicit 60-second maxDuration. Generation STAYS SYNCHRONOUS and the background-and-poll path was correctly NOT built. That escalation point now moves to 2d, whose chain generation is the next-heaviest path.
  - PROMPT TIGHTENING, prompted by two quality problems the compare view made visible on its first real use (the exact purpose of the screen). Both were fixed identically in docs/Relii-Piece2b-Persona-Generation-Prompt.md and lib/persona-scaffold.ts, since the doc is the source of truth and the code quotes it verbatim; the three edited strings were byte-compared to confirm they match.
    - THE WIN CONDITION WAS LEAKING A HIDDEN NUMBER TO THE STUDENT. This was a structural contradiction, not bad luck: Part A forbade volunteering numbers, Part B asked the win condition for "the concrete terms (what, how much, how long)", and Part C pipes the win condition verbatim into the student briefing. So the one field that is both model-written and student-visible was being explicitly invited to state a performance bar. Both candidates of a test pair put "at least a 15% improvement in dispatch efficiency" in front of the student. Part B now says TERMS OF THE CLOSE ONLY (what, scope, how long), with performance thresholds, target percentages, and incumbent metrics pushed into hidden_facts or earns_win, and Part A carries the one-line version. The distinction that matters: "a 60-day trial of the dispatch software" is a term of the close and the student SHOULD see it; "must show a 15% improvement" is the buyer's private bar and must not be handed over.
    - THE TWO CANDIDATES' DECISION CRITERIA CAME OUT IDENTICAL AND GENERIC (both listed efficiency, ease of integration, user-friendliness, support and training, in that order). Two stacked causes: the differ nudge never mentioned decision_criteria, so variation was never asked for, and nothing required the criteria to be specific to this buyer, so both calls independently reached for the same category defaults. Fixing only the nudge would have left candidate ONE generic, since the nudge applies to call two only, which is why both edits were needed. The nudge now varies criteria ranking and substance, and Part A requires criteria rooted in this buyer's own pressures, role, and situation, explicitly ruling out the generic category list.
    - STAGE TWO IS HELD, NOT DONE. The recorded fallback for too-similar candidates (feed candidate one into call two so it has something concrete to differ from) was deliberately not built: it serializes the two calls and would roughly double generation time, from 10 to 20 seconds up to 20 to 40. The owner's instruction was to hold it unless stage one proved insufficient. It did not.
    - VERIFIED AS A DISTRIBUTION, which is the only honest way to test this. Temperature 0.9 with no seed means the same inputs never reproduce the same output, so "re-run the exact case" is impossible; only the inputs repeat. The owner re-entered the same HVAC dispatch inputs three times (one of them as a decision maker, which also covered the standalone decision-maker path): 0 of 6 candidates put a percentage or threshold in the win condition, against 2 of 2 before the fix, and all 3 pairs came back with criteria that differed and read buyer-specific. The limit of that sample is recorded on purpose: six clean candidates rule out a high residual rate but cannot prove the model will never do it again. The compare view is the permanent human backstop, and a mechanical "percentage in win_condition" check is the available escalation if one ever slips through.
  - New code: supabase/piece-2c-candidate-grouping.sql (the three columns), lib/scaffold-candidates.ts (reading a group back, the recent-generations list, and the label helpers; server-only, admin client), app/instructor/scaffold/compare/[groupId]/page.tsx (the aligned compare view). Edited: app/api/instructor/scaffold/generate/route.ts (derives the meeting type, writes the pair, returns the group id), lib/persona-scaffold.ts (the meeting-type helper, meetingType removed from ScaffoldInputs, and the prompt tightening), components/scaffold-form.tsx (the meeting-type dropdown removed, the redirect to the compare view, and a fallback link), app/instructor/scaffold/page.tsx (the recent-generations list), the three start routes (the enabled check), docs/Relii-Piece2b-Persona-Generation-Prompt.md (Parts A, B, and D), and docs/Relii-Piece2-Design-Decisions.md (the corrected 2c chain-role line and the merged form field). No new dependencies; the only schema change is the three approved additive columns.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece, all 2d: the pick-a-winner action (enable the winner, delete the loser, delete-if-unused only), and generating the paired second persona in the chain from the winner. Also unchanged: no rubric or scoring, no .md file or Storage bucket, no per-scenario voice, and no visual polish pass.

- SPRINT PIECE 2d: pick a winner, the decision-maker chain, and cleanup (the fourth and last of Piece 2's sub-sessions). Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app across three separate rounds of testing, one per build. THIS COMPLETES PIECE 2 (admin scaffolding) END TO END. NO schema change of any kind: 2d adds no SQL file and uses only the columns 2a and 2c already added. No Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened.
  - WHAT THIS UNLOCKS: 2c could generate and compare a pair but nothing could be chosen, so no generated persona could ever be used, and every test generation piled up permanently. Now a comparison ends in a decision, that decision produces a real assignable case, the case can be extended into a two-meeting chain, and the leftovers can be cleared.
  - BUILT AND TESTED IN THREE PUSHES, in this order deliberately: pick/enable/delete first, cleanup second, and the chain LAST, because the chain is the heaviest generation path and carried the timeout risk. That order meant an overrunning chain would have left a working, committed state rather than a half-finished piece. It did not overrun.
  - PICK A WINNER. On the compare view each candidate has a "Choose this one" button behind a confirm that names all of what happens. It switches that candidate ON (enabled = true), renames it, and permanently deletes the other one. The route (app/api/instructor/scaffold/choose) is instructor-gated with the role read from the database and runs with the service-role key, server-side only.
  - THE ONE CHECK THE WHOLE PIECE HANGS ON, recorded because it is what makes every delete here safe: every 2d route refuses any scenario whose generation_group_id is empty. Alex, Jordan, and every hand-written scenario leave that column empty by construction (2c), so they are outside all of this whatever id is sent. There is no separate "is this Alex?" test to get wrong.
  - DELETE-IF-UNUSED, AND WHY IT IS TWO CONDITIONS, NOT ONE. A scenario is undeletable if any SESSION references it OR any ASSIGNMENT points at it: sessions.scenario_id and assignments.scenario_id are BOTH declared on delete restrict (supabase/stage-2-data-model.sql), so Postgres itself refuses either case. That makes counting first a matter of correctness and not merely caution, because one delete statement covering several rows is all-or-nothing: a single blocked row would fail the whole statement and delete nothing. Every delete in 2d counts first and then removes only the rows that came back clean, by explicit id list rather than by a sweep. A used case is kept and the admin is told; it can only be switched off.
  - Deleting a candidate is unusually clean compared with every other delete in this project: a candidate is disabled, conversation-only, and has never been role-played, so it has NO recording and there is no Storage step and nothing to orphan. The assignment, student, and practice deletes all had to remove videos first.
  - Order of operations on a pick: the winner is switched ON first, then the loser is deleted. If the delete failed, the admin still has the working winner they asked for, and a leftover disabled loser is harmless and clearable. The reverse order could destroy the loser and then fail to deliver the winner.
  - The winner is RETITLED on pick, from "Scaffold candidate 1 (gatekeeper): Riley, facilities director" to the convention Alex and Jordan already use ("Discovery-Close: Riley (facilities director)"). The candidate title is right for the compare view and wrong everywhere else, because once a winner is switched on that string is what an instructor picks from in the create-assignment list.
  - The compare view decides its state from the ENABLED FLAG, never from the row count: any switched-on row means decided (show the winner, the role-play reminder, and the chain offer), all rows off means undecided (show the comparison and the pick buttons). Row counting would have misread the one case that matters, a pick whose loser had to be kept, which leaves two rows behind a decision that was definitely made.
  - CLEANUP, three controls on the scaffolding page's Recent generations list, and only one is ever offered per generation so the admin is never given an action that would be refused: Turn off (for a live chosen persona), nothing but an explanation (for one that has been role-played, which can never be deleted), or Discard (for one that is off and unused).
    - Per-group Discard is ALL OR NOTHING for the pair and refuses outright if any candidate is live or used, naming which. Simple confirm.
    - Bulk "Discard all unused test generations" is behind the assignment-delete style strong confirmation: a red panel and a typed word. The word is DISCARD and deliberately NOT DELETE, so muscle memory from the assignment confirmation cannot carry anyone through it.
    - THE BULK SCOPE, in full: a row is deleted only if it is generated (generation_group_id not null), switched OFF, has no sessions, and has no assignment pointing at it. The switched-off condition is the one that is easy to miss and was added on review: a winner chosen minutes ago has no sessions and no assignment yet, so a plain "delete anything unused" rule would have destroyed the case the admin had just picked, which is exactly the state between the pick and the winner role-play gate. The rule adopted is simply ON MEANS LIVE, and cleanup never touches a live scenario. To delete a chosen winner you turn it off first, which is a deliberate two-step.
    - The count is fetched when the confirm panel OPENS, not when the page loaded, and it comes from the same server function that decides what to delete (planBulkDiscard), so the number approved is the number acted on rather than a stale page-load figure. The panel also spells out what will NOT be touched, with the current names and counts.
  - TURN OFF IS NEW, and it filled a real gap found while reading: nothing in the app wrote the enabled flag back, in either direction, so the refusal both deletes require ("it can only be switched off") had no control behind it. It is the enable half of the pick in reverse, scoped the same way, with a light confirm because it is safe and reversible.
  - THE CHAIN. From a chosen gatekeeper winner, the compare view offers "Generate the decision maker", which produces two candidates for the second meeting and lands on the same compare-and-pick flow. The offer appears only for a gatekeeper, since only a gatekeeper gates access to somebody. The shared case details CARRY OVER and are read from the gatekeeper's OWN ROW server-side, never taken from the browser, so what carries over is what was actually generated and chosen. The admin sets only the two things genuinely about the second meeting: the decision maker's role (pre-filled from who the gatekeeper gates access to, since that field names this exact person) and the win condition.
  - The load-bearing part of the carryover is the COMPANY DESCRIPTOR THE MODEL INVENTED for the gatekeeper, not the admin's employee-count input. Passing that through is what puts both personas at one company rather than at two similar-sounding ones. It was deliberately NOT pinned mechanically onto the generated candidates: the prompt asks for it and the compare view is where drift would be visible.
  - OWNER DECISION DURING THE BUILD, and the reason it matters. Reading docs/persona-jordan-plant-manager.md showed that Jordan does not merely share Alex's company: Jordan's briefing OPENS by naming the earlier meeting ("You already had a discovery meeting with Alex, the procurement director, and you earned this meeting with Jordan"), and Jordan's persona knows the rep "earned this meeting through your procurement director, Alex". So "coherent, the same way Jordan follows Alex" was ambiguous between same-company-only and full narrative continuity. The owner chose CONTINUITY, because the pedagogy is that the second meeting builds on the first: the persona names the earlier meeting and the gatekeeper, and rewards a seller who references what they learned rather than re-running discovery.
  - THAT REFERENCE IS NARRATIVE ONLY, exactly as it is for Alex and Jordan today. It is written into the scenario text at generation time and never looked up again. There is NO database link, NO prerequisite, and NO unlock: the gatekeeper winner and the decision-maker winner are two INDEPENDENT assignable scenarios, and either can be assigned and practiced on its own. Owner-verified live by assigning the decision maker alone and practicing it without ever touching the gatekeeper. A "chained from" breadcrumb in the stored JSON was considered and REJECTED for exactly this reason: it would be the one thing in the data that reads like a link.
  - Continuity applies ONLY to a chain-generated decision maker. One generated straight from the form has no earlier meeting and is told nothing about one, so it cannot invent a phantom colleague.
  - DOC FIRST, then the code quotes it, which is this project's rule for docs/Relii-Piece2b-Persona-Generation-Prompt.md. Two additions: Part E (the chain instruction block appended to the system message on both candidate calls) and Part C-chain (the two assembly variations plus the extra demeanor line that is what actually rewards building on the first meeting). Both were BYTE-COMPARED against the code after writing, the same check the 2c prompt tightening used.
  - The student briefing still CANNOT leak a hidden number, and that guarantee survived the change: the chain branch of scenario_context adds a name and a role and still never reads hidden_facts at all. That remains a code-level property for every future case, not a spot check.
  - THE VERCEL TIMEOUT DID NOT BITE, for the third time, and this was the escalation point (design decision 7). The chain was named as the heaviest path and was built synchronous first and last. It reuses the existing route and its explicit 60-second maxDuration, and the chain adds inputs rather than a second pipeline: still two calls at the same time, so it is no slower than an ordinary generation. Real chain generations completed comfortably. Generation STAYS SYNCHRONOUS and the background-and-poll design was correctly NOT built. The candidates also came back meaningfully different, so the recorded too-similar fallback (feeding candidate one into call two, roughly doubling generation time) was again NOT needed and remains held.
  - Owner-verified live across three rounds: a winner enabled, appearing in the assignment picker, practiceable, with the loser row gone and nothing else touched; the bulk discard clearing the 2c leftovers while leaving Alex, Jordan, a switched-on chosen persona, and a role-played persona all intact (confirmed by screenshot); a chain generation producing two coherent, meaningfully different decision makers at the gatekeeper's own company with the continuity wording correct in both the briefing and the persona prompt; and the two winners assignable independently with no unlock. Alex and Jordan still score 180 and 220 throughout.
