  - New code: app/api/instructor/scaffold/choose/route.ts (pick, enable, retitle, delete the loser), app/api/instructor/scaffold/discard/route.ts (per-group), app/api/instructor/scaffold/discard-all/route.ts (bulk, with the preview mode), app/api/instructor/scaffold/disable/route.ts (turn off), app/instructor/scaffold/chain/[scenarioId]/page.tsx (the chain form page), components/choose-candidate-button.tsx, components/generation-actions.tsx, components/discard-all-generations.tsx, components/chain-form.tsx. Edited: lib/scaffold-candidates.ts (the usage counting, the discard planning, the live-title helper, per-row state on the recent list), lib/persona-scaffold.ts (Part E and the chain context), lib/persona-assembly.ts (Part C-chain), app/api/instructor/scaffold/generate/route.ts (the chain branch with the server-side carryover), app/instructor/scaffold/compare/[groupId]/page.tsx (the decided state, the pick buttons, the chain offer), app/instructor/scaffold/page.tsx (the per-generation controls and the cleanup section), and docs/Relii-Piece2b-Persona-Generation-Prompt.md (Parts C-chain and E). No new dependencies, NO schema change, no SQL file added.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece and still out of scope: any rubric or scoring (scaffolded cases stay conversation-only), the .md file or Storage bucket, per-scenario voice, professor finalization, visual polish, and any enforced prerequisite or unlock between the two personas.
  - THE ONE REMAINING STEP IS MANUAL AND IS THE OWNER'S: the winner role-play gate (design decision 5). Before Dawn sees a generated case, role-play the enabled winner once through the normal assignment flow. Reading the fields shows whether a case is coherent; only a conversation shows how the persona actually behaves. The compare view's decided state prompts this on screen.

- SPRINT PIECE 3: student and instructor names. Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app (every test passed: the gate blocked both a student and an instructor dashboard until a first and last name were entered and did not return after saving; a student could not start a practice until it was filled; names then appeared on the student home, the results display, the printout, the instructor submissions table, the roster, and the submission detail page; a nameless legacy profile fell back to email everywhere rather than showing blank; the invite flow still worked with no name at invite time; and Alex and Jordan still scored 180 and 220). One additive schema change (two nullable name columns); no Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened, and the invite list, signup trigger, and CSV were untouched.
  - WHAT THIS UNLOCKS: until now every user was identified only by email, because profiles had no name field. Now every user has a first and last name, captured once by a required gate the first time they reach their dashboard, and shown wherever the app used to show an email.
  - Schema (supabase/piece-3-names.sql, run in production). Two columns on profiles, first_name and last_name, both text and both NULLABLE and additive, so existing rows and the signup trigger (which does not supply them) simply leave them empty. Deliberately NO default and NO NOT NULL: the signup trigger inserts a profile with only id, email, and role, so a NOT NULL would break registration, and an empty name is exactly what tells the app to show the gate. The "everyone must have a name" rule is therefore enforced in the APP by the gate, not by the database. This is the only schema change.
  - THE NAME WRITE IS A SMALL SERVER ROUTE, NOT AN RLS UPDATE POLICY, and the reasoning is recorded because the two were weighed. A row-scoped RLS update policy (auth.uid() = id) cannot limit WHICH columns are written, so on its own it would also let a student change their own role (self-promote to instructor) or email from the browser; locking it to the name columns would mean fighting Supabase's default column grants with REVOKE and GRANT, which is fragile and is exactly the "broader profile writes" the piece was told not to open. Instead app/api/profile/name reads the caller's id from the logged-in session cookie (never from the browser), trims and length-checks the two names, and writes ONLY first_name and last_name to that id's own row with the service-role key. So a user can only ever set their OWN name and can never touch any other field or any other user's row, by construction, and NO profile write rule was added to the database (the existing "read own profile" select policy stays the only profiles policy). This mirrors how every other sensitive write in this app is done (delete-student, uninvite, the scaffold routes).
  - THE GATE. Both dashboards read first_name and last_name alongside the role. If either is blank, the page renders ONLY the gate (components/name-gate.tsx) in place of itself, so the student's Practice and Start buttons and the instructor's whole dashboard are not rendered at all until a name exists. That is what makes it blocking without a fragile modal: there is nothing else on screen to click, so a student literally cannot start a practice until it is filled. On save the gate posts to the route and calls router.refresh(); the server re-reads the profile, now sees a name, and renders the real dashboard, so the gate does not return on later visits. A legacy already-registered account with no name sees it on its next login too, since the gate is the capture path for everyone. It applies to students AND instructors identically.
  - WHERE NAMES RENDER, all through one helper (lib/name.ts): displayName returns "First Last" when both are set and the email otherwise, and hasName decides whether a name is set. The email fallback is everywhere, so a nameless profile never shows blank. The places: the student home header (name, email beneath); the results display header (name, email beneath, then the scenario title), positioned so it also prints, which covers the printout in one change; the instructor submissions table Student column (name, email beneath, rows now sorted by the shown name); the roster (registered students via the delete-student row, which also now names the student in its confirm, and registered instructors with name and email); and the instructor submission detail page (the header and the student's transcript speaker label). Pending invites keep email only, correctly, since names are not part of the invite flow.
  - Email is kept ALONGSIDE the name wherever it aids identification (beneath the name on every screen, and in the delete-student confirm as "Name (email)"), so an instructor can still match a name to the account it belongs to. Where displayName already falls back to the email (no name set), the redundant second email line is suppressed, so a nameless row shows the email once, not twice.
  - The instructor dashboard header also shows the instructor's OWN name (a small parity follow-up after the first checkpoint, since the student home already showed the signed-in name and the instructor header still read only email, which looked inconsistent side by side). The instructor's own name also appears in the roster's Registered instructors list.
  - Built and pushed in two checkpoints (the schema plus the gate, the write route, and the student home name; then the names on the results, printout, submissions table, roster, and detail page), each verified live by the owner, with the one small parity follow-up push in between. Then this PROJECT_STATE update.
  - New code: supabase/piece-3-names.sql (the two columns), lib/name.ts (displayName and hasName), app/api/profile/name/route.ts (the scoped server write), components/name-gate.tsx (the blocking gate). Edited: app/student/page.tsx and app/instructor/page.tsx (read the names, show the gate when unset, show the signed-in name in the header), app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx (the name on the results and printed sheet), app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx (the name in the header and transcript label), and components/delete-student-button.tsx (name plus email in the row and confirm). No new dependencies; the only schema change is the two approved additive columns. Nothing touched the invite list, the signup trigger, or the CSV.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece: any names in the invite path or the CSV (that is Piece 7), and any visual polish beyond what showing a name required.

- SPRINT PIECE 4: assignment submission workflow revision. Built and deployed live in three pushes; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app (Start assignment disabled and unclickable; a completed voice+video practice submitted as the graded assignment, keeping its score and leaving the practice pool, appearing on the instructor dashboard as the submission with the Piece 3 name; a second submission cleanly refused; the camera-off warning shown for a voice+video practice with no recording; the student's other practices NOT deleted on submission; and allow-retake returning the submission to the practice pool with its recording intact, re-selectable, the audit still recorded). NO schema change of any kind: the conversion is safe under the existing schema. No Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened.
  - THE MODEL CHANGE: a graded submission is no longer a live one-shot attempt. It is a completed voice+video practice (one with an actual recording and a content score) that the student CONVERTS out of the practice pool into the graded submission. The one-shot "Start assignment" flow is kept INTACT but disabled (grayed and unclickable) and is reachable again by flipping one prop; only the disabled button stands between the student and it.
  - WHY NO SCHEMA CHANGE: sessions.kind already allows both 'practice' and 'submission'; the "a submission must belong to an assignment" constraint is satisfied because practices are assignment-linked; submitted_at already exists; the recording is keyed by session id, so it survives the conversion in place with no file move; and the partial unique index sessions_one_submission_per_assignment (Stage 7) already enforces one submission per assignment, so a second conversion is refused at the database level. Converting a practice also removes it from the FIFO count (kind = 'practice'), which both frees a retention slot and exempts the graded one from eviction, for free.
  - required_mode is REPURPOSED (owner-approved): voice_video now means GRADABLE (a completed voice+video practice can be submitted), text means PRACTICE-ONLY (no submit control at all). Practice stays free and unlimited on any assignment, text practices remain scored and visible to the student, and instructors still never see practices. The submit control is gated on the assignment being gradable (required_mode = voice_video AND its scenario has a rubric), both server-side and in the UI.
  - PUSH 1 (disable Start, retire auto-delete, fix the copy). "Start assignment" is grayed and unclickable via a new disabled prop on components/start-assignment-button.tsx; the start() logic and the whole one-shot flow (app/api/assignment/start, the submission scoring path in session/end, lib/submission's window and lock) are untouched and reachable only if the prop is flipped back. The auto-delete-of-practices-on-submission (the Stage 7 sixth-chunk behavior) is RETIRED: the deletePracticesForAssignment call and its import were removed from app/api/session/end/route.ts, so submitting no longer wipes the student's other practices; only the FIFO retention cap ever deletes practices now. The FIFO prune and the 2a conversation-only prune are untouched. The now-false "submitting permanently clears your saved practices" paragraph was removed from the (now dormant) submit-and-score confirmations in components/conversation.tsx and components/voice-conversation.tsx. lib/practice.ts's deletePracticesForAssignment is left defined but unused, marked retired.
  - PUSH 2 (submit a completed practice). A new student-owned, service-role route app/api/practice/submit converts one of the student's OWN completed voice+video practices into the graded submission: it verifies ownership and that the session is kind = practice, status = scored, mode = voice_video, has a recording (video_path set), is assignment-linked, and that the assignment is gradable (required_mode = voice_video AND a rubric), then UPDATES kind = 'submission' and stamps submitted_at, KEEPING the existing score, status ('scored'), scored_at, and recording untouched (no re-score, no file move). A second submission for the same assignment is refused cleanly, both by a pre-check and by catching the unique-index violation (23505). The new components/submit-practice-button.tsx renders "Submit as graded assignment" with accurate copy (it becomes your official submission, keeps its score, your other practices stay, changeable only if your instructor allows a retake). On the student home (app/student/page.tsx), each kept voice+video practice on a gradable assignment with no submission yet shows the submit control; a voice+video practice with NO recording (camera off or a failed upload) shows the red "Camera must be toggled on in order to be eligible for submission." instead; text practices, and practices on text or conversation-only assignments, show no control.
  - PUSH 3 (allow-retake returns to the pool). "Allow retake" (app/api/instructor/submission/[id]/reset, the retake path) is NO LONGER destructive: instead of deleting the finished submission and its recording, it REVERSES the conversion (kind submission back to practice, submitted_at cleared), keeping the score and the recording, so the student has a re-selectable scored practice again and can submit once more. The reset audit is still written first (a retake is still recorded, with the score the submission had). The interrupted-attempt path (delete the row and any partial recording) is UNCHANGED but now DORMANT, since no new live graded attempts are started under this model; it stays for any legacy in_progress row. A bug trap was avoided (flagged by the Opus advisor): the old route deleted the Storage recording BEFORE the row for both paths, so the retake had to skip BOTH the recording delete and the row delete, not merely swap the row delete for an update, or it would have deleted the very recording it is meant to keep. The confirm copy in components/submission-reset-button.tsx was rewritten from the destructive "permanently delete / type RESET" panel to a plain, accurate confirm (returns to the practice pool, keeps recording and score, nothing deleted, recorded in the reset history); components/reset-history.tsx now reads "Graded retake (returned to pool)" instead of "(score wiped)".
  - DECISION 1 (owner choice), the retention cap on return-to-pool: PRUNE NOW, KEEP RETURNED. prunePractices in lib/practice.ts gained an optional keepId that force-keeps one row and fills the remaining slots with the newest others, so if a returned practice ever pushed the pool over the cap, the oldest OTHER practice would be dropped and the returned one (with its recording) kept. REALITY CHECK found during live testing, recorded because it looks like dead code otherwise: this overflow is STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE under the current "no practice while a submission exists" rule (the practice buttons are hidden once submitted, a pre-existing Stage 7 gate). To submit you convert one of your at-most-3 kept practices, dropping the pool to at most 2, and you cannot practice again until a retake, so at any retake the pool is at most 2 and returning the submission takes it to at most 3, never over. So the keepId prune is a correct, safe BACKSTOP that does nothing under today's rules (the returned practice is always within the cap and always kept). It becomes live automatically if the design ever allows practicing after submitting. Test A (the real, reachable retake behavior) passing is the meaningful verification; Test B (3 practices plus a submission at once) cannot be constructed and is not a bug.
  - DECISION 2 (owner choice), what the student sees after submitting: TOP BANNER ONLY. The converted practice shows as the clear green "Submitted, score, View result" banner at the top and leaves the saved-practices list (it is now a submission, not a practice); the remaining practices simply have no submit control, gated on ANY submission row existing (not only a finished one), so a legacy in_progress submission also correctly hides the controls.
  - OBSERVATION SURFACED FOR LATER (not built, out of Piece 4 scope): whether a student should be able to keep practicing AFTER submitting is a real UX question under the new model (practice is unlimited and free, but the pre-existing !submitted gate hides the practice buttons once a submission exists). The kickoff did not ask to change that gate, so it was left alone. Reconsidering it is a small separate change, and it is the thing that would make Decision 1's overflow reachable.
  - New code: app/api/practice/submit/route.ts (the conversion route) and components/submit-practice-button.tsx (the submit control). Edited: components/start-assignment-button.tsx (the disabled prop), app/student/page.tsx (the disabled Start button and note, video_path on the practices query, the eligibility gate and camera-off warning), app/api/session/end/route.ts (the auto-delete call and import removed), lib/practice.ts (deletePracticesForAssignment marked retired, prunePractices gained keepId), app/api/instructor/submission/[id]/reset/route.ts (the retake path returns to the pool instead of deleting), components/submission-reset-button.tsx (the rewritten non-destructive retake confirm), components/reset-history.tsx (the "returned to pool" label), and components/conversation.tsx and components/voice-conversation.tsx (the removed false clearing paragraph). No new dependencies, NO schema change, no SQL file added.
  - Built and pushed in three checkpoints (disable Start plus retire auto-delete plus fix the copy; the conversion; allow-retake returns to the pool), each verified live by the owner, then this PROJECT_STATE update. The Opus advisor was consulted before the approach, and again on the Push 3 implementation.

- SPRINT PIECE 5: the 30:00 count-up clock. Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app (all four tests passed on a shortened-threshold test build: the clock counted up from 00:00 and turned yellow then red on both a text and a voice+video practice, the 30:00 hard stop auto-ended the practice onto the results screen, a normal manual End still scored, and Alex and Jordan still scored 180 and 220; then the real thresholds were verified on the final build). NO schema change and NO route, Row Level Security, or Storage change: the clock is client-side display plus an auto-end that reuses the existing end action. The scorer was not touched.
  - WHAT IT IS: a clock counts UP from 00:00 on a PRACTICE session in BOTH modes (text and voice+video), ticking in real time in the screen header next to the End button. The text is the default dark gray before 10:00, turns YELLOW at 10:00, and turns RED at 15:00 and stays red through 30:00, which warns the student around the intended fifteen-minute meeting length. At 30:00 the clock freezes and the practice AUTO-ENDS through the same routine the student's End button uses, landing on the results screen exactly as a manual end would. This was built BEFORE Piece 6 (pause) on purpose, because pause has to stop this clock.
  - PRACTICE ONLY, by construction. Because "Start assignment" is disabled (Piece 4), the only live sessions are practices, so the clock is effectively practice-only anyway; it is also gated explicitly on the session NOT being a graded submission (!isSubmission, which is exactly kind = practice), so the dormant one-shot graded flow renders no clock and is completely untouched. The 30:00 here is a NEW, separate mechanism from the dormant lib/submission live/lock window (which also happens to be 30 minutes); the two are not entangled, and lib/submission was not touched.
  - THE AUTO-END REUSES THE EXISTING END ACTION, it does not duplicate anything. At 30:00 the clock calls onExpire, which sets an `expired` flag; a small effect then invokes that screen's OWN existing end routine (doEnd for text, endCall for voice), the same one the End button triggers, which POSTs /api/session/end. So a gradable voice+video practice is scored and becomes a Piece 4 eligible submission candidate, and a conversation-only (null-rubric) practice ends 'completed' with no score, exactly as a manual end does today. It does NOT auto-submit as the graded assignment; the student still chooses whether to submit later.
  - STOPPABLE AT A SINGLE CONTROL POINT, ready for Piece 6. Whether the clock is advancing sits behind ONE clearly-named boolean per screen, clockActive, which is what the shared clock's `active` prop reads. For text clockActive = !isSubmission && !ending && !expired (starts at mount, since the text conversation is already live); for voice clockActive = !isSubmission && status === "live" && !expired (starts when the call goes LIVE, not on the pre-call read screen). Piece 6's pause will stop the clock by AND-ing `&& !paused` at that one spot in each screen and nothing else changes. Each is commented as the single control point. No pause, no pause control, and no per-assignment toggle were built here.
  - THE TIMER MEASURES REAL ELAPSED TIME, not ticks. The shared hook accumulates wall-clock time only while `active` is true, folding each running stretch into an accumulator whenever it stops, so it stays accurate even if the browser tab is backgrounded (it catches up from the clock rather than losing throttled ticks) and it can be cleanly PAUSED later by flipping `active`. It caps and freezes at 30:00, and onExpire fires exactly once. The auto-end is guarded to fire once (a ref), waits out any in-flight text message send before ending, and does not loop on a failed end (the normal error shows and the student can End manually), matching today's manual-end failure behavior.
  - ACCEPTED BEHAVIOR, recorded so it does not look like a bug: reloading a TEXT practice restarts the clock at 00:00 (the clock anchors to when it starts running on the client, not to a server timestamp). This is fine because practices are unlimited and free and the clock is a soft warning plus a soft backstop, not a usage limit; the owner chose this simpler option over anchoring to the session's created_at (which would also have forced text and voice to use different start anchors, since voice cannot use created_at cleanly because of its pre-call screen).
  - Colors are sensible-now, not final: default text-gray-700, yellow text-yellow-600, red text-red-600, monospace with tabular digits and an aria-label. The three time thresholds and the colors all live at the top of the one clock file, which is where the Stage 8.5 accessibility/contrast pass will tune them.
  - Built and pushed in TWO checkpoints, so the time-based behavior could actually be watched live without waiting real minutes: checkpoint 1 shipped the whole feature with SHORTENED test thresholds (yellow 15s, red 30s, auto-end 45s) so the owner saw default to yellow to red to auto-end in under a minute on both modes; checkpoint 2 restored the real 10:00 / 15:00 / 30:00 values (a values-only change, no logic change) and the owner re-verified. One small lint note: the stricter react-hooks/refs rule required syncing the onExpire ref inside an effect rather than writing it during render.
  - New code: components/practice-clock.tsx (the shared count-up hook plus the on-screen PracticeClock; the thresholds and colors live here). Edited: components/conversation.tsx and components/voice-conversation.tsx (each renders the clock for a practice, computes its single clockActive control point, and auto-ends once at 30:00 via its existing end routine). No new dependencies, no schema change, no SQL file, no route/RLS/Storage change.
  - SUPERSEDED IN PART BY PD-4 (2026-07-31), so read the two entries together. Two things above are no longer true. First, the fixed 30:00 is now a PER-ASSIGNMENT length of 5 to 30 minutes, so the auto-end fires at whatever the instructor set (an assignment with none set still means 30:00, which is why nothing legacy changed). Second, the YELLOW at 10:00 and RED at 15:00 thresholds are GONE: the clock text is a single default color for the whole meeting, and it shows its target instead. Everything else in this entry still holds exactly as written, including the auto-end reusing the existing end routine, the practice-only gating, the single clockActive control point that Piece 6's pause hooks onto, and the wall-clock accumulator.

- SPRINT PIECE 7: bulk invite via CSV. Built and deployed live; owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app (a mixed CSV added the valid new students and instructors and skipped every bad row with the right reason without rejecting the whole file; a mixed-case duplicate of an existing invite was caught; a header row was tolerated; the roster then showed the new pending invites; and the single-invite form and normal registration still worked). NO schema change: this is a batch of the existing single-invite insert into allowed_emails. No Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened.
  - WHAT IT IS: on the instructor page, a "Bulk invite (CSV)" box directly under the single-invite form. The instructor picks a .csv with two columns (email and role) and every VALID new row is added to the invite list at once. Both roles can be added. Names are NOT in the CSV (they are captured only by the Piece 3 first-login gate), so the signup trigger and the allowed_emails schema needed no change.
  - PARTIAL SUCCESS is the rule: the whole file is never rejected. Each bad row is SKIPPED and reported with a plain reason so the instructor can fix and re-upload. The reasons, in the order they are checked (first match wins, so the most specific reason shows): not a valid email; missing role; invalid role (must be student or instructor); duplicate within this file; already a registered user; already on the invite list. Registered is checked before invite-list on purpose, because a registered user is also on the invite list and "already a registered user" is the more useful of the two.
  - CASE-INSENSITIVE THROUGHOUT, matching the signup trigger's lower(email) comparison. Every import email is trimmed and lowercased before it is inserted or compared, and the dedup sets are built from allowed_emails and profiles as lowercased emails, exactly the way the roster and the uninvite route already compare. So a mixed-case entry cannot slip past as a near-duplicate of an existing invite.
  - ALL THE REAL WORK IS SERVER-SIDE AND INSTRUCTOR-GATED, mirroring the uninvite route: the browser only reads the file as text and POSTs it; the route confirms the caller is an instructor (role read from the database, never trusted from the browser) and does the parsing, validation, dedup, and insertion with the service-role key, so bad rows cannot be spoofed in. Only the valid rows are inserted, and added_by records who ran the import (an existing nullable column on allowed_emails, verified present in the schema before use; no schema change).
  - CSV PARSING is a small hand-rolled parser (no new dependency): it splits on lines, skips blanks, tolerates an optional header row (a first row whose first cell is "email" or whose second cell is "role" is dropped, so a genuinely malformed first data row is still reported rather than swallowed as a header), and reads the first two comma-separated cells with any wrapping quotes stripped. Insertion is one batch insert; on a rare duplicate-key race it degrades to per-row inserts, so a single collision moves that row to "skipped" instead of failing the batch (partial success at the database layer too).
  - AFTER A RUN the box shows "Added N, Skipped M" with every skipped row listed (its email or the raw line, and the reason), then refreshes the roster so the new pending invites appear under "Invited, pending". The single-invite form, the uninvite and roster behavior, and the signup trigger were left exactly as they were.
  - New code: lib/bulk-invite.ts (the pure CSV parser and the validate/dedup classifier), app/api/instructor/bulk-invite/route.ts (the instructor-gated server route), components/bulk-invite-form.tsx (the file input, format note, and result summary). Edited: app/instructor/page.tsx (renders BulkInviteForm just below AddEmailForm). No new dependencies, no schema change, no SQL file.

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-1: the instructor navigation and information-architecture restructure. Built and deployed live; all owner checks passed on https://relii.vercel.app. This is the first of the eight pre-demo pieces from the July 28, 2026 Tom meeting. It is REORGANIZATION plus a nav shell: no screen changes what it does, no logic was duplicated, no schema change, and no Row Level Security or Storage rule was touched. Visual finish is deliberately left to PD-8.
  - The instructor surface is now five real destinations behind a navigation menu that sits below the instructor's name, email, and the sign-out button, and is identical on every instructor page: Submissions (the DEFAULT landing at /instructor), Invite Users (/instructor/invites), Manage Assignments (/instructor/assignments), Roster (/instructor/roster), and Persona Scaffolding (/instructor/scaffold, which already existed and simply joined the nav, replacing the small header link that used to be the only way in). The current destination is underlined; a sub-page such as a scaffolding comparison or a submission detail still highlights the destination it was reached through.
  - Label note for the record: the July 28 notes said "Create Assignment," but assignment create, edit, and delete already existed (Stage 8 piece 4), so the owner renamed the destination MANAGE ASSIGNMENTS. It is the existing create form plus the list-with-Edit/Delete, relocated, not a new feature.
  - WHY SIBLING ROUTES rather than tabs on one page: the four sections used to be stacked inside the single server component app/instructor/page.tsx, which did every read on every visit. Each destination is now its own server component owning only the reads its own screen needs, so each view has a real web address, loads less, and can be polished independently in PD-8. The Submissions page's profiles read was narrowed from every user to role = student, which is all it needs (the enrolled count and the student name/email map); the full roster of both roles plus the invite list is read on the Roster page, where it is actually shown.
  - THE SHARED SHELL is app/instructor/layout.tsx, which wraps every route under /instructor. It draws the header and the nav once, and holds both gates for the whole instructor surface: signed-in-and-an-instructor (role read from the database, never trusted from the browser), and the required NAME GATE. With no first and last name set it renders the gate INSTEAD of the page, so no instructor destination can be reached, not even by typing its address. The name check lives in the layout and ONLY there: a page that redirected on a missing name would bounce against the layout forever. Each page independently re-checks the user and the role for itself (it needs the user's id for its own reads anyway) through one shared helper, lib/instructor-auth.ts. Net gain: the name gate now also covers the submission detail view and the scaffolding subroutes, which never had that check.
  - "ACTIVE ASSIGNMENTS" is now a visible label above the assignment dropdown on Submissions (July 28 aligned decision 8), so the dropdown reads as the control for moving between active sessions. IMPORTANT and recorded deliberately: the dropdown still lists EVERY assignment. There is no active or inactive state in the data, so this is the agreed label for the control, not a filter. PD-3 (Available Date) is what may make the word literal later.
  - BACK-LINKS AND HEADINGS were corrected for the new structure. The scaffolding subroutes already pointed at /instructor/scaffold and the submission detail already pointed at /instructor?assignment=<id>, so both targets were right and only the wording changed ("Back to Persona Scaffolding", "Back to Submissions"). The scaffolding page's old "Back to dashboard" link was REMOVED as redundant, since the nav is now on every page. Because the layout owns the page title, every page heading underneath it was demoted from h1 to h2 (the scaffolding page, both scaffolding subroutes including their two explanatory states, and the submission detail), so no page stacks two titles. On the submission detail the name and email are the STUDENT'S while the header above shows the signed-in instructor's, so that line now reads "Student: <name>" to remove the ambiguity.
  - ONE REAL BEHAVIOR CHANGE, accepted knowingly: the invite forms and the pending-invite list are no longer on the same page, so a successful invite no longer makes that list update in front of you. Both forms still report exactly what they did (the bulk form still lists every added and skipped row with its reason), and the two pages link to each other. The alternative, duplicating the roster read onto the Invite Users page, was rejected as duplicated logic. Owner-verified live: a bulk CSV import's new pending invites appear on the Roster page.
  - New code: app/instructor/layout.tsx (the shell), lib/instructor-auth.ts (the shared requireInstructor helper), components/instructor-nav.tsx (the nav, the only new client component, since it reads the current path to mark where you are), and the three relocated pages app/instructor/{invites,assignments,roster}/page.tsx. Edited: app/instructor/page.tsx (reduced to Submissions, about 360 lines lighter, plus the Active Assignments label and an empty state that now points at Manage Assignments instead of saying "below"), app/instructor/scaffold/page.tsx, both scaffolding subroutes, app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx, and components/assignment-selector.tsx (one attribute: an id, so the new visible label names the dropdown, replacing the aria-label it carried when it had no visible label). No new dependencies, no schema change, no SQL file.

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-2: editable persona scaffolding. Built and deployed live; all seven owner checks passed on https://relii.vercel.app. This is the second of the eight pre-demo pieces. It re-opens the Piece 2 persona-generation integrity, so the whole design is built around keeping the no-drift guarantee true after an edit. NO schema change, no Row Level Security or Storage rule touched, and no new dependency.
  - WHAT IT DOES: an instructor can customise a generated candidate persona before choosing a winner. The edit saves the changed STRUCTURED FIELDS and then RE-RUNS THE EXISTING FIXED TEMPLATE (lib/persona-assembly.ts, the same pure function generation uses) to rebuild that candidate's scenario_context and persona_system_prompt. There is NO new AI generation call on the edit path, and deliberately no OpenAI import in the edit route at all. Edited fields in, template-composed text out, nothing re-invented, so the fields an instructor compares on screen and the persona a student actually meets still come from one source and cannot drift apart.
  - LOCKED BEHAVIOR, confirmed by the owner at the kickoff: editing applies ONLY to candidates BEFORE a winner is selected and enabled. Once a winner is chosen and switched on, it is not edited through this path. EDITABLE (eight fields): persona_name, role, demeanor, decision_criteria, hidden_facts (each a fact and its detail), objections, earns_win, fails_win. READ-ONLY (three locked pairs, structural to the case and the chain): meeting type / chain position, win condition / close terms, company type / size. Owner decision recorded July 30, 2026: earns_win and fails_win ARE editable, because they are persona behavior like objections and decision criteria; only the win condition itself is the locked "terms of the close."
  - THE INTEGRITY RISK THIS PIECE HAD TO SOLVE, and the fix. The assembly template takes an OPTIONAL chain context (the chosen gatekeeper's name, role, company, and win condition), and for a decision maker generated FROM a chosen gatekeeper that chain is what writes the "you already had a discovery meeting with X, and you earned this meeting" narrative into BOTH text fields. That chain was built at generation time and then THROWN AWAY: generation_fields stored only the candidate and the admin's inputs, and nothing on the row pointed back at its gatekeeper. So rebuilding such a candidate without it would have silently deleted that narrative and turned a second meeting back into a standalone one, on ANY edit, including editing the persona name or role that appear in the narrative. THE FIX: generation_fields is now VERSION 2 and stores the chain, so re-assembly is fully self-contained from the candidate row. Nothing looks the gatekeeper up again, so the two scenarios stay independently assignable with no prerequisite, exactly as before.
  - The version bump is UNCONDITIONAL, on every new generation including a gatekeeper that has no chain. Version 2 has to mean "provenance is fully recorded," because that is what lets an ABSENT chain on a version 2 decision maker be read as proof it never had one.
  - HOW THE CHAIN IS RESOLVED ON AN EDIT: from an explicit table keyed on the row, never a guess. A chain is only ever built in the "generate from a chosen gatekeeper" branch, and that branch forces the new persona to be a decision maker, so a chain always implies a decision maker and a GATEKEEPER NEVER HAS ONE. That gives: gatekeeper at any version, no chain; version 2 decision maker with a stored chain, that chain; version 2 decision maker with none, standalone; version 1 decision maker, GENUINELY AMBIGUOUS and REFUSED, with a message telling the instructor to regenerate the case and edit the new candidates. Legacy version 1 rows cannot be recovered because version 1 stored no chain either way and nothing points back at the gatekeeper. Regenerate-only was the owner's confirmed choice; a text-sniffing rescue was considered and deliberately NOT built, to keep the one route that must be beyond doubt simple.
  - THE ROUND-TRIP GUARD (the extra safety net). Before overwriting anything, the route re-assembles the UNEDITED stored fields with the resolved chain and requires them to reproduce the saved text EXACTLY. The saved text was itself produced by this same template at generation time, so a mismatch means the reconstruction is wrong (a missing or wrong chain, or a bug in how it is stored) and the edit is refused with nothing written. It is strict on version 2 rows and warns rather than refuses on version 1 gatekeepers, which predate chain storage. IT ASSUMES lib/persona-assembly.ts IS FROZEN: if that template is ever changed later, existing version 2 candidates will no longer reproduce their saved text and will need regenerating before they can be edited. That is the honest outcome, because after a template change a rebuild really would drift. The assumption is written as a comment on the guard itself. NOTE FROM TESTING: the guard never fired in any of the seven live checks, which is the expected result, and it is a backstop rather than part of the normal path.
  - HOW THE LOCKED PAIRS ARE ENFORCED: not by the form hiding them. The server merge starts from the STORED candidate and overlays ONLY the eight editable fields, so the locked fields are never read from the request at all. A hand-built request that tries to change the win condition or the company changes nothing, because nothing in the route ever looks at those keys.
  - OTHER GUARDS ON THE EDIT ROUTE, each a real one: instructor only, with the role read from the DATABASE and never trusted from the browser; it refuses any scenario with no generation_group_id, which is the same single condition that keeps Alex, Jordan, and every hand-written scenario out of every scaffold route; and it refuses once ANY row in the generation group is enabled, which is the exact "candidates only, before a winner is chosen" rule (checking only the edited row would leave a losing candidate that survived the pick still editable). The edited candidate passes the SAME validateCandidate a generated one does (every field filled, at least 3 hidden facts and 3 objections), reused rather than rewritten, so an edit can never produce a candidate weaker than the generator is allowed to produce; its per-field problems go back to the form, so trimming to two objections says exactly that.
  - TWO WRITE DETAILS THAT ARE CORRECTNESS, NOT TIDINESS. First, generation_fields is written by SPREADING the stored object and replacing only the candidate, so version, candidate_index, inputs and above all CHAIN carry through untouched; rebuilding that object from scratch is exactly how the chain would get dropped on the first edit, which is the failure this piece exists to prevent, arriving one step later. Second, the persona_name COLUMN is updated alongside the JSON, because two existing routes read the column while reading the rest from the JSON: choosing a winner builds its live title from the column, and generating a decision maker takes the gatekeeper's name from the column. A stale column would put the old name on the live scenario and into the next chained persona.
  - EDITS CARRY INTO THE WINNER WITH NO EXTRA WORK, because choosing a winner reuses the SAME row (it sets enabled = true and retitles it, and creates nothing), so an edit to that row's fields and text is already there when it goes live.
  - THE EDIT SURFACE is a dedicated page at /instructor/scaffold/edit/[candidateId], reached from a "Customise [name]" link in the compare grid sitting directly above each candidate's "Choose this one" button, shown only while the generation is undecided. A dedicated page rather than inline editing because the compare view is a fixed two-column table whose whole job is reading two candidates side by side, and eight editable fields (five multi-line lists plus paired fact-and-detail rows) would destroy that. Saving returns to the comparison, so the edit is visible there in one click. The three locked pairs are shown on the edit page as plain text with a one-line reason, so they read as structural rather than missing. Two states are explained rather than 404'd, the same way the chain page does it: a generation that is already decided, and a version 1 decision maker that cannot be rebuilt. Visual finish is deliberately left to PD-8.
  - VERIFIED BY THE OWNER LIVE, all seven: editing a standalone candidate rebuilds its text with the locked fields unchanged and no AI call; editing a CHAIN-generated decision maker's name and role PRESERVES the gatekeeper narrative in both the briefing and the persona instructions (the make-or-break regression check); the three locked pairs cannot be edited; an edit shows immediately in the compare view; choosing an edited candidate carries the edits and the correct live title into the enabled scenario; editing a GATEKEEPER, choosing it, then generating its decision maker produces a persona that names the EDITED gatekeeper (the second-order path where a stale persona_name column would have shown); and editing the same candidate TWICE succeeds, which is what proves the round-trip guard is stable rather than merely passing once. Before the build, the assembly behavior was also proved locally in both directions: an edit with the stored chain keeps the narrative, and the same edit without it loses it.
  - New code: app/api/instructor/scaffold/edit/route.ts (the save-and-rebuild route), app/instructor/scaffold/edit/[candidateId]/page.tsx, components/edit-candidate-form.tsx. Edited: lib/scaffold-candidates.ts (the GENERATION_FIELDS_VERSION constant, the optional chain on the GenerationFields type, the shared candidateScenarioTitle helper, and resolveChainForReassembly), app/api/instructor/scaffold/generate/route.ts (stores the chain, stamps version 2, uses the shared title helper), and app/instructor/scaffold/compare/[groupId]/page.tsx (the Customise link plus one line of explanation). UNTOUCHED by design: lib/persona-assembly.ts and lib/persona-scaffold.ts, so the template and the generation contract were not modified by this piece.
  - NO SQL TO RUN. generation_fields is already a jsonb column, so version 2 adding an optional chain is a CONTENT change, not a column change. supabase/piece-2c-candidate-grouping.sql was left untouched on purpose: it is the record of what was actually run, and editing it would invite the question of whether there is something to re-run. There is not.
  - CARRIED FORWARD: the winner role-play gate still applies (design decision 5). Role-play an edited, enabled winner once end to end before Dawn sees the case, since reading the fields shows coherence but only a conversation shows behavior.

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-3: the Available Date. Built and deployed live; all eight owner checks passed on https://relii.vercel.app. This is the third of the eight pre-demo pieces. ONE additive schema change (a single nullable column), run in production before the code went live; no Row Level Security or Storage rule was touched, and no new dependency.
  - WHAT IT DOES: an instructor can set an Available Date on an assignment, and students see that assignment listed but LOCKED until the date arrives. It exists so a whole semester of role-plays can be created at the start of the term and open on their own schedule, instead of the instructor creating each one by hand on the day. The student dashboard is now ordered by that date, soonest at the top.
  - AVAILABLE DATE COEXISTS WITH THE DUE DATE, and due_at was NOT repurposed. They answer two different questions (when can you start it, when is it owed), an assignment can carry either, both, or neither, and the two lines are shown independently. The due-date behavior on every screen is byte-for-byte what it was.
  - Schema (supabase/pd-3-available-date.sql, run in production). One column on assignments: available_date, timestamptz, NULLABLE, deliberately with NO default and NO not-null rule. It mirrors due_at exactly (same type, same nullability), which is what let the existing date picker, the UTC date formatting, and the "left blank means not set" handling all be reused rather than reinvented. A NULL available date MEANS AVAILABLE NOW, which is the whole reason it is nullable: every assignment that existed before this piece simply leaves it empty and keeps working, appearing immediately and unlocked, with no backfill and no migration of existing rows. Owner-verified after running the SQL that every live assignment came back with an empty available_date and an unchanged due date.
  - THE RULE LIVES IN ONE FILE, lib/available-date.ts, holding two small pure functions: isLockedUntil (a date is set AND that moment is still in the future) and formatAvailableDate (the same UTC-read plain date the due date already uses). The student home and all four server routes call the SAME two functions. This is the point of the file: a lock that the screen and the server each decided for themselves would eventually disagree, and the disagreement would be invisible until a student hit it.
  - SORTING, and the one non-obvious part of it: the student home orders by available_date ASCENDING with NULLS FIRST, then created_at descending as a tiebreaker. Nulls-first has to be asked for explicitly (Supabase and Postgres both put nulls LAST on an ascending sort by default), and without it every pre-PD-3 assignment, which is every assignment that exists today, would have been buried at the bottom of the list. Nulls first is also correct on the merits: a null means available now, so those belong above anything with a future date. Note that a null therefore also sorts above an assignment whose date has already PASSED, which is the owner's chosen behavior. The created_at tiebreaker keeps assignments that share a date (or are both empty) in the newest-first order the page used before, instead of coming back in an arbitrary order that could change between reloads.
  - THE LOCK ON SCREEN: a locked assignment card is grayed (muted background, title, meta line, and instructions) and carries one clear line, "Locked. Available on <date>." Its practice buttons, its Start assignment block, and its submit control are NOT RENDERED, rather than rendered-and-disabled, so there is nothing to click at all. The saved-practices section is hidden too unless practices actually exist. Everything the student needs in order to plan (title, scenario, persona, required mode, due date, instructions) stays readable, because the point of showing a locked assignment is that the student can see what is coming.
  - WHAT THE LOCK DELIBERATELY DOES NOT HIDE, and why the condition is written the way it is: the lock branch is gated on there being NO SUBMISSION ROW AT ALL, not on "the student has not submitted yet". If an instructor moves an already-open assignment's date into the future, a student who has already submitted still sees their green Submitted banner and View result link, a student with an interrupted attempt still sees that notice, and any practices they already have stay reviewable and deletable. The lock stops NEW practice and NEW submitting; it never hides work that is already done. On a normal locked assignment (nobody has touched it, which is the ordinary case) none of this is reachable and the card shows only the lock line.
  - ENFORCEMENT IS FRONT END PLUS SERVER, on all four start/submit routes: session/start (text practice), voice/start (voice practice), practice/submit (converting a practice into the graded submission), and assignment/start (the dormant one-shot graded start). Each already loaded the assignment, so it cost one selected column and one check apiece, and each refuses with 403 and a plain message naming the date. The sprint plan described this as a student-dashboard gate and it would have been defensible to stop at the screen, but every other "what may a student start" rule in this app (a turned-off scenario, a practice-only assignment, one submission per assignment, a locked submission) is enforced in the route, and a browser-only lock would have been the single exception. The camera-lock precedent is front-end-only for a reason that does not apply here: the webcam recording physically happens in the browser, so there is no server moment to check, whereas starting a practice is a server write. assignment/start is DORMANT today (the one-shot Start button has been disabled since Piece 4) and was covered anyway, so the rule is already in place if that flow is ever switched back on rather than being a hole somebody has to remember.
  - MIDNIGHT UTC IS KEPT, deliberately and with the tradeoff recorded. The date picker sends a plain day and the database stores it as midnight UTC, exactly as due_at already does, and it is read back in UTC everywhere so a date picked as "Aug 14" always reads "Aug 14" on screen. The consequence, which is real: an assignment unlocks at midnight UTC, which is the previous evening in US time zones (about 8pm Eastern the day before). The owner confirmed keeping it rather than introducing time zone handling for this one field, because inventing a second date convention would make the Available Date and the due date behave differently on the same card, which is worse than the offset.
  - ACCEPTED BEHAVIOR, recorded so it does not look like a bug: the lock is decided when the page loads (every student page is server-rendered per visit), so an assignment that unlocks while a student is looking at the page appears on their next visit or refresh. No timer, no polling, and no background job was added for this. The server check is what makes that safe: a stale page cannot start anything it should not, it just looks stale.
  - Forms: both components/create-assignment-form.tsx and components/edit-assignment-form.tsx gained an "Available date (optional)" picker directly above the due date, with the note "Students see this assignment listed but locked until this date. Leave blank to make it available now." Clearing it on the edit form sets it back to null, which means available now. Both write the column straight from the browser under the existing "instructors manage assignments" rule, exactly the way they already write the due date, so NO server route was needed for setting it. The instructor's read-only assignment card shows "available Aug 14" beside "due Aug 20".
  - FOLD-IN COPY FIX, done here because PD-3 touched this file: app/student/page.tsx rendered an em dash as the no-score placeholder. It is now a plain hyphen. This was the stray em dash logged in Open Issues after PD-1; the no-dash convention applies to app copy, not only to documents.
  - Owner-verified live, all eight: an assignment with a FUTURE date shows grayed and locked with no practice buttons, no submit control, and the "available on <date>" line; a PAST date and a BLANK date both behave exactly as before; the list is sorted by Available Date with the available-now assignments at the top; a legacy assignment still works and still shows its due date independently; the no-score placeholder is a plain hyphen; and the SERVER refusal was checked deliberately by leaving a student page open with the practice buttons showing, locking the assignment from another tab, and clicking Practice on the stale page, which was refused with "This assignment is not available until <date>."
  - New code: supabase/pd-3-available-date.sql (the column) and lib/available-date.ts (the shared rule). Edited: app/student/page.tsx (the available-date select, the nulls-first sort, the locked card, and the em dash fix), components/create-assignment-form.tsx and components/edit-assignment-form.tsx (the date picker), app/instructor/assignments/page.tsx (the select and the pass-through), and the four routes app/api/{session/start, voice/start, practice/submit, assignment/start} (the server refusal). No new dependencies.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece: any notification or email when an assignment unlocks; any change to due_at; and any change to the "Active Assignments" label above the Submissions dropdown, which is a different surface with no active or inactive state in the data (PD-1 correctly left it as a label). The grayed-lock styling is clean and legible but gets its visual finish in PD-8.

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-4: variable meeting length. Built and deployed live; all owner checks passed on https://relii.vercel.app. This is the fourth of the eight pre-demo pieces. ONE additive schema change (a single nullable column plus a range rule), run in production before the code went live; no route, Row Level Security, or Storage rule was touched, and no new dependency.
  - WHAT IT DOES: an instructor sets a Meeting length on an assignment, 5 to 30 minutes, and that is what the student's practice clock counts up to before the practice automatically ends. It replaces the single hardcoded 30 that Piece 5 shipped, so a short case can be a real 5 minute meeting instead of every role-play sharing one length. The clock is unchanged in every other way: it still starts at 00:00, still counts UP (it is not a countdown), and still ends the practice through the SAME routine the student's End button uses, landing on the results screen exactly as a manual end does. Only the moment it fires changed.
  - Schema (supabase/pd-4-meeting-length.sql, run in production). One column on assignments: duration_minutes, integer, NULLABLE, deliberately with NO default and NO not-null rule, plus a named check constraint (assignments_duration_minutes_range) saying the value must be null or between 5 and 30. A NULL duration MEANS 30 MINUTES, which is exactly how the app behaved before this piece, so every assignment that existed beforehand simply leaves it empty and keeps auto-ending at 30:00 with no backfill and no migration. Owner-verified after running the SQL that every live assignment came back with an empty duration_minutes. The check has to be added inside a DO block that looks for it by name first, because Postgres has no "add constraint if not exists"; that is what keeps the file safe to re-run, matching how every other SQL file in this project is written.
  - THE NUMBERS LIVE IN ONE FILE, lib/meeting-length.ts: the 5, the 30, the default of 30, and one helper (resolveMeetingMinutes) that turns whatever is stored into the minutes the clock should run. The helper can never return null and it clamps out-of-range values, which is the point: a practice ALWAYS has a working auto-end, so no data state can leave a practice running forever. The clock, the student session page, and both instructor forms all read from this one file, so retuning the range or the fallback is a one-line change in one place. This mirrors lib/available-date.ts from PD-3.
  - THE CLOCK IS STILL ONE CONTROL POINT AND PIECE 6 IS STILL READY. components/practice-clock.tsx now takes the auto-end as an autoEndSeconds prop threaded into the internal count-up hook, instead of reading a module constant. The wall-clock accumulator is untouched, so the clock is still accurate when the tab is backgrounded (owner-verified), and clockActive is still the single boolean per screen that Piece 6's pause will AND onto. No second timer was built anywhere.
  - THE COLOR THRESHOLDS WERE REMOVED, deliberately, and this is the one behavior PD-4 takes away. Piece 5 turned the text yellow at 10:00 and red at 15:00. Those are fixed marks, so on a 5 minute meeting they never fire and on a 10 minute meeting the yellow lands exactly at the end, which is a warning that arrives with the auto-end. A warning that only works at one meeting length is worse than none, so the text is now a single default gray for the whole meeting. YELLOW_AT_SECONDS, RED_AT_SECONDS, and the color logic are gone from the file rather than left dormant. If a proportional warning is ever wanted (for example the last fifth of the meeting, whatever its length), that is a PD-8 or later decision and the one clock file is still where it goes.
  - THE CLOCK NOW SHOWS ITS TARGET, "03:12 / 05:00" rather than "03:12". This was the optional item in the PD-4 brief and it was taken for two reasons. A 5 minute auto-end with no visible target is a genuine surprise mid-conversation, and it also made the legacy case testable: a legacy assignment's clock reading "/ 30:00" proves the null-means-30 fallback resolved correctly without anyone sitting through 30 minutes to watch it fire. The target is in a lighter gray beside the elapsed time, and the aria-label reads "Elapsed time 03:12 of a 05:00 meeting". Its visual finish, like everything else, is PD-8.
  - DATA FLOW, one read and one prop: app/student/session/[id]/page.tsx already loaded the assignment row to get the title for a locked submission, and it now selects duration_minutes on that same read and runs it for PRACTICES too (it previously only ran for submissions). It resolves the minutes server-side and passes them as a meetingMinutes prop to both components/conversation.tsx (text) and components/voice-conversation.tsx (voice+video), each of which hands them to the one existing PracticeClock. Both fall back to 30 if the prop is ever absent.
  - PRACTICE ONLY, unchanged from Piece 5. Both screens still render the clock only when the session is NOT a graded submission, so the variable length only ever affects practices. A submission renders no clock at all, and the value is simply unused there.
  - NO SERVER END-ROUTE CHANGE. The auto-end is client-side exactly as before: the clock fires onExpire, the screen sets expired, and the screen calls its own existing end routine, which POSTs the SAME /api/session/end the End button uses. That route has no 30-minute logic in it and was not touched. The dormant graded-submission lock window in lib/submission.ts (which also happens to be 30 minutes) is a completely separate mechanism and was deliberately not entangled with this.
  - Forms: both components/create-assignment-form.tsx and components/edit-assignment-form.tsx gained a "Meeting length (optional)" number field constrained to 5 through 30, sitting under Required mode, with the note that the practice ends automatically at this length and that blank means 30 minutes. Blank saves as null, and clearing it on the edit form sets it back to null. Both write the column straight from the browser under the existing "instructors manage assignments" rule, exactly the way they already write the two dates, so NO server route was needed. The browser's number input, a friendly check in the form, and the database constraint all say the same 5 to 30, so a bad value cannot get in by any route.
  - THE INSTRUCTOR CARD ALWAYS SHOWS THE LENGTH, including for an assignment that has none set, where it reads "30 min meeting". This is deliberately different from how the two dates behave (they are hidden when empty): an empty date genuinely means there is no such date, but an empty duration is not "no meeting length", it is 30 minutes, so hiding it would leave the instructor guessing what a blank field does. The card now reads like "Voice + video, 30 min meeting, available Aug 14, due Aug 20".
  - Owner-verified live: a 5 minute assignment auto-ended a practice at 05:00 in BOTH text and voice+video; a legacy null-duration assignment still reads 30:00; the timer text stayed one color throughout with no yellow at 10:00 and no red at 15:00; the count-up stayed accurate across a backgrounded tab; the form constrained to 5 through 30 and a blank saved as null; and the clock stayed a live practice-only element, absent from the results/replay screen, the instructor submission view, and the recording (which captures the webcam, not the app screen).
  - New code: supabase/pd-4-meeting-length.sql (the column and the range rule) and lib/meeting-length.ts (the shared numbers and the resolver). Edited: components/practice-clock.tsx (the autoEndSeconds prop, the target display, and the removal of the color thresholds), app/student/session/[id]/page.tsx (the duration read and the pass-down), components/conversation.tsx and components/voice-conversation.tsx (the meetingMinutes prop into the clock), components/create-assignment-form.tsx and components/edit-assignment-form.tsx (the length field), and app/instructor/assignments/page.tsx (the select and the pass-through). No new dependencies.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece: showing the meeting length on the STUDENT assignment card on the home page (owner's call, it belongs to PD-7's display and copy batch; the in-call "/ 05:00" already tells the student the length at the moment it matters); practice pause, which is still the deferred Piece 6; any change to /api/session/end; and any entanglement with the graded-submission lock window.
- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-5: Recording Length capture and display. Built and deployed live; all six owner checks passed on https://relii.vercel.app. This is the fifth of the eight pre-demo pieces. ONE additive schema change (a single nullable column plus a not-negative rule), run in production before the code went live; no Row Level Security or Storage rule was touched, and no new dependency.
  - WHAT IT DOES: every practice and every submission now carries how long IT ran, shown in five places under the exact label "Recording Length". It is always the length of that ONE session and never a cumulative total across a student's attempts; nothing anywhere adds two sessions together.
  - THE LOCKED RULE, which lives in lib/recording-length.ts so every screen agrees: when a video recording exists (a voice+video session with the camera ON) the number is the RECORDING'S OWN LENGTH. Otherwise (a text session, OR a voice+video session where the student declined the camera so there is no recording) it is the ELAPSED CONVERSATION TIME, start to end. A camera-off voice call shows elapsed time, never a blank. Modes are still only text and voice_video; "voice only" is not a separate mode, it is voice_video with the camera declined.
  - THE BUILD-TIME VERIFICATION THAT SET THE APPROACH. Three things were confirmed in the repo before any SQL was written. sessions had created_at, submitted_at and scored_at but NO completed_at or ended_at. /api/session/end wrote status 'completed' with no timestamp at all, so a CONVERSATION-ONLY session (which stops at 'completed' and is never scored) had no end moment recorded anywhere and its elapsed time was NOT derivable. And no recording duration was stored either: the video routes save only video_path, and the MediaRecorder never measured itself. A fourth thing was found during that check and is what decided the design: THE VOICE SCREEN HAS AN IDLE PRE-CALL STATE. The session row is created the moment the student clicks Practice (voice + video), and the page then sits on a "Start call" screen for as long as they like before the meeting begins.
  - WHY A STORED NUMBER AND NOT AN ended_at TIMESTAMP. Adding an end timestamp and subtracting created_at was considered and REJECTED, for two reasons. It is not the recording's length (the recorder starts a moment after the call connects, so the subtraction answers a different question than the locked rule asks), and because of the pre-call screen above it is not even the elapsed conversation time for a voice session: it would silently count the waiting, and the stored number would not match the count-up clock the student actually watched. One column holding the measured answer is both smaller and more accurate than a timestamp something else has to derive.
  - Schema (supabase/pd-5-recorded-seconds.sql, run in production). One column on sessions: recorded_seconds, integer, NULLABLE, deliberately with NO default and NO not-null rule, plus a named check constraint (sessions_recorded_seconds_not_negative). NULL means "no measured length", which is true of every session finished before this shipped and of any attempt that never reached its normal ending. Owner-verified after running the SQL that every existing practice and submission came back NULL. The check is added inside a DO block that looks for it by name first, matching how every other SQL file in this project is written and keeping the file safe to re-run.
  - WHERE THE NUMBER IS WRITTEN: /api/session/end, at the 'completed' transition. That one update was chosen because it is the single point BOTH screens and BOTH endings pass through, which is precisely what fixes the conversation-only gap found in the verification: a no-rubric session that stops at 'completed' and is never scored now gets a length like everything else. It is WRITE ONCE, guarded on the column still being null: ending can be retried (a scoring failure drops the student back to the live screen to click End again), and the first measurement is the honest one, so a retry must not re-derive and count the time spent failing to score.
  - HOW EACH SCREEN MEASURES. The VOICE screen measures and sends its own number, because neither measurement is knowable on the server: with the camera on it times the RECORDER itself, from recorder start to recorder stop, so the value is literally the clip's length; with the camera off it times from the call going LIVE to End. That live moment is the same instant PD-4's clock starts counting from (clockActive is status === "live"), so a camera-off call's stored length matches the clock the student was watching. The end moment is taken the instant End is pressed, before the upload and scoring wrap-up, since several seconds of uploading is not part of the meeting. The TEXT screen deliberately sends NOTHING and lets the server use now minus created_at: text has no pre-call gate, so that genuinely IS the elapsed conversation time, and unlike a browser timer it survives a text session being reloaded and resumed part way through (text reloads resume losslessly). The same server fallback covers any voice call that somehow sends no number.
  - LEGACY ROWS SHOW A DASH, not an estimate. Deriving scored_at minus created_at for old rows was considered and rejected by the owner: on an old voice session that number is inflated by however long the student sat on the "Start call" screen, and an honest blank beats a wrong length an instructor might rely on. The owner is curating fresh demo sessions, which all carry measured lengths.
  - THE FIVE PLACEMENTS, all reading one shared helper (recordingLengthText) so the wording and the mm:ss format cannot drift apart: app/student/page.tsx on each saved-practice row AND on the "Submitted ..." summary line (that card shows the submission as a line, not a table row); app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx at the top left directly under the scenario title; app/instructor/page.tsx as a COLUMN in the submissions table, placed between Mode and Score because the two read together ("V+V, 12:34"); and app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx as a line directly under the existing submitted date.
  - DATE TAKEN PARITY: the student's view of a practice or submission now shows the date the instructor view already showed, under the Recording Length on the results page. The formatting moved out of the instructor page into lib/date-taken.ts and BOTH pages import it, so the two can no longer drift. A submission shows submitted_at, identical to the instructor's value; a practice is never submitted, so it shows created_at, which is the same date the student home already lists it under.
  - PIECE 4 CONVERSION IS SAFE: /api/practice/submit changes only kind and submitted_at, so a practice converted into a graded submission KEEPS the length measured when it was practiced. No re-recording is needed for a submission to show a real number.
  - Owner-verified live, all six checks: a camera-on voice practice showed the recording's length on the practice row, the results page, the instructor column and the instructor submission view; a text practice showed elapsed time, not blank; a camera-OFF voice practice showed elapsed time, not blank; a conversation-only practice (the case that previously had no end timestamp at all) showed a sensible length; Date Taken appeared on the student results page matching the instructor view; and a student with several practices showed each one's own length, never a sum.
  - New code: supabase/pd-5-recorded-seconds.sql (the column and the rule), lib/recording-length.ts (the locked rule, the label, the mm:ss formatting, the dash, and the validation the server uses on what the browser sends), and lib/date-taken.ts (the shared date formatting). Edited: app/api/session/end/route.ts (accepts an optional recordedSeconds and stores the length write-once at the completed transition), components/voice-conversation.tsx (measures the recorder and the live call, sends the number), and the four display surfaces app/student/page.tsx, app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx, app/instructor/page.tsx, and app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx. No new dependencies. components/conversation.tsx (text) needed NO change, by design.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece: practice pause, still the deferred Piece 6; the student-home meeting-length display, which is PD-7; any change to scoring, the transcript, the recording upload, the retention cap, or the graded-submission lock window. The visual finish of the new column and the new lines is PD-8.
- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-6: student practice interactions. Built and deployed live; all owner checks passed on https://relii.vercel.app. This is the sixth of the eight pre-demo pieces. NO schema change, no new dependency, no server route touched, and no Row Level Security or Storage rule touched: both behaviors are screen-side only, in two components.
  - Two SMALL, INDEPENDENT behaviors bundled into one piece. They share nothing but the session screen: one is on the voice+video practice screen, the other is on the text practice screen, and neither can affect the other.
  - BEHAVIOR 1, THE HIDABLE LIVE TRANSCRIPT (voice+video). The live transcript window now sits under its own labeled bar reading "Live transcript", with a "Hide transcript" / "Show transcript" button on the right of that bar, directly above the transcript it controls. The transcript is SHOWN BY DEFAULT (owner decision), so nothing changes for a student who does not touch it; the button hides it and shows it again, as many times as they like. It exists because a student who wants to practice eye contact with the camera should be able to stop reading along, and it is the practice-screen half of the same idea PD-8 will style.
  - THE FIRST ATTEMPT AT THAT CONTROL FAILED THE OWNER'S CHECK, and the reason is worth keeping. It was originally a small gray text-xs button tucked at the right end of the red-dot status line, and on the live site the owner saw the transcript but could not find any control at all. The code was verified to be genuinely deployed (the production JS chunk on relii.vercel.app was fetched and confirmed to contain the button, and BOTH PD-6 behaviors compile into that ONE chunk, so the text-practice change passing proved the voice code was loaded too). So this was never a deploy or a rendering failure: the control was on screen and simply did not read as a control. The fix was to stop hiding it inside a line of status text and give it its own bordered bar with a label, using the same full-size button styling as the End session button. The general lesson for PD-8: a control that borrows the styling of adjacent status text is invisible in practice, however present it is in the DOM.
  - HIDING IS DISPLAY ONLY, AND THAT IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A PROMISE. The button sets ONE piece of screen state (transcriptShown) that is read in exactly two places, both of them layout: the button's own label, and whether the transcript list is drawn. The capture path never sees it. The transcript arrives over the OpenAI data channel into handleEvent, is assembled in turnsRef (a ref, so it is independent of what is rendered), and is pushed to the server by saveTranscript() turn by turn and once more right before ending. None of those functions reads transcriptShown or the screen at all, so hiding cannot pause, drop, or shorten the capture.
  - AND THE SCORE CANNOT MOVE EITHER, for the same structural reason: /api/session/end reads the transcript BACK FROM THE DATABASE (session.transcript) and scores that. The browser sends only the session id (plus PD-5's measured length). There is no path by which an on-screen toggle could reach the scorer.
  - When the transcript is hidden the space is not left blank: a short line explains that the conversation is still being captured and saved, that the score is not affected, and how to bring the transcript back. The hidden transcript is kept in the page (not thrown away and rebuilt), so showing it again returns the student to a full, correctly ordered conversation scrolled to the newest line.
  - The control appears in graded voice+video submissions as well as practice, which is deliberate and safe: it is the same display-only toggle, the saved transcript and score are untouched by it, and a graded submission's actual protections (the camera lock, the one-attempt rule, the lock window) are all elsewhere and unchanged.
  - BEHAVIOR 2, THE VERTICALLY EXPANDING TEXT INPUT (text practice). The message box was a single-line <input>, so a long message scrolled sideways and the student could not see what they had written. It is now a box that GROWS DOWNWARD as they type: it resizes on every keystroke to fit the content, up to a maximum of 160 pixels (about six lines, one named constant MAX_INPUT_HEIGHT_PX at the top of the file), and past that it stops growing and scrolls inside itself so it can never swallow the conversation above it. It also SHRINKS back, both after a message is sent and when the student deletes a line. The Send button is aligned to the bottom of the box so it stays put as the box grows.
  - SENDING WAS PRESERVED, and the current trigger was verified before changing anything: the old single-line field sat inside a form, so Enter already submitted it and the Send button submitted it too. Both still work identically. Because a multi-line box has to be told what Enter means, it now follows the standard chat convention that matches the old behavior: ENTER SENDS, SHIFT+ENTER STARTS A NEW LINE. The send logic itself was not rewritten, only lifted into a shared submitMessage() that the form and the Enter key both call, so the sending, the error handling, and the put-the-text-back-if-it-fails behavior are the exact same code as before. One addition: an Enter pressed while the browser is mid-composition (accents, or a Chinese/Japanese/Korean input method) does not send, so it finishes the character instead of firing off a half-typed word.
  - New code: none. Edited: components/voice-conversation.tsx (the transcriptShown state, the toggle button in the status row, and the hidden-state note) and components/conversation.tsx (the textarea with its resize effect, the constant, the Enter/Shift+Enter handler, and the submitMessage split). The lint and the production build both pass; the one lint error in the repo is a pre-existing one in components/submission-live-gate.tsx, untouched by this piece.
  - OWNER-VERIFIED LIVE, all checks passed. On voice+video practice: the transcript shows by default, the labeled bar's button hides it and shows it again, and after hiding it for a call the SAVED transcript and the score came out complete and unaffected, confirming the display-only claim end to end. On text practice: the box grows as several lines are typed, stops at the maximum and scrolls inside itself, Send still sends, and Enter sends while Shift+Enter makes a new line. Nothing changed for graded submissions, scoring, or transcript content. Note the sequence: the text half passed on the FIRST live check, and the voice half passed on the SECOND, after the control was rebuilt as its own labeled bar (see the note above about why the first attempt failed).
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece: practice pause, still the deferred Piece 6 (unchanged by this work, and both screens still have their single clockActive control point ready for it); any change to scoring, to the transcript's CONTENT, or to the session flow; and the visual finish of the toggle and the box, which is PD-8 (its exact placement, its styling, and the 160 pixel maximum are all tuning left for that pass).
- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-7: the display and copy batch. Built and deployed live to https://relii.vercel.app (commit 62fdb8a); ALL TWELVE OWNER CHECKS PASSED. This is the seventh of the eight pre-demo pieces and the LAST functional one: only PD-8, the visual polish pass, is left. NO schema change, no new dependency, no new file, no server route touched, and no Row Level Security or Storage rule touched. Three files were edited, all of them pages: app/student/page.tsx, app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx, and app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx. The production build passes.
  - ITEM 1, THE SCORE SURFACES: AUDITED, NOTHING TO CHANGE. Every place the app shows a score total already reads "achieved / total", so this item was a consistency check that came back clean rather than a fix. Nine surfaces were checked: the student results page, the student home practice rows, the student home Submitted line, the instructor submissions table, the instructor Average score tile, the instructor submission view, and three more found during the audit that were not on the brief's list (the submit-this-practice confirmation, the reset history's prior score, and the instructor CSV export).
