The two remaining instructor tiles are deliberately NOT score surfaces: the Submissions tile is a count of students ("3 / 25 enrolled") and Awaiting review is a plain count with no denominator to show. The itemized rows inside the rubric already showed points earned over points possible per item.
  - ITEM 2, THE HEADING RENAME, AND WHAT IT ACTUALLY SAID. The brief expected the heading to read "Rubric breakdown". It did not: that phrase existed only in a code comment at the top of the instructor submission page and in the mockups HTML. The REAL heading over the itemized rubric on both screens was "Itemized score", and that is what was renamed. It now reads "Rubric score" on the student results page and on the instructor submission view, so both audiences call the same list by the same name. IN-FLIGHT ADJUSTMENT, flagged to the owner: the brief wrote the new wording in lowercase ("rubric score"); it was shipped in sentence case, "Rubric score", to match every other heading on those two pages (Transcript, Your recording, Downloads). Changing it to all-lowercase is a one-word edit in two files if the owner wants the literal wording.
  - ITEM 3, THE PROCESSING NOTICE. The student home assignments area now carries one static line: "After you finish a practice or a submission, video and transcript processing may take up to three minutes." It sits directly under the "Your assignments" heading and above the list of assignment cards, so it covers the whole area rather than repeating on every card, and it is rendered only when the student actually has assignments (the empty state stays a single clean sentence). It is STATIC TEXT: nothing here measures, polls, or waits on the real processing, and no timing behavior changed. Its visual finish is PD-8.
  - ITEM 4, THE MEETING LENGTH ON THE STUDENT CARD (the PD-4 carry-forward). The student home assignment card now shows the meeting length on its detail line, in the same shape and position the instructor's assignment card uses ("Discovery-Close (with Alex) - voice + video required - 30 min meeting - due Aug 14"). This needed a QUERY CHANGE, which is the part worth remembering: duration_minutes reached the session page (where the clock runs) but was never fetched by the student home, so the column was added to that page's select and to its AssignmentRow type. The number itself is resolved through the existing lib/meeting-length.ts helper, NOT recomputed, so a null duration shows 30 exactly as the clock and the instructor card resolve it, and the card can never disagree with the clock the student will actually see. The length is always shown, including on a practice-only assignment, because a null is not "no meeting length", it is the standard 30 minutes.
  - OWNER-VERIFIED LIVE, all twelve checks passed, run as a signed-in student and a signed-in instructor. As the student: the meeting length shows on the assignment card, the processing notice appears once above the cards, the saved practice rows and the Submitted line both read "achieved / total", and on an opened result the big total reads "achieved / total" with the heading above the itemized list reading "Rubric score". As the instructor: the submissions table's Score column and the Average score tile both read "achieved / total", and the submission view shows the same total shape with the same "Rubric score" heading. The cross-account check also passed: an assignment with NO meeting length set reads "30 min meeting" on the instructor card AND on the student card, which is what proves the student card resolves the same value the practice clock counts up to rather than holding a second copy of the number. The sentence-case wording "Rubric score" was accepted as shipped. Before that check, the production build compiled clean, the diff was reviewed line by line and touched only the three pages, and the deployment was confirmed live (the login page's etag changed and the CDN cache reset after the push).
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece: practice pause, still the deferred Piece 6; any behavior, route, Row Level Security, or Storage change; and all visual finish (the spacing and styling of the notice, where exactly the meeting length sits on the line, and the weight of the renamed headings), which is PD-8.
- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-8a: the shared visual layer (the foundation half of the visual polish pass). Built and deployed live to https://relii.vercel.app (commit 37af7f6). NO schema change, no copy change, no layout rework, no new dependency, and no behavior, route, Row Level Security, or Storage change. The production build passes.
  - WHY IT WAS SPLIT, and the one thing that governs both halves: the app's visual inconsistency was a MISSING SHARED LAYER, not a per-screen problem. There was nothing shared to be consistent with, so all 92-plus container class strings, all the button recipes, and all the heading sizes were invented per screen. PD-8a builds the layer and proves it on the shared chrome; PD-8b applies it to the rest and makes each screen logical. Doing it screen by screen would have produced the same inconsistency in nicer clothes.
  - THE FONT BUG, which was the single highest-value change in the piece. app/layout.tsx has loaded Geist and Geist Mono from Google Fonts since Stage 1 and exposed them as --font-geist-sans and --font-geist-mono, but app/globals.css ended with `body { font-family: Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif }`, which overrode them. The app downloaded a font it never rendered, on every page, for the whole project. The body rule is gone and the two variables are wired through --font-sans and --font-mono in the @theme inline block, which is what Tailwind's own base styles read. Verified on the live site rather than by reading the CSS: the deployed stylesheet resolves --default-font-family to var(--font-geist-sans), the <html> element carries the class that defines it, the Geist woff2 is served (HTTP 200), and a headless render of https://relii.vercel.app/login shows Geist letterforms.
  - THE TOKENS, taken from docs/Relii-Core-Screen-Mockups.html and living in app/globals.css. This project is Tailwind v4, so they are in the CSS-first @theme block, NOT a tailwind.config.js (there is no such file and one should not be added). Three namespaces, named so a screen says what a colour is FOR rather than repeating a hex code: surface-primary/secondary/tertiary plus surface-info/danger/success/warning (backgrounds), content-primary/secondary/tertiary plus content-info/danger/success/warning (text; called "content" so the class reads text-content-secondary rather than text-text-secondary), and line-tertiary/secondary (borders). Plus --radius-md 8px and --radius-lg 12px, and a three-step heading scale (--text-page-title 24px, --text-section 20px, --text-subsection 18px).
  - TWO TOKEN DECISIONS WORTH KNOWING. First, re-stating --radius-md and --radius-lg re-points every rounded-md and rounded-lg ALREADY WRITTEN anywhere in the app at the mockup's radii, with no file needing to be edited; that is why corners changed everywhere from one two-line change. Second, the heading scale values were deliberately set equal to the sizes the app already used most (24 / 20 / 18), so applying it app-wide was visually almost a no-op: the only headings that actually moved were the four instructor destination titles (Submissions, Manage Assignments, Roster, Invite Users) that were stuck at body size while the other five instructor screens were at 20px. PD-1's h1-to-h2 hierarchy decision is preserved.
  - THE PAGE BACKGROUND moved from white to the mockup's cream (#faf9f5) with cards white on top of it. The color-scheme: light pin from Stage 4 and the comment explaining it are untouched. Contrast was measured rather than assumed before shipping: secondary text on cream is 6.2:1 and tertiary is 3.1:1, both BETTER than the text-gray-400 and text-gray-500 they replace on white. A new @media print rule forces the printed page back to white with dark text, and the student results page's print view was verified to still come out clean.
  - THE PRIMITIVES, in components/ui (a new folder, which is where PD-8b should look first). Button, with variants primary / secondary / destructive / caution, sizes xs / sm / md, a fullWidth flag, and a companion buttonClasses() for links that must look like buttons. Card, with tones default / muted / info / success / warning / danger, paddings none / sm / md / lg, an `as` prop (div, li, section, article) so an assignment card is a real <li>, and a companion cardClasses() for elements that already exist. Neither file carries "use client", so both work in a server-rendered page and inside an interactive client component. Button deliberately does NOT default its `type`, because a <button> inside a <form> submits by default and several forms in this app rely on exactly that.
  - THE VARIANTS ARE DERIVED, NOT INVENTED. They are the recipes already written by hand across nearly forty controls: bg-black (30 uses) became primary, the border-gray-300 outline became secondary, bg-red-600 became destructive, and bg-amber-600 (the submission reset / allow-retake control) became caution. bg-gray-800 (9 uses) had no distinct job and folds into primary or secondary case by case in PD-8b.
  - ONE DELIBERATE DEVIATION FROM THE MOCKUPS, recorded so PD-8b can revisit it: the mockup's primary button is a SOFT BLUE (info background, info text, info border), not a solid dark button. PD-8a's primary is bg-content-primary, the tokenized version of today's black. The reason is the gap between the two sessions: 8a converts only the chrome, so a blue primitive would have left the app with blue buttons on the chrome and black buttons everywhere else across the Aug 3 demo. Switching to the mockup's blue is one line in components/ui/button.tsx once every screen uses the primitive.
  - WHERE THE PRIMITIVES WERE APPLIED (the chrome only, by design): app/instructor/layout.tsx (the header), components/instructor-nav.tsx (the five-destination nav), components/sign-out-button.tsx, the assignment cards on app/student/page.tsx and app/instructor/assignments/page.tsx, and the practice and start-assignment controls inside those cards. The live session screens, the results page, and the submission views kept everything EXCEPT their heading sizes; they are PD-8b.
  - COMMITTED THE MOCKUPS. docs/Relii-Core-Screen-Mockups.html was untracked and living in one working copy; it is now in git, because PD-8b's brief points at it. Standing rule for it: it is the source for LAYOUT AND VISUAL LANGUAGE ONLY, never for copy. The shipped app supersedes it on wording (it still says "Rubric breakdown", which PD-7 renamed to "Rubric score"). It also has only five frames (the student home, the two session modes, the student results page, and one instructor screen that predates PD-1's restructure), so four of the five instructor destinations are not covered by it at all. Its icons are the Tabler webfont and were ignored on purpose: no icon library is being added.
  - THE LAYOUT PROBLEM LIST FOR PD-8b, recorded from an actual sweep of the code during this piece and NOT fixed here, deliberately, so 8b lands as one coherent pass. Information hierarchy: the student assignment card leads with detail before context, and its most important control is often the least prominent thing on it; the instructor Submissions page puts three summary tiles above the table when the table is the reason the page exists; the results page shows the total score in a gray box below the header rather than as the page's subject; the roster's three groups are separated only by small gray h3s. Inconsistent patterns doing the same job: SIX different confirmation flows with six different looks (delete student is a red outline trigger, un-invite a gray one, delete assignment a small gray one leading to a second red type-DELETE step, submission reset an amber one, delete practice a bare text link, discard-all its own thing); the submissions table and the roster lists are both "a list of people" and behave nothing alike; empty states range from a bordered box to a bare gray sentence. Invisible-control candidates, which is the PD-6 failure mode of a control styled like the status text beside it: the practice Delete in components/delete-practice-button.tsx is text-gray-400, the exact colour of the date metadata next to it, and is the strongest single case in the app; the Video / Transcript / CSV download links in the instructor submissions table are text-xs text-gray-500, styled as metadata rather than as actions, while "View" beside them is blue; the "Compare" link on the scaffolding page is a plain underlined line inside a paragraph of gray text. Reads as assembled rather than designed: PD-1 created the five instructor destinations by RELOCATING sections out of one long page verbatim, so they have never been designed as a set, and the mockups cover only one of them; the scaffolding chain, edit, and compare sub-pages each invented their own page header; roughly 39 elements still use bg-gray-50, a COOL gray that now sits on a warm cream page and reads slightly blue (harmless but visible, and the clearest single marker of which screens 8b has not reached yet).
  - VERIFICATION DONE: a full `npm run build` passes (not just a typecheck, which is how PD-1 caught a client/server boundary error in this same nav component). Lint shows exactly one error, the pre-existing one in components/submission-live-gate.tsx that predates this work and was left alone. The font, the tokens, the radii, and the print rule were each confirmed in the DEPLOYED stylesheet on relii.vercel.app, and the login page and the full chrome (instructor header, nav, both assignment cards, the locked card, the notice, the saved-practices rows) were rendered headlessly against that deployed stylesheet and checked. NOT done by the builder and left for the owner: clicking through the signed-in student flow and the five instructor destinations, which needs a login.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece: every screen other than the chrome; all copy; any layout rework (the look changed, the layouts did not, and nothing was moved to match mockup composition); the invisible-control scan, the video duration fix, and the generic-wording change; and practice pause, still the deferred Piece 6.
- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-8b: the global conversion (the second of the three PD-8 sessions). Built and deployed live to https://relii.vercel.app (commit 3568162). 38 files edited, 1 file added. NO schema change, no copy change, no layout change, no behaviour, route, Row Level Security or Storage change, and no new dependency. The production build passes.
  - WHY THIS SESSION EXISTED. PD-8a was scoped as plumbing and it worked, but after it shipped the only visible change on the instructor screens was the page background, because 8a converted the chrome and left the content alone. The measured gap: 205 hand-written text-gray-* instances, 52 bg-gray-*, 65 border-gray-300, and 41 form controls with no primitive at all. The 8a handoff had counted BACKGROUNDS (52) and not TEXT, and text is four times larger and far more visible because it is on every screen at every size. That undercount is the whole reason 8a looked like it did nothing.
  - TOKEN FIX 1, A REAL ACCESSIBILITY BUG. content-tertiary was #888780, which measures 3.21:1 on a panel, 3.42 on the page and 3.61 on a card. That token is documented as the one for metadata, email addresses and small captions, which is body-size text, and WCAG 2.1 AA requires 4.5:1 for body text. So it FAILED AA on every surface in the app. It is now #6e6d66, which measures 4.63 / 4.93 / 5.20 and is the first step darkening along the token's own hue that clears AA everywhere, so the palette keeps its warmth. Relii goes into a public university, where AA is a procurement requirement rather than a preference. Worth noting how this was missed: the PD-8a comment in globals.css estimated "tertiary 3.1:1" and shipped anyway, so the figure was seen but the conclusion was not drawn. That comment now carries the real per-surface numbers for all three text tokens. Every other token in the layer already passed.
  - TOKEN FIX 2, THE PRIMARY BUTTON. It is now white on the solid accent #185fa5 (6.52:1), instead of the near-black PD-8a shipped. The fill and its hover shade are new tokens, accent-solid and accent-solid-hover (#134c84, 8.77:1), so the LAYER owns the colour rather than the button variant. This is a deliberate deviation from the mockups, which use a pale blue button: PD-8a's own sweep found that the most important control on a screen is routinely the least prominent thing on it, and a pale primary works against fixing that. It answers the open question PD-8a recorded, and the switch was made BEFORE the sweep so no screen was ever half-converted.
  - THE FORM CONTROL PRIMITIVE, components/ui/field.tsx, was the centrepiece, because form controls turned out to be the most repeated pattern in the app and its biggest unconverted surface. It holds Input, Select, Textarea, FileInput, Label and HelpText, each with a matching classes() export, following the same conventions as button.tsx and card.tsx (no "use client", so they work in a server-rendered page and inside a client component alike). The look is derived rather than invented: the recipe the app already used most, re-expressed in tokens. THE ONE THING GENUINELY ADDED IS A VISIBLE FOCUS STATE, which almost none of these controls had, so before this a keyboard user could tab through the instructor forms with nothing on screen telling them where they were.
  - TWO SMALL DESIGN CALLS INSIDE THAT PRIMITIVE, both recorded because they changed how something looks. First, the app had TWO label recipes, a medium-weight one on the login, register and name screens and a lighter gray one on the instructor forms; they are now one, so the instructor labels read a little darker and heavier than before. That is the intended fix, and it is the specific thing the owner could see was wrong: a form label is not metadata. Second, the components take ComponentPropsWithRef rather than the plain attribute types, so `ref` passes through like any other prop, which is what lets the text practice message box keep its auto-grow.
  - THE SWEEPS, all landing at ZERO across every tracked file: text-gray-* 205 to 0, bg-gray-* 52 to 0, border-gray-300 65 to 0, border-gray-* overall 123 to 0, divide-gray-* 1 to 0. Mapped by role, not by shade: 300 and 400 to content-tertiary, 500 and 600 to content-secondary, 700 / 800 / 900 to content-primary, the gray-50 and gray-100 panels to surface-secondary, and the border shades to the two line tokens. Status colours went the same way (red, green, amber and blue text and backgrounds onto the danger, success, warning and info tokens), with each pairing contrast-checked BEFORE the swap: danger on danger 6.1:1, warning on warning 5.9:1, success on success 5.4:1. 52 hand-written buttons became the Button primitive, 12 links and anchors took buttonClasses(), and 25 hand-written boxes became Card. The most visible single consequence is that the gray-50 panels, which were a COOL gray sitting on a warm cream page and read faintly blue, now match the page.
  - THE HAZARD, AND HOW IT WAS HANDLED. Converting a button must never introduce a type attribute: a <button> inside a <form> submits by default, several forms here rely on that, and components/conversation.tsx has four buttons with no explicit type, so a helpful addition there could have stopped the text practice screen sending messages with the build still passing. The conversion was scripted to carry every existing attribute across verbatim and add nothing, and afterwards every changed file was audited to confirm it carries EXACTLY the same number of type attributes as before. Zero drift.
  - BEHAVIOURS CONFIRMED INTACT: the PD-6 hidable transcript (shown by default, its own labeled bar) and the PD-6 auto-growing message box, which keeps its ref, its keydown handler, its Enter-sends / Shift+Enter-newline logic and its 160px cap because it takes only its LOOK from the primitive's classes() export rather than being swapped for the component; the PD-4 clock reading elapsed over target and its aria-label; all PD-7 copy; and the print:hidden and print:grid-cols-1 rules on the student results page.
  - VERIFICATION DONE: a full `npm run build`, not just a typecheck. Lint is back to exactly one error, the known pre-existing one in components/submission-live-gate.tsx, which was left alone because it is a setState-in-effect issue about the live-attempt marker with nothing to do with styling. The tokens, the accent, the compiled focus ring and the print rule were each confirmed in the DEPLOYED stylesheet. The live login page was rendered headlessly (accent button, tokenised controls, Geist). The signed-in screens cannot be reached without a login, so the instructor Create Assignment form, the Submissions tiles and table, a strong-confirm panel, and the student results page were rendered against the DEPLOYED stylesheet using the real converted class strings; the focus ring was confirmed visible that way too. The results page print view was re-verified: white background, the save-a-copy bar hidden, the grid collapsed to one column. STILL NEEDS THE OWNER'S CLICK-THROUGH: the signed-in student flow and the five instructor destinations.
  - INVENTORY 1, WHAT IS STILL HAND-WRITTEN, and it is all deliberate. Nine raw <button> elements: five confirmation-flow TRIGGERS that keep their light outline look (delete student, delete assignment, discard all, and the two submission reset controls), and four controls that render as plain text (three in delete-practice-button, one "Remove" in edit-candidate-form). Sixteen light status BORDERS with no token to map to: border-red-300 (6), border-amber-300 (7), border-amber-200 (2), border-green-300 (1). One bg-red-500, the live recording indicator dot, left bright on purpose because content-danger is a deep red that would read as decoration rather than a signal. Seventeen raw form elements, but every one of them takes its styling from controlClasses() through a shared constant, except the camera checkbox in voice-conversation.tsx, which is deliberately not given the text-box treatment.
  - INVENTORY 2, CONTROLS THAT STILL READ AS TEXT (the PD-6 failure mode, for PD-8c). The role-based sweep PRESERVES this problem by design, because mapping text-gray-400 to content-tertiary keeps a control exactly the colour of the metadata beside it. Twenty-five controls render without a border, background or padding box. Most are legitimate inline links with a permanent underline (the Back to X links, Roster, Invite Users, Sign in, Create your account). The genuine candidates are the ones with only a hover underline or none at all: components/delete-practice-button.tsx line 57, the practice "Delete", which has NO underline and is the exact colour of the date next to it and remains the clearest case in the app; the Video / Transcript / CSV download links in the instructor submissions table (app/instructor/page.tsx lines 354, 361, 367), styled as metadata while "View" beside them is coloured; "Review" on the student home (app/student/page.tsx line 448); the confirm and cancel pair in delete-practice-button (lines 41 and 48); "Remove" in edit-candidate-form (line 216); and "Compare" on the scaffolding page (line 74).
  - INVENTORY 3, A CURIOSITY WORTH KNOWING. A handful of gray utility rules are still emitted into the shipped CSS. Nothing in the markup uses them: Tailwind v4 scans .md files as well as code, and the only remaining references anywhere are the PROJECT_STATE.md prose describing this very work plus one documentation comment in field.tsx. The app's own history file is now the only thing keeping those rules in the bundle. Harmless, and not worth chasing.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece, all of it PD-8c: every hierarchy and layout decision (the student assignment card, the results page score, the instructor Submissions tiles, the roster groups); the six confirmation flows; the invisible controls above; the video duration fix; the Bunzl placeholder in components/create-assignment-form.tsx; and all copy. Practice pause is still the deferred Piece 6.

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-8c: the structure pass (the third of the PD-8 sessions). Built and deployed live to https://relii.vercel.app (commits 6c7ed6a and 5f1fbde). 48 files edited, 3 files added, 1 dependency added. NO schema change, no copy change, no behaviour, route, Row Level Security or Storage change. The production build passes.
  - WHY THIS SESSION EXISTED. After PD-8b the owner compared the live app against docs/Relii-Core-Screen-Mockups.html and said the buttons had changed colour but nothing resembled the better organised layout of the mockups. That was correct, and it was not a failure of 8a or 8b: both did exactly what they were scoped to do. The gap was that THREE things had been scoped out of every session so far. Density and type scale, because 8a deliberately set the heading scale equal to sizes already in use so it would be near-invisible, which meant type density had never been touched at all. The page container, because nobody had ever picked one. And icons, which 8a explicitly cut. 8a and 8b converted COLOUR; this session converted STRUCTURE.
  - THE PAGE CONTAINER, MEASURED RATHER THAN CHOSEN. The app had grown SIX unrelated page widths (max-w-5xl, 3xl, 2xl, 6xl, md, sm). They are now one token, --container-page at 56rem (896px), and all six are gone. The mockup uses 720px, but that was a trap worth checking: its submissions table was drawn with SIX columns, before Recording Length existed (PD-5) and before the Actions cell grew, while the live table has SEVEN. Rendered at the new density with deliberately long names, emails and assignment titles, that table stands 176px tall at 720px, 161px from 800 to 880, and settles at 146px from 896px on, with no further improvement at any greater width. 896 is therefore the exact point where it stops wrapping, which makes it the narrowest honest page width. Login and register keep a narrower form INSIDE the shared container, which is a different thing from six unrelated page widths.
  - THE DENSITY AND TYPE SCALE, the largest visible change in the piece and the one that actually closes the gap the owner saw. The scale is now the mockup's own, by role: page-title 22, stat 22, wordmark 18, card-title 14, section 13, body 13, meta 12, micro 11. The thing to understand about it is how FLAT it is. There is no 20px or 18px heading step any more, because the mockup separates levels by WEIGHT and COLOUR far more than by size, and that is what lets a whole screen sit in one dense column. Buttons, form controls and cards came down with it (button 13px at 6 by 12 padding, card padding 14px instead of 16 and 20), and the submissions table went from 14px cells with 12px padding to 12px cells and 11px headers with 6px padding.
  - ONE HIERARCHY COMPLAINT WAS ANSWERED BY DENSITY, NOT BY MOVING ANYTHING. PD-8a flagged that the Submissions page puts three summary tiles above the table when the table is why the page exists. The mockup ALSO puts the tiles above the table; the only difference was that its tiles are small and flat. So the tiles did NOT move. They became borderless cream panels, which is why Card gained a `bordered` flag. Worth remembering for PD-8d: check whether a hierarchy complaint is really a density complaint before reordering anything.
  - CONTRAST AT THE NEW SMALL SIZES. AA requires 4.5:1 for text under 18px, which now includes everything except the page title, so the whole palette had to hold at 11px and 12px. It does: PD-8b left content-tertiary at 4.63 to 5.20 and content-secondary at 5.8 to 6.5. One deliberate deviation from the mockup: its table headers are tertiary, and ours are SECONDARY, because a column header carries real meaning and should not be the quietest thing on the page. Tertiary is kept for genuinely decorative captions.
  - ICONS, reversing PD-8a's cut. @tabler/icons-react (3.46.0), NOT the CDN webfont the mockups link. A CDN icon font is an external runtime request, so a slow or blocked network in the demo room would render every icon as an empty box in front of the stakeholder; a bundled package cannot fail that way. TREE-SHAKING VERIFIED BY MEASUREMENT, not assumption: the client chunks went from 1052k to 1056k, so the two icons used in CLIENT components plus the shared component factory cost about 4KB out of a 66MB unpacked package. Every other icon sits in a SERVER component, so it renders into the HTML as inline SVG and costs the browser no JavaScript at all. Next.js already lists this package in its default optimizePackageImports, so no config change was needed.
  - THE TWO ICON RULES, which matter more than which icons were picked, and which live in components/ui/icon.tsx rather than at each call site. An icon that IS the control uses <ActionIcon>, which REQUIRES a label and renders both aria-label (for a screen reader) and a title (so a sighted user gets a tooltip). An icon beside its own text label uses <Icon>, which is aria-hidden so a screen reader does not announce the label twice. And icon-only is only for unambiguous actions: the Actions column's Video, Transcript and CSV are three DIFFERENT downloads, so three bare icons there would be a guessing game, and they are icon plus text.
  - THE APP SHELL. The instructor surface used to open with a 24px "Instructor dashboard" title, then the name, then the email, then the nav, and only then the page's own heading, so the page you had actually navigated to announced itself FOURTH. It is now a small Relii wordmark in the accent colour with a lightweight qualifier beside it, the user block compact on the right, and nav below, which makes the page heading the first real thing on the page. The student side gets the same shell (components/app-header.tsx) so the two surfaces read as one product. Presentation ONLY: PD-1's five destinations, their routes and their active-state behaviour are untouched, the name gate and the requireInstructor check are exactly where they were in app/instructor/layout.tsx, and sign out is still a real button rather than something behind a menu.
  - A THIRD PRIMITIVE, Badge (components/ui/badge.tsx), which PD-8a's handoff predicted and the density pass is what finally justified. The mockup says every short status as a pill, and at 12px a bare coloured word reads as just another sentence fragment in a row. Used for the submissions Status column and the practice-row scores.
  - INVISIBLE CONTROLS: ONE OF FIVE RESOLVED, AND THE HONEST DETAIL MATTERS. The instructor Actions links (Video / Transcript / CSV) ARE resolved: they now carry icons, sit under a "View" that has an eye icon and the accent colour, and read as a group of actions rather than three metadata words. The other four are NOT fixed and were deliberately not touched: delete-practice-button.tsx line 59 (still the exact colour of the date beside it, with no underline at all), the "Review" link on the student home, "Remove" in edit-candidate-form.tsx, and "Compare" on the scaffolding page. TWO OF THOSE GOT RELATIVELY WORSE, which PD-8d should know: on the student practice row the score became a Badge, so "Review" and "Delete" now sit beside something with real visual weight and read as even quieter than they did before.
  - VERIFICATION DONE: a full `npm run build`, which a new dependency makes non-optional. Lint is unchanged at exactly one error, the known pre-existing setState-in-effect issue in components/submission-live-gate.tsx. The type-attribute discipline PD-8b established held: zero drift, audited file by file. The tokens and the container were confirmed in the DEPLOYED stylesheet, and the live login page was rendered headlessly. The signed-in screens cannot be reached without a login, so the new shell, the nav, the flat tiles, the seven-column table with its pills and icon actions, the student home, and the results page were all rendered against the DEPLOYED stylesheet using the real class strings and the REAL Tabler SVG. The results page print view was re-verified carefully, because density affects print more than anything else here: white background, the back link and the save-a-copy bar and the recording all hidden, the grid collapsed to one column, and noticeably more fits on a page than before. STILL NEEDS THE OWNER'S CLICK-THROUGH: the signed-in student flow and the five instructor destinations.
  - BEHAVIOURS CONFIRMED INTACT: the PD-6 hidable transcript and its labeled bar, and the PD-6 auto-growing message box with its ref, keydown handler, Enter-sends / Shift+Enter-newline logic and 160px cap; the PD-4 clock reading elapsed over target and its aria-label; all PD-7 copy; and every print rule.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece, all of it PD-8d: the hierarchy items that density did not answer, the six confirmation flows unified, the four remaining invisible controls, the video duration fix, and the Bunzl placeholder in components/create-assignment-form.tsx. Practice pause is still the deferred Piece 6.

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-8d: the screen-building pass (the fourth of the PD-8 sessions). Built and deployed live to https://relii.vercel.app (commit eb22c6f). 12 files edited, 3 files added, 1 file deleted. NO schema, route, Row Level Security or Storage change, and no new dependency. The production build passes.
  - WHY THIS SESSION WAS A DIFFERENT KIND OF WORK, and the lesson to carry forward. PD-8a, 8b and 8c were each briefed on a PROPERTY: tokens, then colour, then density and container and icons. A property is something you apply to whatever markup already exists. The owner looked at the result three times and said the app still was not as logical as the mockups, and he was right each time. The reason is that the mockups differ from this app in STRUCTURE and CONTENT, not in styling, so no amount of property conversion can reach them: nobody had ever been asked to build a specific screen against a specific frame. This session did that for three of the five frames. If a future session hears "it still does not look like the design" after a styling pass, this is why.
  - A REAL CONTAINER BUG, FOUND BY MEASURING RATHER THAN BY READING THE SOURCE. The owner reported that the page content looked narrower and inset relative to the header and nav above it. Reading the code that is impossible, because both use the same classes, and it would have been easy to dismiss as a perception issue. It is real. `body` carries `flex flex-col`, and an `mx-auto` on a flex item in a COLUMN flex container overrides `align-items: stretch` and makes the item shrink to fit its own content. So `max-w-page` never actually applied ANYWHERE, and neither did the six widths before it. Measured at a 1440px viewport: the header and nav wrapper rendered 178px wide and the page content 172px, with the nav starting at x=655 and the page heading at x=658. That 3px inset is exactly what he could see. Adding `w-full` to every page container fixes it: both become exactly 896px and every left edge lands on 296. This had been latent since Stage 1 and is probably the single largest reason nothing had resembled the mockup's tidy column. THE LESSON: when the owner reports something visual that the source says is impossible, measure the rendered page before disagreeing.
  - THE COLLAPSIBLE CATEGORY RUBRIC (components/rubric-score.tsx) was the centrepiece, and it is SHARED by the student results page and the instructor submission view so the two views of one submission cannot drift. Both used to render every scored item as a flat card in one column, ignoring structure the rubric has carried since Stage 3: every item already had its category, and the screen simply never used it. Now there is a chip row per category (Intro 26/30), a collapsible row per category with its earned over possible on the right, items nested with their feedback beneath, the first category open and the rest collapsed. No schema change, no new data, no new query.
  - THE PRINT TRAP, which is the thing that would have shipped broken. Student print-to-PDF is a shipped feature, and a collapsed category prints as a heading with nothing under it, so a student saving their results would have got six headings and no feedback at all. Collapsed content therefore STAYS IN THE DOM as `hidden print:block`, and the chevrons are `print:hidden` so a printed sheet never shows a collapsed chevron above expanded content. This was verified by actually forcing print, not by reasoning: the real component was rendered to HTML with the real 29-item rubric and a realistic 18-item discovery score, dropped into the real built stylesheet with `@media print` forced on, and screenshotted. All six categories came out expanded with every item and every comment.
  - WHY NOT <details>, recorded because it is the obvious question. It was the first choice and it is genuinely better: server-rendered, no JavaScript, keyboard accessible for free. It was rejected for one concrete reason: CSS CANNOT FORCE IT OPEN, because the `open` attribute is the only thing that reveals its content, so the print requirement above could not be met. The chip jump also has to OPEN its target before scrolling, since landing on a closed row is worse than having no chip, and that needs state too. Accessibility is kept by hand instead: each category header is a real button carrying aria-expanded and aria-controls, verified to resolve to a real panel id.
  - TWO DETAILS IN THAT COMPONENT WORTH KNOWING. Chip labels come from an EXPLICIT map (Intro, Facts, Needs, Presentation, Closing, Style), not a truncation, so renaming a category can never produce a nonsense chip; an unmapped name falls through to itself. And category totals are computed from the items ACTUALLY SCORED, not from the rubric's full point values. That matters and it is visible: today's scorer only scores the content items for the meeting (18 of 29 on a discovery run), so the chips correctly read Presentation 3/20 rather than 3/60, and a category with nothing scored does not appear at all instead of showing a misleading 0 of 60.
  - THE RESULTS SCREEN now leads with the score. It sits top right at stat size with the total beside it and the percentage below in the success colour, instead of in a grey box below the header, so the page answers "how did I do" before "who and when". The mode is a Badge, PD-5's Recording Length and Date Taken fold into one header line instead of three stacked ones, and Back to home became a small accent link rather than a bordered button competing with the page's real actions. The explanatory line about delivery items being added later was KEPT, restyled, because it is what tells a student why the total is out of what it is.
  - THE STUDENT HOME got the mockup's card composition, a status pill, and the owner's addition: a PER-ROW DOWNLOAD, which did not exist anywhere before. A student could not save a practice without first opening its results page. It reuses the results-page logic, now shared in lib/recording-download.ts rather than written twice, and the signed links are minted server-side in ONE batch call after the same ownership check the results page already makes, so no Storage rule changes and no route was added. Where there is no recording (a text practice, or a voice practice with the camera declined) the control renders DISABLED with an explanation rather than hidden, so the row keeps its shape and never offers a file that does not exist.
  - THE PRACTICE ROW'S THREE ACTIONS BECAME ONE GROUP, which is why components/delete-practice-button.tsx no longer exists. Review, Download and Delete now share components/practice-row-actions.tsx and one look: a right-aligned cluster of small bordered icon buttons, each an ActionIcon carrying a real accessible name and a hover tooltip. This was deliberately treated as ONE design decision rather than three patches: the old Delete was the app's clearest invisible control (the exact colour of the date beside it, with no underline at all), and PD-8c had made Review and Delete read as quieter still by giving the score beside them a Badge. Note the trade recorded here: the words Review and Delete are no longer visible text, they are accessible names and tooltips on unambiguous glyphs.
  - THE INSTRUCTOR TABLE took the mockup's fixed layout, with explicit widths set for the REAL seven columns rather than the six the mockup was drawn with, so Recording Length keeps its shipped label on one line and Actions has room for its group. The Actions cell became one coherent group instead of four loose things and a button: View leads, and the three downloads sit beneath it behind a hairline as a labelled cluster, so a reader sees "open this" and "take a copy" as two ideas. The EMPTY STATE, which the owner flagged specifically, is now a real empty state rather than a lone thin card beside three tiles of zeros; the tiles stay, because they are still informative at zero. The resets line, which used to float below the page as bare unstyled text, is now the same bordered disclosure in both its empty and non-empty states.
  - SHIPPED COPY BEAT THE MOCKUPS throughout, per the standing rule. The card's status pill says "Saved practices: 2 of 3", the app's own wording, not the mockup's "Practiced 2 of 3"; and rather than having the same fact in two places, that string MOVED into the pill and was removed from below.
  - VERIFICATION DONE: a full `npm run build`. Lint unchanged at exactly one error, the known pre-existing setState-in-effect issue in components/submission-live-gate.tsx. Type-attribute discipline held for a third session: zero drift on every PRE-EXISTING file (the two new components author their own buttons with an explicit type, which is correct rather than drift). The print view was force-verified as described above. Every chip target was confirmed in a real browser to resolve to a real element and to flip its panel from display:none to display:block. PD-3's locked card still renders with no practice, Start or submit control; PD-6, PD-4 and all shipped copy are intact. The print proof was then re-run against the LIVE deployed stylesheet, not just the local build, and came out identical. STILL NEEDS THE OWNER'S CLICK-THROUGH, as always: the signed-in student flow and the five instructor destinations.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece, all of it PD-8e: the six confirmation flows unified; the two remaining invisible controls outside the practice row ("Remove" in edit-candidate-form.tsx, "Compare" on the scaffolding page); the video duration fix (recorded_seconds is already selected and recordingLengthText already imported on both view pages, so it stays a small separate job); the Bunzl placeholder at components/create-assignment-form.tsx; and the text and voice practice screens. Practice pause is still the deferred Piece 6.

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-8e: the instructor Submissions table (the fifth of the PD-8 sessions). Built and deployed live to https://relii.vercel.app (commits ee9fd12 and c80bd3a). 3 files edited, 1 file added. NO schema, route, Row Level Security or Storage change, no new dependency, and no change to the submission detail page, the download routes or any confirm flow. The production build passes.
  - WHAT KIND OF SESSION THIS WAS: a build to an owner-approved SPEC, not a design exercise. The owner had reviewed a rendered target and approved every decision in it, so the brief was the specification and it outranked the builder's judgment wherever the two disagreed. That is worth recording as a mode: the earlier PD-8 sessions were briefs to interpret, this one was a drawing to hit.
  - THE MEASURABLE OUTCOME, which is the whole point of the piece. The old 7-column table had an Actions cell that stacked four controls (View, a Download label, three download links, and the retake button), and that stack is what made rows tall and ragged. Splitting Actions into two one-icon columns, Review and Download, took rows from 126px each to between 53 and 71px, 61px being typical, measured at the 896px container with deliberately long names and titles. The table fits with no horizontal scroll and the Length header sits on one line. Roughly half the row height, from one structural change rather than from any colour or spacing choice.
  - THE COLUMNS are now Student, Assignment, Mode, Length, Score, Status, Review, Download, table-layout fixed, with the approved widths 21/16/7/8/10/17/9/12.
  - THE DOWNLOAD MENU is native <details> and <summary>: server-rendered, no JavaScript, keyboard accessible for free. The disclosure triangle is suppressed so the icon is the only affordance, and the summary carries a real accessible name and a tooltip naming the student. AN UNAVAILABLE DOWNLOAD IS DIMMED AND LABELLED, NEVER DROPPED: a text submission's first item reads "No recording", so every row's menu has the same shape and an instructor is never left wondering whether a control is missing or simply does not apply to that row. Verified in a browser: the summary is focusable, opens and closes on activation, and its three items resolve to the right routes. KNOWN AND ACCEPTED: a native <details> does not close on an outside click, and no JavaScript was added to solve it.
  - ALLOW RETAKE AND RESET LIVE IN THE STATUS CELL, and this is a CORRECTNESS decision rather than a layout one, so do not "improve" it back into an actions column. An interrupted attempt has no score and nothing worth viewing, so routing its recovery through Review would strand it: the action belongs to the state it changes. Both stay LABELLED TEXT rather than icons, because they are consequential and rare, and icon-only is for routine, universally understood actions. Every existing confirm behaviour is untouched. (SUPERSEDED IN PD-8f, and note WHICH HALF was superseded: the two controls moved out of the Status CELL into their own Manage COLUMN once the wider page made a ninth column fit, but the correctness argument survived the move intact, because Manage sits directly beside Status rather than beside Review and Download. They are still not in an actions column.)
  - STATUS IS COLOURED TEXT RATHER THAN A BADGE, in this column only. A pill per row is noise in a table this dense, and the column header supplies the context the pill was compensating for. This is a deliberate, owner-approved reversal of PD-8c's choice; the Badge primitive stays in use everywhere else in the app.
  - THE HEADER READS "Length", not "Recording Length". A deliberate, owner-approved deviation from PD-5's otherwise locked label, and ONLY in this table header: the full label is what forced the header onto two lines. Both detail views (student results and instructor submission) keep the full label unchanged.
  - ALSO IN THIS PIECE: the assignment selector moved up beside the page heading, recovering a whole row of vertical space above a table whose entire point is density; the empty state now states what is actually TRUE (how many enrolled students still have the assignment open) and offers one next action; the resets line reads as the same quiet bordered element whether or not there are resets; and a footnote explains the two icons, which is what makes icon-only defensible for repeat users of a dense table.
  - ONE THING TRIED, MEASURED, AND UNDONE, recorded because the measurement is the useful part. Truncating the email and the assignment title was tried to make row heights uniform. Measured both ways, the heights were IDENTICAL (71/61/61/53), because the Status cell and a wrapped student name are what set the row height, not those two cells. So it chopped legible text for no gain and was reverted. Row height in this table is driven by the Status cell's two lines and by long student names wrapping, and neither is worth trading legibility for.
  - CARRY-OVER 1: the student practice row's Review is a VISIBLE TEXT LINK again, with Download and Delete still icon buttons. PD-8d had made all three icons. An eye glyph is ambiguous for "open my results", this app already uses an eye-like control to mean show/hide on the PD-6 transcript toggle, and three same-weight icons left the row with no hierarchy between its primary action and its destructive one. NOTE THE DELIBERATE ASYMMETRY WITH THE INSTRUCTOR TABLE, which is icon-only with a footnote: students are novices on a screen they visit occasionally, instructors are repeat users of a dense table they read all day. Different users, different screens, different answers.
  - CARRY-OVER 2, AND THE PATTERN BEHIND IT: a third full resting-affordance audit. THE RULE is that an icon is not an affordance; a control must be distinguishable from adjacent text at REST through colour, weight or a border, because a hover-only treatment cannot advertise anything (the reader has to already know the control is there to hover it, and a touch screen has no hover). This rule has now had to be applied THREE separate times, because each earlier fix added an icon and left the resting state untouched. Every button, Link, anchor and summary in the app was re-checked with the hover variants stripped; one genuine case remained, the resets disclosure summary, now accent-coloured at rest, and the practice row's icon buttons gained the accent colour at rest on top of their border. WHEN FIXING AN INVISIBLE CONTROL, CHECK THE RESTING STATE FIRST AND THE ICON SECOND.
  - THE DISCREPANCY THAT WAS FLAGGED HERE WAS REAL, AND IT SHIPPED WRONG BEFORE BEING CORRECTED (commit 83cd698, same day). The PD-8e brief described the two reset controls backwards and the approved footnote said Allow retake "deletes the recorded grade". The route is the authority, and it is unambiguous: RETAKE IS NON-DESTRUCTIVE (it returns the submission to the student's practice pool, kind submission to practice with submitted_at cleared, KEEPING the score and the recording), and INTERRUPTED/RESET IS THE DESTRUCTIVE ONE (it removes the recording from Storage and deletes the row permanently). Four things were then fixed with no behaviour or route change: the footnote's retake clause now describes the return-to-pool; Reset is named in the footnote for the first time, having previously been the unexplained one; the two controls no longer look identical (both were amber, so the routine action shouted as loudly as the permanent one, and Reset now wears the danger tokens while Allow retake is a quiet bordered action with no warning colour); and both confirms now state what actually happens.
  - A CONSEQUENCE OF ALLOW RETAKE THAT NOBODY HAD DOCUMENTED, now in its confirm copy: the route re-runs prunePractices with keepId after returning the row. The returned practice carries its ORIGINAL date, so force-keeping it can push the pool over the cap, and a student already at KEPT_PRACTICES loses their OLDEST OTHER practice and that practice's recording. The confirm reads the cap from the constant so the copy cannot drift.
  - THE LESSON, which is the durable part: a brief can be wrong about the code, and this one was. WHEN A BRIEF DESCRIBES WHAT AN EXISTING ROUTE DOES, READ THE ROUTE BEFORE WRITING COPY THAT DESCRIBES IT. The builder did notice the contradiction and flagged it, but shipped the approved wording anyway rather than checking the route first, which put a factually wrong sentence about a destructive action in front of instructors. Flagging is not enough when the claim is checkable in thirty seconds.
  - VERIFICATION DONE: a full `npm run build`. Lint back to exactly one error, the known pre-existing setState-in-effect issue in components/submission-live-gate.tsx (two unused-import warnings introduced by this work were cleaned up). Type-attribute discipline held for a fourth session: zero drift on every pre-existing file. The w-full container fix from PD-8d is intact, with no bare mx-auto on a flex child anywhere. Rendered and measured against the built stylesheet: the whole table with four realistic rows, the row heights before and after, the header on one line, and both download-menu states. NEEDS THE OWNER'S CLICK-THROUGH: the live signed-in screens, and in particular that an interrupted row still offers Reset and a scored row still offers Allow retake with its confirm intact, which could only be verified here from the rendered markup.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece, all of it PD-8f: the six confirmation flows unified, the two remaining invisible controls outside the practice row, the video duration fix, the Bunzl placeholder, and the text and voice practice screens. Practice pause is still the deferred Piece 6. (PD-8f was then rebriefed off this list by a live click-through, so these items moved on to PD-8g. See the PD-8f entry below.)

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-8f: the click-through fixes (the sixth of the PD-8 sessions). Built and pushed (commit 7f6dfa0). 12 files edited. NO schema, route, Row Level Security or Storage change, no new dependency, and no change to the reset routes, the confirm mechanics or the audit history. The production build passes.
  - WHAT KIND OF SESSION THIS WAS, and why its contents are not what PD-8e predicted. PD-8e handed forward a list. The owner then clicked through the live site and briefed six specific things he could see, and that brief REPLACED the handed-forward list, which was explicitly put out of scope. The handed-forward items are all still outstanding and are now PD-8g. Worth recording as a pattern: a handoff list is a builder's guess at what matters next, and an owner's click-through outranks it.
  - THE CONFIRM WAS TRAPPED IN A TABLE CELL, and this is the one worth remembering. The reset confirm panel rendered inside the Status <td>, which is a 17% column of a table-layout: fixed table, so roughly 180px. That is why it came out as a narrow vertical ribbon and why a long student email pushed straight through its own rounded border. It looks exactly like a page-width problem and it is not: widening the page could not have moved it, because the cell is a fixed percentage of whatever the table is. THE LESSON: when something overflows inside a fixed-layout table, look at the cell before looking at the page.
  - HOW IT WAS FIXED, with no JavaScript positioning, no popover and no overlay. The TRIGGER stays in the Status cell; the PANEL is now a SECOND TABLE ROW beneath that submission's row, with one cell spanning all eight columns. A trigger in one row and a panel in another cannot share React state directly, so components/submission-reset-button.tsx became three pieces: SubmissionResetProvider (a context holding which row's confirm is open, rendering NO element of its own, which is what makes it legal directly inside <tbody> where only <tr> may live), SubmissionResetTrigger, and SubmissionResetConfirmRow. Only one confirm can be open at a time, which is also the behaviour wanted. Measured with deliberately long names and emails at the new container: each panel is 1050px wide, nothing overflows, and no child crosses the padding edge by even 1px.
  - THE CONFIRM COPY. Both confirms now name the STUDENT and the email is gone from them entirely (the dashboard already had the display name in scope). Both are much shorter, and the retake confirm drops the score, which the Score column already shows. ONE DELIBERATE RETENTION: the prune warning stays, compressed to a single line ("If they already have 3 saved practices, their oldest one and its recording are dropped"), because it describes a genuinely destructive consequence of an otherwise non-destructive control and it was undocumented until PD-8e. The number is still read from KEPT_PRACTICES so the copy cannot drift from the cap. The Reset confirm still says plainly that the attempt and its recording are permanently deleted.
  - THE TABLE INSTRUCTIONS moved ABOVE the table, because they explain controls the reader is about to use, and they are one sentence per line rather than one running paragraph. The last line is a MEANING correction, not a formatting one: "Practice sessions never appear here" is misleading under the Piece 4 model where a submission IS a converted practice, and it now reads "Only the Practice sessions selected for submission appear here." The identical wrong sentence in the table's EMPTY STATE was corrected to match; that was not in the brief and is flagged here as the one in-flight adjustment, made because leaving a knowingly wrong sentence ten lines from its correction is worse than a small scope stretch.
  - THE REVIEW LAYOUT, applied to BOTH the student results page and the instructor submission view: the player and the transcript side by side, the transcript matching the player's height and scrolling inside itself, and the rubric FULL WIDTH beneath them, which is where PD-8d's collapsible categories read best. The two screens showing the same material in two different arrangements was exactly the inconsistency this sprint exists to remove. HOW THE HEIGHTS MATCH WITH NO MEASURING: both columns are the same width, the video is pinned to 16:9 with aspect-video, and the transcript's scroll box takes the SAME ratio, so the two bodies compute to identical heights. Measured: player 524 by 295, transcript box 524 by 295.
  - THE PRINT TRAP, which is the thing that would have shipped broken, and it is the same family as PD-8d's collapsed-rubric trap. Student print-to-PDF is a shipped feature, and a fixed-height, internally scrolling transcript prints CLIPPED: a student saving their results would have got only the first screenful of their own conversation. In print the ratio and the scroll are both switched off (print:aspect-auto, print:overflow-visible, print:max-h-none) and the grid collapses to one column. VERIFIED BY FORCING PRINT, not by reasoning: the built stylesheet was rewritten with every @media print block turned into @media screen, the real class strings rendered against it, and the result measured and screenshotted. On screen the transcript scrollHeight is 1566 against a 293 box; in print it is 1406 against 1406, aspect-ratio auto, overflow visible, the last turn inside the box, one column, the recording hidden, and all three rubric categories expanded with every item and comment. One cascade detail that had to be checked rather than assumed: @media print is emitted AFTER the md breakpoint block, so the print rules win on source order at equal specificity.
  - THE NAV moved from text-meta (12px) to text-card-title (14px), up one step on the PD-8c scale with no new size invented. It was reading as metadata when it is the app's primary wayfinding. Measured: the five destinations need 555px on one line (up from 487px at 12px) against 1072px available at the new container, so it stays on one line down to roughly a 600px viewport.
  - THE PAGE CONTAINER went from 56rem (896px) to 70rem (1120px), and A SECOND TOKEN CAME WITH IT, because that one token drives every screen including the transcript and every instruction block, and long-form prose at 1120px is genuinely worse prose. --container-reading (42rem, 672px) now carries long-form text; tables, dashboards and card grids keep the full width. WHERE IT WAS APPLIED: the submissions-table instructions, the roster instructions, the Invite Users / Manage Assignments / Persona Scaffolding intro paragraphs, the student home's processing notice, the transcript turn text on both review screens, the rubric's per-item feedback, and the reset confirm text.
  - A NEAR-MISS WORTH RECORDING. The token was first named --container-prose, on the reasonable assumption that Tailwind v4's max-w-prose reads it. It does not: max-w-prose is a HARDCODED 65ch utility and that static rule beats the --container-* theme namespace, so the token compiled to nothing at all and every block silently kept Tailwind's own 65ch. Nothing in the source looked wrong and the build passed. It was caught only by grepping the BUILT stylesheet for the emitted rule, which is the same habit that caught the font bug in PD-8a. Renamed to --container-reading, used as max-w-reading, and confirmed emitted.
  - THE NINTH-COLUMN MEASUREMENT the owner asked for, which was his call to make and not the builder's. "Allow retake" stacking under "To review" was the approved PD-8e design, not a width symptom, and widening did not change it. At 896px a ninth column did not fit; at 1120px IT DOES. Measured with four realistic rows: splitting the control into its own column at widths 20/15/6/7/9/9/12/9/13 gives row heights 71/53/53/53 against the 8-column 71/61/61/53, so the rows carrying a control get 8px SHORTER (the control no longer adds a second line to the Status cell), no header wraps, no cell text wraps that did not already, and there is no horizontal scroll. At the old 896px the same 9 columns wrap the student name, the assignment title in every row, and even "In progress", with a tallest row of 89px, which is exactly why it was rejected then. THE OWNER RULED: ADOPT IT, on the grounds that the stacking was one of his original complaints, so this fixes a reported problem rather than tidying. Built and re-measured; see the PD-8f follow-up entry below.
  - THE 8-COLUMN WIDTHS STILL BEHAVE at the new container: Student 225, Assignment 171, Mode 75, Length 86, Score 107, Status 182, Review 96, Download 128, every header on one line, no horizontal scroll, row heights 71/61/61/53. "Data center scenario 1" NO LONGER STACKS (it wrapped at 896px and does not at 1120px). Honest caveat: a genuinely long title such as "Bunzl discovery meeting with Alex, week 3" still wraps to two lines at 1120px, and does so in the 9-column layout too, so that is a title-length fact rather than anything the width or the column count fixes.
  - VERIFICATION DONE: a full `npm run build`. Lint back to exactly one error, the known pre-existing setState-in-effect issue in components/submission-live-gate.tsx. Type-attribute discipline held for a FIFTH session: zero drift on every pre-existing file (the only type= added anywhere is the reset component's own "interrupted" / "retake" prop, not an HTML attribute). The w-full container fix from PD-8d is intact, measured: the chrome wrapper and the page main both render exactly 1120px at the same x. RENDERED AND MEASURED against the real built stylesheet with the real Geist font, using the real class strings: the whole submissions table with both confirms open at realistic name lengths, the 8- versus 9-column comparison at both container widths, the nav at both font sizes, the student results page on screen and with print forced, the instructor submission review layout, and the roster instructions. NEEDS THE OWNER'S CLICK-THROUGH, as always: the live signed-in screens, and in particular that opening a confirm on a real row still cancels and still resets.
  - Deliberately NOT in this piece, all of it PD-8g: the remaining confirmation flows unified, the two controls then called invisible, the video item, the Bunzl placeholder, and the text and voice practice screens. USE THE PD-8g LIST IN CURRENT STAGE, not this sentence: the counts were re-checked against the code on 2026-08-02 and two of them were wrong here (four confirmation flows remain, not six, and the two "invisible" controls are really an accent-colour consistency item). The interrupted practice behaviour (a practice abandoned by a refresh stays in_progress and is invisible on the student home, app/student/page.tsx) was out of scope here and was recorded at the time as a gap parked against Piece 6; TOM RULED ON 2026-08-02 THAT IT SHIPS AS IT IS FOR v1, so it is a decision and not a gap, and Piece 6 is now the practice pause only.

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-8f FOLLOW-UP: the owner's two rulings, both on questions the piece raised WITH MEASUREMENTS ATTACHED (commits 9e6329a and 09a1719). 3 files edited. No schema, route, Row Level Security or Storage change, no new dependency, and no behaviour change to either reset control. The production build passes.
  - THE PATTERN WORTH KEEPING, which is really what these two commits are about. Both of them are the owner overruling an earlier decision because a MEASUREMENT contradicted it, and in one case the measurement contradicted his OWN number. PD-8f did not decide either question itself; it built the thing, measured both options and reported the numbers. That is the mode to repeat when a call is genuinely the owner's: measure it, report it, do not quietly pick.
  - RULING 1, THE NINTH COLUMN IS ADOPTED. Allow retake and Reset now live in a Manage column at the measured widths 20/15/6/7/9/9/12/9/13, and the Status cell is text only. The owner's reasoning is worth recording: the stacking was one of his ORIGINAL complaints, so moving the control fixes a reported problem rather than merely tidying. Re-measured at the shipped layout: Student 214, Assignment 161, Mode 64, Length 75, Score 96, Status 96, Manage 128, Review 96, Download 139; NO header wraps; row heights 71/53/53/53; no horizontal scroll; nav still one line at 546px of 1072px; both confirm panels 1050px with nothing past the padding edge. SUBMISSION_COLUMNS went 8 to 9 and the confirm row's colSpan followed automatically, which is the entire reason it is a constant.
  - WHERE MANAGE SITS, and why it is not the last column. It is directly after Status, not after Review and Download. PD-8e's correctness argument survives the move that way: an interrupted attempt has no score and nothing worth viewing, so its recovery belongs beside the state it changes rather than among the routine icon actions. The last-column arrangement was built and measured too and is equally clean (no header wraps, same row heights), but it lands the measured widths on different columns (Review 128, Download 96, Manage 139). Flipping it is a reorder of three <td>s and three <th>s.
  - THE COPY MOVED IN THE SAME EDIT, and this is the durable point. The instruction block said "Allow retake, under a submission's status" and "Reset, under an interrupted attempt". Moving the control made both FALSE. They now name the Manage column and still say which state each applies to, and two source comments describing the old location went with them. TABLE_INSTRUCTIONS now carries a standing note that it is part of the table's LAYOUT, not decoration, because this is the SECOND time in this sprint that copy describing a control went stale relative to the control (PD-8e shipped the retake clause backwards; PD-8f moved the controls). MOVE A CONTROL, EDIT THAT CONSTANT IN THE SAME CHANGE.
  - RULING 2, THE READING MEASURE IS 30rem, NOT 42rem. PD-8f shipped 42rem on the owner's estimate and then measured real prose at that width: 96.6 characters per line, 20 past the comfortable 45 to 75. The owner's ruling was to take the measurement over his own number. Re-measured the same way at 30rem (480px): 69.0 characters per line, inside the range. Method, so it can be repeated: render a fixed-width box of real prose at text-body, divide the character count by the rendered line count (box height over line-height). The '0' advance at 13px Geist measures 8.738px, which is why Tailwind's 65ch would have been about 568px here rather than the 465px estimated earlier.
  - RE-VERIFIED AFTER BOTH CHANGES, in that order, because the owner asked for it after and not before. THE BUILT STYLESHEET was grepped rather than trusted: it emits --container-reading:30rem and .max-w-reading{max-width:var(--container-reading)}, and the DEPLOYED stylesheet was checked the same way. That check exists because of PD-8f's own near-miss, where --container-prose compiled to NOTHING and the source looked fine. PRINT WAS FORCED AGAIN, not reasoned about: one column, recording hidden, aspect-ratio auto, overflow visible, the transcript's 1606px of content in a 1606px box with the last turn inside it, and all three rubric categories expanded with every item and comment. The nine-column table, both confirm panels, the nav and the header-wrap check were all re-rendered at the final layout.
  - Type-attribute discipline held again: zero drift on all three files.

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-8g: the eleven-item demo pass (the seventh of the PD-8 sessions), built for the Tuesday August 4 2026 demo. Built and deployed live to https://relii.vercel.app (commits 8dc6373 and 67cacdc). The owner ran the SQL on 2026-08-03 and the push followed. 24 files edited, 1 file added, 1 file renamed. ONE SCHEMA CHANGE, now applied: supabase/pd-8g-company-name.sql adds assignments.company_name. It was run in Supabase BEFORE the push, deliberately, because the create and edit forms write that column straight from the browser and would otherwise have failed with "column does not exist". No Row Level Security, Storage or route-behaviour change, no new dependency, and no change to any confirm flow, the reset routes, the recording or upload path, or the print view. The production build passes and lint is back to exactly the one known pre-existing error.
  - WHAT KIND OF SESSION THIS WAS: an owner-briefed list of ELEVEN specific demo-facing items, which REPLACED the handoff list this file had been carrying since PD-8e. That is now the third time in this sprint that an owner brief outranked a builder's handoff list, so treat the handoff list as a guess and the brief as the queue. What was left over is PD-8h and was marked explicitly droppable.
  - THE ONE SCHEMA TOUCH, and why it needed no server route. company_name is one additive nullable text column with no default, the same shape as available_date (PD-3) and duration_minutes (PD-4), and it is written STRAIGHT FROM THE BROWSER by the create and edit forms under the existing "instructors manage assignments" rule, which is FOR ALL and therefore already covers a new column. It is shown on the instructor's own assignment card on its own line under the title, and it is deliberately NOT shown to students and NOT sent to the AI.
  - THE DOWNLOAD MENU IS GONE, REVERSING PD-8e ON THE OWNER'S INSTRUCTION. The Download column is now three separate icons (video, transcript, scores) rather than one icon opening a native <details> menu: one click instead of two, and every download visible without opening anything. THE TWO PROPERTIES THAT MADE THE MENU DEFENSIBLE WERE CARRIED OVER DELIBERATELY, because they are what a straight swap would have lost: an unavailable download is DIMMED AND NAMED rather than dropped (a text submission's video icon greys out, so every row keeps its shape), and every icon keeps its aria-label and its title, both naming the student and the file. components/submission-download-menu.tsx was RENAMED to components/submission-downloads.tsx in the same change, because a file called "menu" that is not a menu is exactly the drift this project keeps correcting.
  - TABLE_INSTRUCTIONS WAS EDITED IN THE SAME CHANGE, which is the standing rule that PD-8f wrote after this went wrong twice. The Download line now says there are three icons and what a greyed one means. That constant is part of the table's LAYOUT, not decoration.
  - MEASURED, against the real built stylesheet with the real Geist font, at the real 1120px container. THE NAV at the new order and the longer "AI Persona Framework" label needs 573.8px of 1072px on ONE LINE (rows measured, not estimated), so it holds down to roughly a 630px viewport; the five items measure Submissions 83.1, AI Persona Framework 149.9, Manage Assignments 140.2, Roster 43.2, Invite Users 77.4. THE THREE DOWNLOAD ICONS need 70.0px of the 119.1px inside the 139.1px column: one line, no wrap, no horizontal scroll (table 1070px in a 1070px wrapper), row heights unchanged at 53px, and the dimmed row measures identically.
  - THE 24px WORDMARK IS LARGER THAN THE 22px PAGE TITLE, which is the consequence the brief asked to have checked rather than silently compensated for. It reads fine, and the reason is that size is the ONLY axis on which the wordmark wins: it is accent blue (#185fa5) against the page title's near-black (#2b2b29), it sits in the chrome band above the nav rule rather than at the top of the content, and it carries far less visual mass (48.9px wide against the title's 130.6px, because "Relii" is a short word). Nothing was compensated: the page title is untouched at 22px.
  - THE BETA BADGE uses the shared Badge primitive (tone info, so it ties to the accent wordmark) rather than a one-off span. It went into components/app-header.tsx, which covers every instructor page through the layout and the student home. IN-FLIGHT ADDITION, flagged: it also went on the LOGIN and REGISTER screens, which have no header at all and name the product with their own <h1>. The brief said "every page, both audiences", and the login screen is the first thing anyone sees at a demo. Two lines each to revert. The student practice and results screens have no wordmark anywhere, so there was nothing to sit beside.
  - THE QUALIFIER MOVED OUT OF THE HEADER LINK in the same edit, so the link is exactly the wordmark. Its accessible name is now "Relii" rather than "Relii Beta instructor", and neither the badge nor the word "instructor" is a click target that silently navigates home.
  - THE RENAME IS LABEL-ONLY AND THE ROUTE IS STILL /instructor/scaffold. Renaming the path would break bookmarks and existing links and would show nobody anything. Every occurrence of the old name, so the rename is provably complete: components/instructor-nav.tsx (the nav label), app/instructor/scaffold/page.tsx (the page heading), the four "Back to Persona Scaffolding" links across the edit, compare and chain subroutes (chain has three, one per state), lib/instructor-auth.ts (a comment listing the five destinations), and SIX USER-FACING ERROR STRINGS in the scaffold API routes (choose x2, disable, edit, generate) that named "persona scaffolding" or "the scaffolding page". Those last six are display strings only; nothing compares against them, unlike CONFIRM_WORD. Source comments and the module names (lib/persona-scaffold.ts, components/scaffold-form.tsx, the route folder) keep the old word on purpose, because they describe the code rather than the destination.
  - A LABEL COLLISION THE BRIEF DID NOT FORESEE, and the one thing on this list that needs the owner's word. The brief renamed the creation form's "Chain position" select to "Persona Role". That form ALREADY had a field called "Persona role", the persona's job title ("Fleet manager", which feeds candidate.role), two fields above it. Two identical labels on one form is worse in front of a stakeholder than either name, so the job-title field was renamed "Persona job title" and the select took "Persona Role" as briefed. Label only on both; the state, the request fields and the stored values are untouched, PD-2's locked pairs are still read-only, and chain_role still holds gatekeeper / decision_maker. THE READ-ONLY LOCKED-PAIR LABELS STILL SAY "Meeting type and chain position" on the edit and compare screens; the brief scoped item 1 to the creation form, so they were left, and they are the obvious follow-up if the owner wants the rename to be total.
  - "FIND THE RIGHT STATE, DO NOT JUST SWAP STRINGS", and the state turned out to be a wider question than `decided`. Two of the compare page's cards were wrong AFTER a pick, and both were already gated on `decided` (is a row switched ON), so gating harder on the same flag would have changed nothing. The state that was actually being read is a group that has come down to ONE ROW WITH THE WINNER TURNED OFF, which is reachable in two clicks (choose a winner, which deletes the loser, then use "Turn off" in Recent generations). In it, "Both are saved and turned OFF" claimed two rows where there was one, and "This generation has 1 candidate saved rather than the usual two" flagged as an anomaly what is the normal result of having picked. So the page now carries a SECOND flag, `picked` (a row is on OR the group is down to one row), and the two cards key on it: the first is removed, and the second becomes "This is your selected candidate." `decided` is unchanged and still drives the green live-case panel and the Choose footer, which are genuinely about being switched on. The same test names the Recent generations link: "Compare" while two candidates are undecided, "View Persona" once the pick is made.
  - ALSO ON THAT PAGE: the heading is "Personas" in BOTH states (it used to rename itself from "Compare the candidates" to "The chosen persona" under the instructor), the undecided paragraph is the owner's exact new sentence, and the "Candidate 1" / "Candidate 2" labels are gone from all three places they appeared (the comparison table head, the "Where the argument landed" cards, and the assembled-text disclosures). The persona's own name is the heading now; the "Chosen" marker stayed, because that carries state rather than an index, and moved up beside the name.
  - THE VIDEO ITEM, built exactly as Tom's ruling 3 specified and no wider. recorded_seconds now sits beside the player on both review screens (right-aligned on the same line as the section heading, measured at one line in the 524px half-column) and both video elements carry preload="metadata". THE DEFECT IS IN THE FILE, NOT THE PLAYER: a browser-made WebM carries no duration in its header, because MediaRecorder is writing a live stream, so the native scrubber reads 0:00 until it seeks to the end. Both edits land INSIDE the student page's print:hidden recording section, so the print view is structurally untouched rather than merely believed to be.
  - THE RUBRIC ATTACHMENT IS A PLACEHOLDER AND SAYS SO THREE TIMES: the label reads "(not yet available)", the file input is disabled at 50% opacity, and the line beneath says plainly that scoring still uses the scenario's rubric. Nothing is wired to it: no state, no upload, no bucket, and the insert does not mention it. A field that looks live and quietly does nothing is worse than no field, most of all in front of a stakeholder who tries clicking it.
  - RESETS SHOW THE STUDENT'S NAME, resolved by joining the audit row's student_id to the studentById map the dashboard already builds for the table, so there is no second query and the audit table stores exactly what it stored before. THE SNAPSHOT EMAIL IS STILL THE FALLBACK, and that is the load-bearing half: submission_resets keeps the email deliberately, as a record of who it was at the time, and a reset for a since-deleted student has no profile to join to. A blank student in an audit log would be worse than an email.
  - VERIFICATION DONE: a full `npm run build`. Lint back to exactly one error, the known pre-existing setState-in-effect issue in components/submission-live-gate.tsx. Type-attribute discipline held for a SIXTH session: the only type= added anywhere is type="text" on the two new company-name inputs, which is the discipline rather than drift. No bare mx-auto on a flex child was introduced. RENDERED AND MEASURED against the real built stylesheet: the header at 24px with the badge, the nav at the new order, the nine-column table with the three-icon Download cell in both the available and dimmed states, the create form with the disabled rubric field, the assignment card with the company line, the student card's instructions header, the recording-plus-duration row, and the Personas page after a pick. The LOGIN page was rendered from a real local production server rather than a harness, because it is public. NEEDS THE OWNER'S CLICK-THROUGH, as always: every signed-in screen, and in particular that an assignment saves with a company name once the SQL is run, that a real reset row shows a real name, and that the Personas page reads correctly on a generation that was chosen and then turned off.
