  - Deliberately NOT in this piece, all of it PD-8h and all of it marked droppable by the owner: the five confirmation components and their six confirms, the twelve untokenized status borders, the Bunzl placeholder on the title field, the two accent-colour links, and the text and voice practice screens.

- PRE-DEMO SPRINT, PD-8g FOLLOW-UP: four fixes from the owner's live click-through (commit d4d6727). 3 files edited. Copy and labels only: no behaviour, route, schema, Row Level Security or Storage change, and no new dependency. Everything else on that click-through passed. The production build passes and lint is unchanged at the one known error.
  - THE ONE THAT MATTERS, and it is the same lesson twice in two days: A CONDITIONAL-STATE BUG LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A WRONG STRING. The note under the Personas header still keyed on `decided` (is a row switched ON) when the two cards below it had already been moved onto `picked` (has a pick been made at all). So the chosen-then-turned-off state fell through to the ELSE branch and told the instructor to "read them field by field and decide which one is the more appropriate buyer" on a page showing ONE already-chosen persona. Swapping the string would have fixed the symptom in that state and broken the genuine compare state. THE RULE: when a page has several things describing the same fact, they must all ask the SAME question, and moving one onto a new flag means auditing the others in the same change. This one was missed precisely because it was not one of the two cards the earlier brief named.
  - THE SECOND SENTENCE WAS ALSO FACTUALLY WRONG and is deleted. The decided note said the comparison was "kept below for reference"; choosing a winner DELETES the loser, so what is below is the chosen persona on its own. The note is now just "You have already picked a winner from this generation." That sentence is true in every picked state, including the rare one where the loser could not be deleted and two rows really are shown, which is why it no longer says either way.
  - VERIFIED BY EVALUATING EVERY STATE, not by reading the ternary. The two flags were re-implemented against five row shapes and the branch each one lands in was printed: two-off (compare copy, both-off card, Choose footer), one-on (picked copy, green panel, selected card, no footer), ONE-OFF (picked copy, NO green panel, selected card, footer kept so it can be turned back on), two-with-one-on (picked copy, green panel, retained-loser warning), and three-off (compare copy, anomaly warning). All four visible states were then rendered against the built stylesheet.
  - A NOTE ON WHAT STATE C DOES NOT SAY, recorded rather than built because the brief did not ask for it. A chosen persona that has been turned OFF now reads almost identically to one that is ON, minus the green "is switched ON" panel. The only positive signal that it is off is the Choose footer being offered, which is real but indirect. If that ever confuses someone, the fix is a line in the selected-candidate card, not another flag.
  - THE CHAIN-POSITION RENAME IS FINISHED, label-only. "Meeting type and chain position" became "Meeting type and Persona Role" on the edit screen's locked panel and in the compare table's field list, and the generate route's refusal now reads "Pick a Persona Role." THE PAIR WAS KEPT rather than replaced wholesale with "Persona Role", because that row renders BOTH halves ("Discovery. Gatekeeper."), so a label naming only one would describe half its own cell. chain_role, meeting_type, the generation_fields shape and PD-2's locked-field logic are all untouched, the panel is still read-only, and chainRoleLabel() still formats the VALUE rather than the label.
  - WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY LEFT SAYING "chain position", and this is the part to not "finish" later by mistake: the two AI PROMPT lines in lib/persona-scaffold.ts (line 95, the input list in Part A, and line 188, the assembled INPUTS block). Those are text sent to the model, not labels on a screen. Editing them would change what the model is told and therefore what it generates, which is a behaviour change and was out of scope. Source comments and the module names keep the old word for the same reason they did in PD-8g: they describe the code, not the destination.

- CREATE ACCOUNT PASSWORD RULES, a small standalone fix on 2026-08-04, done ahead of the 2:30pm demo at which the stakeholder creates her own student and instructor accounts. NOT PD-8h and not part of any PD piece; it is filed here on its own so a later reader does not go looking for which piece it belonged to. 1 file edited (app/register/page.tsx). Copy plus one attribute: no behaviour, route, schema, Row Level Security or Storage change, and no new dependency. The production build passes, and the change is live and verified on https://relii.vercel.app/register.
  - THE HELPER TEXT WAS FACTUALLY WRONG, AND THE WRONG NUMBER WAS IN TWO PLACES, not one. On screen it read "At least 6 characters." while the Supabase project requires 8, and it said nothing at all about "Prevent use of leaked passwords" being ON. The second 6 was `minLength={6}` on the password input, which is the app's OWN client-side validation: it let a 6 or 7 character password clear the browser check and then fail at the server, so the client and Supabase were enforcing different rules. Both are now 8 and they agree. The copy now reads, one sentence per line, "At least 8 characters." and "Common passwords are rejected, so choose something you do not use elsewhere." No character-class requirements are set in the dashboard, so none are claimed on screen.
  - THE SUPABASE SETTINGS WERE READ FROM THE LIVE PROJECT RATHER THAN ASSUMED, which is what makes the new copy trustworthy. Probing /auth/v1/signup with the public anon key returned three distinct answers: a 7-character password gave error_code weak_password with reasons ["length"] and "Password should be at least 8 characters."; "password123" (11 characters, so long enough) gave reasons ["pwned"] and "Password is known to be weak and easy to guess, please choose a different one."; and a 5-character common password gave BOTH reasons joined into one message. So 8 is confirmed as the real minimum and the HaveIBeenPwned check is confirmed ON.
  - THE ERROR PATH NEEDED NO CHANGE AND IS VERIFIED WORKING, which was the second half of the ask. The register page's signUp handler already calls setError(signUpError.message), so Supabase's weak-password message is shown VERBATIM in the red line under the form rather than swallowed into a generic "something went wrong". Someone who types a breached password is told it is known to be weak and to choose a different one, which is the whole point. NOTE WHICH MESSAGE FIRES FIRST: with minLength now at 8, a short password is stopped by the browser's own native tooltip before any request leaves the page, so Supabase's length message only appears if that native check is bypassed. The breached-password message is the one that actually reaches a real user, and it is unaffected by minLength.
  - ONE ORDERING DETAIL WORTH KNOWING AT THE DEMO: the invite-list RPC runs BEFORE signUp, so an email that is not on the invite list gets "This email is not on the invite list. Please contact your instructor." and never reaches the password check at all. Password feedback of any kind only appears for an invited email. (That earlier branch does collapse its own failure into a generic message, but that is the RPC failing, not a password being rejected, and it was out of scope here.)
  - A COMMENT WAS ADDED SAYING BOTH NUMBERS WILL DRIFT SILENTLY. The minLength and the helper text are a hand-kept copy of Authentication > Sign In / Up in the Supabase dashboard, and nothing in this codebase enforces that they stay in step: change either setting there and this page goes on stating the old rule with no build break, no failing test and no warning to anyone. That is the SAME STALE-COPY PATTERN as the reset controls and the video item, where what is displayed is a copy of a fact that lives somewhere else. The comment also records that the error path is the backstop and that passing signUpError.message straight through must be preserved.
  - VERIFIED LIVE, not just built: the deployed page was fetched and carries minLength="8" and both new sentences, and it was rendered headless at 900px to confirm the two sentences each start on their own line (the second wraps naturally) with the rest of the form untouched.

- GATE 1: THE CLASS SPINE. DONE. The SQL was run in Supabase by the owner and verified on 2026-08-25. Schema and backfill only, all of it in one file, supabase/gate-1-class-spine.sql. NO application code changed, so nothing was deployed and the live app is unchanged. The whole contract of this piece was that nothing changes on screen, and the owner's click-through confirmed it: student dashboard, instructor Submissions, a submission detail view with all three downloads, Roster, Manage Assignments with an edit saved, the AI Persona Framework generating a persona end to end, and a student results page with its rubric categories intact.
  WHAT IT ADDED. Two new tables: classes (id, name, instructor_id, created_at, with a unique rule on instructor_id plus name) and class_members (class_id, user_id, created_at, composite primary key). A nullable class_id column on SEVEN existing tables: rubrics, scenarios, assignments, sessions, submission_resets, assignment_deletes and allowed_emails. An index on each of those seven columns, plus classes(instructor_id) and class_members(user_id).
  EVERY class_id IS NULLABLE ON PURPOSE. DO NOT ADD NOT NULL. Nothing in the app sets class_id on insert yet; that is G3's job. A NOT NULL constraint would break every create path in the app (creating an assignment, generating a persona, starting a session) the moment it ran, in production. Tightening is a cleanup step AFTER G3, and the safe way to do it then is "add constraint ... check (class_id is not null) not valid" followed by a separate "validate constraint", which avoids a long table lock.
  THE ON DELETE CHOICES, each made deliberately. classes.instructor_id is ON DELETE RESTRICT: not cascade (which would destroy a class full of graded student work when an instructor account went away) and not set null (which cannot work against a NOT NULL column and would surface as a confusing constraint error). Restrict makes the account deletion fail loudly instead, and deliberate deletion with an export in front of it is G4. class_members cascades on both links, because a membership row holds no content of its own. The four content tables and allowed_emails link to classes with ON DELETE RESTRICT so a class cannot be deleted out from under its content.
  THE TWO AUDIT TABLES GET A PLAIN uuid WITH NO FOREIGN KEY, and this is deliberate rather than an oversight. submission_resets and assignment_deletes already store instructor_id, student_id and assignment_id as plain copies with no links, specifically "so a later account or assignment delete can never remove or blank a log entry". A real link to classes would contradict that and would let an old log row block a class deletion forever. G4 decides what an export-first delete does with the log.
  THE FOREIGN KEY ON allowed_emails.class_id IS LOAD BEARING, not decoration. The signup rule uses that value to create the membership row. If it could point at a deleted class, that insert would fail INSIDE the signup rule and kill the signup with an unreadable error. The restrict link makes that unreachable.
  ONE DELIBERATE SECURITY-RULE TOUCH, which is not a change to any existing rule. The two new tables have Row Level Security switched ON with NO policies attached. This was necessary, not optional: Supabase publishes every table in the public schema over its web API, so a new table with security off is readable by anyone holding the public anon key, which would expose every class and every membership row. On with no policies means ordinary users cannot touch these tables and only the service-role key and the security-definer signup trigger can, exactly like session rows. The profiles table is the proof this pattern works here: it has had security on with a select-only policy and no insert policy since Stage 1, and handle_new_user has inserted into it at every signup ever since. G2 adds the read policies. Every OTHER policy in the system still says public.is_instructor(); G1 changed none of them.
  THE BACKFILL. All existing data is demo and test data; no real student has ever used Relii. It all went into ONE class, "Relii Demo (Historical)", owned by michaeltbanta@gmail.com (the seeded first instructor from schema.sql). The alternative, splitting rows by created_by, was rejected because it would scatter Alex, Jordan, the Great Northwoods rubric and the sessions that point at them across several classes behind a restrict foreign key, and a split demo is a broken demo. It was NOT given to Tom's production account (tbbanta@ksu.edu, which does not exist yet) because locked decision 3 says a new instructor starts empty. Sessions took their class from their SCENARIO, not their assignment, because assignment_id is nullable (free practice has no assignment) while scenario_id is not null, so the scenario is the only link that is always there. Every existing student profile got a class_members row. Every existing instructor other than the owner got their own empty default class.
  THE BACKFILL IS SAFE TO RUN TWICE, and the "other instructors" step is guarded on "owns no class yet" rather than on "is not me". Written the obvious way (role = instructor and id <> owner), a re-run after a new instructor had registered would have handed that instructor a SECOND class and broken the "exactly one class" check. The ownership guard is re-run safe regardless, and it excludes the owner automatically.
  THE SIGNUP RULE, public.handle_new_user() in schema.sql, was rewritten. Everything it did before it still does in the same order, including the exception for an email that is not on the invite list, which is the only thing standing between Relii and open registration. What is new is three branches at the end: an invited INSTRUCTOR gets a class of their own, created for them and owned by them, named from their email's local part plus "'s Class" (tbbanta@ksu.edu becomes "tbbanta's Class"), and it starts empty. An invited STUDENT joins the class named on their invite. A STUDENT whose invite names no class simply gets no membership row and registers normally. The trigger on_auth_user_created did not need re-attaching, because "create or replace function" leaves the existing trigger pointing at the new body.
  THE STAGE 4 REVOKE WAS RE-RUN at the end of the file. supabase/stage-4-security-hardening.sql deliberately revoked execute on handle_new_user() from public, anon and authenticated so nobody can call it by hand over the REST API. Postgres retains an existing function's privileges across "create or replace", so in principle the revoke survived on its own; re-running it costs one line and removes any chance that this piece quietly reopened a hole a previous piece closed on purpose.
  HOW IT WAS VERIFIED, in both halves. A single union query returned zero for all ten checks: no null class_id on any of the seven tables, no student without a class_members row, no session whose class disagreed with its scenario, and no instructor owning other than exactly one class. Then the seven-item click-through above. Then both signup branches were exercised with throwaway accounts, and both were cleaned up afterwards.
  TWO THINGS THAT LOOK LIKE FAULTS AND ARE NOT, learned while writing the test plan. First, a NEW INSTRUCTOR DOES NOT SEE AN EMPTY SURFACE after G1. They see all the demo data, exactly as they would have before, because no page filters by class until G3; an empty surface after G1 would mean something was wrong. Second, THE STUDENT BRANCH CANNOT BE TESTED BY INVITING THROUGH THE APP, because components/add-email-form.tsx inserts { email, role } only, so an invite created after the backfill has a null class_id and produces no membership row. Testing it requires setting that invite's class_id by hand before registering. Related sequencing trap: run the verification queries BEFORE creating any throwaway invite, or a legitimately null fresh invite reads as a backfill failure.

- GATE 2: CLASS-OWNED SECURITY RULES, AND THE PERSONA LEAK CLOSED. DONE. The SQL was run in Supabase by the owner and verified on 2026-08-26. Three new files, no application code changed: supabase/gate-2-policies.sql (the migration), supabase/gate-2-revert.sql (the undo, written first) and supabase/gate-2-policies-tests.sql (four read-only checks). The full reasoning, including everything a future session should not re-derive, is in the G2 block in Current Stage above; this entry records what was run and what it proved.
  WHAT IT CHANGED. Six of the eight public.is_instructor() policies now call a new helper, public.instructs_class(target uuid): instructors manage rubrics, instructors manage scenarios, instructors manage assignments, the instructor half of "students read own sessions, instructors read submissions", instructors read reset log, and instructors read assignment delete log. The student half of the sessions policy is byte for byte unchanged, and the kind = 'submission' restriction was kept, so an instructor still cannot read a practice row.
  THE HELPER, public.instructs_class(uuid), mirrors is_instructor() exactly: language sql, SECURITY DEFINER, stable, search_path pinned to public, execute granted to authenticated. THE security definer IS LOAD BEARING AND MUST NEVER BE REMOVED. G1 switched Row Level Security on for the classes table with no policies attached, so a non-definer function would read zero rows, return false for every instructor, and blank every instructor screen that still reads through RLS. Check D in the tests file exists specifically to catch that, and it passed with security definer true.
  THE PERSONA LEAK WAS CLOSED AT THE COLUMN LEVEL, because it was never a row problem. A student is legitimately entitled to the scenario row for their own assignment (both the session page and the results page read it), so no row policy however tight could stop them reading persona_system_prompt on exactly the case they are about to be graded on. The migration revokes the table-wide SELECT on scenarios from anon and authenticated FIRST, then grants back the twelve safe columns in a loop over information_schema.columns. THE ORDER IS THE WHOLE TRICK: a column-level revoke is silently ignored while a table-level grant stands, so doing it the other way round runs, reports success and changes nothing. The loop shape also means no column list was ever hand-written, so it cannot be wrong and a re-run repairs drift.
  VERIFIED BEFORE IT WAS WRITTEN: all six reads of persona_system_prompt or scenario_context in the app use the service-role key (app/api/chat, app/api/assignment/start, app/api/voice/token, app/api/session/start, app/api/instructor/scaffold/edit, and lib/scaffold-candidates.ts, which is marked server-only and backs the AI Persona Framework compare, edit and chain pages). Every RLS-path read of scenarios selects only title, persona_name, rubric_id, id or enabled, and there is no "select *" anywhere in the codebase on any table. The plan's original count of four such reads was two short; both extras were service-role, so the conclusion held unchanged.
  THE ASSIGNMENTS POLICY IS THE ONE THAT TOLERATES A NULL class_id, written as "public.instructs_class(class_id) or class_id is null" in both using and with check. This is not untidiness. components/create-assignment-form.tsx (line 111) inserts and components/edit-assignment-form.tsx (line 166) updates, both from the browser through RLS, and neither sets class_id because nothing does until G3. A flat check would compare an empty value and refuse the insert, breaking assignment creation in production the moment the file ran. THE COST: an assignment created between G2 and G3 has no class and any instructor can manage it. That window is bounded at both ends and G3 closes it.
  TWO POLICIES WERE DELIBERATELY LEFT ON is_instructor(): instructors read invite list and instructors add to invite list, both on allowed_emails. The add form (components/add-email-form.tsx line 27) inserts from the browser without a class_id, so a scoped with-check would break inviting anyone, and scoping only the read half would make every invite created after G1 vanish from the Roster. Both move in G3, in the same piece as the fix that makes the form set class_id. The migration says all of this in its own comments so nobody "finishes the job" later without reading why. FOUND WHILE BUILDING: the Roster page reads allowed_emails with the service-role key, so that read policy has no reader in the app at all today. That does not make scoping it safe; it means the damage would surface later through the web API instead of immediately on screen.
  HOW IT WAS VERIFIED, in both halves. Check A listed every policy on all eight tables and showed six saying class ownership and the two allowed_emails ones still saying is_instructor. Check B read information_schema.column_privileges and showed exactly two NO columns out of fourteen, persona_system_prompt and scenario_context. Check C confirmed service_role kept table-wide SELECT (without it every role-play would fail) and that PUBLIC did not appear, which is the silent-failure case. Check D confirmed instructs_class exists with security definer true, alongside an expected second row for is_instructor, which the two allowed_emails policies still use. Then the click-through, run against both a NEWLY CREATED student account and the existing instructor account: create assignment, edit assignment, invite, student dashboard, practice and submit, the full text and voice role-play, the results page, Submissions with detail and all three downloads, Roster, Manage Assignments, and a full persona generate including the compare page. Nothing failed.
  ==> IF A NEW COLUMN IS EVER ADDED TO scenarios, RE-RUN supabase/gate-2-policies.sql. <== A column added after G2 is granted to nobody, so ordinary users cannot read it and any page reading it as the logged-in user fails with "permission denied for column". Service-role code is unaffected either way, so the failure will only ever appear on a student page or an instructor page that reads through RLS. This is written into the migration's comments too, at the top of section 9.

- GATE 3a: EVERY ROW GETS A CLASS. DONE. Built 2026-08-28, run and verified by the owner 2026-08-29. One commit, 9b01017. Twelve application files plus four SQL files: supabase/gate-3a-repair.sql (step 1, the data repair), supabase/gate-3a-policies.sql (step 3, the four policy changes), supabase/gate-3a-revert.sql (undoes step 3 only) and supabase/gate-3a-tests.sql (groups A, B and C, matching the three steps). The kickoff prompt is in the repo at docs/Relii-Gate3a-ClassOnWrite-Kickoff-Prompt.md.
  THE ORDER WAS INVERTED ON PURPOSE AND IT HELD. Normally the SQL runs before the push. Here the policy half could not, because tightening the assignments rule before the deployed form set a class would have made creating an assignment fail on the live site. So: step 1 (pure data repair, safe at any time), then the deploy, then step 3. Each SQL file states the ordering in its own header, and step 3 opens with a pre-flight that raises "Gate 3a stopped" rather than running against unrepaired data. Nothing had to be reverted.
  THE NINE WRITE PATHS, all now setting class_id, and the list is complete rather than assumed: a sweep for every insert and upsert on a class-carrying table found exactly ten insert statements and zero upserts, which are these nine (bulk invite has two, a batch and a per-row fallback). 1 components/create-assignment-form.tsx and 2 components/add-email-form.tsx, both from a new prop. 3 bulk-invite and 4 scaffold/generate, from the caller. 5 assignment/start, 6 voice/start and 7 session/start, from their SCENARIO. 8 assignment delete audit, from the assignment with the scenario as a fallback. 9 submission reset audit, from the session with the assignment as a fallback. THE EDIT ASSIGNMENT FORM IS DELIBERATELY NOT A WRITE PATH: it never touches class_id, so once a row carries one it passes both halves of the tightened rule unchanged. That was tested and it does.
  THE HELPER SHAPE, and it is not what the kickoff prompt assumed. requireInstructor() in lib/instructor-auth.ts now ALSO returns classId, with no filtering added. It CANNOT BE USED BY AN API ROUTE, because it calls redirect(), a page mechanism that throws instead of returning JSON; seven pages import it and zero routes do. So the lookup was factored out as getInstructorClassId(userId), exported from the same file, called by requireInstructor() and directly by the four routes that need it. ONE LOOKUP, TWO CALLERS. IT MUST USE THE SERVICE-ROLE KEY: G1 switched RLS on for classes with NO policies and G2 added none, so an ordinary read returns zero rows for everybody. Adding a read policy to classes was considered and rejected as a new security rule on a table the piece was not asked to touch. Both browser forms carry a guard that refuses to write when the lookup comes back empty.
  THE REPAIR COVERED SEVEN TABLES, NOT THE TWO THE PROMPT NAMED, approved by the owner before it was written. Break risk, read through RLS by live pages and targeted by step 3: assignments, scenarios, rubrics. The invite hole: allowed_emails, and student profiles with no class_members row. Hygiene, which changed nothing on screen: sessions, submission_resets, assignment_deletes, repaired because G3b filters on those columns and a half-empty column produces a page that looks scoped and is not. Attribution: allowed_emails and the memberships went straight to "Relii Demo (Historical)"; the tables the prompt did not name used the class owned by the row's created_by with the historical class as a fallback, which is G2's own ruling applied to rows that had no class at all.
  THE READ-POLICY TRAP, and the canary that proves it was avoided. CLASS MEMBERSHIP IS STUDENTS ONLY: an instructor owns a class through classes.instructor_id and is NOT in class_members, so a policy written as "is a member of this class" denies every instructor and does it QUIETLY, rendering a perfectly healthy empty page. The new helper public.can_read_class(target uuid) answers both halves, calling G2's public.instructs_class(target) for the owner half rather than duplicating it. SECURITY DEFINER IS LOAD BEARING ON IT for the same reason it is on instructs_class. THE CANARY IS Manage Assignments, which reads BOTH scenarios and assignments as the logged-in user; it was run FIRST in the click-through and passed, listing its assignments with the scenario dropdown populated.
  THE SPECIMEN TEST PASSED, which is the one that proves the repair and the policy change agree with each other. null-student@yahoo.com was invited after G1, so its invite carried no class and it had no membership row. After step 3 an account in that state reads no assignments at all and its dashboard is empty. It shows its assignments.
  HOW IT WAS VERIFIED. Group A after step 1 (every blocking and hygiene count zero, plus the specimen named explicitly by email rather than inferred from a count). Group B after the deploy, one query per write path. Group C after step 3 (the four rules in words, no rule still saying "auth.uid() IS NOT NULL" and none still saying "class_id IS NULL", both helpers SECURITY DEFINER, both halves of the trap present, and the five Gate 2 rules confirmed untouched). Then the full click-through on relii.vercel.app: create an assignment, edit it, single invite, bulk CSV invite, the specimen login, text and voice role-plays, a generated persona, a reset and a delete, the student results page, Submissions with detail and all three downloads, Roster and Manage Assignments.
  TWO FACTS FOUND WHILE BUILDING IT THAT CHANGE WHAT G3b HAS TO DO, both written up in the G3a block in Current Stage above: the four instructor pages are MIXED rather than all service-role (the dashboard selector and both reads on Manage Assignments already go through RLS, so step 3 partly scoped them by policy alone), and BOTH SCORING READS ARE SERVICE-ROLE, so tightening the three read policies could not have broken scoring a role-play.
- Phase 5 (legal): the two policy pages and the consent click-through. Built and deployed live (commit 58dcecc); supabase/phase-5-consent.sql run by the owner on 2026-09-08 and owner-confirmed working on https://relii.vercel.app. ONE ADDITIVE SCHEMA CHANGE (public.consents) plus a replacement of public.handle_new_user(); no Row Level Security or Storage rule was weakened, and no invite, class or isolation code was touched. Full detail in Current Stage above.
  - /privacy and /agreement, readable SIGNED OUT, rendered from docs/Relii-Privacy-Policy.md and docs/Relii-clickthrough-agreement.md rather than re-typed, so the documents stay the single source of truth. Verified by a text-equivalence diff against the live pages: 29 and 26 lines, identical character for character.
  - A consent checkbox on the register page that blocks the button, and a signup trigger that REFUSES a signup carrying no accepted version and writes the consent row in the same transaction as the account.
  - public.consents: one row per acceptance, two version columns (privacy 2026-09-07, agreement 2026-09-05), RLS on with NO policies at all, so the service-role key and the trigger are the entire access path.
  - A consent gate for accounts that predate the click-through, mounted on both dashboards ahead of the name gate, plus /api/consent. It is also the re-consent mechanism for when Tom revises a document.


## Open Issues

- ==> **THE EMPTY AND DROPPED STUDENT TURNS ARE STILL OPEN, AND AS OF 2026-09-14 THERE IS A NAMED HYPOTHESIS AND A CHEAP DECISIVE TEST FOR THEM. RAISED FROM D3.** Two of Tom's five September 11 failures were blank turns and one was truncated; **`language: "en"` addresses none of them.** <==

  **THE FILTER IS THE CENTRE OF IT.** `components/voice-conversation.tsx` line **456**, in `saveTranscript()`, filters `t.done && t.text.trim()`, so a student turn with genuinely no text is **dropped from the saved transcript entirely rather than saved blank**. It has done so since `427b51d`, 2026-06-22. **This means "no blank turn in the transcript" is not evidence that no turn was lost**, and D3's live run in B1 lost one outright: `Right.` produced no turn at all, twenty-one seconds into a running call.

  **THE HYPOTHESIS.** A turn whose text is only bidi or zero-width marks (**U+202B, U+202C, U+200B**) passes `trim()`, which strips whitespace and U+FEFF but not those, and renders as nothing. The transcriber is known to emit them: Tom's Arabic turn extracts wrapped in U+202B and U+202C. **That would make "dropped" and "blank" the same defect seen through one filter.** **THE CAVEAT: the PDF exporter may have added those marks itself during right-to-left layout, which would collapse the explanation.**

  ==> **THE TEST: DUMP THE CODEPOINTS OF A BLANK-RENDERING TURN. IN B1, BY REPRODUCING ONE. NOT ON TOM'S DATA.** Do this before touching `turn_detection`, whose `silence_duration_ms` of 1600 is a tuned value that nothing measured so far implicates. <==

NOT ONE OF ITS 42 TURNS RENDERS BLANK, AND NOT ONE CARRIES ANY OF THE HYPOTHESISED MARKS.** <==

- ==> **FLOAT DUST IN TOM'S DISCOVERY RUBRIC IS TOLERATED ON PURPOSE, AND IT IS TOLERATED BECAUSE IT IS INERT RATHER THAN BECAUSE IT IS HIDDEN (owner ruling, 2026-09-13).** No normalising, no backfill, no parser change. **The rubric row and every `score_total` stay exactly as they are.** <==

  **WHERE IT LIVES AND WHERE IT DOES NOT.** The dust is in the `items` of rubric `64f3f919`, on the Discovery criterion only: `55.00000000000001`, `49.50000000000001`, `44.00000000000001`, `38.5`, `27.500000000000004`, `5.500000000000001`. All six reproduce byte for byte as `100 * 0.55 * m` in IEEE 754 doubles, with the six multipliers taken from row 3 of the uploaded workbook, `0.55` from B7 and `100` from D18. **IT IS NOT FROM `lib/rubric-xlsx.ts`**, whose `numericValue()` reads `cell.result ?? cell.value` and never multiplies, **AND IT IS NOT FROM THE COPY IN `docs/`** (md5 `e7ea15ecf67c5ff48538ddb745e1fcea`), whose cached values on that row are clean `55 / 49.5 / 44 / 38.5 / 27.5 / 5.5` with the last four carrying no formula at all. It came in with whichever workbook was actually uploaded. **THAT FILE HAS NEVER BEEN READ, so anything more specific would be a guess.**

  **IT REACHES STORED TOTALS, AND NEW ONES WILL STILL CARRY IT.** `lib/scoring.ts` line 437 sums `points_earned` with plain float addition (`scoredItems.reduce((sum, item) => sum + item.points_earned, 0)`) and `app/api/session/end/route.ts` line 272 stores that sum as `score_total`. **This is not a legacy condition that ages out**: every future score against this rubric is born the same way.

  ==> **WHY IT IS TOLERATED: THE AUDIT SAYS IT IS INERT, NOT MERELY INVISIBLE. NOTHING COMPARES, THRESHOLDS OR SORTS ON A TOTAL.** The owner audited every read of `score_total` and `score.total` on 2026-09-13 and that search comes back empty. **RE-RUN IT, DO NOT RE-REASON IT.** The audit is one command:

  ```
  grep -rn "score_total\|score\.total\|max_total" --include="*.ts" --include="*.tsx" app lib components | grep -vE "formatPoints\("
  ```

  **Every surviving hit must be a type declaration, a `select()` column list, a null check, a pass-through, a comment, or one of the consumers below. A hit that is an inequality, a `sort`, an `.order()`, or a threshold ENDS THE TOLERANCE.** **Run verbatim on 2026-09-13 it returned 48 hits and every one was accounted for.** The count is a tripwire, not a target: it moves whenever any of these files is edited, so a moved count means re-read the hits, not that something is wrong.

  | Consumer | Why the dust cannot escape |
  | :-- | :-- |
  | Every screen, the submit confirm, the reset history | `formatPoints`, which rounds to one decimal |
  | The per-submission CSV, `app/api/instructor/submission/[id]/csv/route.ts` line 136 | `formatPoints` |
  | The percentage, `app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx` line 252 | `Math.round((score.total / score.max_total) * 1000) / 10` |
  | The instructor average tile, `app/instructor/page.tsx` line 429 | `toFixed(1)` |
  | `app/api/admin/class/[id]/export/route.ts` | Carries no score at all |

  ==> **THE ROUNDING AT LINE 252 IS LOAD-BEARING. IT IS NOT A SECOND LINE OF DEFENCE, AND AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS ENTRY SAID IT WAS.** The correction is recorded because the measurement was right and the MECHANISM was wrong, and the mechanism is the part a future session acts on.

  **What is true:** `49.50000000000001 / 55.00000000000001` and `49.5 / 55` really are the same double, both `0x3feccccccccccccd`.

  **Why, and it is NOT cancellation.** The two relative perturbations are different sizes, `1.4354e-16` on the numerator and `1.2919e-16` on the denominator. They leave a residual of about `1.29e-17` at the value 0.9, which is under half an ULP there (`5.55e-17`, one ULP being `1.11e-16`), so it rounds back to the same double. **IDENTITY BY ROUNDING, NOT BY CANCELLATION.**

  **AND IT DOES NOT GENERALISE, WHICH IS THE PART THAT MATTERS. Three of six plausible pairs off Tom's own ladder are NOT bit-identical**, measured 2026-09-13:

  | Pair | Same double as the clean ratio? | Renders |
  | :-- | :-- | :-- |
  | `49.50000000000001 / 55.00000000000001` | yes | 90 |
  | `13.500000000000002 / 55.00000000000001` | yes | 24.5 |
  | `4.500000000000001 / 55.00000000000001` | yes | 8.2 |
  | `38.50000000000001 / 55.00000000000001` | **no** | 70 |
  | `49.50000000000001 / 55` | **no** | 90 |
  | `49.5 / 55.00000000000001` | **no** | 90 |

  **All six render the percentage a clean pair would, and they do so BECAUSE OF the `Math.round`, not in spite of it.** On half these cases the rounding at `app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx` line 252 is **the only thing standing between the dust and what a student reads.** The inert claim rests on that rounding, exactly as much as it looks like it needs to. **Anyone who removes or loosens it is removing the guard, not tidying a redundant one.** <==

  The only other comparison in reach is the `score.max_total > 0` divide-by-zero guard on line 249, and `55.00000000000001 > 0` answers the same as `55 > 0`. **No sort or `.order()` anywhere in the app names a score or a total.**

  ==> **THE TRIGGER THAT ENDS THE TOLERANCE, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THIS ENTRY. IF RELII EVER EXPORTS A SCORE TO CANVAS OR ANY UNIVERSITY GRADEBOOK, A RAW FLOAT LEAVES THE BUILDING AND "INERT" STOPS BEING TRUE.** Every consumer above is one we control and one that rounds on the way out. A gradebook export hands the number to something we do not control, which may store it, display it, or arithmetic on it however it likes. **WHOEVER BUILDS THAT EXPORT IS THE ONE WHO BREAKS THIS ASSUMPTION AND HAS TO REVISIT IT.** Do not build it and leave this entry standing. <==

  **THE THREE OPTIONS ARE NOT ONE DECISION AND THEY DO NOT RIDE TOGETHER.** A future session that treats them as a single "fix the dust" task is doing three different things with three different risks:

  1. **Normalising the rubric row** is a WRITE TO A LIVE ROW in Tom's class, the row every future score is graded against. Cheapest to state, and it needs the same md5-guarded discipline as the persona update.
  2. **Normalising at parse time** is a CODE CHANGE affecting FUTURE UPLOADS ONLY. It fixes nothing that already exists. **It is the one with real value, because it stops new rubrics being born dusty**, and it is the only one of the three that touches no data.
  3. **Backfilling `score_total`** is a WRITE TO GRADED STUDENT WORK, some of which may already be reviewed by an instructor. **THIS ONE NEVER TRAVELS WITH THE OTHER TWO.**

- ==> **CLOSED 2026-09-11 BY COMMIT `73185da`. THE MEASUREMENT THAT CLOSES IT IS THE ONE THIS ENTRY ASKED FOR: B1 session `0ae456b3`, stored `2026-09-03 23:47:11+00`, read "Sep 3, 2026, 11:47 PM" on relii.vercel.app before the deploy and reads "Sep 3, 2026, 6:47 PM" after it.** The hour was the discriminator and the hour moved. Every human-readable timestamp in the app now resolves through one constant in `lib/app-time.ts`; the reset history's `" UTC"` suffix is gone; and the Available Date unlocks at 12:00 AM Central rather than midnight UTC, which was a second defect this entry did not name. **THE ENTRY'S OWN WARNING WAS THE RIGHT ONE AND WAS FOLLOWED: the three functions did NOT all want the same answer.** `formatDateTaken` and `formatShortDate` moved to Central; `formatDueDate` did not move, and neither did the three other date-only formatters. They are named in `lib/app-time.ts`'s header as deliberately absent. Full detail in Current Stage. **THE ORIGINAL ENTRY IS KEPT BELOW, because the day-boundary case it recorded is the clearest statement of what the defect cost a student.** <==

- ==> **EVERY DATE IN THE APP RENDERS IN UTC, AND A GRADE SAVED IN THE US EVENING PRINTS AS THE NEXT DAY. MEASURED 2026-09-11, AND THE OWNER HAS ALREADY RULED ON THE FIX.**
  **THE MEASUREMENT, TAKEN BEFORE ANY CODE WAS WRITTEN.** `formatDateTaken()` in `lib/date-taken.ts` calls `toLocaleString` with no `timeZone`, and the pages that use it are server components, so the date is formatted with whatever timezone the Vercel runtime has. B1 session `0ae456b3` is stored at **2026-09-03 23:47:11 UTC**, which is 18:47:11 US Central; relii.vercel.app renders it **"Sep 3, 2026, 11:47 PM"**. **THE HOUR IS THE DISCRIMINATOR, NOT THE DATE**, and the hour is the UTC one.
  ==> **AND THE DAY-BOUNDARY CASE IS NOW OBSERVED RATHER THAN PREDICTED, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THIS ENTRY.** The 4b proof run saved a real review at `reviewed_at` **2026-09-11 03:21:00 UTC**, which is **2026-09-10 22:21 US Central**. The student's results page reads **"Reviewed by your instructor on Sep 11, 2026, 3:21 AM."** for a button Tom pressed on the evening of the 10th. A student comparing that to a due date, or to their own memory of the week, is reading a date that is a day out. <==
  **IT IS NOT A 4b DEFECT AND 4b DELIBERATELY DID NOT FIX IT.** The owner's ruling of 2026-09-11: the new sentence uses `formatDateTaken` UNCHANGED, so that it agrees with every other date in the app, and **a separate small session immediately after 4b moves timestamps to US Central across the whole app, before students register the week of 2026-09-14.** Putting a timezone on one new student-facing string would have made it disagree with the "Date Taken" rendered directly above it, which is the two-records-disagreeing problem this project keeps paying for.
  **WHAT THE FIXING SESSION SHOULD KNOW.** The call sites are `lib/date-taken.ts` (`formatDateTaken`, used by the student results page, the instructor submission page and 4b's new status sentence and CSV Status line) and `app/student/page.tsx` (`formatDueDate`, which ALREADY pins `timeZone: "UTC"` on purpose so a date picked as "Aug 14" always shows as Aug 14, and `formatShortDate`, which pins nothing). **THOSE THREE DO NOT ALL WANT THE SAME ANSWER**: a due DATE with no time is correctly UTC-pinned today, while a timestAMP with an hour on it is the thing that is wrong. Decide per function, not with one find and replace. <==

- ==> **A SCALE THAT MAY NOT DISCRIMINATE IN THE MIDDLE, AND IT IS ONE OBSERVATION WITH TWO HALVES RATHER THAN TWO ENTRIES. RAISED 2026-09-10 FROM PHASE 2c.**
  **HALF ONE, FROM THE RUBRIC.** Tom's ladder has a large cliff between its bottom two rungs. On Discovery, Minimal is 27.5 and Unsatisfactory is 5.5 on the 55-point criterion; on Presentation, Minimal is 40 and Unsatisfactory is 8 on the 80-point criterion. **Missing a criterion outright drops a student a very long way.**
  **HALF TWO, FROM THE GRADER.** On the fixed build a carefully structured Presentation conversation scored **200 out of 200**, the top rung on all seven criteria. The grader appears to reach the top rung readily.
  ==> **TOGETHER THEY SUGGEST A SCALE THAT IS BIMODAL IN PRACTICE: students clustering near the top, and anyone who misses a criterion dropping a long way, with little separation in between. THAT IS A PROBLEM FOR TOM SPECIFICALLY, BECAUSE DISCRIMINATING BETWEEN STUDENTS IS THE WHOLE POINT OF THE EXERCISE.** <==
  ==> **A CONCRETE EXAMPLE OF THE CLIFF, FROM THE OWNER'S OWN CLICK-THROUGH ON 2026-09-10, FILED HERE RATHER THAN ACTED ON. On a two-and-a-half minute call where nobody closed, Close scored 3 out of 30: the bottom rung.** That is not a misgrade, it is Tom's ladder working exactly as designed, and it is the clearest single illustration of what the cliff does to a total. **It is the example to put in front of Tom when he spot-checks the first real batch**, because it makes the question concrete: is one un-attempted criterion meant to cost that much? <==
  **ITS WEAKNESS, STATED HONESTLY: IT IS A SAMPLE OF ONE ON SYNTHETIC INPUT.** The transcript was a scripted conversation built to hit every criterion of the rubric. That is not a student, and 200/200 may be entirely correct for that transcript. Nothing here has been measured against real student work, because none exists yet. **THE OWNER'S CLICK-THROUGH NARROWS THAT SLIGHTLY WITHOUT CLOSING IT**: his 68.5 and 113 came from a real person speaking into a real microphone rather than from a script, and both landed mid-ladder, which is the discrimination this entry doubts. That is two more samples, not a distribution, and neither is a sales student being graded for credit.
  **THE ACTION IT IMPLIES IS NOT CODE.** Tom spot-checks the first handful of real scores against his own judgment before releasing them, and Phase 4's instructor override is the mechanism for correcting what he disagrees with. Do not tune the prompt or the ladder off a sample of one. <==

- ==> **CLOSED 2026-09-10 BY PHASE 3a, COMMIT `d5326da`. THE INSTRUCTOR'S PLAIN DELETE NOW PROCEEDS WHEN NO FINISHED WORK EXISTS**, so an invisible session no longer holds its assignment hostage and the rubric-repair path no longer runs through the confirm that destroys real submissions. What counts as unfinished lives in `lib/assignment-sessions.ts` and is shared by the page, the button and the route. **TOM'S 2026-08-02 RULING IS UNTOUCHED**: an abandoned practice is still invisible to the STUDENT; only the instructor's delete changed. Proved with a failed-scoring VOICE practice carrying a real recording, beside a matching assignment holding finished work that still demanded the typed DELETE. **THE ORIGINAL ENTRY IS KEPT BELOW, because the coupling it describes is the reason the fix took the shape it did.** <==
  **AN UNFINISHED SESSION IS INVISIBLE TO EVERYONE AND CANNOT BE DELETED, AND IT HOLDS ITS ASSIGNMENT HOSTAGE. FOUND 2026-09-10 DURING 2b-2's CLEANUP, TWICE, BY ACCIDENT.**
  **THE MECHANISM.** A session that is not in the FINISHED status for its assignment appears on no screen anybody can reach. `app/student/page.tsx` lists a practice only at the finished status for its kind (`scored` for a graded assignment, `completed` for a conversation-only one) and its own comment says a graded practice sitting at `completed` is one whose SCORING FAILED and must stay out of that list. The instructor sees only `kind = submission`. So nothing lists the row and nothing can delete it. And an assignment with ANY session cannot be deleted with the ordinary two-step confirm: it demands the instructor type the word DELETE, which is the guard that also destroys real submissions.
  **THERE ARE TWO WAYS IN AND ONE OF THEM IS ROUTINE.** A scoring failure (the practice stops at `completed`). And a student closing the tab or reloading mid-practice, which leaves `in_progress` and is the case Tom RULED SHIPS AS IS on 2026-08-02. Both were hit in one session: one deliberately, one by opening a voice screen and not starting the call.
  ==> **WHY IT IS WORSE THAN UNTIDINESS, AND THIS COUPLING IS THE POINT: A RUBRIC CAN NEVER BE REPLACED ON AN EXISTING ASSIGNMENT.** The upload happens at creation and nowhere else. So "delete the assignment and create it again" is THE ONLY REPAIR PATH FOR A MIS-PARSED OR WRONG RUBRIC. One abandoned practice blocks that repair's clean route, and the only route left is the typed-DELETE that takes real student submissions with it. Two decisions that are each defensible on their own collide exactly here. <==
  **NOT FIXED IN 2b-2, DELIBERATELY.** Tom's ruling about the abandoned practice stands and this is a different fix from that one. It is a Phase 3 item, written into Next Steps: let an instructor clear an assignment's unfinished practices, or let the plain delete proceed when every session on it is unfinished.
  **THE WORKAROUND USED, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE ONLY ONE THERE IS.** Finish the session so it becomes ordinary and deletable. For an abandoned voice practice, start the call and end it. For a failed-scoring practice, repair whatever made scoring fail and end it again. <==

- ==> **CLOSED 2026-09-10 BY PHASE 3a, COMMIT `d5326da`. THE SESSION PAGE YIELDED.** Its status check moved BELOW the rubric lookup (it could not have been fixed in place, because up on line 43 the page did not yet know whether the case was graded), and a graded case with no score now renders a retry screen instead of redirecting. **EXECUTED, NOT REASONED ABOUT**: a real scoring failure was produced by stripping a rubric's levels, and after a RELOAD the retry screen appeared instead of ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS; the restored rubric then let the retry score the session. **THE CONDITION IS THE LITERAL COMPLEMENT OF THE RESULTS PAGE RULE**, and a submission renders a message rather than being left on the redirect under a comment, because leaving it there is the exact move that kept this loop alive. **THE ORIGINAL ENTRY IS KEPT BELOW as the record of how two correct-sounding comments hid one unreachable screen.** <==
  **A GRADED PRACTICE WHOSE SCORING FAILED IS A REDIRECT LOOP, AND THE STUDENT-FACING NOTE POINTS AT IT. FOUND 2026-09-10.** `app/student/session/[id]/page.tsx` line 43 sends any session that is not `in_progress` to its results. The results page sends a graded session that is not `scored` back to the session page. A graded practice sitting at `completed` IS a scoring failure, so those two rules point at each other and the browser gives up with ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS.
  **WHAT IT COSTS.** The retry offered by "The AI scoring encountered a problem. Ask your instructor to provide a score or try again." works ONLY while the student stays on the tab, where the conversation screen is still mounted and End is still there. Reload, or come back later, and that session is unreachable forever.
  **BOTH FILES CARRY COMMENTS ASSERTING THE OPPOSITE**, which is how it survived: the results page says that student "must still be sent back to end the session again rather than shown a permanent no-score page", and the session page's own check makes that impossible. Two correct-sounding comments, one unreachable screen.
  **OUT OF SCOPE FOR 2b-2 BY THE KICKOFF'S OWN WORDS** ("the results page redirect is NOT in scope and does not change"), so it was measured and left. Whoever fixes it should decide which page yields, and the cheap answer is probably the session page: a graded session at `completed` with no score is exactly the case that must NOT be redirected away. <==
  ==> **PHASE 2c MADE THIS MORE REACHABLE AND DID NOT FIX IT, DELIBERATELY.** Part (c) of the 2c fix turns an unmatched level into a REFUSAL, and a refusal leaves a graded practice sitting at `completed` with no score, which is exactly this state. Parts (a) and (b) should make refusals rare, and a loud rare failure is the right trade against a silent wrong grade. **But the trade is not free, and this issue is now the thing standing between a scoring failure and a student who can recover from it.** It rose in priority on 2026-09-10 without changing in nature. <==


- ==> **TWENTY-FIVE SESSIONS HAVE NO `assignment_id`, AND THIS IS A FACT ABOUT THE DATA RATHER THAN A RULE ABOUT THE CODE.** Counted 2026-09-10. They are pre-Stage-7 test practices, from before a practice was always a practice FOR an assignment. **THEY ARE NOT CORRUPTION.** Every current start route sets an assignment, so no NEW row can be one, but the column is nullable and these are here. **THE RULE THAT GOVERNS THEM, WRITTEN INTO `app/api/session/end/route.ts`, `app/student/session/[id]/page.tsx` and `app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx`: A SESSION WITH NO ASSIGNMENT IS CONVERSATION-ONLY.** No assignment means no rubric means nothing to score. **THE COUNT IS RECORDED HERE, AND NOT JUST THE RULE, ON PURPOSE**: a rule on its own reads like a defensive branch against something theoretical, and the next session to meet these rows without this note will read them as damage and go looking for the bug that made them. The 2b-1 kickoff asserted no such row existed; it was written from the start routes rather than from the data. <==

- ==> **NOTHING YET MEASURES THAT THE SCORER IS DETERMINISTIC, AND TOM'S FAIRNESS ARGUMENT RESTS ON IT.** The fixed seed (7) and zero temperature are the CLAIM. The MEASUREMENT is scoring one transcript twice and comparing the level names, and it has never been run against the new level-based scorer. Owner's ruling on 2026-09-10: it belongs to 2b-2 or 2c, not to 2b-1. It is the kind of property that is assumed right up until it is not, and a student disputing a grade is the worst moment to find out. Note the shape of the check has changed with the scorer: it used to be "within a few points", and with levels it is exact equality of the six chosen names, which is a stricter and much more legible test. <==

- **THE DEPLOY ORDER FOR 2b-1 IS A RULE, NOT A PREFERENCE, AND IT IS RECORDED IN CASE ANY OF THIS IS EVER REPLAYED. THE MIGRATION MUST RUN BEFORE THE CODE IS PUSHED.** Migration first leaves the live site on the old code, which reads `scenarios.rubric_id`, a column the migration does not touch, so the window between the two is behaviour-neutral for a real user. Push first would hand the NEW scorer the legacy 29-item Great Northwoods rubric through `assignments.rubric_id`, the scorer would refuse it as "not a levels rubric", and **every session ending would fail to score**. The order was followed on 2026-09-10 and the deploy was confirmed by a real request (the prerendered `/login` etag changed and `age` reset to 0) rather than by elapsed time, which is the trap 2a hit when the proxy masked 404 as 401.

- **CLOSED BY 2b-2 (2026-09-10): the knowingly wrong type cast in `app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx` is gone.** It cast `rubric.items` to the flat `RubricItem[]` when every reachable rubric is a levels rubric. It now casts to `RubricCriterion[]`, and the comment in its place explains why that one is TRUE rather than merely newer: 2b-1's migration put the flat shape out of reach, and the scorer refuses a rubric whose criteria carry no levels, so a rubric that reaches that page and was scored is a levels rubric. `items` is jsonb, so no cast there can ever be checked by TypeScript; what makes it safe is structural.

- **CLOSED BY 2b-2 (2026-09-10): the instructor view and the student results page agree again.** `app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx` and `lib/instructor-submission.ts` now resolve the rubric through the ASSIGNMENT like everything else. **ONE RULE GOVERNS EVERYWHERE: the rubric comes from the assignment or there is none.** Where there is none, the instructor page says the score cannot be itemized and the CSV says the same thing in one line of its header block, both proved live by detaching a rubric for two minutes and putting it back (supabase/phase-2b2-no-rubric-probe.sql).

- ==> **CLOSED, AND IT HAD BEEN CLOSED FOR A DAY WITHOUT THIS ENTRY SAYING SO. THE ESLINT ERROR IN `components/submission-live-gate.tsx` WAS FIXED BY PHASE 3a, COMMIT `d5326da`.** Re-measured 2026-09-11 during 4b's gate: `eslint .` exits 0, the file reports **0 messages**, and `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` is confirmed CONFIGURED AT SEVERITY 2 in the resolved config (16 react-hooks rules, `eslint-plugin-react-hooks@7.1.1` via `eslint-config-next@16.2.7`), so this is the rule finding nothing rather than the rule being absent. The fix preserved the decide-once property exactly as this entry demanded: the comment now in the file explains that the functional update was replaced rather than the guard deleted, because Strict Mode re-runs the effect after its own cleanup and setting the state unconditionally would flip a legitimate live attempt to LOCKED.
  ==> **RECORDED AS A DOCUMENTATION DEFECT IN ITS OWN RIGHT, NOT JUST TICKED OFF.** Phase 3a's own Current Stage header says it fixed "TWO RECOVERY FIXES PLUS ONE LINT ERROR", but this entry was never closed, so for a day Open Issues asserted a live lint error on a STUDENT-FACING file with students arriving the week of 2026-09-14. It was found only because 4b ran the linter and got an answer that disagreed with the prose. **THAT IS THE read-the-query-not-the-prose HAZARD AGAIN**: a document describing a check is not the check. <==
  **THE ORIGINAL ENTRY IS KEPT BELOW, because the reasoning about WHY the obvious fix was wrong is the part worth having.**
  ==> **PRE-EXISTING AND NOT FROM PHASE 2a: AN ESLINT ERROR IN `components/submission-live-gate.tsx`, WHICH IS STUDENT-FACING AND STUDENTS ARRIVE THE WEEK OF 2026-09-14.** Found on 2026-09-09 while linting the whole project during Phase 2a. Git confirms the file is untouched by that piece, so it predates it. The error is `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` at line 46: `setAllowed((prev) => (prev === null ? present : prev))` is called synchronously inside an effect body, which React flags as capable of triggering cascading renders. **THE EXISTING COMMENT ABOVE THAT LINE SAYS THE PATTERN IS DELIBERATE**, decided only once so that a re-run of the effect under React Strict Mode in development cannot flip a legitimate live attempt to locked, which is a real correctness concern and not a style choice. **SO THE FIX IS NOT OBVIOUS AND MUST NOT BE A REFLEX**: whoever takes it needs to preserve the decide-once property, and the safe shapes are a ref or a lazy initialiser rather than deleting the guard. **IT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT TOUCHED DURING PHASE 2a**, which was contracted to change nothing a user can see and had no business editing a student-facing live gate. **IT IS RECORDED HERE RATHER THAN LEFT IN A SESSION TRANSCRIPT** because a finding that lives only in a chat log gets rediscovered later as though it were new, and this one sits on the path a student walks into a graded submission. `npm run build` SUCCEEDS: this is a lint error, not a build failure, so nothing is broken today. <==
- ==> **STANDING RULE, ADDED 2026-09-09 AFTER IT ALMOST COST A FALSE PASS. A GREEN RESULT IS EVIDENCE ONLY WHEN YOU KNOW WHICH ARTIFACT PRODUCED IT.** During the Phase 2a parser work, `lib/rubric-xlsx.ts` was refactored to import its types from `lib/types.ts` using the `@/` path alias. The behavioural proof was then re-run by compiling that file with a standalone `tsc` and running the output. **THE COMPILE FAILED with `TS2307: Cannot find module '@/lib/types'`, because the `@/` alias is defined in tsconfig.json and a standalone `tsc` invocation does not read it. It therefore emitted nothing, and the runner silently executed the PREVIOUS build.** Every value and every refusal message printed correctly, because the pre-refactor code was genuinely correct. **A FAILED COMPILE FOLLOWED BY A STALE ARTIFACT LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A CLEAN RUN IF YOU ONLY READ THE OUTPUT OF THE COMMAND AFTER IT.** WHAT CAUGHT IT: printing the emitted files' MODIFICATION TIMESTAMPS next to the compile's exit code, and noticing the .js was older than the .ts it supposedly came from. **THE RULE: before trusting a build-and-run proof, delete the previous artifact first (`rm -f` the output), check the compiler's exit code, and print the artifact's timestamp. Do all three, because the first alone can be defeated by a cached output directory and the second alone does not tell you whether anything was written.** THIS IS THE GATE 5 LESSON IN A NEW COSTUME: a thing proved at the layer you changed is not proved at the layer that consumes it. Same shape as query 4 of gate-3b-tests.sql above, which was correct when written and stale when it mattered. <==
- ==> **RECORDED 2026-09-09 SO A FUTURE DEPENDENCY SCAN FINDS A DECISION RATHER THAN A SURPRISE: exceljs@4.4.0 IS THE APP'S SEVENTH RUNTIME DEPENDENCY, IT IS PINNED WITHOUT A CARET, AND IT IS 21 MONTHS WITHOUT A RELEASE.** Published 2024-12-20, MIT, not deprecated. It was chosen over the npm `xlsx` package, which carries CVE-2023-30533 and which SheetJS no longer publishes fixes to on npm. **THE PIN IS DELIBERATE AND EVERY OTHER DEPENDENCY CARRIES A `^`**: the parser's correctness is verified against this exact version's handling of cached formula results, and a silent minor bump is not something to discover through a wrong grade. THREE THINGS A SCAN WILL FLAG, ALL ASSESSED: (1) `npm audit` reports moderate GHSA-w5hq-g745-h8pq on `uuid`. **IT IS NOT REACHABLE.** The advisory affects uuid v3, v5 and v6 only when a `buf` argument is passed; exceljs has exactly three uuid call sites, all `uuidv4()` with no arguments, all in `cf-rule-ext-xform.js`, which is conditional-formatting WRITE support, and this app only ever reads. `npm audit fix --force` would downgrade to exceljs@3.4.0, a breaking change, and must not be run. (2) It pulls **96 packages** for one parser. (3) Three transitives are deprecated: `glob@7.2.3` and `fstream@1.0.12` via archiver and unzipper, and `uuid@8.3.2`. None carries an open advisory beyond the one above. Owner verified the reachability argument against the ExcelJS source independently on 2026-09-09. <==
- ==> **WHAT THE PHASE 2a UPLOAD SIZE GUARD ACTUALLY DELIVERS, WRITTEN OUT BECAUSE THE SHORT VERSION ("ZIP BOMBS ARE HANDLED") IS FALSE AND A FUTURE SESSION READING THE SHORT VERSION WILL NOT RE-EXAMINE IT.** `lib/rubric-xlsx.ts` refuses an upload on two limits before ExcelJS is asked anything, both defined in `lib/types.ts` and shared with the form so the two cannot drift: **MAX_RUBRIC_FILE_BYTES 512 KB** on disk, and **MAX_RUBRIC_UNCOMPRESSED_BYTES 20 MB** expanded. Grounded in measurement rather than habit: the two real files are 55,994 and 55,050 bytes on disk, 557,384 and 528,870 expanded, a natural ratio of about 10x. **WHAT IT CATCHES:** an honest workbook that is genuinely too big, a naive zip bomb that declares its true expanded size, and the realistic event, which is Tom uploading the wrong file. Each refusal names the fault and the limit in plain words. **WHAT IT DOES NOT CATCH, AND THIS IS THE PART THAT MATTERS: A CRAFTED ARCHIVE THAT LIES.** The uncompressed size in a zip's central directory is written by whoever made the file. The expanded check patches nothing and measures nothing; it READS A DECLARATION. An attacker declares 1 KB and ships a deflate stream that inflates to gigabytes, because the inflater works from the stream and never consults the declaration. **SO THE ONLY LIMIT THAT BINDS A HOSTILE FILE IS THE 512 KB BYTE CAP, AND DEFLATE'S MAXIMUM RATIO IS ABOUT 1032:1, WHICH MEANS 512 KB OF HOSTILE INPUT CAN STILL REACH ROUGHLY 500 MB IN THE FUNCTION.** **THAT RESIDUAL IS ACCEPTED, NOT SOLVED, AND THE REASONING IS:** the real fix is a bounded inflate, ExcelJS does not expose one, and replacing the unzip layer is not justified by this threat model, because the route sits behind `requireInstructorRequest()`, instructors are added only by a super admin, and no anonymous caller can reach it. **IF THAT CHANGES, THIS DECISION CHANGES**: opening rubric upload to a wider group, or to any unauthenticated path, is the trigger to revisit it. The owner made this call on 2026-09-09 after identifying the lying-central-directory limitation, which the first version of the code comments had overstated as a zip-bomb defence. **BOUNDARY FIXTURES EXIST AND ARE TWO-SIDED**: `just-over-the-size-cap.xlsx` is exactly cap plus one byte and must refuse ON SIZE specifically, and `near-the-size-cap-valid.xlsx` is a real 502,352-byte rubric that must still PARSE, because "refuses things over the cap" is also satisfied by a guard that refuses everything. Both read the cap out of `lib/types.ts` so they cannot disagree with it. **A SMALL FINDING FROM THAT BOUNDARY TEST, KEPT AS AN EXAMPLE OF WHY ONE-BYTE FIXTURES BEAT COMFORTABLE ONES:** `formatBytes()` originally rounded to nearest, so a file one byte over the cap produced "That file is 512 KB and the limit is 512 KB". It now rounds UP, so anything over the limit always displays as over it. A 3 MB test file would never have surfaced that. <==
- ==> **FIXED 2026-09-04. QUERY 4 OF supabase/gate-3b-tests.sql COULD NOT RUN, FOUND BY THE G5 RUN THE SAME DAY.** It is now one row PER CLASS, the shape query 5 already had, with the same eight counts and the same email switch and oldest class first. Kept here as the record of what was wrong. THE ORIGINAL PROBLEM: It errors with "more than one row returned by a subquery used as an expression". It was written when an instructor owned exactly one class and uses its `my_class` CTE as a SCALAR in nine places, and G6 made several classes normal operation. **NOTHING IS BROKEN IN THE APP and no G5 evidence was lost**, because query 5 of the same file answers the same question per class and ran fine. THE FIX was to rewrite query 4 per class the way query 5 already is, and it was DONE AFTER the run rather than during it, because editing a checks file mid-run would have meant the items already measured were measured against a different file. The comment above the query now carries the whole story, including that a query which STOPS is the benign failure: the dangerous version would have silently reported the oldest class as though it were the whole account, which is the exact shape of bug Gate 6 fixed in the application. This is the same shape of staleness as item 5 of the gate-4b-tests.sql handoff, which G6 at least rewrote in place with a note. <==
- ==> **DOCUMENTED 2026-09-04, AND IT STAYS HERE BECAUSE THE HAZARD IS PERMANENT RATHER THAN OUTSTANDING. THE PERMISSION-DENIED MESSAGE ON scenarios CARRIES A POSTGRES HINT THAT WOULD UNDO GATE 2 IF FOLLOWED.** Observed for the first time during the G5 student test on 2026-09-04, when a student asked the web API for persona_system_prompt. The full body is: code 42501, message "permission denied for table scenarios", hint "Grant the required privileges to the current role with: GRANT SELECT ON public.scenarios TO authenticated;". **THAT HINT IS EXACTLY THE WHOLE-TABLE GRANT SECTION 9 OF supabase/gate-2-policies.sql REVOKES, and running it would hand every student the hidden persona prompt and the case context in one line.** The correct response to this error is to RE-RUN SECTION 9 ON ITS OWN, which grants every column except the two secret ones. **A WARNING BLOCK IS NOW IN supabase/gate-2-policies.sql AT SECTION 9**, where whoever meets the error will actually be looking: it says do not run the hint, gives the standing query that finds a genuinely ungranted column, says to re-run section 9 alone rather than the whole file (sections 2 to 7 drop and re-create policies, and G3b and G4a have moved the policy surface since), and notes that a student asking for one of the two secret columns is the leak staying closed rather than a fault. Note also that the message names the TABLE and not the column, which is why the G5 expectation was written to accept either wording; a stricter expectation would have recorded a correct result as a mismatch. <==
- MINOR AND OPTIONAL, from the G5 run: public.class_exports holds an unused delete token for the historical class taken on 2026-09-02 by Gate 4b's substitution test. It authorises nothing: it is two days past a one-hour TTL, and its fingerprint no longer matches a class that has since grown from 40 sessions to 41. It can be left alone. It is recorded because it is a live example of the object the owner's 2026-09-04 ruling avoided creating a second one of, when he directed G5's crafted delete requests to take their export from the EMPTY class B3 rather than from the historical class.

- THE G1-TO-G3 NULL INVITE WINDOW IS CLOSED (2026-08-29). RESOLVED BY G3a. Both invite paths now set class_id, so a student who accepts an invite gets a class_members row at signup, and the repair backfill caught every invite and every student already through the hole, including null-student@yahoo.com. Kept here as the record of what the problem was: components/add-email-form.tsx used to insert { email, role } straight from the browser with no class_id, and a database-level guess was not available because single invites do not record added_by (only the server-side bulk-invite route sets it). The reason G2 could not simply tighten the three "auth.uid() is not null" read policies at the time was exactly this: a class-scoped read would have given any account from that window an empty dashboard, so the backfill and the policy change had to ship in the same piece, and in G3a they did.
- supabase/gate-1-class-spine.sql IS ONLY RE-RUNNABLE WHILE michaeltbanta@gmail.com EXISTS AS AN INSTRUCTOR. Its section 6 backfill opens by looking that account up in profiles and raises "Gate 1 stopped" if it is missing. Because the whole backfill is one DO block inside one script, that exception aborts the ENTIRE file, including the section 7 signup function and the section 8 revoke, so a later re-run (to re-apply the trigger, say) would apply nothing and report a confusing error. If that account is ever deleted or its role changes, edit the email in section 6 to another instructor before re-running. This does not affect the database as it stands; everything in the file has already been applied successfully.
- THREE OLDER MIGRATION FILES WOULD SILENTLY REVERT THE ISOLATION GATE IF RE-RUN, and this is the same shape of hazard as the gate-1 entry above. supabase/stage-2-data-model.sql, supabase/stage-8-reset-audit.sql and supabase/stage-8-assignment-delete-audit.sql all still contain their ORIGINAL public.is_instructor() policy bodies, because each one created the rules that G2, G3a and G3b later replaced in place. Every one of the three says in its own header that it is safe to run twice. THAT IS STILL TRUE OF EACH FILE IN ISOLATION AND IS NO LONGER TRUE IN SEQUENCE: re-running any of them today would put "any instructor" back on rules the gate tightened to "this instructor", with NO error, nothing visible on screen, and no failing check anywhere. The only thing that would catch it is query 1 or query 2 of supabase/gate-3b-tests.sql. IF ONE OF THEM EVER HAS TO BE RE-RUN, run supabase/gate-2-policies.sql, then supabase/gate-3a-policies.sql, then supabase/gate-3b-policies.sql afterwards, in that order. Nothing is wrong with the database as it stands; this is a note about what not to do.
- supabase/phase-5-consent.sql NOW OWNS public.handle_new_user(), AND RE-RUNNING supabase/gate-1-class-spine.sql WOULD SILENTLY REMOVE THE CONSENT CHECK. Same shape as the three files in the entry above, and it is here rather than only in a code comment because Open Issues is where a session actually looks. Phase 5 replaced the signup function with the Gate 1 body PLUS one block: it refuses a signup carrying no accepted version and writes the public.consents row in the same transaction as the account, which is the entire enforcement behind the registration checkbox (the checkbox and the disabled button are the browser's two layers, and a crafted request goes straight past both). Gate 1 section 7 still holds the older body. Re-running Gate 1 would restore open, UNRECORDED registration with no error, nothing visible on screen and no failing check anywhere: every existing test still passes, because every existing test predates consent. THE ONLY THING THAT WOULD CATCH IT is a signup that produced no row in public.consents. IF GATE 1 EVER HAS TO BE RE-RUN, RUN supabase/phase-5-consent.sql AFTERWARDS. Gate 1 section 7 carries the same warning at the top of the function. Nothing is wrong with the database as it stands; this is a note about what not to do.
- FOUR PASSAGES IN TOM'S LEGAL TEXT WERE LISTED FOR HIM RATHER THAN FIXED (2026-09-08), which is what the Phase 5 kickoff asked for. THE OWNER IS TAKING THEM TO TOM; the app is built against the text as it stands either way, and none of them blocks anything. (1) PRIVACY POLICY LINE 22 reads "Your account is not accessed through or linked to a university course", which is the opposite of how Relii is used and sits immediately before the sentence saying faculty may access completed role-plays. Looks like a dropped word or an inverted "not". (2) THE AGREEMENT NAMES ITS OWN BUTTON TWICE, DIFFERENTLY: line 7 says clicking "I Agree", line 51 says clicking "Submit", and our button says "Create account". The button was deliberately left alone, because the checkbox label is the operative sentence. (3) THREE NAMES FOR ONE DOCUMENT: "Relii.io Pilot Agreement" (line 1), "Relii Website Pilot Research Agreement" (line 51), and the privacy policy's "Pilot Research Program". (4) PRIVACY POLICY LINE 32 says recordings "can be scheduled for deletion by you"; a student can delete a practice today, but nothing SCHEDULES a deletion, so it is a promise slightly ahead of the product. IF ANY OF THESE COMES BACK REVISED: replace the file in docs/, bump the matching constant in lib/legal.ts, and re-run the text-equivalence check whose command is in Current Stage above.
- A CRAFTED SIGNUP THAT SUPPLIES A VERSION STRING IS ACCEPTED, AND THIS IS A KNOWN ACCEPTED LIMIT RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT (2026-09-08). The database refuses a signup with NO accepted version, which is what makes the checkbox consent rather than decoration, and that was tested. It cannot refuse one that names a version, because signup is a public browser call with a public anon key and the anon key is in the client bundle by design. CLOSING IT ENTIRELY means moving registration behind our own server route with the service-role key and switching off anon signups in the Supabase dashboard: that was priced during the Phase 5 plan and REJECTED for launch week, as a rewrite of the most dangerous path in the app plus a second dashboard setting nothing in the codebase keeps in step (the same stale-copy pattern as the password rules two entries below). The residual risk is small: the address must already be on the invite list, and a request that transmits an acceptance is itself an assertion of acceptance. Revisit after launch if it ever matters.

- ==> ONE FOREIGN KEY PATH PER TABLE PAIR IS LOAD BEARING, NOT TIDINESS. ADDING A SECOND FOREIGN KEY BETWEEN TWO TABLES THE APP JOINS WILL BLANK A PAGE WITH NO ERROR ANYWHERE. <== This cost Relii two production outages on 2026-08-31 and 2026-09-01 and it is the single most repeatable mistake in the schema. POSTGRES ALLOWS TWO FOREIGN KEYS BETWEEN THE SAME PAIR OF TABLES. THE SUPABASE API DOES NOT: it resolves an embedded join, written in the app as "scenarios ( title, persona_name )", by finding THE foreign key between the two tables, and with two it returns HTTP 300 and PGRST201 instead of rows. Every embedded read in this codebase destructures only `data` and ignores `error`, so the page renders EMPTY: nothing on screen, nothing in the logs, and every row still perfectly intact in the database. THE THREE PAIRS THAT CARRY A COMPOSITE KEY AND MUST STAY AT ONE PATH EACH: assignments to scenarios, scenarios to rubrics, sessions to scenarios. THE THREE EMBEDDED READS THAT DEPEND ON IT, which is the whole list as of 2026-09-01: app/student/page.tsx, app/instructor/page.tsx and app/instructor/assignments/page.tsx, all three assignments to scenarios. IF YOU EVER NEED A SECOND KEY between a joined pair, the choice is to make the new one REPLACE the old (what Gate 4a did in the end) or to name the key in every embed as "scenarios!the_key_name ( ... )" (what Gate 4a tried first and threw away, because a convention enforced by nothing is a permanent trap). HOW TO CHECK IN TEN SECONDS, and do this rather than reasoning about it: request /rest/v1/<table>?select=id,<other>(<col>) with no hint and see whether it returns 200 with rows or 300 with PGRST201. Nothing in the build, the typechecker or any SQL test will catch this; only that request will.

- FOUR OLDER FILES STOP BEING RE-RUNNABLE THE MOMENT GATE 4a RUNS, AND THIS IS THE GOOD CASE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS A SEPARATE ENTRY FROM THE THREE SILENT-REVERT FILES ABOVE. supabase/schema.sql, supabase/stage-3-seed.sql, supabase/piece-1-jordan-seed.sql and supabase/piece-2a-test-scenario.sql all insert into rubrics, scenarios, assignments, sessions or allowed_emails WITHOUT a class_id. Once Gate 4a makes class_id required, every one of those inserts FAILS LOUDLY with a not-null violation, which is exactly what should happen: a row in no class is invisible to every page and manageable by nobody. Contrast the entry above, where the danger is that re-running a file succeeds and silently un-scopes the gate. THE COROLLARY, and it is the one to remember: supabase/schema.sql line 87 RE-CREATES public.is_instructor(), so re-running schema.sql resurrects the function Gate 4a deletes. It would come back uncalled by anything, so nothing would be less safe, but query 3 of supabase/gate-4a-tests.sql would start failing and nobody would know why.
- A SMALL UPSIDE OF DROPPING is_instructor(), recorded because it makes the hazard two entries above QUIETER rather than louder. supabase/stage-2-data-model.sql, supabase/stage-8-reset-audit.sql and supabase/stage-8-assignment-delete-audit.sql all still contain their original is_instructor() policy bodies, and re-running any of them today silently un-scopes rules the gate tightened. Once Gate 4a deletes the function, those three files ERROR OUT instead, with "function public.is_instructor() does not exist". A file that fails loudly is strictly better than one that succeeds and quietly reverts a security fix. The recovery instruction in that entry (run gate-2, then gate-3a, then gate-3b policies afterwards) still stands, and gaining a loud failure does not make any of those files safe to re-run.

- ==> A REVERT UNDOES ONLY WHAT ITS OWN PIECE ADDED. That is now a rule, it came out of a real defect found while building Gate 4a, and all four revert files were audited against it on 2026-08-31. <== THE DEFECT: supabase/gate-2-revert.sql ended with "drop function if exists public.instructs_class(uuid)", which was correct when Gate 2 was the newest piece. Gate 3a then built public.can_read_class(uuid) ON TOP of instructs_class (it calls it, deliberately, so there is one definition of "owns this class"), and three Gate 3a read policies use can_read_class. Because these are old-style SQL functions with string bodies, POSTGRES RECORDS NO DEPENDENCY ON WHAT A FUNCTION BODY CALLS, so that drop SUCCEEDED and can_read_class then failed at RUNTIME with "function instructs_class(uuid) does not exist". Every read of rubrics, scenarios and assignments through the security rules would have errored at once: the student home, the student session and results pages, the instructor dashboard selector and Manage Assignments. All after the file reported success. It had been true since 2026-08-29 and nothing would have caught it.
  THE OWNER'S RULING, 2026-08-31: DELETE THE LINE, do not make it conditional. A leftover helper nobody calls costs nothing; a conditional is a second thing that can be wrong later. Done. Section 5 of that file now carries the one-line reason instead of the drop. A SECOND THING THE DELETION FIXED: section 4 of supabase/gate-3a-revert.sql re-creates a rule that CALLS instructs_class(), so running gate-2-revert and then gate-3a-revert used to fail outright.
  THE AUDIT OF THE OTHER THREE, so nobody repeats it. supabase/gate-3b-revert.sql: CLEAN, it drops no function at all, only its own two policies. supabase/gate-4a-revert.sql: CLEAN, it drops only the constraints and the requirement Gate 4a itself added, and re-creates is_instructor(). supabase/gate-3a-revert.sql: CORRECT TODAY AND ANNOTATED, because it is the same shape waiting to happen. Its "drop function if exists public.can_read_class(uuid)" is right only while nothing outside Gate 3a calls can_read_class, and a search on 2026-08-31 confirmed the only three callers are Gate 3a's own read rules, which sections 1 to 3 of that file replace first (Gate 3b's two invite rules use instructs_class, and Gate 4a added no rule). THE TEST IS WRITTEN INTO THE FILE ABOVE THAT LINE: if any later piece writes a rule or a function that calls can_read_class(), delete the line the same way the Gate 2 one was deleted, because Postgres will not stop it.

- AN INSTRUCTOR ACCOUNT CAN NO LONGER BE DELETED FROM THE SUPABASE DASHBOARD IN ONE STEP, and this is G1's on delete restrict working as designed rather than a bug. Once an instructor registers they own a class, and classes.instructor_id is ON DELETE RESTRICT, so deleting the auth user fails with a foreign key error. There is no in-app path either, because app/api/instructor/delete-student/route.ts (line 80) refuses to delete anything but a student. To remove one by hand: delete their class row first (delete from public.classes where instructor_id = ...), then the allowed_emails invite row, then the user under Authentication, Users, which cascades the profile. A student needs none of this, because class_members.user_id cascades and the existing Roster delete-student control cleans up fully.
