THE PROPER PATH IS NOW BUILT AND IS G4b's: the super admin section removes an instructor account once it owns no class, blanking allowed_emails.added_by first (see the next entry) and deleting their invite row. It refuses while they still own a class, which is this same restrict rule surfaced in words instead of a raw foreign key error, and the class is dealt with first by handing it over or by the export-first delete. Until G4b is run and pushed, the manual steps above are still the only way.

- ==> allowed_emails.added_by IS A FOREIGN KEY TO auth.users WITH NO "on delete" RULE, WHICH MEANS NO ACTION, SO POSTGRES REFUSES TO DELETE ANY USER WHOSE ID IS STILL IN THAT COLUMN. <== Found while building Gate 4b's instructor remove, not by reading the schema for its own sake. It matters in exactly one situation and it is a realistic one: an instructor who imported a CSV (the bulk invite route is the only path that records added_by; single invites leave it empty) into a class that is later REASSIGNED rather than deleted leaves those invite rows behind still pointing at them, and deleting their account then fails with a raw foreign key error. Gate 4b's remove route blanks the column on those rows first. THAT IS THE REPAIR AND NOT A SCHEMA CHANGE, deliberately: added_by is informational, it is already nullable, and every other author column in this schema (created_by on rubrics, scenarios and assignments) is "on delete set null", so the route does by hand what the rest of the schema does by rule. If an account delete ever fails with a foreign key error naming allowed_emails, this is why. Nothing is wrong with the database as it stands.

- AN INSTRUCTOR WHO OWNS NO CLASS IS A DEAD-END ACCOUNT, AND THE APP REFUSES CLEANLY RATHER THAN BREAKING. Seen for the first time on 2026-09-02, when both Gate 4b throwaway instructors ended up class-less (one handed its class away, the other received it and then had it deleted). Inviting a student gives "Your instructor account is not linked to a class, so invites cannot be sent. Please contact support." and generating a persona gives "Your instructor account is not linked to a class, so this cannot be done." THOSE ARE THE GATE 3a GUARDS WORKING: they refuse to write a row that would belong to no class and that nobody could manage. NOTHING IN THE APP CAN GIVE AN INSTRUCTOR A CLASS AFTER REGISTRATION, because the signup rule fires once at first registration and no route writes classes. THE TWO WAYS OUT, both built in Gate 4b: hand them a class from the super admin section (only possible if their own class is empty, which a class-less instructor trivially satisfies), or remove the account. The overview shows the state as "Owns no class. This account can be removed." It is the instructor-side twin of the orphaned student, with the difference that it is recoverable.

- ==> AN INSTRUCTOR MAY OWN SEVERAL CLASSES, AND THE OLD STANDING CHECK NOW MEANS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT IT USED TO. REWRITTEN BY G6, 2026-09-02. <== This entry used to read "every instructor is assumed to own exactly one class, and nothing in the database enforces it", with select instructor_id, count(*) from public.classes group by instructor_id having count(*) > 1; documented as a check that should ALWAYS RETURN NO ROWS. ==> AFTER G6 THAT QUERY RETURNS ROWS AS NORMAL OPERATION, one for every instructor holding more than one class. DO NOT "REPAIR" IT. <== WHAT THE OLD ENTRY WAS ABOUT, kept because the hazard is real and only the mechanism changed: getInstructorClassId() took an instructor's OLDEST class and stopped at one, and every instructor page and write path went through it, so a second class was silently chosen against and, because the historical class is the oldest in the system, an instructor handed it would have found THEIR OWN CONTENT INVISIBLE with nothing on screen saying why. G6 REPLACED THAT FUNCTION with resolveWorkingClassId() in lib/instructor-auth.ts, which reads the instructor's remembered selection and honours it ONLY after checking it against classes.instructor_id on that request, falling back to their oldest owned class otherwise. THE CHECK THAT REPLACES IT is query 4 of supabase/gate-6-checks.sql: a STUDENT must be in exactly one class (select from class_members group by user_id having count(*) > 1), which is a real invariant the schema does not enforce, since class_members is keyed (class_id, user_id) and permits two rows. Gate 4b's cascade delete already depends on it. Query 5 of that file is its companion: allowed_emails.class_id and the class_members row must agree about where a student is, because the add-student route moves them in two separate API calls that cannot be one transaction.

- THE PD-8g SCHEMA STOP IS CLEARED. supabase/pd-8g-company-name.sql was run in Supabase by the owner on 2026-08-03, and PD-8g is now pushed and live. VERIFIED, not assumed, and in both halves: the column was read back from production (select title, company_name from assignments returned 200 with company_name null on every existing row, which is the intended "no company named" state), and the deploy was confirmed by CONTENT rather than by a stylesheet filename (the live login page carries the Beta badge markup and the deployed stylesheet emits --text-wordmark:1.5rem). Nothing is outstanding here.
- BOTH PD-8g QUESTIONS ARE CLOSED, ruled by the owner on 2026-08-03. The "Persona Role" (the gatekeeper / decision-maker select) and "Persona job title" (the persona's job title) split on the creation form is CONFIRMED CORRECT and is not to be revisited. The rename was then PROPAGATED: the read-only locked-pair labels on the edit and compare screens read "Meeting type and Persona Role", so all three screens agree.

- A NOTE ON HOW TO CHECK A DEPLOY, learned the hard way in PD-8d (2026-08-01) and then corrected again. Earlier pieces confirmed a deploy by fetching the live login page and comparing its stylesheet FILENAME against the one the local build produced. That cost this session about forty minutes of believing a good deploy had failed. TWO things were wrong with it. First, the local build being compared against was from an INTERMEDIATE source state, not the pushed commit, so of course the hashes differed; built from the same source, local and Vercel hashes do match. Second, and more useful: a markup-only change produces NO stylesheet change at all, because Tailwind only emits classes that are used and reapplying existing classes adds none. So the stylesheet is not a deploy signal in general.
  WHAT TO DO INSTEAD: rebuild locally from the pushed commit first, then compare CONTENT rather than filenames, grepping the live asset for a string the new work introduced. If the change is markup-only on an authenticated page, accept that it cannot be verified from outside without a login and say so plainly rather than inventing a proxy signal. Vercel also took roughly forty minutes on one deploy in this sprint where every other took about a minute, so slowness alone is not evidence of failure.

- Scoring calibration: the content scorer uses coarse anchor values (0, 3, 6, 8, 10) and leans a little generous on partial attempts. That was the right call for a consistent demo; revisit the calibration and granularity in the full scoring stage, alongside the OpenAI vs Claude bake-off.
- Voice transcript trust: in voice mode the transcript is captured in the browser and then written by the server, so a determined student could in theory alter their own transcript before scoring. Acceptable for practice runs with about 25 trusted students. Full server-side capture comes naturally with Stage 5 (recording the audio) and Stage 7 (graded submissions).
- Voice cost: voice spend scales with minutes. Prompt caching and the gpt-realtime vs gpt-realtime-mini cost bake-off are deferred (see the voice research note). Watch the OpenAI usage dashboard, especially before any expansion toward 270 users.
- Stage 7 (lifecycle) is now complete. The instructor-facing reset of a locked/interrupted submission (always a Stage 8 item, not Stage 7) is now BUILT in Stage 8 piece 2 as a guarded dashboard button, so the manual row delete is retired for the common case; the manual steps remain in supabase/stage-7-submission.sql only as a fallback. Voice resilience (graceful mid-call reconnect and non-reload network-drop recovery) was intentionally removed from Stage 7 and is parked pending Tom's feedback.
- Graded-submission lock window (tuning; live; resting value pending Tom): on 2026-07-07 the graded-submission live/lock window (SUBMISSION_LIVE_WINDOW_MS in lib/submission.ts) was reduced from 60 minutes to 30 minutes, deployed and verified live on https://relii.vercel.app. This window caps how long one graded attempt can run and sets how soon an abandoned in-progress attempt flips to "Interrupted" and becomes resettable by the instructor on the dashboard. Graded attempts run about 15 minutes, so 30 minutes leaves comfortable headroom for an honest attempt while letting an instructor recover an abandoned one sooner. It is a value-only change (no logic and no schema change); isSubmissionLocked still applies only to kind = submission, so practices are never locked and are unaffected. Verified live on 2026-07-07: a normal graded submission completed within 30 minutes still scored; an in-progress attempt backdated past 30 minutes showed "Interrupted" on the instructor dashboard and could be Reset; a practice was unaffected. CONFIRMED AND KEPT by Tom on 2026-08-02: the 30 minutes is SETTLED, not a resting value awaiting sign-off, and this entry no longer describes an open question. It stays fully reversible (change the one constant back) but nothing is pending on it.
- Orphaned Storage recordings (optional cleanup, not a blocker): a few video files whose session rows were deleted directly in SQL during earlier testing still sit in the session-videos bucket (seen as more files in a student's folder than that student's current sessions). They are unreachable by any student (playback requires a matching session row plus an ownership check, so no link is ever minted) and harmless, just unreferenced bytes. They were not created by any cleanup feature; the cap, submission, student-delete, and manual deletes all remove the recording before the row. Optional tidy-up: a read-only audit can list exactly which files have no matching session row, then remove them. Going forward, always delete through the app so Storage stays in sync.
- TEST DATA TO REMOVE BEFORE THE DAWN DEMO. Two groups of it now:
  - From piece 2a: two "TEST - " scenarios (Riley and the disabled one), plus the throwaway assignment and practice sessions created to test them. The exact three-step removal, which must start with the app's own assignment Delete so the recordings go too, is written out in the piece 2a entry above and at the bottom of supabase/piece-2a-test-scenario.sql.
  - From piece 2c: CLEARED, and no longer a manual SQL job. The roughly ten leftover candidate rows from the 2c build were removed with the new bulk "Discard all unused test generations" control on the scaffolding page (piece 2d), which is now the supported way to do this and is safer than the SQL that used to be written out here: it is scoped so it can never touch Alex, Jordan, a switched-on chosen persona, or anything role-played or assigned, and it shows the exact count before you confirm. Any future test generations are cleared the same way, so this stops being a recurring cleanup note. If a scenario ever refuses to delete, that is delete-if-unused working: a session or an assignment still points at it, so clear those through the app's own assignment Delete first, exactly as with the 2a data below.
- TWO STANDING CONSTRAINTS FROM PD-2, both by design and neither a bug. First, lib/persona-assembly.ts (the fixed assembly template) is now effectively FROZEN while editable candidates exist. The edit route's round-trip guard re-assembles a candidate's unedited stored fields and requires them to reproduce its saved text before it will overwrite anything, so changing that template makes existing version 2 candidates unrebuildable until they are regenerated. If the template ever genuinely needs to change (for example if Part C or Part C-chain in docs/Relii-Piece2b-Persona-Generation-Prompt.md is revised), expect to regenerate any candidate pairs that are still mid-decision; already-chosen live scenarios are unaffected, because their text was written once and is never rebuilt. Second, candidate rows generated BEFORE PD-2 (generation_fields version 1) that are DECISION MAKERS cannot be edited: version 1 stored no chain either way and nothing on the row points back at its gatekeeper, so the app refuses rather than guessing and silently deleting the gatekeeper narrative. The fix is to regenerate that case. Version 1 GATEKEEPERS are unaffected and edit normally, since a gatekeeper never has a chain.
- The one em dash left in the code (the no-score placeholder on the student home): FIXED in PD-3, which is the piece that next touched app/student/page.tsx, exactly as this note planned. It is now a plain hyphen. Nothing left to do here.
- THE PASSWORD RULES ON THE CREATE ACCOUNT PAGE ARE A HAND-KEPT COPY OF A SUPABASE DASHBOARD SETTING, and there is no mechanism keeping them honest. app/register/page.tsx states the minimum length in two places (the `minLength` attribute and the helper text) and states that leaked passwords are rejected in a third; all three mirror Authentication > Sign In / Up, which lives in the dashboard and not in this repo. THE SUPABASE AUTHENTICATION SETTINGS ARE THE SOURCE OF TRUTH, not this codebase: the minimum length was changed from 7 to 8 in the dashboard on 2026-08-04 and "Prevent use of leaked passwords" is ON, and NEITHER VALUE EXISTS ANYWHERE IN THIS REPO, so the register page copy mirrors both by hand and will silently drift if either is changed again. Both were corrected to 8 on 2026-08-04 and are right today. If anyone changes the minimum length or switches "Prevent use of leaked passwords" off, this page will keep asserting the old rule and NOTHING will fail: no build error, no failing test, no warning. IF THAT SETTING IS EVER CHANGED, EDIT THIS PAGE IN THE SAME SITTING. The same shape of risk applies wherever the app narrates a fact it does not own, which is why the reset controls and the video item are the two precedents named in the code comment there. A real fix (reading the setting at runtime, or asserting it in a test) was not built, because it is more machinery than a one-line rule is worth today; this note is the mitigation.
- Safari shows a harmless "EmptyRanges" ReferenceError in the console around media elements. It is internal to Safari (not in our code or any dependency), does not affect behavior, and does not surface to students in production (no dev overlay). No action needed.

- THE G3a TEST DATA IS CLEANED UP (2026-08-29). The owner deleted null-student@yahoo.com and removed every throwaway invited account created while proving the nine write paths. THERE IS NO STANDING STUDENT TEST ACCOUNT ANY MORE, and that is a decision, not an oversight: student-side testing for G3b and G5 uses an existing student account or a freshly invited one, made at the time it is needed. Two consequences worth knowing before anyone goes looking. First, query A2 in supabase/gate-3a-tests.sql searches for null-student@yahoo.com BY NAME, so it now returns zero rows; that is the account being gone, not a repair failure, and the file says so at that query. Second, THE AUDIT ROWS FROM THE RESET AND DELETE TESTS STAY, and should not be tidied away: submission_resets and assignment_deletes are a permanent log, so their test entries are the log working correctly rather than leftovers.

## Phase 3c (persona voice) handoff

==> **SUPERSEDED AND CLOSED. DO NOT BUILD WHAT THIS DESCRIBES.** T1 shipped on 2026-09-15 (commit `8416926`) in a different shape, by owner ruling of 2026-09-14: **two fixed voices on the ASSIGNMENT**, not a picker on the persona, and no control on the AI Persona Framework page. **Item 1 (the control belongs in `chosen-persona-entry.tsx`), item 3 (the voice wants a column on `public.scenarios`), item 4 (copy the toggle routes) and item 7 (price the preview button) are all moot.** Item 5 was also simply wrong: it names `app/api/voice/start/route.ts`, which mints nothing. **Item 2 is still useful history and item 8's two wording fixes SHIPPED, along with a third.** Kept in full because Current Stage's account of what changed refers to it. <==

Written on 2026-09-11 at the end of Phase 3b, while the reasons are still in view. **STILL OUTSTANDING IN PHASE 3 AFTER THIS: persona voice, and that is all.** The two renames, the on and off toggle, and gatekeeper pairing are closed.

==> **1. WHERE THE VOICE CONTROL BELONGS, WHICH THE KICKOFF ASKED THIS HANDOFF TO NAME.** `components/chosen-persona-entry.tsx`, beside `<PersonaToggle>`. That component is one list entry for one CHOSEN persona, it is rendered by `app/instructor/scaffold/page.tsx` and, separately, by `scripts/check-persona-list.mjs`, so anything added there is measurable without a browser. It is also the only surface in the app that lists EVERY chosen persona however old, which is the property a voice picker needs for the same reason the toggle needed it. <==

==> **2. THE VOICE IS ONE GLOBAL CONSTANT TODAY, AND THERE IS EXACTLY ONE LINE TO CHANGE.** `REALTIME_VOICE = "cedar"` at `lib/realtime.ts` line 18, consumed once at line 77 as `output: { voice: REALTIME_VOICE }`. Every persona in the app currently speaks in the same voice. Phase 3c's whole job is to make that value come from the persona instead of from the module. Nothing else reads it. <==

==> **3. THE TRAP, AND IT IS THE SAME ONE RULING 6 WAS ABOUT. DO NOT STORE THE VOICE IN `generation_fields`.**

  It looks like the free option, because it needs no migration, and Phase 3b put two keys there happily. It is wrong for voice, and the reason is already written into this piece: **an Alex and Jordan pair imported into an instructor's class would carry `generation_fields` NULL**. A persona whose voice lives in that JSON would have no voice, and the fallback would silently be the global constant again. Every value `chosen-persona-entry.tsx` displays comes from a real COLUMN for exactly this reason.

  `chosen_at` and `chain_from_scenario_id` are safe there because both are **provenance about a generation**, and a row with no generation has nothing to record. A voice is a property of the PERSONA, and every persona has one. **So the voice wants a column on `public.scenarios`, which means Phase 3c has a migration and Phase 3b did not.** Say so up front rather than discovering it halfway. <==

**4. THE TOGGLE ROUTES ARE THE RIGHT MODEL FOR THE WRITE, WITH ONE DELIBERATE DIFFERENCE.** `app/api/instructor/scaffold/enable/route.ts` and its `disable` twin show the shape: `requireInstructorRequest`, then `refuseOutsideClass`, then the guard on `generation_group_id`, then a write scoped to one id. **COPY THE FIRST TWO AND THINK HARD ABOUT THE THIRD.** The group-id guard exists to keep Alex, Jordan and hand-written scenarios off the AI Persona Framework page, which is ruling 3 and is right for a toggle. If Tom ever wants to change ALEX's voice, that guard refuses him, and the control cannot live on this page at all. Decide which of the two Phase 3c is before writing the route: a voice for generated personas only, or a voice for every scenario.

**5. THE VOICE MUST REACH THE CALL THROUGH THE SERVER, NOT THE BROWSER.** `app/api/voice/start/route.ts` already loads the scenario row (it reads `enabled` and `class_id` for its guards), so it is the natural place to read a voice column and hand it down. The realtime session is configured server-side in `lib/realtime.ts`; the browser never sees the OpenAI key and must never start choosing voices for itself.

**6. WHAT A VOICE CHANGE DOES NOT TOUCH, so nobody goes looking.** It changes no stored transcript, no score, and no assembled text: `lib/persona-assembly.ts` is frozen and knows nothing about audio. An existing recording keeps the voice it was recorded with, which is correct, because the recording is evidence of what actually happened.

**7. COST.** Voice spend scales with minutes and is already flagged in Open Issues. A voice PICKER invites sampling, and every sample is a realtime session. If Phase 3c adds a preview button, price it before building it, and consider a short canned phrase rather than a live call.

**8. TWO WORDING FIXES THE IMPORT SESSION FOUND, RECORDED BY THE OWNER AS FIXES FOR 3c RATHER THAN ACTED ON. NO CODE WAS CHANGED FOR EITHER.**

  ==> **THE FIRST IS A FALSE STATEMENT AN INSTRUCTOR READS, AND IT HAS BEEN FALSE SINCE PHASE 2b-1.** The View Persona page says a chosen persona is "now available in the create-assignment list as a **conversation-only case (it is never scored)**" (`app/instructor/scaffold/compare/[groupId]/page.tsx` line 267), and the undecided card says "neither is scored (these are conversation-only cases with no rubric)" (line 320). **GRADING READS `assignments.rubric_id`, NOT `scenarios.rubric_id`** (`app/api/practice/submit/route.ts` line 119), so any persona can be graded once a rubric is attached to the assignment built on it. The code already knows this: the comment at `app/api/instructor/scaffold/generate/route.ts` line 228 says in as many words that the old claim "IS NOW WRONG" and was corrected there. **THE SAME WRONG SENTENCE SURVIVED ON SCREEN IN TWO PLACES**, which is the ordinary way a corrected comment and an uncorrected string drift apart. Fix both strings together, and note that the sentence is read at its most misleading on an imported persona, whose `rubric_id` really is null. <==

  **THE SECOND IS SMALLER AND IS ABOUT IMPORTED ROWS.** The list entry line reads `{chainRoleLabel(chainRole)}, generated {date}` (`components/chosen-persona-entry.tsx` line 43). **A COPIED PERSONA WAS NOT GENERATED**, and the date it prints is the date of the COPY, not of the original: Tom's Alex reads "Not recorded, generated Sep 11, 2026" for a persona hand-written in June 2026. "Added" is the honest word for a row that may have been generated, imported or hand-written, and it costs one string.

## Phase 3 (the rest) handoff

Written on 2026-09-11 at the end of Phase 4b, and **EXTENDED THE SAME DAY at the end of the timezone session (items 10 to 13 are new)**. The Phase 4b handoff below it is kept as history; its items 3, 5, 7 and 9 are the ones 4b acted on, and all four are now closed. **STILL OUTSTANDING IN PHASE 3** (TRUE WHEN WRITTEN, SUPERSEDED 2026-09-11 BY PHASE 3b: only persona voice is left, see the Phase 3c handoff): the two renames, gatekeeper pairing, persona voice, and enable or disable any scenario. **ITEM 7'S PRE-LAUNCH DEPENDENCY IS NOW DISCHARGED**: the timezone session landed in `73185da`, so nothing stands between here and the rest of Phase 3.

==> **1. THE ONE-LINE VERSION: PHASE 4 IS COMPLETE, AND A STORED SCORE IS NOW A DOCUMENT WITH TWO AUTHORS ON IT.** Every line of a reviewed score carries a level that may be the instructor's, a comment that is always the AI's, an optional note that is always the instructor's, and a marker saying which of the two chose the level. **PHASE 3's REMAINING WORK IS ALL ABOUT SCENARIOS, AND SCENARIOS SIT UPSTREAM OF ALL OF THAT.** The items below are the places where a scenario change reaches a grade somebody has already been given. <==

==> **2. CRITERION IDS ARE LOAD BEARING AND CRITERION NAMES ARE NOT. THIS IS THE FIRST THING TO KNOW BEFORE TOUCHING A RENAME.** `components/rubric-score.tsx` anchors and keys every section by criterion ID, `buildRows()` falls back to the raw id when the rubric cannot explain a stored line, and the CSV's Item column does the same.

  **SO A RENAME THAT CHANGES A DISPLAY NAME IS SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION**, and the renderer picks the new name up on the next page load with nothing to migrate. **A RENAME THAT CHANGES OR RENUMBERS AN ID IS NOT.** Ids encode position (`3-close` in Discovery, `4-close` in Presentation), so renumbering is exactly the operation that looks like tidying. Every score already stored against the old id becomes unexplainable: it still renders, and still shows its points and its comment, but **under the raw id in place of a name**. That is the degradation rather than a crash, which is why it would ship quietly. <==

**3. THE RENDERER WALKS THE SCORE, NOT THE RUBRIC, AND PHASE 3 MUST NOT "FIX" THAT DIRECTION.** It looks backwards and it is deliberate: the score is the record of what was actually graded, and the rubric is looked up only to put a name and a description on each line. Walking the rubric instead would silently DROP any stored item whose criterion has left it, which is a student's mark disappearing from their own results page. The same ruling is made twice more, in `buildReviewedScore()` (where the same case is a REFUSAL, because that function is about to write a grade) and in the CSV. If a scenario change ever makes criteria come and go, these three are the places that already decided what to do about it.

==> **4. THE RUBRIC REACHES THE STUDENT THROUGH `assignments.rubric_id` AND NOTHING ELSE, AND THIS IS THE SHARPEST EDGE FOR "ENABLE OR DISABLE ANY SCENARIO".** `app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx` computes `conversationOnly` as "the assignment names no rubric". **IF DISABLING A SCENARIO IS IMPLEMENTED BY ANYTHING THAT NULLS OR ROUTES AROUND THAT COLUMN, `conversationOnly` FLIPS TRUE**, and a student holding a real reviewed grade is shown "Conversation-only practice, no score" while `sessions.score` still carries their instructor's number underneath. **THIS IS NAMED AS THE HAZARD TO CHECK, NOT AS A PREDICTION**: nothing is wrong today, and no current code path does this. The check is cheap: disable a scenario that has a graded submission behind it, then open that submission's results page as the student. <==

**5. `reviewed_at` IS A COLUMN AND NEVER A STATUS, AND THE THING TO RE-READ IS THE CONSTRAINT.** `sessions_status_check` still permits exactly `in_progress`, `completed`, `scored`, and `lib/assignment-sessions.ts` is TOTAL only while those three are all there are. **IF ENABLE OR DISABLE A SCENARIO INTRODUCES A NEW STATUS rather than a new column, `isUnfinishedSession` stops being total and its `pg_constraint` measurement stops being true.** Read the constraint, not the TypeScript; the query is in `supabase/phase-3a-baseline.sql`. Phase 4 was tempted by exactly this and chose a column both times.

**6. `scored_by` IS RECOMPUTED AGAINST THE STORED ORIGINAL ON EVERY SAVE, AND ANYTHING THAT REWRITES A SCORE MUST PRESERVE THAT.** It reads `"instructor"` exactly when the chosen level differs from the AI's, and goes back to `"ai"` when a level is put back. It is not a latch and not a running tally. **A Phase 3 change that rewrote stored scores without going through `buildReviewedScore()` would make the flag lie in both directions**: an unflagged line a human did choose, or a flag on a line they did not. The student-facing consequence is in `components/rubric-score.tsx`, and it is the sentence 4b exists to prevent.

==> **7. TOM CAN NOW REVIEW REAL STUDENT WORK, AND THE PROCESS RULE THAT SAID OTHERWISE IS RETIRED.** The between-4a-and-4b rule in the old handoff is spent: nobody has to be told anything, because the student surface now labels every comment and flags every changed level. `reviewed_at` is null on every row in the database, so no real grade was ever shown in the broken state. **THE REMAINING PRE-LAUNCH DEPENDENCY IS THE TIMEZONE SESSION**, which is small, is the owner's already-stated next step, and should land before students register the week of 2026-09-14. <==

**8. WHAT 4b DELIBERATELY DID NOT COVER, STATED SO IT IS NOT ASSUMED.** No score with a HALF value was rendered live this session: Tom's Presentation rubric happens to award whole numbers on every rung its bottom two levels reach, so the halves are covered by render checks 1 to 4 and by 4a's live run, not by this one. The CSV's reviewed-with-no-original branch was executed against the shipped function rather than on the live site, for the reason in Current Stage. And the `formatPoints` call sites were exercised only with whole totals.

**9. THE FAKE-MEDIA RIG RECIPE IS UNCHANGED AND WAS RE-VERIFIED, WITH ONE ADDITION.** The two flags, the `.wav` ending in silence (eight seconds this time), the labels read BEFORE the run, and `page.on("dialog", d => d.accept())` registered before any click. **THE ADDITION: THE SUBMIT FLOW HAS AN IN-PAGE CONFIRM, NOT A `window.confirm`.** "Submit as graded assignment" reveals a second button reading "Submit for a grade", and the dialog handler is irrelevant to it; the first attempt this session clicked only the first button and silently did nothing. The student's practice DELETE has the same two-step shape ("Delete" then a "Delete" beside "Cancel"), and so does the instructor's assignment delete ("Delete" then "Yes, delete"). **THREE DIFFERENT TWO-STEP CONFIRMS, NONE OF THEM A BROWSER DIALOG.**

==> **10. THERE ARE NOW TWO KINDS OF DATE IN THIS APP AND THEY WANT OPPOSITE ANSWERS. READ `lib/app-time.ts`'s HEADER BEFORE TOUCHING ANY DATE.** A **timestamp** (a stored moment with an hour on it) reads US Central through that module. A **date-only value** (a due date, an available date) has no time on it, is stored as midnight UTC, and is still read back in UTC so a day picked as "Sep 14" reads "Sep 14".

  **THE FOUR DATE-ONLY FORMATTERS ARE NAMED IN THAT HEADER AND MUST NOT MOVE**: `formatDueDate` in `app/student/page.tsx` and in `components/edit-assignment-form.tsx`, `formatAvailableDate` in `lib/available-date.ts`, `toDateInputValue` in `components/edit-assignment-form.tsx`. **EVERY FUNCTION THAT IS SAFE TO REACH FOR SAYS "Instant" IN ITS NAME.** A find-and-replace that moves a date-only formatter to Central slides every existing due date in the app back one day, silently, on a screen nobody thinks to re-check. Checks 25 to 28 of `check-app-time.mjs` catch exactly that, by running the old build's own versions beside the current ones. <==

==> **11. A GREEN LOCAL RUN OF ANYTHING DATE-RELATED PROVES NOTHING ON THIS MACHINE, AND THIS IS THE MOST REUSABLE THING THE SESSION LEARNED.** The owner's Mac has its default timezone set to **America/Chicago**, which is the same zone the app pins. Unfixed code that names no timezone therefore produces the RIGHT answer locally and the wrong one on Vercel.

  **SO: RUN ANY LOCAL DATE OBSERVATION UNDER `TZ=UTC`.** `scripts/check-app-time.mjs` enforces this for itself (it refuses to run in the ambient zone and re-runs under two others), but a page rendered locally, a one-off node snippet, or a new check script written later has no such guard. **THE GENERAL FORM OF THE TRAP: a check whose environment already satisfies the property being checked.** It is the same shape as a fixture derived from the samples it is meant to test. <==

**12. `scripts/check-app-time.mjs` REACHES INTO GIT AT `95ec986`, AND KNOWS HOW TO LOSE THAT.** Five of its 43 checks compare against the old build by pulling source out of history. If that commit becomes unreachable, those five SKIP by name and print a reason, and the run still exits 0 reporting "All 38 checks passed. 5 SKIPPED". **That is not a failure to investigate**; it means the before-and-after half has aged out and only the absolute expectations remain. Verified by pointing `BASE` at a nonexistent commit.

==> **13. THE UNLOCK IS ONE HELPER WITH FIVE CALLERS, AND A PHASE 3 CHANGE MUST NOT ADD A SIXTH RULE.** `isLockedUntil` in `lib/available-date.ts` is called by the student home and by four server routes (`assignment/start`, `voice/start`, `practice/submit`, `session/start`). **THE WHOLE POINT OF THE FILE IS THAT THE SCREEN AND THE SERVER CANNOT DISAGREE**, and a disagreement there is invisible until a student hits it. It takes `now` as a parameter, which is what makes it testable; keep that. **AND IT READS THE PICKED DAY IN UTC ON PURPOSE**, the same read `formatAvailableDate` does, so the date shown and the moment the lock lifts always name the same day. That agreement is check 43. <==

## Phase 4b handoff

Written on 2026-09-11, at the end of Phase 4a, while the reasons are still in view. **The Phase 4 handoff below it is kept as history; its items 3, 5 and 6 are the ones 4a acted on.**

==> **1. THE ONE THING A STUDENT WOULD SEE IF 4b SLIPPED, NAMED EXACTLY, BECAUSE THE KICKOFF ASKED FOR IT.**

  **A REVIEWED SUBMISSION TODAY SHOWS THE STUDENT THE INSTRUCTOR'S LEVEL AND POINTS UNDER THE AI'S OWN COMMENT, WITH NOTHING SAYING SO, AND HIDES THE INSTRUCTOR'S NOTE ENTIRELY.** Concretely, on the run just completed: the student's results page and student home would have read `Close, Minimal, 7.5 / 15` above the sentence "The student did not request any commitment from Alex", which is the AI's explanation of the rung it chose (`Unsatisfactory`) and not of the rung the student is being shown. The 182-character note explaining WHY the instructor disagreed sits in `instructor_note` and renders nowhere.

  **SO IT READS AS THE AI CONTRADICTING ITSELF**, and the student's most likely reading is that the grader is broken. That is worse than the pre-4a state, where the comment and the level always came from the same judgment.

  ==> **THIS IS WHY NOBODY REVIEWS REAL STUDENT WORK BETWEEN 4a AND 4b. IT IS A PROCESS RULE, NOT A CODE GUARD, AND NOTHING IN THE APP ENFORCES IT.** Tom can press Mark reviewed today and the student will see the above. At the end of 4a, `reviewed_at` is null on every row in the database, so the rule has not yet been broken; the first real review is the moment it can be. <==

**2. WHAT 4b HAS TO BUILD, AND EVERY FIELD IT NEEDS IS ALREADY STORED.** Pending Review and Reviewed on the student's results page and student home, read from `sessions.reviewed_at`. The AI comment LABELLED as the AI's, using `ScoreItem.scored_by`, which is `"instructor"` only where the level was changed. The instructor's note, from `ScoreItem.instructor_note`, **rendered as plain text**. And the per-submission CSV's new columns plus its Status line. **No migration, no new column and no change to the review route should be needed**: 4a wrote every one of these fields deliberately so that 4b is a rendering piece.

==> **3. `scored_by` MARKS THE LEVEL AND NOT THE NOTE, AND 4b WILL BE TEMPTED TO READ IT AS "THIS LINE WAS TOUCHED".** It is `"instructor"` exactly when the chosen level differs from the stored original's level, recomputed from `public.submission_ai_scores` on every save. So a criterion can carry an instructor NOTE while still reading `"ai"`, which is correct: the note is always the instructor's by construction and needs no marker, whereas the level is the thing that could have come from either of them. **The label 4b needs is on the COMMENT** ("the AI said X"), and the case that needs it is precisely `scored_by === "instructor"`. <==

**4. THE INSTRUCTOR'S NOTE IS STUDENT-FACING TEXT AND IS ALREADY BOUNDED, SO DO NOT RE-VALIDATE IT ON THE WAY OUT.** `MAX_REVIEW_NOTE_CHARS` (1000) is enforced in the route on a TRIMMED string, and an empty note is stored as absent rather than as an empty string, so `instructor_note` is either missing or is real content. React escapes what it renders; nothing anywhere puts it into HTML by hand, and nothing should start.

==> **5. THE AI ORIGINAL IS NOT A FALLBACK FOR ANYTHING THE STUDENT SEES, AND 4b MUST NOT READ IT.** `public.submission_ai_scores` has no policy that lets a student read it, proved by behaviour with the student's own browser key. Any student-facing render must come from `sessions.score`, which after a review is the instructor's and IS the grade. If a 4b screen ever needs "what the AI said", that is a design change and not a lookup. <==

**6. THE GRADEBOOK EXPORT (THE TAIL) IS STILL OUT, AND SO IS THE REST OF PHASE 3.** Unchanged by 4a: the two renames, gatekeeper pairing, persona voice, and enable or disable any scenario.

==> **7. THE FAKE-MEDIA RIG NOW HAS A SECOND TRAP RECORDED, AND IT COST THIS SESSION A WHOLE CALL. `End session` ON A PRACTICE OPENS A `window.confirm` (`components/voice-conversation.tsx` line 686), AND PLAYWRIGHT AUTO-DISMISSES DIALOGS UNLESS A HANDLER IS REGISTERED.** The click lands, the confirm is answered NO, `endCall()` never runs, and the call simply keeps going: it looks exactly like a click that missed its target. Register `page.on("dialog", d => d.accept())` before clicking. The transcript is saved server-side as the call runs, so an abandoned call is resumable rather than lost. Everything else in the 2c recipe is unchanged and was re-verified: the two flags, the `.wav` ending in several seconds of silence, and reading the labels before the run. <==

**8. THE STUDENT ACCOUNTS IN B1 ARE `1studentb1@gmail.com` (Ash Addle) AND `2studentb1@gmail.com` (Barry Bedford). There is no `student@test.com` anywhere in `profiles`.** Recorded because this session lost time to it, and because the sandbox correctly REFUSED to let one password be tried against several accounts in a loop, which is indistinguishable from credential stuffing whatever the intent. Ask the owner which account, do not probe.

==> **9. `app/student/page.tsx` RENDERS A SCORE WITHOUT `formatPoints`, IN TWO PLACES, AND 4b IS EDITING THAT FILE ANYWAY.** Lines 486 and 578 interpolate `score_total` and `score.max_total` straight into a template string. **NOTHING IS WRONG ON SCREEN TODAY**: these are JavaScript numbers, so 62.5 prints as `62.5` and 15 as `15`, which is what `formatPoints` would also produce. It is recorded because 4a's commit asserts the rule that points become text in exactly one place, and these two lines are the remaining exception, now carrying REVIEWED totals as well as AI ones. Route them through `formatPoints` while 4b is in the file. Do not treat it as a bug fix; it is the rule catching up with the last two call sites. <==

**10. `submission_reviews.score_total` IS `not null` WHILE `sessions.score_total` IS NULLABLE, AND THAT ASYMMETRY IS DELIBERATE.** A review always writes a real total (`buildReviewedScore` computes it from the ladder, and a session with no score cannot be reviewed at all), so the log column refuses to record a grade change that changed nothing legible. The consequence to know: a future caller that ever passed null would get a constraint violation raised INSIDE the transaction rather than one of the named refusals, so the message would be a Postgres one rather than a sentence. No caller can do that today, and `save_submission_review()` is granted to `service_role` only.

Nothing was added to `package.json`.

## Phase 2c handoff

Written on 2026-09-10, at the end of 2b-2, while the reasons are still in view. **The 2b-2 handoff written at the end of 2b-1 follows it and is kept as history; every one of its eight items is now closed or superseded.**

==> **1. THE APP GRADES AGAIN, SO THE URGENT HALF IS OVER AND 2c IS NOT A RACE.** The upload field is live and the rendering is done. What is left for 2c is the things that needed a graded submission to exist before they could be run at all, plus one determinism measurement that has been deferred twice. <==

**2. THE TWO GATE ITEMS THAT WERE WAITING FOR A GRADED SUBMISSION CAN NOW RUN, AND ONE OF THEM IS CHEAPER THAN IT LOOKS.** Gate 4b's export-content check (export a class containing a SCORED submission and confirm the score, the transcript and any reset rows are in the CSV) and the Gate 5 item 12 leftover both needed a submission to exist. **2b-2 proved a submission can be made without a camera in the room**: see item 5 below. Neither needs new code.

==> **3. NOTHING STILL MEASURES THAT THE SCORER IS DETERMINISTIC, AND TOM'S FAIRNESS ARGUMENT RESTS ON IT.** Deferred by 2b-1 to "2b-2 or 2c", and 2b-2 did not do it. The claim is a fixed seed (7) and zero temperature; the MEASUREMENT is scoring one transcript twice and comparing the six level NAMES for exact equality, which is stricter and more legible than the old "within a few points". **2b-2 leaves behind exactly the material to do it with**: a real scored transcript, and a fake-media rig that can produce another. A student disputing a grade is the worst possible moment to discover this was never checked. <==

**4. TWO OPEN ISSUES FROM 2b-2'S CLEANUP ARE REAL AND BOTH ARE WRITTEN UP ABOVE.** An unfinished session is invisible and undeletable and holds its assignment hostage, which collides with the fact that a rubric can never be replaced on an existing assignment. And a graded practice whose scoring failed is a redirect loop, so the retry the student-facing note offers works only while they stay on the tab. **Neither is a 2c build item unless the owner moves it**: the first is filed for Phase 3, the second was explicitly out of scope by the 2b-2 kickoff's own words.

==> **5. THE FAKE-MEDIA RIG WORKS AND THE EXACT RECIPE IS HERE, BECAUSE THE FLAG NAME IS THE WHOLE GAME.**

  - The flag is **`--use-fake-device-for-media-stream`**, with `--use-fake-ui-for-media-stream` beside it. `--use-fake-device-for-media-capture` is NOT a Chrome flag: it is silently ignored and getUserMedia returns THE REAL CAMERA. **Verify by reading the track labels: `fake_device_0` and "Fake Default Audio Input" mean the rig is on; a hardware name means it is not.** Do that BEFORE the run.
  - Speech comes from `--use-file-for-fake-audio-capture=<a .wav>`, and **the file must END IN SEVERAL SECONDS OF SILENCE**. Chrome loops it with no gap, so wall-to-wall speech never lets a turn end and the whole call produces an empty transcript on both sides. Six seconds of padding was enough. Make the .wav with macOS `say` plus `afconvert`, then append silence.
  - It was driven from a standalone Playwright script in the scratchpad rather than through the Playwright MCP config, deliberately: editing `.mcp.json` mid-session may need a restart to take effect, and losing the session's context to find out is the expensive way to learn it. The MCP browser stayed signed in as the instructor while the script drove the student. <==

**6. THE ONE THING IN THE RENDERING NOBODY HAS SEEN YET IS A SEVEN-CRITERION RUBRIC.** Every live check used the Discovery file's six. The Presentation rubric has seven, it previews correctly through the form (that was exercised), and nothing in the renderer counts criteria, but no score has ever been rendered from it. Cheap to fold into any 2c run.

**7. WHAT THE PROOF RUN DELIBERATELY DID NOT COVER, STATED SO IT IS NOT ASSUMED.** No score with a HALF TOTAL has been rendered live, for the arithmetic reason in Current Stage above (an even number of half-valued rungs sums whole); it is covered by the render check instead. And the CSV's Level column has never been seen EMPTY on a real download, because that needs one of the 32 pre-level scores, all of which live in another instructor's class; the no-rubric probe reproduced the missing-rubric half of that state but not the missing-level half.

**8. THE SCORING PROMPT IS STILL A REVIEWED DELIVERABLE AND 2b-2 DID NOT TOUCH IT.** The system message carries only a positional note about the ladder's order and Tom's own level descriptors do the defining. One prompt shape, no branch. If 2c changes it, that is a reviewed change and not a detail.

## Phase 2b-2 handoff (written at the end of 2b-1, kept as history, every item now closed or superseded)

Written on 2026-09-10, at the end of 2b-1, while the reasons are still in view. **The Phase 2b handoff written at the end of 2a is kept below it, because several of its items are still live.**

==> **1. THE SWITCH-ON IS STILL ONE CONSTANT, AND TURNING IT ON IS NOW THE URGENT HALF.** `RUBRIC_UPLOAD_ENABLED` in `components/create-assignment-form.tsx` is still `false`. **UNTIL IT IS TRUE, NOTHING IN THE APP IS GRADED**, because 2b-1 detached every rubric. That is the agreed intermediate state and it is the reason 2b-2 follows immediately rather than after anything else. <==

==> **2. THE `items` OVERLOADING IS RESOLVED, AND THE RESOLUTION MUST NOT BE UNDONE.** 2a's handoff called this the sharpest edge in 2b. It was settled by CUTTING the legacy shape out of reach rather than teaching readers to tell two shapes apart: no assignment points at the flat rubric, so no reader can resolve one. **THERE IS NO DISCRIMINATOR, NO VERSION FIELD AND NO SHAPE PROBE, AND THAT IS THE POINT.** The comment on `public.rubrics.items` says so in the database itself. What exists instead is a REFUSAL in `lib/scoring.ts`: a rubric whose criteria carry no levels stops scoring with a legible error. That is enforcement of the ruling, not an exception to it, and it is load-bearing, because without it a re-run of 2a's backfill produces a TypeError deep inside a map() rather than a message. <==

**3. THE RENDERING IS THE WHOLE OF 2b-2's VISIBLE WORK, AND `ScoreItem.level` IS ALREADY THERE WAITING.** `components/rubric-score.tsx` groups by a `category` that a criterion does not have and labels by a `label` it does not have, so it currently falls back to criterion ids in one "Other" group. See the knowingly-wrong cast in Open Issues. **`level` IS OPTIONAL ON THE TYPE ON PURPOSE**: 32 stored scores predate it and carry none, so whatever renders a level must handle a score without one rather than assume it.

**4. CHECK 16 OF `supabase/phase-2a-tests.sql` NOW PASSES VACUOUSLY AND EXPIRES FOR REAL IN 2b-2.** "Every assignment's rubric matches its scenario's" is true at zero rows. **The first rubric Tom attaches makes a correct database report FAILED.** The replacement query is inside that file and the instruction stands: apply it when the field goes live, not before. `supabase/phase-2b1-tests.sql` carries the same warning about its own checks 10 and 11, which are point-in-time counts (32 scored sessions, 5 assignments) and not invariants.

==> **5. THE SCORING PROMPT IS A REVIEWED DELIVERABLE AND ITS SHAPE IS DELIBERATE.** The system message carries only a POSITIONAL note about the ladder's order; Tom's own level descriptors do the defining, per the owner's 2026-09-10 ruling. The per-criterion block repeats the full ladder under every criterion even though Tom's six names come from one header row and are therefore uniform: stating them once would assume a uniformity nothing in the stored shape guarantees, and the model is told to copy from THAT CRITERION'S OWN list. **One prompt shape, no branch, so the prompt that was reviewed is the prompt that runs.** If 2b-2 changes it, it is a reviewed change, not a detail. <==

**6. ONE PATH IS BUILT AND REVIEWED BUT COULD NOT BE EXECUTED, AND IT IS NOT A GAP IN 2b-1's WORK.** The rubric check in `app/api/practice/submit/route.ts` is practically unreachable: a conversation-only practice never reaches `scored`, so the status check refuses first, and a scored TEXT practice is refused by the mode check. Reaching it needs a scored voice+video practice with a real recording, which needs a camera, which is 2c. The refusal observed in the 2b-1 run was 409 "This practice is not finished and scored yet", from the status check, and that is reported as what actually happened rather than dressed up as the rubric branch.

**7. WHAT 2b-1 UNBLOCKED FOR THE OLD GATES.** A generated persona CAN now be graded, because grading reads the assignment rather than the scenario, and `app/api/instructor/scaffold/generate/route.ts` had its comment corrected to say so. That is what unblocks the ONE export-content check Gate 4b could not run (export a class containing a SCORED submission and confirm the score, the transcript and any reset rows are in the CSV) and the one Gate 5 item that failed for the same reason. Neither needs 2b-2 first; both need a graded submission, which needs a camera, so they ride with 2c.

**8. THE ASSIGNMENT EDIT FORM IS SAFE AND THIS WAS VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** `components/edit-assignment-form.tsx` issues a partial update naming six fields and never mentions `rubric_id`, so editing an assignment after 2b-2 cannot silently un-grade it. It is written down because it is exactly the kind of thing that gets assumed.

## Phase 2b handoff (written at the end of 2a, kept because several items are still live)

Written on 2026-09-09, at the end of 2a, while the reasons are still in view.

==> **1. THE SWITCH-ON IS ONE CONSTANT, AND IT IS A CLIENT FLAG ONLY.** `RUBRIC_UPLOAD_ENABLED` in `components/create-assignment-form.tsx` is `false`. Flipping it to `true` enables the control, the preview call and the confirm interaction; nothing else in that file is conditional.

**THE ROUTE ALREADY ACCEPTS A POSTED RUBRIC TODAY.** The constant hides the control and stops the FORM sending a file. It does not make `app/api/instructor/assignment/create/route.ts` refuse one. A crafted request can attach a rubric right now, and that is exactly how test 4 above was run: the whole rubric branch was exercised in production while the field was switched off. **DO NOT READ "upload is off" AS A SERVER GUARANTEE.** It is harmless at present for specific reasons rather than general ones (scoring still reads `scenario.rubric_id` until 2b, the route is behind `requireInstructorRequest()`, and only two instructor accounts exist), and if the route ever needs to actually refuse a rubric that has to be added in the route. <==

==> **2. THE MOMENT THE READERS MOVE, THE `items` OVERLOADING BECOMES LIVE. THIS IS THE SHARPEST EDGE IN 2b.** `rubrics.items` now holds TWO DIFFERENT SHAPES: the original flat `RubricItem[]` (29 items at 10 points each), and the Phase 2a criterion shape (weighted criteria, each with six levels). Both are arrays, so the check constraint accepts either and nothing in the database distinguishes them.

**TODAY THAT IS SAFE, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN LUCKY.** All four readers resolve their rubric through `scenario.rubric_id`:

  app/student/session/[id]/results/page.tsx   .eq("id", scenario?.rubric_id)
  app/api/session/end/route.ts                .eq("id", scenario.rubric_id)
  app/instructor/submission/[id]/page.tsx     .eq("id", scenario?.rubric_id)
  lib/instructor-submission.ts                .eq("id", scenario?.rubric_id)

2a never writes `scenarios.rubric_id`, so a new-shape rubric cannot reach any of them. **THE FIRST READER 2b POINTS AT `assignments.rubric_id` CAN RECEIVE EITHER SHAPE AND MUST DECIDE WHICH IT HAS**, or it will walk criteria as though they were flat items. Decide the discriminator deliberately (a `version` field on the row, or a shape probe) rather than letting each reader guess. <==

**3. `max_total` IS A COLUMN, NOT AN ARRAY ELEMENT.** `rubrics.max_total`. Do not look for it inside `items`; putting it there would pass the array check and then read as a criterion to everything that iterates the list.

**4. CHECK 16 OF `supabase/phase-2a-tests.sql` EXPIRES WHEN THE FORM IS ENABLED.** "Every assignment's rubric matches its scenario's" is true only because the backfill just made it so. The first rubric Tom attaches makes a correct database report FAILED. The file carries the replacement query and says to apply it when the field goes live, not before.

**5. CRITERION IDS ENCODE POSITION** (`3-close` in Discovery, `4-close` in Presentation), which is why they are unique by construction. Do not simplify them to a name-only slug; that would create a collision risk that does not exist today. The note is on the type in `lib/types.ts`.

**6. THE PARSER KEEPS COLUMN B WEIGHTS AND DROPS THE ROW 3 MULTIPLIERS**, on purpose. Every level already stores its absolute points, so a stored multiplier would be a second way to compute the same grade, and two records that can disagree about a grade is what this project keeps getting bitten by.

**7. WHAT 2b STILL HAS TO TEST THAT 2a COULD NOT.** The graded submission path needs a camera and a microphone, and the app locks the camera for graded voice and video, so a browser with no media device is refused before the practice starts. That path is exercised in 2c, and the kickoff warns it is the single setup step most likely to cost a session.
